# Platform Adaptation Notes

Field-tested pitfalls and conclusions per platform; each entry records only the pitfall and the fix. Protocol-interop conclusions all come from real buses and real panels. Linux is organized by distribution → desktop environment, Windows / macOS by version; add new conclusions to the matching section and sync the Chinese version ([docs/zh/adaptation.md](/zh/adaptation.md)) in the same batch.

## Cross-Platform (build chain / FFI)

### Build and Linking

- Library packages must not carry a `link` section (moon would generate a main-less exe and the build fails); all link flags come from `scripts/prebuild.py` via the `--moonbit-unstable-prebuild` hook, which emits link_configs auto-propagated to every main package depending on the library module. Never hand-write `cc-link-flags` in any package.
- prebuild script constraints: stdout must carry only the final JSON (progress goes to stderr); all flags go in the single link_flags string with hand-controlled ordering (GNU ld is single-pass); the script's cwd is the consumer's project root, so library paths must be absolute.
- Recent moon deprecates `moon.mod.json` / `moon.pkg.json` (`moon fmt` migrates in one shot); `moon doc` only accepts the new format, and older moon does not understand it.
- MSVC misreads BOM-less UTF-8 source as cp936; Chinese comments produce fake preprocessing errors (the reported line has no such directive): CMake adds `/utf-8` for MSVC.
- Recent moon deprecates implicit trait-method promotion: call sites use the explicit static form `ViewLike::method(obj)`; `impl Trait for X` declaration sites need explicit `pub extend X with Trait::{...}` (generate the method list from `moon check --no-render` output, don't hand-copy); black-box tests must qualify in-package symbols as `@<package>.xxx`. These warnings are missed by incremental builds — a clean full build surfaces them all.
- The mooncakes package-page README renderer only displays images with absolute external URLs (field-tested across the 0.5.3–0.5.5 releases: package-relative image paths render neither as plain Markdown nor as HTML — HTML blocks are dropped entirely — while an external CI badge displays fine); switch hero images to jsdelivr absolute URLs (`cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/<user>/<repo>@master/...`, balanced reachability inside and outside China; `@master` references lag a few hours behind due to CDN caching). GitHub-side rendering via the camo proxy is unaffected.
- The toolchain triple (moon / moonc / core) must ship as one matched set (field-tested during the 0.5.3 release): `moon upgrade` aborted midway in a non-interactive terminal (`IO error: not a terminal`) after swapping the binaries but before touching `~/.moon/lib/core`, leaving moonc 0.10.14 paired with core 0.10.12 — the old core lacks `StringView::exact_view` (four "no method" errors in sysdata), and the mismatch only surfaced when a version bump in moon.mod forced a full re-check; earlier incremental `moon check`/`moon test` greens were false. Nightly version numbers are delisted the same day (the CLI server 403s any named historical version), so pinning via `MOONBIT_INSTALL_VERSION` is not viable — the only repair is rerunning `install/unix.sh` with no version to install the latest full bundle (binaries and core replaced in one pass), matching CI. Verify by checking that `moon version --all` and `~/.moon/lib/core/moon.mod` agree, then bumping the version to force a full re-check: 133 tests green.

### CI runner environment vs. the development machine (2026-10-15, first time the three jobs reached the test step)

- **Environment**: GitHub Actions ubuntu-latest / windows-latest / macos-latest. The development machine is Ubuntu 24.04 + X11 + XFCE: session bus present, notification service present, display server present. None of that is guaranteed on a runner, so tests that were green locally failed on CI - each for its own reason, not one shared cause.
- **Notifications (a real contract bug, fixed)**: on the runner the session bus answers but no notification service is installed, so `@dbus.notify_caps()` returns `NfFailed(ServiceUnknown)` while `notify_supported()` says there is no backend. The old `core/notify_caps()` forwarded the dbus error directly, which made the two probes disagree. Now `notify_caps()` decides "is there a backend" through `notify_backend()` first and only asks `GetCapabilities` once the D-Bus backend is confirmed. One-line contract: when `notify_supported()` is false, `notify_caps()` must give `Unsupported`.
- **Declarative mount (the assertion encoded a dev-machine accident, fixed)**: `mount_window_render(render, ...)` is `mount_window(render(), ...)`, so render runs **exactly once, regardless of whether the window mounts**. The test also demanded "zero runs when no app comes back", which only holds on a machine where mounting succeeds. Now asserted as "exactly once".
- **The text/layout family needs real fonts**: when Pango measures nothing, "text width = 0" cascades into link widths, textarea display rows and mdview baselines, and the failing assertion does not show where it started. The Linux job now opens with a "fonts and Pango measurement" step: `fc-list` count, `fc-match`, plus a small C probe that measures `"AAAA"` in `"Sans 14"` (development-machine baseline: 48x23) and fails the step if it measures nothing - separating "code broken" from "machine has no fonts". `fonts-dejavu-core` joined the apt list so at least one family exists.
- **Windows manifest-migration loop**: PowerShell has no `Select-String -QuietPattern` parameter (the form is `-Pattern … -Quiet`). With the wrong name the loop dies on parameter binding in the **first** round, which looks like "migration did not converge" while nothing actually ran.
- **Verification**: the local control experiment `env -u DISPLAY moon test core ui dbus sys` (551/551) reproduces the runner's "no display service" shape; the text-family claim waits for the CI diagnostic step to report.

### MoonBit cfg(platform=)

- **Decision (2026-10-11): platform dispatch drops cfg for good, even if the toolchain later fixes it** — MoonBit-side cross-platform selection is always "runtime probe + pattern matching", probed in the shape of render's existing degradation probe (paint a stroke and verify pixels, non-zero session handle); now a hard rule in AGENTS.md.

### Native render/text matrix and the three-layer text divergence (new-stack decision, 2026-10-11)

- **Platform matrix**: Linux = GTK3 window + cairo raster + Pango text (system libraries, dynamic linking, no LGPL obligations); Windows = pure-MoonBit software rasterizer + DirectWrite text + WIC image decoding (all OS components, zero third-party dependencies); macOS = CoreGraphics + CoreText (stub placeholder, backend not yet developed). vcpkg static linking of cairo/pango was evaluated and dropped: the dependency weight (glib/fontconfig/harfbuzz and friends) plus the LGPL static-linking "must provide a relinking path" obligation contradict this library's "native, lightweight, no-strings" positioning.
- **The three-layer text divergence and countermeasures**: ① **Height** — line height is unified as `max(engine natural line height, ceil(size×K))`, with K a constant measured across the three platforms against the fattest font (Microsoft YaHei); line height becomes a pure function of size, fallback fonts no longer affect it, the surplus is centered with half-leading and the rounding rule is fixed; fonts whose natural height exceeds the floor grow the line in place (graceful degradation, no glyph clipping). ② **Width** — wrapping/truncation/ellipsis are entirely MoonBit component-layer decisions (the platform only supplies measurement); textarea's wrap_units/layout_lines pure functions stay; CJK advance≈em shows no real difference, Latin differs by one word (wrap/ellipsis edge points), no content loss. ③ **Pixels** — the vector layer (rects/paths/AA/25 blend modes/gradients) is the MoonBit rasterizer's single implementation on all three platforms, bit-identical, assertions shared cross-platform; text metrics/pixels are inherently per-platform (the old stack already had three engines: Pango/DirectWrite/CoreText), so assertions split into "structural (shared) / numeric (per-platform baseline directory)" tiers, and the default font is encoded per platform into the theme (Windows pins the YaHei family, Linux follows fontconfig).
- **Linux dividend**: cairo and the MoonBit rasterizer can be pixel-diffed against each other — cairo serves as the reference answer for accepting the self-built engine (the acceptance method of rasterizer batch W4).
- **Platform C stub organization**: a single multi-branch `stub.c` + prebuild macros (see the win/stub.c precedent in the "Windows window foundation W2" section); the render/text stub merges follow the same pattern, with the Windows side pulling in no C render/text libraries (only WIC counts as an OS component).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · pure-MoonBit rasterizer (W4, Windows/mac render backend)

- **Architecture**: `render` gains four pure-MoonBit files — `raster_path.mbt` (path commands / affine matrix / adaptive Bézier flattening / arc→Bézier with kappa), `raster_fill.mbt` (4×4 supersampled nonzero-winding scanline fill + polyline-offset stroking with miter limit 10 / butt caps), `raster_blend.mbt` (25 blend modes: nine Porter-Duff closed forms in premultiplied space + 16 W3C formulas in non-premultiplied space + gradient evaluation), and `raster.mbt` (the `Raster` session, method-for-method mirroring `Cairo`); the `Painter` facade becomes a two-backend enum with **runtime probing** (`Cairo::over` failing to open a session ⇒ pure path), no compile directives (AGENTS rule 11). The stubs merge into `render/stub.c`: the real cairo implementation hangs off the `WM_STUB_CAIRO` macro compiled only by prebuild (Linux), non-Linux keeps placeholders to satisfy the linker, and the probe falls to the pure path when it sees the placeholder. The text layer probes via `Painter::cairo_id()`: with cairo it paints into the session context; the pure backend returns None (W5's DirectWrite shape paints straight into the Bitmap), and `Painter::raster()` fetches the pure session.
- **Coverage quantization**: 4×4 supersampling, 16 samples per pixel, coverage 0..256 (16 per sample) — the per-pixel difference from cairo's analytic coverage is absorbed by the "parity tolerance band ≤16 (one sample step)"; full-coverage and zero-coverage pixels are exact. Crossing tests use the half-open `(ay<=ys)!=(by2<=ys)` to avoid double-counting vertices; the nonzero sweep collects spans where winding ≠ 0, and open polylines auto-close for fill (**the closing edge IS the wrap edge, so the edge count is n, not n+1 — writing n+1 indexes pts[n] out of bounds; unit tests with all-closed paths never see it, the real window crashed within 45 frames. Lesson: open paths need dedicated cases**).
- **Premultiply rounding must be two steps, never merged**: source premultiply lives in the 255 domain (`(v·a+127)/255`), coverage scaling in the 256 domain (`·cov/256`); merging into one 65536-domain step swallows the 255/256 ratio (opaque full coverage measured 254 instead of 255, off-by-one everywhere). The Normal composite `s+(d·inv+127)/255` matches Bitmap::paint exactly, so rect paths stay bit-identical.
- **Known divergences from cairo (inside the tolerance band)**: ① 16-level quantized AA coverage vs cairo's analytic coverage; ② nearest-neighbor blit vs cairo bilinear (1:1 and opaque pixels are bit-exact; scaled sampling differs — the sample-domain clamping semantic is mirrored: sampling never leaves the source sub-rect, so sprite atlases don't bleed); ③ gradient stops interpolated non-premultiplied (CSS/W3C) vs cairo's premultiplied space (semi-transparent stop midpoints differ, opaque stops identical); ④ stroke device width = user width × sqrt(|det|), an approximation for non-uniform pen scaling; ⑤ skewed-clip degrades to the bounding box. The 25 W3C blend values pass the same exact assertions on both platforms (cairo's operators and the hand formulas agree in the 8-bit domain).
- **MoonBit pitfalls hit this batch**: sin/cos live in `moonbitlang/core/math` (`@math.sin`; Double has no such methods); uppercase `let` is illegal — use `const`; scientific notation `1e-9` fails to parse, spell it `0.000000001` (repeated an old pitfall; suggest grepping `1e-`); trailing commas on match arms are illegal, including after tuple arms `(a,b,c)`. Test-side: tests that used `Cairo::over` directly became "backend probe + structural assertions" shared cross-platform; decode-dependent tests gained a `decode_available()` probe guard (missing environment skips instead of hard-failing).
- **Verification** (2026-10-11, Windows machine): render 43/43 (including the new "path/stroke/arc two-backend parity (AA tolerance)" test), icons 4/4 (**all 803 icon paths produce pixels** through the pure rasterizer), core 62 / input 10 / mdview 26 still green, theme 7/8 (the remaining font-measurement case belongs to W5); native-window smoke 45 frames with clean exit (every real-window frame goes through the pure rasterizer); whole-repo check 287/68 unchanged. The Linux cairo path is untouched bit-for-bit (stub content moved verbatim); on Linux the parity test IS the "cairo vs pure" acceptance — pending a user-side rerun.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows branches of the system-capability stub (W14: files / env / subprocess / file dialog)

- **The third-party `chensuiyi/subproc` had to go — and master had already dropped it**: the package describes itself as "Synchronous **Linux** process management" and relies on Linux-only machinery (pidfd, procfs, poll). Locally, `.mooncakes/chensuiyi/subproc/src/native.c:7` dies under MSVC with `fatal error C1083: cannot open include file 'poll.h'`, which blocked the whole `sys` chain (and downstream chart/ui/examples/native-app) from even compiling on Windows. The other registry candidate, `trkbt10/subprocess`, covers subprocesses on Unix/macOS only (Windows side is IPC sockets), so it doesn't fit either. **Fix: port master's already-proven three-platform implementation** (master's implementation calls its own C stubs `proc_spawn/proc_wait/proc_getpid`, zero third-party deps) into `sys/sys_stub.c` as C, symbols `yue_sys_proc_*`, semantics bit-for-bit: POSIX branch shared by Linux/macOS (`fork` → `setsid`/`setpgid` on macOS → `dup2` temp files → `execvpe` on Linux / `execvp` + `*_NSGetEnviron` on macOS, `_exit(127)` on exec failure, `_exit(126)` on `chdir` failure); Windows uses `CreateProcessW` + `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, `STARTF_USESTDHANDLES` with inheritable handles, environment block sorted by name and terminated with a double wide NUL, empty block = inherit parent environment. **Key design difference: only the pid is returned, never a handle** — Windows `wait` reopens it via `OpenProcess(SYNCHRONIZE|TERMINATE|QUERY_LIMITED)` and closes immediately, which removes an entire cross-call handle table and its leak surface (the original plan of "return hProcess + `proc_drop`" was scrapped).
- **`__uuidof` does not exist in MSVC's C mode**: writing `__uuidof(FileOpenDialog)` fails with `error C4233: nonstandard extension used: keyword only supported in C++` (the coclass declarations in the SDK headers are C++-only). In C, take the `extern const GUID` constants from the headers (`CLSID_FileOpenDialog` / `IID_IFileDialog` / `IID_IShellItem`), which come from **uuid.lib** — hence `scripts/prebuild.py`'s Windows branch now issues `ole32.lib shell32.lib uuid.lib` for `sys` (MSVC takes them as positional arguments; `-l` triggers D9002).
- **UCRT has no `_getwcwd`**: the name looks like the wide getcwd but is declared nowhere in the SDK — MSVC only emits `warning C4013: undefined, default external return int`, truncating the returned `wchar_t*` to int and then dereferencing it: compiles, crashes. Use the Win32 `GetCurrentDirectoryW` instead (two passes: size 0 to query, then fetch).
- **Environment variables must go wide, and Windows has no "exists but empty" state**: `getenv`/`_putenv_s` are the ANSI versions and round-trip values through the process ANSI code page (936 here), so a UTF-8 Chinese value comes back as mojibake (鍊糀) — caught live by `sys/envx_wbtest.mbt`. Switched to `GetEnvironmentVariableW`/`_wputenv_s` (query size with 0 first). Related semantic difference: on Windows `_wputenv_s(name, L"")` **is the delete operation**, so reading back an empty-value write yields `None`, not `Some("")`; the test now asserts per platform. Files follow the same rule: UTF-8 in, wide API out (`FindFirstFileW`/`_wfopen`/`_wremove`) — the A versions misfire on Chinese user-directory names.
- **`ShellExecuteW` succeeds when the return value is > 32**: it returns the legacy HINSTANCE-style status (≤32 are `SE_ERR_*`), so `>32 ? 0 : -1` is the correct judgement. Its `open` verb carries the system associations (URL → default browser, document → associated app), which is exactly the role `xdg-open` plays in the POSIX branch.
- **In the placeholder branch, disk I/O that has nothing to do with graphics must still be real**: `render/stub.c`'s non-Linux branch had `fsw_write_file` as `return 0` (placeholder to keep linking), while the PNG encoder itself lives in pure MoonBit (`png.mbt`) and only needs one write — so chart export (`ci_save`) failed with `WriteFailed` on Windows every time. Rule of thumb: a placeholder should only cover "the backend capability this platform hasn't implemented yet", not standard I/O.
- **Three platform splits in the tests**: ① in the Windows CRT, `/tmp` means "tmp under the current drive root", so if that directory doesn't exist the write fails — temp paths must come from `%TEMP%`/`$TMPDIR` per platform (separator too); ② cmd emits `\r\n`, so assertions on `echo` output can't hard-code `\n`; ③ **when tests run from Git Bash, PATH contains `/usr/bin/*.exe` (`echo`/`cat`/`sleep`/`true`), so POSIX command names "appear to work" and mask the real differences** — from cmd they all fail; hence every round-trip goes through a platform dispatcher (POSIX `sh -c`, Windows `cmd /c`). Unix-convention-specific assertions (exit 127 for a missing command, self-inflicted SIGTERM, forced `LC_ALL=C`, `/etc/hostname`) run on the POSIX side only; Windows gets the equivalent (`CreateProcessW` failure surfaces as `SpawnFailed`) or prints a skip.
- **Without a text layer, self-drawn widgets collapse to size 0**: on the pure-rasterizer platform `@text.measure` returns `None` and `attach_text_measure`'s fallback is `(0.0, 0.0)`, so width-from-text buttons/labels/links neither measure nor get hit — 20 assertions in `ui` are of this kind. Handling: add **runtime probes** at the test layer (`ui_text_guard()` measures `"Ag"`, `ui_decode_guard()` decodes one GIF); when the probe fails, print a skip and keep running the rest. They turn green again with W5 (DirectWrite) / W9 (WIC). Same treatment for theme's "control height baseline". No estimated widths were smuggled into the library: that would be silent fake data, contrary to the "probe + semantic degradation" decision.
- **Verification** (Windows 10 19045 + MSVC 14.44 + SDK 10.0.26100, moon 0.1.20260920): `moon test -p sys` **163/163** (first time this package compiles and runs on Windows after dropping subproc), chart 189/189, ui 110/110 (20 probe-skipped), theme 8/8, core 70, render 43, input 10, mdview 26, icons 4 all green; per-package `moon check --deny-warn` zero warnings; `moon check ./examples/native-app --deny-warn` passes (previously blocked at the dependency stage); whole-repo red dots stay at 287 errors/68 warnings. `text` remains red on Windows — that is W5's scope (splitting assertions into "shared structural / per-platform numeric baseline"). The look of the real IFileDialog (filters / multi-select / initial folder) is left for the user to confirm.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · batch-A multi-window host on Windows (W15: slot reuse + idempotent wm_init)

- **Batch A only fixed the GTK branch; the same defect remained on Windows**: d1fb1e8 changed `wm_window_new` in the GTK branch of `win/stub.c` to reuse slots by scanning `area == NULL`, while the `_WIN32` branch of the same function still did `wm_next_id++` — repeatedly opening/closing secondary windows exhausts the 32 slots and `open_window` silently returns `None`. Fixed identically, with the marker changed to `hwnd == NULL` (set on a successful create, cleared by the memset in `wm_close`, so a torn-down slot and a never-used one are equally reusable).
- **What actually blocked multi-window was a non-idempotent `wm_init`**: `app_window()` calls `@win.init_backend()` once per window, and Win32's `RegisterClassExW` **fails when the class name is already registered** (`ERROR_CLASS_ALREADY_EXISTS`) — so the first call succeeds and every later one reports "backend unavailable", making `App::open_window` always return `None` on Windows (the main window was fine because it is the first call, which is why every single-window smoke passed). Now the whole body is skipped when the waker window already exists, and `ERROR_CLASS_ALREADY_EXISTS` counts as success. The GTK branch uses `gtk_init_check`, already idempotent, so this only surfaces on Windows — **"the same path called twice" is a shape batch A introduced, invisible to all single-window smokes**.
- **How it is proven (probe package `experiment/multiwin_probe`)**: ① 40 create/destroy rounds, more than the stub's `WM_MAX_WINDOWS=32`, still succeed (`opened=40 failed=0`) — the probe **creates without showing**, so no window flashes on the desktop. ② Two rounds of "open a secondary window → pump 60 ms → close it", printing `sub_wid/primary/active_is_sub/count` and `before/after/main_running`: a new window becomes the active one (`active_is_sub=true`), `primary=false`, and after closing it the main window keeps `running=true` with the count back down. ③ A final `MULTIWIN-DONE` line and exit code 0. Run with `moon run experiment/multiwin_probe`; headless environments print `MULTIWIN SKIP`. Visual confirmation (occlusion/redraw between two windows, preedit inside the secondary window) stays with the user per AGENTS rule 6; the `examples/native-app` start-up smoke on Windows prints `NATIVE-APP-MW wins=1 ...` and exits 0 (clicking its buttons needs injection, i.e. W7).
- **Side fix of the same class**: the `_WIN32` branch calls `snprintf` without including `<stdio.h>`, and MSVC only warns `C4013: implicit declaration, assuming external return int` — the same silent shape as the missing `_getwcwd`. After adding the header the stub compiles warning-free.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · three-platform data roots for the history family (W14e, ported from master)

- **Ported, not re-invented**: the Windows data-root branches for browser history/bookmarks were already in `origin/master` (`bh_browser_defs_win` / `bh_mac_root` / `bh_root_win`); the original port into this branch only carried the Linux half, which is why `bh_supported`/`bm_supported` had degraded to "Linux only". Same approach as W14b: check the other branch first.
- **Only the data root and directory names differ; the logic is shared**: Linux = `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` else `$HOME/.config`; Windows = `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, where Chromium-family profiles sit one level deeper at `<vendor>/<product>/User Data` (that `User Data` layer does not exist on Linux; casing follows the installer, e.g. `Google/Chrome/User Data`, `Microsoft/Edge/User Data`); macOS = `$HOME/Library/Application Support/<vendor>/<product>` (no User Data layer). Profile candidates (`Default`, `Profile 1..24`), the directory scan looking for `History`/`Bookmarks`, and the SQLite/JSON parsing are one shared path, so only the definition table `bh_defs()` and root resolution branch by platform.
- **Proof (real machine with Chrome/Edge installed)**: `bh: profiles=2 items=5` and `bh: downloads=3` are readings from **the actual local browser databases** — on Windows this path used to be gated off and always skipped — and the `bm_bookmarks` end-to-end parse likewise flipped from "skip" to real execution; `moon test -p sys` 163/163, `moon check ./gui/sys --deny-warn` and `./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` warning-free. One stale test assertion (`bm_supported() == (platform=="linux")`) moved to the three-platform rule.
- **Worth noting**: `fsx_list_dir`/`sys_read_text_file` go through the wide-character routing added in this batch, so browser stores under Chinese user directories (`C:\Users\张三\…`) are readable too — not a coincidence, it only holds after the A-version APIs were replaced with the W versions.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · DirectWrite text layer (W5, Windows text lands)

- **`dwrite.h` does not compile in C — use a C++ TU**: `DWRITE_MAKE_OPENTYPE_TAG` / `DWRITE_MAKE_FONT_FEATURE_TAG` expand `static_cast` into enum initializers, so C mode fails with a train of C2275/C2065 (measured). Two options: hand-declare the COM interfaces (one wrong vtable slot away from a crash — rejected) or use a C++ translation unit. **moon's native-stub accepts `.cpp`** (probe-verified: an `extern "C"` round trip worked), so the platform split became: `stub.c` Pango branch (prebuild with `-DWM_STUB_PANGO`) + `stub.c` placeholder branch (macOS etc.) + `dwrite_text.cpp` (Windows, compiled by native-stub, dwrite.h directly visible). Link inputs are issued by prebuild for the text package: `dwrite.lib d2d1.lib ole32.lib uuid.lib gdi32.lib`. **On non-Windows platforms that `.cpp` compiles to an empty TU**, so Linux/mac build chains need a C++ front end (the old shim was C++, so the environment has one).
- **The line box comes from a one-glyph layout's line metrics, not from font design metrics**: measured, a 14px default-family DWrite layout reports 19 (font ascent+descent+lineGap rounded to whole pixels), while computing from design metrics gives 17; worse, with an empty family name, taking the collection's *first* family (not the system default) is off by two more pixels — and that is exactly the number the mixed-font "line box must cover the drawn height" assertion eats. Fix: `pt_font_line_height` builds a `"M"` layout and reads `DWRITE_LINE_METRICS` (height/baseline), making the three paths self-consistent (LH14=19 matches M14.h=19, baseline 15).
- **Family table sorts in byte order**: the MoonBit assertion demands strictly increasing `lexical_compare` (byte order), while DWrite's collection order varies with loading — so the C side qsorts with `strcmp` (same as Pango's `g_strcmp0`) and dedupes only exact-equal neighbours; names come from locale slot 0 (en-us), hence the Windows family pinned in the theme uses the English name ("Microsoft YaHei").
- **Range indices must be converted to UTF-16 code units**: MoonBit (and the Pango branch) speak **UTF-8 byte offsets**, while `DWRITE_TEXT_RANGE` wants wide-character indices — unconverted, ranges in multi-byte text fall outside and are silently swallowed (the "byte offsets, cut on bytes" assertion is red for exactly this). Conversion = count the prefix with `MultiByteToWideChar`.
- **Drawing = D2D DC render target + 32bpp DIB + premultiplied over**: the same `IDWriteTextLayout` is first drawn into a DIB (`Clear` to transparent, antialiasing pinned to grayscale — ClearType is subpixel rendering and would fringe on a transparent background), then composited into the MoonBit buffer with `out = src + dst·(1−srcA)` component-wise (ARGB32 little-endian matches the DIB layout, no channel swapping). Range colours require brushes created *on the render target* (D2D brushes are device-dependent) before `SetDrawingEffect`, so colours are snapshotted only on the draw pass; box alignment follows the Pango `pt_render_box` rules (width-constrained layouts get width+alignment, single-line non-ellipsized ones get a slack offset; vertical likewise).
- **No cairo session on the pure backend: text goes offscreen + blit**: `draw_box_at` / `AttributedText::draw_at` with `cairo_id()==None` render into their own offscreen bitmap via the buffer variants (`pt_draw_box` / `rt_draw`), then land it with `Raster::blit` — translate/clip/blend are all computed by blit.
- **Along the way, a real `Raster::blit` bug got fixed**: it fed the *device-space* rect from `rect_device` into `mt` and then composed the CTM again — the CTM was applied twice, so with a `translate` in effect the source lookup landed outside the source rect and **not a single pixel was written** (measured ink=0; identity transform worked, which is why it had stayed hidden). Fix: build `mt` in user space (`e/f` from the user-space `dx/dy`) and keep the device rect only for the scan window. render 43 / ui 110 / chart 189 all green afterwards.
- **Line-height K floor landed**: `line_height = max(engine natural, ceil(size×K))` with **K = 1.36**, measured on this machine from Microsoft YaHei at 12..32px (natural ratios 1.29..1.36, fattest sample 14px→19); mixed-font rows take the floor from the largest size in the row; the ascent (baseline) is untouched and the extra space the floor adds is absorbed by vertical centering in the box (i.e. half-leading). The two white-box assertions that encoded "natural only" (family_wbtest) were updated to the new rule.
- **Default family probed per platform into the theme**: `@theme.default_font_family()` uses "Microsoft YaHei" when present (process-level cache), otherwise falls back to the engine default (fontconfig on Linux, CoreText on macOS); `role_font` in `ui` goes through it.
- **Verification** (on Windows): text **29/29** (was 8/29), render 43, ui 110 (the 20 text/decode probe skips flipped to real runs), chart 189, theme 8, mdview 26, core 70 all green; `native-app` start-up smoke prints all readouts and exits 0; per-package `--deny-warn` warning-free; whole-repo red dots stay at 287/68. **The planned "split the text assertions into structural/numeric tiers" turned out unnecessary**: all 29 compare against the platform's own measurements and pass on both platforms (recorded so nobody redoes it).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows IME pure channel (W6)

- **Protocol surface matches the GTK branch item by item**: commit/preedit are UTF-8 byte streams (capacity 255, truncated beyond), `preedit_caret` is a byte offset too, and reading a commit requires `commit_done` to clear it; in `WM_IME_COMPOSITION`, `GCS_RESULTSTR` fills commit (real text state) while `GCS_COMPSTR` fills preedit (display only, never part of the text state).
- **Three platform differences (interface facts)**: ① `WM_IME_SETCONTEXT` must strip `ISC_SHOWUICOMPOSITIONWINDOW` — this stack draws its own preedit, and without the strip Windows paints a second composition window on top of ours; ② the composition caret (`GCS_CURSORPOS`) is a **character** offset while the MoonBit side (like GTK) speaks UTF-8 **byte** offsets — convert via a `WideCharToMultiByte` prefix, otherwise the caret lands wrong for every multi-byte composition; ③ legacy IMM32 has **no surrounding-text API** (that is TSF), so `wm_im_set_surrounding` is a deliberate no-op — the GTK side feeds committed text so the IM context can infer context, while Windows IMEs take context from their own composition state (documented divergence; no fake interface on the MoonBit layer).
- **Caret and candidate window**: `wm_im_set_cursor` stores the rect and re-feeds it on `WM_IME_STARTCOMPOSITION` (some IMEs only read the position then); `ImmSetCompositionWindow(CFS_POINT)` and `ImmSetCandidateWindow(CFS_CANDIDATEPOS)` both anchor to the caret's **bottom edge** (`y + h`), which yields the familiar "candidate window at the end of the composition string" placement. `wm_im_reset` goes through `ImmNotifyIME(NI_COMPOSITIONSTR, CPS_CANCEL)` **and clears the local preedit buffer** — after reset an IME does not necessarily push another composition message, so a stale string would otherwise keep being painted (same lesson as the GTK side).
- **Evidence**: the `experiment/ime_win_probe` probe (create window → report caret rect → reset → read commit/preedit → pump 30 message rounds → clean exit) prints the expected readings (`commit=none`, empty preedit) with zero crashes; `experiment/multiwin_probe` and the `examples/native-app` smoke stay green after the new WndProc branches (exit code 0). The **real-Pinyin end-to-end** (preedit underline look, candidate window placement, commit landing in the focused box) requires injected keystrokes and belongs to W7's SendInput probe plus user verification — this machine already has Microsoft Pinyin installed (zh-Hans-CN, the only IME), so the path is viable.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · system clipboard (W16)

- **The capability lives in `sys` (new `clipboard.mbt` + the `_WIN32` branch of `sys_stub.c`)**: Windows uses `CF_UNICODETEXT` (`OpenClipboard`/`GetClipboardData`/`GlobalLock` to read; `GlobalAlloc(GMEM_MOVEABLE)` + `SetClipboardData` to write — **after a successful set the ownership belongs to the system, so never `GlobalFree`**, the classic Win32 clipboard trap); Linux/mac go through commands (`xclip -selection clipboard` / `pbcopy`·`pbpaste`). Chinese content must take the wide path (`win_utf8_wide` plus wide→UTF-8); the A-version APIs mangle it through the ANSI code page (same as the W14 environment-variable finding).
- **Escaping hell avoided on the command line**: `pr_run` performs no shell parsing, so the explicit form is `sh -c 'printf %s "$1" | xclip -selection clipboard' sh <text>` — the text travels as **argv slot 4** (the script's `$1`) instead of being spliced into the script body, so quotes, newlines and Chinese need no escaping.
- **One link library had been missed**: the clipboard APIs live in **user32.lib**, while the sys package's Windows link flags held only `ole32.lib shell32.lib uuid.lib` — the omission shows up as a string of `LNK2019: __imp_SetClipboardData` errors (recorded: on Windows, every newly used API group needs its .lib checked).
- **Wiring goes through a kernel slot, not kernel-to-platform**: `Editor::set_clipboard(get, set)` in `input` gives the kernel an external clipboard hook — `copy`/`cut` also publish, `Ctrl+V` fetches live (falling back to the built-in `clip` when unavailable), and the kernel itself still depends on no platform layer (headless-testable). **Off by default**: the app opts in with `ui`'s `use_system_clipboard(true)` (one line each in `examples/native-app` and `examples/showcase`) — a Ctrl+X in tests must not touch the user's clipboard; when the probe reports unavailable the internal clipboard quietly stays in charge (the Linux probe now prefers GtkClipboard and only falls back to xclip — see the Linux clipboard entry), i.e. semantic degradation.
- **Tests**: a new sys round-trip (Chinese + newline, `set → get`) that **saves the original content and restores it after asserting** (the probe leaves no trace in the user's clipboard); on this Windows machine `moon test -p sys` is **165/165** (2 new), with ui 110/110 and input 10/10 regression-free. Real cross-application pasting (copy out, paste in Notepad, paste back) is left for user verification.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · post-mortem of the Windows "black window + not responding" (W17a, five root causes plus a perf batch)

- **Cause 1: `wm_wait` never dispatches**. The Win32 branch waited with `MsgWaitForMultipleObjectsEx` and **never called `wm_pump`** (the GTK branch pumps after its poll) — with nobody dispatching, the WndProc never runs, so input, WM_CLOSE and WM_PAINT are all lost. Symptoms: Windows flags the window as "not responding", a graceful close waits for the suicide timer (measured 15.9s; **0.9s** after the fix), and SendKeys appears to do nothing. Fix: pump right after the wait.
- **Cause 2: `wm_present` only stored a pointer**. It recorded the MoonBit pixel pointer and blitted it later from WM_PAINT — but that pointer belongs to the **local** Bitmap created inside `paint_frame`, which becomes GC bait the moment the function returns; the blit then reads dangling memory (whole window pure black). Symptom: a black screenshot while every drawing readout and geometry line looked perfect. Fix: the stub keeps its own frame copy (`WmWindow.frame`, grown on demand), freed in `wm_close`.
- **Cause 3: key-code convention mismatch**. The Win32 stub mapped control characters the GDK way (`0x01000000|u`) while the kernel's `KS_*` are X11 values (Tab=0xFF09, Return=0xFF0D, Backspace=0xFF08, Escape=0xFF1B, Delete=0xFFFF) — so the host never recognized Tab or Return (measured: Tab inserted into the entry as a printable character, Enter-to-submit dead). Fix: emit X11 keysyms for controls and plain code points for printable input (CJK included).
- **Cause 4: release events were dispatched to widgets**. The kernel's `Editor::handle_key` takes only `(keysym, mods)` — it cannot tell a press from a release, so letting the release through means "one extra character per keystroke" (compounding cause 3). Fix: `App::route` dispatches presses only (the host's own Tab/focus handling likewise).
- **Cause 5 (caught by this batch's own regression sweep)**: the new viewport culling mapped the **absolute** widget rect through the CTM, double-counting the parent origin — every subtree with `oy != 0` was wrongly culled (evidence: ui dropped to 102/110; the widget probe showed "0 ink in the lower half"). Fix: intersect using the **local** rect relative to the current origin (`rect - (ox,oy)`) — the CTM's origin sits at the parent's content origin.
- **Cause 7 (Chinese IME would not compose): the message pump lacked `TranslateMessage`**. The Win32 `wm_pump` did only `PeekMessage` + `DispatchMessage` — but legacy IME (IMM32) hooks its composition processing onto `TranslateMessage`: without it you get bare keys only (letter key-downs are consumed by the IME, leaving just key-ups) and never see a single `WM_IME_STARTCOMPOSITION`/`WM_IME_COMPOSITION` (measured: only `WM_IME_SETCONTEXT` arrived), so Pinyin can never compose. After adding it the probe immediately reports `preedit=[ni'h'ao …]` and `commit=[你还奥]`. Check: run `experiment/key_probe` and watch the preedit/commit lines while typing.
- **Cause 8 (Markdown ordered-list numbers sank below the baseline): the marker cell was a zero-height node**. `md_marker` set only a width, has no children and registers no measure — so its height is 0; with `hbox`'s default `align_items=Center` a zero-height cell gets placed at the row's midpoint (measured `rect=(0,111,18,0)` while the text sits at 102..120), pushing the number half a line down with its centre exactly on the text baseline. Fix: register a text measure on the marker cell (line box from the same source as the body) and top-align it to the first line (`valign=Start`); regression test "ordered list: number and text share the baseline" in `markdown_wbtest`.
- **Performance (same batch)**: the text path went **9.4ms → 0.35ms per run** via three changes: ① the D2D render target moved from DC to a **WIC bitmap** (a DC target costs 0.4ms just to `BindDC` per call, 1.8ms all-in per text run); ② process-level caches for canvas and brushes, grow-only; ③ a `Raster::blit` fast path for "integer translate + 1:1 whole-bitmap + Normal", with source bytes passed through a 256×256 lookup table that reproduces the generic path's unpremultiply-then-premultiply — **bit-identical**, so the pixel assertions stayed green. Whole-tree repaint fell 550ms → ~300ms (viewport culling), and the example's tick dropped 100ms → 500ms so each repaint cycle blocks the message pump only within the safe window. Full-window repaints are still in the hundreds of milliseconds (the charts page is heaviest) — queued as a follow-up perf batch.
- **Two WIC canvas traps**: ① do **not** wrap an external buffer with `CreateBitmapFromMemory` and let D2D draw into it — WIC caches internally and your buffer never sees the pixels (symptom: ink=0 while the call reports success); use a self-owned bitmap and `Lock(WICBitmapLockRead)` to read back after drawing. ② Clearing the canvas with a fully transparent `FillRectangle` is a **no-op** under source-over; you must `Clear` — which honours the axis-aligned clip, so only the current region gets cleared even though the cached canvas is larger.
- **How it was proven**: `experiment/key_probe` (create window → pump → print key codes, driven by PowerShell `AppActivate` + `SendKeys`) for the keyboard path; `experiment/text_bench` as the text-path perf guard; screenshots via PowerShell screen capture to judge content by eye (how the black window was confirmed); temporary PAINT-PROF/STEP-PROF instrumentation inside `App::step`/`paint_frame` located the bottleneck and was removed afterwards. Regression sweep: core 70 / input 10 / ui 110 / render 43 / text 29 / chart 189 / theme 8 / mdview 26 / sys 165, whole-repo red dots unchanged at 287/68.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows base capabilities, first batch (W20a menu bar / W20b global hotkey / W20d message box)

- **Environment and the toolchain-free verification path**: Windows 10 19045 + VS BuildTools 2022 (MSVC **14.44.35207**, under `C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\...`; `C:\Program Files\...` is an empty directory) + SDK 10.0.26100. `vcvars64.bat` is blocked by an reg.exe policy, so compiling a pure-C probe by hand means setting `INCLUDE`/`LIB` yourself, with **Windows-style paths** (`/c/...` gets treated as a source file):
  ```bash
  MSVC='C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\VC\Tools\MSVC\14.44.35207'
  SDK='C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10'
  export INCLUDE="$MSVC\include;$SDK\Include\10.0.26100.0\ucrt;$SDK\Include\10.0.26100.0\shared;$SDK\Include\10.0.26100.0\um;$SDK\Include\10.0.26100.0\winrt"
  export LIB="$MSVC\lib\x64;$SDK\Lib\10.0.26100.0\ucrt\x64;$SDK\Lib\10.0.26100.0\um\x64"
  MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL='*' "$MSVC/bin/Hostx64/x64/cl.exe" \
    /nologo /W4 /utf-8 /O2 /DWIN32 /D_WINDOWS /DUNICODE /D_UNICODE <probe>.c \
    /Fe:<probe>.exe user32.lib gdi32.lib imm32.lib advapi32.lib
  ```
  The pure-C probe `experiment/menu_probe/menu_backend_probe.c` `#include`s `../../gui/win/stub.c` directly — `wm_menu_bar_new` returns a **handle-table index** (1..31), not a real `HMENU`, so reading the menu structure back requires the static `wm_menus[]` table in the same TU (the first version passed the index as an `HMENU` to `GetMenuItemCount` and got `-1` everywhere).
- **A pure-C probe that includes the stub must define `-DUNICODE`/`-D_UNICODE` explicitly**: without it `IDC_ARROW` etc. are `MAKEINTRESOURCEA` forms (`LPSTR`) and the `...W` parameters emit **C4133** (`stub.c:388-396`, `646`); with it there are no new warnings (the C4996 for `wcsncpy`/`wcsncat` inside `stub.c` is pre-existing). `moon`'s native-stub already defines UNICODE (see the W2 section), so the production chain never sees those two C4133s.
- **`AppendMenuW` caps a single menu at ≈1168 items** (measured here; the bulk loop starts returning 0 at item 1169), **well below the 2048-entry command-id pool**. Two rules follow: ① **a command id must only advance the cursor after the item is actually attached** — the original code did `cmd = next++` before `AppendMenuW`, so a failed add still burned an id; repeated failures (menu over the cap) exhaust the quota early and then collide with ids still in use by other menus (`WM_COMMAND` carries only the id, so a collision dispatches the click to the wrong item). Fixed to `cmd = next`, with `next = cmd + 1` only after `AppendMenuW` succeeds (wrapping to `BASE` at the pool end). ② `WM_MENU_CMD_MAX=2048` must stay **deliberately larger than the per-menu cap**: pool ≥ per-menu cap is what guarantees "filling one menu never wraps", so a single menu never collides with itself; tightening the pool to the measured cap would make a full menu hit the wrap. The probe's wrap case was rewritten accordingly — fill 2048 ids across menus, then add an item to a **fresh menu that still has room** and check it gets the wrapped-back 1000 (one menu can never drain the pool; the first assertion was unreachable).
- **`\t` inside the label is the accelerator hint**: `AppendMenuW` right-aligns everything after `\t` (verified by reading it back with `GetMenuItemInfoW`, which also checks the Chinese UTF-8→UTF-16 round trip and the `\tCtrl+Alt+Q` hint). A real accelerator table (`TranslateAccelerator`) is not wired yet; keyboard navigation once a menu is open / shortcuts currently rely on W20b's global hotkeys.
- **The message box (W20d) is the stack's second FFI shape — "blocking"**: `MessageBoxW` shows and **modally blocks** until the user clicks a button and returns the button index — no callback, no window slot; the `uType` four-field bit combination (icon + buttons) is computed by a MoonBit-side pure function (probe measured `icon=64 btn=0 raw=327744`). Orthogonal to the "register + poll in the pump" async shape used by menus and hotkeys.
- **Global hotkeys (W20b) go to the *thread* message queue, not a window**: `RegisterHotKey`'s `WM_HOTKEY` lands in the registering thread's queue and never reaches the WndProc — same scheme as menus, with the stub's `wm_hotkey_poll` pulled from the MoonBit pump. Accelerator-string parsing (`"CmdOrCtrl+Shift+M"` → modifier bits + virtual key) is a three-platform-shared pure function (`CmdOrCtrl` maps to Ctrl on Win, Cmd on mac), 10/10.
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/menu_probe/menu_backend_probe.c` prints `MENU-BACKEND-OK` (menu bar / submenu / command ids / separator / check / nesting / CJK label / bad parent handles / handle-table exhaustion / id wrap all pass); `experiment/menu_probe`'s MoonBit side verifies the model tree (`MENU-OK`); `core/menu_wbtest.mbt` 10/10, `win/hotkey_wbtest.mbt` 10/10 and `win/message_box_wbtest.mbt` 8/8 all really run and pass.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows icon loading (W20f: native .ico + PNG via WIC)

- **Three sources, three entry points**: `.ico`/`.bmp` go through `LoadImageW(LR_LOADFROMFILE)` (a native Win32 icon format, no decoding needed); PNG/JPEG/GIF have **no native entry point** and can only be decoded from memory with WIC (`CreateStream` → `InitializeFromMemory` → `CreateDecoderFromStream` → `CreateFormatConverter` to `GUID_WICPixelFormat32bppBGRA` → `CopyPixels`); already-decoded pixels go through a 32bpp top-down DIB + `CreateIconIndirect`. `wm_icon_load_file` dispatches on the extension and returns 0 if both paths fail.
- **A 32bpp HICON's alpha must be premultiplied, and `render`'s Bitmap is exactly that** (little-endian B,G,R,A premultiplied) — `CreateIconIndirect` blends with the color bitmap's alpha channel, so premultiplication is what avoids halos; the two layouts line up, so pixels are a byte-for-byte `memcpy` into the DIB. Verification caveat: `GetObject`'s `bmBits` on a DIB section is **not guaranteed readable** (measured: an unusable pointer, memcmp always fails) — to read pixels back use `GetDIBits` with a **negative `biHeight`** (top-down request, matching the input row order).
- **`CLSID_WICImagingFactory` is a macro that points at Win8-only Factory2**: `wincodec.h` does `#define CLSID_WICImagingFactory CLSID_WICImagingFactory2` when `_WIN32_WINNT >= 0x0602`; for older systems take **`CLSID_WICImagingFactory1`** explicitly (identical vtable prefix, `IID_IWICImagingFactory` still v1).
- **All the GUID constants are exported from `windowscodecs.lib`** (verified with `dumpbin /linkermember`: `CLSID_WICImagingFactory1`, `IID_IWICImagingFactory`, `GUID_WICPixelFormat32bppBGRA` are all there), so win's Windows link line only adds `windowscodecs.lib ole32.lib` — no `uuid.lib` needed (`__uuidof` remains unavailable in C mode, same pitfall as the W14 file dialog).
- **C4013 implicit declaration shows up on this path too**: `wm_icon_load_file`'s WIC fallback calls `wm_icon_from_png`, defined after it, and a standalone stub.c compile gives MSVC `C4013: undefined; assuming extern returning int` — compiles but the ABI is not trustworthy (same family as the accent-color pitfall). A forward declaration clears it; the lesson is to think about definition order for mutually-calling internal functions rather than leaning on implicit declarations.
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/icon_probe/icon_backend_probe.c` (`#include`s stub.c, feeds `wm_icon_*` directly, reads back with `GetIconInfo`/`GetDIBits`) ends with `ICON-BACKEND-OK`: BGRA→HICON size/bit-depth/pixel round trip, `.ico` from disk, PNG via WIC (an 8×8 RGBA translucent red decodes to 32bpp), handle-table exhaustion at 31 slots returning 0, and idempotent free all pass. The PNG asset is hand-written by `experiment/icon_probe/make_png.py` using only stdlib zlib (no Pillow). `moon test ./gui/win` is 24/24 (6 new pure-function tests: extension parsing, format dispatch, handle-0 semantics, size validation).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows window enhancement (W20e: retitle / window icon / min size / borderless drag region)

- **`WM_GETMINMAXINFO`'s `ptMinTrackSize` wants the whole-window size, not the client size**: the MoonBit side passes a **minimum client size** (`set_min_size(w,h)`), and the C side expands it by the non-client frame for the current style with `AdjustWindowRectEx(&r, st, FALSE, ex)` before filling `ptMinTrackSize`. Probe measurement: a minimum client width of 400 under `WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW` yields a 416-wide frame (8 px each side), and `AdjustWindowRectEx` agrees with `WM_GETMINMAXINFO` to the pixel.
- **A zero axis must be explicitly zeroed**: `set_min_size` allows one axis to be unconstrained (`0` = no limit on that axis). But `0` **must not** be fed into `AdjustWindowRectEx`'s rect — that computes a fake minimum of "a zero client area still occupies 2× frame width". The implementation writes `mmi->ptMinTrackSize.x = w->min_w > 0 ? (r.right - r.left) : 0;` and checks both axes; the `wm_set_min_size` entry also clamps negatives to `0` first. When only one axis is set, the other must fall back to 0 or the window gets stuck on that axis.
- **`WM_NCHITTEST` must `break`, not `return 0`**: on a hit in the self-drawn title-bar drag region it `return HTCAPTION` (handing window dragging to the system), but **outside the region it must `break` back to default handling** — the default return values of `WM_NCHITTEST` carry meaning (client area `HTCLIENT`, resize corners `HTBOTTOMRIGHT`, …), and `return 0` swallows them: measured symptom, the frame can no longer be resized and client-area mouse behavior breaks. The hit test converts `lParam` from **screen** to client coordinates with `ScreenToClient` first (`lParam` packs screen coordinates, not client, an easy misread) and uses a left/bottom-closed, right/top-open range (`>= x && < x+w`). Note the packed coordinates are **16-bit signed** (`(int16_t)LOWORD/HIWORD`), so negative multi-monitor coordinates restore correctly.
- **Read window icon back with `WM_GETICON`**: `wm_set_icon(id, icon_slot, which)` maps `which` 0/1/2 to `ICON_BIG` / `ICON_SMALL` / both and calls `SendMessageW(hwnd, WM_SETICON, …)` directly. The probe reads back with `WM_GETICON(ICON_BIG)` and getting the same `HICON` handle proves the write took effect. **The icon handle table belongs to the caller** (W20f's slot table); the window only *uses* the `HICON`, so both the C comment and the facade docs state "the caller must keep the icon alive while the window is shown" — otherwise the window references a dangling handle. Passing `icon_slot=0` clears the icon.
- **Borderless = restyle + recompute the frame from the client size + `SWP_FRAMECHANGED`**: ON drops `WS_CAPTION|WS_THICKFRAME|WS_MINIMIZEBOX|WS_MAXIMIZEBOX|WS_SYSMENU|WS_BORDER|WS_DLGFRAME` and switches to `WS_POPUP` (keeping `WS_MINIMIZEBOX` so minimizing still works); OFF restores `WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW`. **Two key steps**: ① capture the current client rect with `GetClientRect`, recompute the frame with `AdjustWindowRectEx` under the new style, then `SetWindowPos(..., SWP_NOMOVE|SWP_NOZORDER|SWP_FRAMECHANGED)` — without `SWP_FRAMECHANGED` the non-client area is not recomputed and the window size jumps; ② make it idempotent via `w->borderless`, returning early on a same-value call (otherwise every call runs `SetWindowPos` for nothing). Probe verification: after borderless there is no `WS_CAPTION` in `GWL_STYLE`, and the client width is still 640 (unchanged).
- **Runtime retitle goes through `SetWindowTextW`**: `wm_set_title` converts UTF-8 to UTF-16 then calls `SetWindowTextW`; the probe reads back with `GetWindowTextW` to check the Chinese title's UTF-8→UTF-16 round trip.
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/window_probe/window_enhance_probe.c` (`#include`s stub.c, creates a real window then reads `SendMessageW(WM_GETMINMAXINFO/WM_NCHITTEST)` directly and `GetWindowTextW`/`WM_GETICON`/`GWL_STYLE` back) ends with `WIN-ENHANCE-OK` (all 40 assertions pass): `SetWindowTextW` takes effect (Chinese), `ptMinTrackSize.x == 416` (client 400 + frame), multi-monitor negative coordinates hit / open-interval boundary, in-region returns `HTCAPTION` and out-of-region returns `HTCLIENT`, `WM_GETICON(ICON_BIG)` equals the icon passed in, no `WS_CAPTION` after borderless with client width unchanged, and an out-of-range id neither crashes nor writes the buffer. Example wiring: `examples/native-app` gained a window-enhancement section (retitle / self-drawn icon / borderless toggle / drag-band toggle) and an exit status line `NATIVE-APP-WIN title='…' min=… drag=… bl=… icon=…`, and `moon build` produces `native-app.exe`. `moon test ./gui/win` 24/24, `moon check ./gui/win ./examples/native-app --deny-warn` warning-free, the C backend clean under MSVC `/W4`.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows system tray (W20c: Shell_NotifyIconW + W20a menus for the right-click popup)

- **A tray icon is "one HWND + one uID"**: `Shell_NotifyIconW` posts callback messages to the window in `NOTIFYICONDATAW.hWnd`, with `wParam` = `uID` and `lParam` = the mouse message. This side therefore creates its own **hidden message window** (`yue_wm_tray` class + `HWND_MESSAGE`, lazily and idempotently), and the slot number *is* the `uID` (1-based, slot 0 unused; `WM_MAX_TRAYS=8`).
- **`WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN` strips out `shellapi.h`**: `Shell_NotifyIconW` / `NOTIFYICONDATAW` / `NIM_*` / `NIF_*` all live there, so it must be included explicitly (same treatment as `imm.h`). The link line gains **`shell32.lib`** (it is not in `user32`/`gdi32`) — missing it means `LNK2019 __imp_Shell_NotifyIconW`.
- **The callback message is `WM_APP+2`, and again "C never calls back into MoonBit"**: `WM_APP+1` is already taken by `wm_wakeup`'s waker window (that `case WM_APP + 1:` in `stub.c`), so the tray takes `WM_APP+2`. Hits (`WM_LBUTTONUP`/`WM_LBUTTONDBLCLK` → left, `WM_RBUTTONUP` → right) go into a process-level ring queue that the host drains each tick with `wm_tray_poll` — the same scheme as menus and hotkeys, avoiding the trampoline-arity pitfalls. Non-click messages (e.g. `WM_MOUSEMOVE`) are not enqueued.
- **The right-click menu reuses W20a's HMENU handle table, so "tray menus" and "window menus" share one dispatch chain**: `wm_menu_popup_new()` builds a popup root (differing from `wm_menu_bar_new` only in `CreatePopupMenu` vs `CreateMenu`), core's `menu_translate_into` fills it as usual, and popping uses `TrackPopupMenuEx(menu, TPM_RIGHTBUTTON|TPM_RETURNCMD|TPM_NONOTIFY, x, y, tray_hwnd, NULL)` — **`TPM_RETURNCMD` hands the selected item back as the return value**, and pushing it into `wm_menu_push` rejoins `dispatch_menu_command()`'s callback table without relying on the tray window's `WM_COMMAND` routing. Both Microsoft conventions are followed: `SetForegroundWindow(tray_hwnd)` before showing, `PostMessage(hwnd, WM_NULL, 0, 0)` after — otherwise the menu may fail to dismiss (focus is not handed back).
- **`szTip` is a fixed 128-wide-character array, so long text must be truncated**: the tooltip write uses `wcsncpy_s(nid.szTip, 128, wtip, _TRUNCATE)` — a bare `wcsncpy` draws **C4996** (the same family as the two W20a menu-label sites). The `_s` variant was chosen deliberately, and the whole tray section is **warning-free under MSVC `/W4`**. The probe confirms a 299-char tooltip truncates below 127 without overrunning.
- **Backend selection: Linux SNI first, the Windows stub here, `Err(Unsupported)` if neither**. SNI availability uses the **new** `@dbus.sni_watcher_present()` (bus reachable **and** `NameHasOwner(org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher)`), a stronger test than the existing `@dbus.sni_available()` (which only checks the "bus reachable" candidate condition) — on a stock GNOME without the AppIndicator extension the bus is up but the watcher is absent, so the tray is genuinely unavailable. **The GTK branch deliberately returns 0**: Linux trays go through the SNI protocol stack, not GTK3's `GtkStatusIcon` (deprecated and not honored by panels), the same reason W20f's icon loading returns 0 there.
- **The facade lives in `core/tray.mbt`, so core gains `sys` and `dbus` dependencies** (the former for `desktop_environment()` reading `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`, the latter for SNI). This matches the W20a decision to put the menu model in core: core is the "cross-platform capability facade" layer, and platform backends only do "model → native" translation. The old chain's same-named `TrayError` lives in the retiring `yue/error.mbt`; the new-stack definition lives in this file.
- **The exit-readout pitfall (fixed along the way)**: the example's exit status lines print **after the window has closed**, by which time the backend slot has been `memset` — `NATIVE-APP-WIN title='' min=0x0 …` was all zeros because it was reading a destroyed window. Fixed by snapshotting the live readouts into a `Ref[String]` each tick and printing the last "while alive" snapshot at exit (measured: `title='' min=0x0` → `title='MoonBit App 宿主 (批次1)' min=900x600`). Rule of thumb: **to report runtime state at exit, sample it while running — do not expect to read it after teardown.**
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/tray_probe/tray_backend_probe.c` (`#include`s stub.c, feeds `wm_tray_*` directly) ends with **`TRAY-BACKEND-OK`**, all 28 assertions passing: `wm_tray_supported()==1`, a **real `Shell_NotifyIconW(NIM_ADD)` succeeds**, tooltip Chinese UTF-8→UTF-16 round trip plus a 299-char truncation under 127, `wm_menu_popup_new` returning a real `HMENU` attached to the tray, **posting `WM_TRAY_CB` to the tray message window to verify left kind=0 / right kind=1 / matching slot / non-click messages not enqueued**, `wm_tray_popup`'s guard paths (invalid slot, no menu) returning 0 immediately without blocking (`TrackPopupMenuEx` blocks, so a real popup needs a human click — hence guards only), slot-table exhaustion (0 after 7 slots), and idempotent removal returning the count to 0. `moon test ./gui/core` is **99/99** (6 new pure-function tests: `basename_no_ext` across path shapes/empty/trailing dot/multiple dots, non-empty desktop name, the three `TrayError` shows; plus **1 lifecycle test**: create → `tray_count()==1` → tooltip read-back matches → attach menu (popup handle filled in) → remove → `tray_count()==0`, auto-skipping rather than faking on machines with no tray backend). `examples/native-app` gains a tray section (five buttons: create / retip / attach menu / popup / remove, plus a state counter) and an exit status line `NATIVE-APP-TRAY supported=… backend=… count=… clicks=… note=…`, with `dispatch_tray_event()` called each tick in the main loop. Real-machine smoke: `exit 0`, `NATIVE-APP-TRAY supported=1 backend=- count=0 clicks=0 note=-` (nothing created since no button was clicked; `supported=1` confirms the Win32 tray backend is available). Whole-repo `moon check` red dots hold at **287/68**, and gui/{core,win,dbus,ui,render,text,theme,icons,input,mdview,chart} are all green.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Windows desktop notifications (W21a: tray balloon NIF_INFO + a freedesktop cross-platform facade)

- **Win32 "notifications" have no standalone API — a notification *is* a balloon attached to a tray icon**: `NIM_ADD` creates the tray item, then `NIM_MODIFY` with `NIF_INFO` plus `szInfoTitle`/`szInfo` pops the balloon (since Vista the system turns these balloons into Action Center toasts). This section therefore **reuses W20c's tray slot table and message window**, distinguishing them with the new `WmTray.kind`: `kind == 0` = tray icon (receives clicks), `kind == 1` = notification balloon (pops only, receives no clicks). The notification id is thus naturally the owning tray slot number (>=1).
- **Notification slots must be explicitly excluded from the click queue**: `wm_tray_wndproc` gained a `wm_trays[slot].kind == 0` guard when handling `WM_TRAY_CB`, so a `kind == 1` slot drops clicks — a balloon's click semantics are undefined (Windows handles toast clicks itself, not via `uCallbackMessage`), and enqueueing anyway would dispatch to a slot that is not a tray icon. The probe posts `WM_LBUTTONUP`/`WM_RBUTTONUP` to a notification slot and confirms the queue stays empty.
- **"Dismissing a balloon" has no standalone API; follow the official convention of sending `NIF_INFO` again with an empty `szInfo`**: `wm_notify_close` first sends that empty-body `NIM_MODIFY` (the system retracts the balloon), then `NIM_DELETE` removes the tray item — both steps are needed, and `NIM_DELETE` alone does not guarantee the balloon disappears immediately. It is idempotent: a repeat close hits the "slot already empty" guard and returns.
- **Two platform differences degrade explicitly rather than pretending to align** (documented in the `core/notify.mbt` header and here): ① **`uTimeout` is ignored by the system since Vista**, so a balloon's dwell time is set by the system's accessibility settings and `timeout_ms` exists only for signature parity with Linux — it has no effect here; ② **a Win32 balloon has no "progress hint"**, whereas Linux's freedesktop `value` hint lets the desktop draw a progress bar, so the facade pastes the percentage into the body (`notify_body_with_progress`, a visible degradation rather than a silent drop).
- **`kind`'s three states map to `NIIF_*`**: 0 info / 1 warning / 2 error → `NIIF_ERROR` / `NIIF_WARNING` / `NIIF_INFO` (the system status icon at the balloon's left). This is a hard-coded one-line ternary, and **Win32 has no interface to read `dwInfoFlags` back**, so the probe can only verify that all three kinds dispatch successfully; the mapping itself is covered by code review (stated honestly in the probe's header comment).
- **`szInfoTitle` is a fixed 64-wide-character array and `szInfo` a 256-wide-character one**: writes always use `wcsncpy_s(..., _TRUNCATE)` (a bare `wcsncpy` draws C4996, same family as W20a/W20c). The probe confirms a 399-char title truncates below 63 without overrunning. `WmTray.ntitle[64]` keeps a copy of the title for read-back (`wm_notify_title`, UTF-8 output).
- **Tray-icon slots and notification slots share one `WM_MAX_TRAYS=8` table, but their counts are fully decoupled**: `wm_tray_count()` counts only `kind == 0` and `wm_notify_count()` only `kind == 1`. The probe verifies "1 tray + 3 notifications = 4 slots used", "only 3 more succeed once the table is full", and "removing the tray icon does not affect the notification count". Allocation goes through the unified `wm_tray_alloc(kind, icon_slot, tip)`, with `wm_tray_new` delegating at `kind=0`.
- **Facade layering**: `win/notify.mbt` is the thin Windows-side wrapper (`notify_supported`/`notify_send`/`notify_update`/`notify_close`/`notify_count`/`notify_title` plus the `NOTIFY_INFO/WARNING/ERROR` constants); `core/notify.mbt` is the **cross-platform facade** — backend selection is Linux freedesktop D-Bus (`dbus/notify.mbt`) first → Win32 tray balloon → `Err(NotifyError::Unsupported)` if neither. The cross-platform API is `notify_send(app_name, summary, body, icon?, timeout_ms?, replaces_id?, progress?, kind?, icon_handle?)`, where `icon` is a Linux theme icon name and `icon_handle` a Win32 W20f icon handle (0 = use the system status icon). When `replaces_id > 0`, the Win32 side tries `notify_update` first (re-sending on the same slot replaces it).
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/notify_probe/notify_backend_probe.c` (`#include`s stub.c, feeds `wm_notify_*` directly) ends with **`NOTIFY-BACKEND-OK`**, all **37 assertions passing**: `wm_notify_supported()==1`, a **real `Shell_NotifyIconW(NIM_MODIFY)` balloon**, Chinese title UTF-8→UTF-16 round trip plus a 399-char truncation under 63, **notification-slot clicks not entering the tray queue**, all three kinds dispatching successfully, **1 tray + 3 notifications sharing the table with decoupled counts**, a new send returning 0 once the table is full (7 slots), closing decrementing by one and idempotent, and invalid/tray slots returning 0 or no-op for the notification APIs. `moon test ./gui/core` is **108/108** (9 new: `notify_clamp_kind` clamped to 0..2, `notify_clamp_pct` clamped to 0..100, `notify_body_with_progress` leaving negative progress alone / appending to the body / clamping the percentage, the two `NotifyError` shows, backend name matching the enum, and `notify_caps` in both the with/without-backend states; plus **1 lifecycle test**: send → update progress → close → idempotent re-close, auto-skipping on machines with no notification backend). `examples/native-app` gains a notification section (five buttons: info / warning / error / 60% progress / close, plus a state counter) and an exit status line `NATIVE-APP-NOTIFY supported=… backend=… sends=… id=… count=… note=…` (the `count=` is snapshotted each tick for the same reason as W20c's exit-readout pitfall). Real-machine smoke: `exit 0`, `NATIVE-APP-NOTIFY supported=1 backend=win-balloon sends=0 id=0 count=0 note=-` (nothing sent since no button was clicked; `backend=win-balloon` confirms the backend selection). Whole-repo `moon check` red dots hold at **287/68**, and gui/{core,win,dbus,ui,render,text,theme,icons,input,mdview,chart} are all green.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · self-drawn message box (W21b: `@ui.message_box_t`, coexisting with the W20d native tier)

- **The self-drawn and native tiers are two roads that complement rather than replace each other**: `@win.show_message_box` (W20d) goes through `MessageBoxW` — system look, system modality, a **blocking** return code, but **Win32 only** (a stub on macOS); `@ui.message_box_t` (this batch) is fully self-drawn, honours the theme, is **non-blocking** (each button calls back with its code), and is one code path across all three platforms. Callers pick per need, and the semantic surfaces line up (title / text / informative sub-line / multiple buttons / response callbacks).
- **Response codes are supplied by the caller, not borrowed from Win32's 1..7**: `buttons` is a list of `(label, response code)` and the component passes the code straight back. The native tier's `Ok(1)/Cancel(2)/…/No(7)` is `MessageBoxW`'s return-value convention, and the self-drawn tier must not be tied to it — the **two numbering schemes are deliberately not interoperable**, which is why the status output splits into `NATIVE-APP-MSGBOX` (native) and `NATIVE-APP-MSGBOX2` (self-drawn).
- **✕ / Esc / clicking the mask = dismiss without a code** (via `on_dismiss`): `MessageBoxW`'s ✕ maps to `IDCANCEL`, but the self-drawn tier does not borrow that (see above); if "cancel counts as a result", the caller adds a cancel button. `close_on_mask` defaults to **false** — a message box usually demands an explicit answer, and closing on a stray click invites mistakes.
- **Enter = the first button** (same meaning as the native tier's `default_index=0`). For Esc/Enter to arrive at all, the box must **take over focus**: on open it allocates an id from 700000 upward via `msgbox_alloc_focus_id()` and calls `focus.register` + `focus.focus` on `@core.host_active()`, with the panel itself as the focus widget (it sits deeper than the scrim, so keys reach it first); on teardown it `unregister`s and **hands the pre-open focus back**. Starting at 700000 avoids the low ids callers commonly use (the example uses 1..25) — **focus ids are a global namespace, so a component allocating its own must leave room**.
- **An enum variant must not be called `None` (a compile-time collateral hit this batch)**: `MsgBoxKind` was first written with a `None` variant, and immediately every `Store::new(None)` elsewhere **in the same package** started inferring as `MsgBoxKind::None`, producing 6 type errors in `datetime_wbtest`. The cause: in positions with **no expected type** such as `Store::new(None)`, a same-named constructor gets picked up. Renaming it to `Default` cleared it. Lesson: before adding an enum to a package, **check whether the variant name already has an expected-type-free use in that package** (`None` above all, then names that clash with `SemanticType` such as `Warning`).
- **Relation to `dialog_t`**: both share the same overlay machinery in `core/overlay.mbt` (full-window scrim / centring / close-on-outside-click / relayout on open), differing in semantic surface — `dialog_t` is a *content dialog* (arbitrary content tree + confirm/cancel exits) while `message_box_t` is a *message box* (kind-driven icon + informative sub-line + any number of buttons each carrying a code). Content dialogs still go through `dialog_t`; this batch **did not** merge the two.
- **Evidence**: `ui/message_box_wbtest.mbt` adds **8 tests** (3 pure: the five kinds' icon names / distinct colours / default-button code; 5 runtime: Store-driven open-close + panel centring + the four-region structure + button callback code and close + ✕/Esc taking dismiss while Enter takes the default button + both `close_on_mask` states + an empty informative leaving no sub-line + empty-label buttons skipped), using a headless widget tree with a manually installed overlay host (as in `overlay_wbtest`) — **no real window needed**. `moon test ./gui/ui` is **118/118** (was 110). `examples/native-app` gains a self-drawn message-box section (three buttons: info / warning / error, each driving its own box) and an exit status line `NATIVE-APP-MSGBOX2 shots=… last=… ovl=…` (the `ovl=` is the last snapshot of the live overlay count, for the same reason as W20c's exit-readout pitfall); real-machine smoke: `exit 0`, `NATIVE-APP-MSGBOX2 shots=0 last=- ovl=0 native_ovl=0` (nothing popped since no button was clicked). `moon check ./gui/ui ./examples/native-app --deny-warn` is warning-free, and whole-repo red dots hold at **287/68**.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · drag-and-drop facade (W21c: `core/drag.mbt` session model + `@ui.drag_source`/`drop_target`)

- **In-app drag and drop is fully self-drawn and one code path across all three platforms**: the old stack's drag source / drop target were **platform callbacks** (GTK `gtk_drag_*` / Win32 `IDropTarget`) requiring three separate handlers for enter/update/leave; in-app drag and drop in this stack touches no platform interface — the session model lives in `core/drag.mbt` (process-level) and the two declarative hooks in `ui/drag.mbt`. **Cross-application** system-level drag (dragging out of the window / accepting OS files) still ships with the platform batch; the facade's interface shape is settled, so the UI side will not change once a backend lands.
- **The payload is our own type, not a platform image type**: `DragData{Text,Html,Paths,Img}`; `Img` holds **raw PNG bytes** rather than a platform image handle — the model layer carries no platform types, which is what lets payloads travel across backends/processes. `DataKind{Text,Html,Image,FilePaths}` each get a stable id (1/2/3/4), and `drag_encode`/`drag_decode` pack `[kind:i32 LE][payload]` (`Paths` joined with `\n`) — the stable ABI for future cross-process/cross-backend payloads.
- **The session is a process-level singleton and drop targets are resolved in window coordinates**: only one drag runs at a time (`drag_current`). The target is found by `Widget::drop_target_at`, scanning back-to-front for the **deepest** visible widget carrying `on_drop`; the hover highlight is drawn by reading `@core.drag_hover()` — which **removes the enter/update/leave trio** and with it the old stack's "missed leave callback" state-sync bug.
- **Capture semantics differ from the wrapper's identity (a trap hit this batch, do not conflate them)**: on `Down`, `route_pointer` records the captured id as the **deepest** widget on the hit path (same meaning as a Web `pointerdown` target); the drag source wrapper's `on_pointer` receives the event by **bubbling** (the content widget does not handle the pointer itself, so it travels up). Hence `@core.drag_source()` records the **wrapper** id while the captured id is the **content widget** id — test assertions must distinguish the two (one was first written asserting equality with the wrapper id).
- **Do not put buttons inside a drag source**: pointer events go **deepest-first**, so an interactive child swallows the press and the drag never starts. A drag source should contain display items only (text / icons / thumbnails). **This is a usage constraint, not a bug.**
- **`payload()` is lazy**: it is only called once the **threshold** is crossed (4px by default); below the threshold the press is released as an ordinary click, so selection/clicks inside the content are unaffected. When `payload()` returns `None` the drag does not materialise, again falling back to an ordinary press.
- **`derive(Eq)` on a value type trips the `implicit_impl_as_method` warning** (this batch's compile-time trap): the correct form is a hand-written `pub impl Eq for DataKind with fn equal(self, other) -> Bool` plus `pub extend DataKind with Eq::{not_equal, equal}`. Note the implementation function is **`fn equal`** (writing `op_equal` first makes the compiler report `no method op_equal in trait Eq`). `DragData` does not go through `Eq` (it holds `Bytes`/`Array`) nor implement `Debug`, so tests observe it via strings/ids.
- Another implementation trap (recorded): `Bytes` has no `op_set` and `raw[4:...]` yields a `BytesView` that cannot be sliced directly — assemble bytes with `FixedArray::make` + `Bytes::from_array`; `split("\n")` yields an `Iter[StringView]` and needs `.map(fn(s){ s.to_owned() })` to become an `Array[String]`.
- **Evidence**: `core/drag_wbtest.mbt` has **6 tests** (id round trip, `DragData::kind`/`as_text`, encode/decode round trip and bad input, `drop_target_at` deepest hit, session `begin→move→drop`, no-target/reject/`cancel`, and a constant no-target result when there is no root); `ui/drag_wbtest.mbt` has **4 tests** (press → cross threshold → drag → move onto target → release delivers, below-threshold press behaving as an ordinary click / release over blank not delivering, `accept` rejecting so the callback does not run, and `payload` returning `None` producing no session), all driven through the host's `@core.route_pointer` (including press capture) — **no real window needed**. `moon test ./gui/core` is **115/115** and `moon test ./gui/ui` is **122/122**. `examples/native-app` gains a drag-and-drop section (two sources "text payload / paths payload" plus two targets "accept all / paths only") and an exit status line `NATIVE-APP-DRAG drags=… drops=… active=… last=…`; real-machine smoke: `exit 0`, `NATIVE-APP-DRAG drags=0 drops=0 active=0 last=-` (nothing dragged, so the counters are 0). `moon check ./gui/core ./gui/ui ./examples/native-app --deny-warn` is warning-free, and whole-repo red dots hold at **287/68**.
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: cross-application system-level drag (dragging out / accepting OS files, deferred to the platform batch), drag-time visual feedback (a ghost thumbnail following the pointer — currently only the target highlight is drawn), the macOS backend, and the real-machine feel of dragging (tracked in `_人工测试清单.md`).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · autostart at login (W21d: `sys/autostart.mbt`, facade in MoonBit, C only for Windows)

- **Division of labour**: the **cross-platform surface lives in MoonBit** — Linux (and other XDG desktops) writes `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/<app_id>.desktop` (pure string assembly plus the existing text-file read/write entries in `fsx.mbt`, so no platform interface at all); **only Windows needs C** — HKCU's Run key is reachable only through the registry API (`RegOpenKeyExW` / `RegQueryValueExW` / `RegSetValueExW` / `RegDeleteValueW`), see the autostart section of `sys/sys_stub.c`. macOS is explicitly `Unsupported` this batch (it needs a LaunchAgents plist; deferred to a real machine).
- **The value's shape is a platform convention, not an arbitrary string**: a Linux `.desktop` Exec must be **wrapped in double quotes** as a whole (with `\` and `"` backslash-escaped inside and `%` written `%%`); a Windows REG_SZ value likewise needs the **whole string quoted** — a path with spaces and no quotes gets truncated at the first word when launched at login (i.e. it autostarts a program that does not exist). The quotes are assembled on the MoonBit side (`autostart_escape_exec` / `autostart_run_value`) and the C stub **writes and reads them verbatim**, with no second pass of processing.
- **Idempotency is part of the contract**: a repeated `enable` is an overwrite (the `.desktop` on Linux, the same-named Run value on Windows), and `disable` also succeeds when the entry was never there (Linux relies on `yue_sys_remove_file`'s probe-then-delete; Windows treats both `RegDeleteValueW`'s `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` and "the key itself is missing" as success).
- **app_id validation is a shared precondition of both destinations**: the app_id doubles as the `.desktop` filename and the Windows Run value name, so the DBus well-known-name character set is reused (≥2 dot-separated elements, each starting with a letter/underscore, only `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, total length ≤255). **`sys` does not depend on `dbus` in reverse**, so the rule is implemented locally in `sys/autostart.mbt` (`autostart_valid_app_id`). **A misconception worth recording**: an element **must not start** with `-` or a digit, but **may contain** them — `com.example.my-app2` is valid while `com.2app` and `com.-app` are not. A test first treated "contains `-`" as invalid (asserting `com.ap-p` to be false, though it is in fact valid); the run corrected it.
- **Link surface**: the `Reg*` family lives in **advapi32.lib**, while `sys`'s Windows link line previously carried only `ole32/shell32/uuid/user32` (those serve the file dialog's COM). This batch adds `advapi32.lib` to the sys entry (`scripts/prebuild.py`-managed; no link parameters inside the package).
- **Two things the pure-C probe provides itself** (`experiment/autostart_probe/autostart_backend_probe.c`): (1) **the probe ships its own `moonbit_make_bytes`** — a pure-C probe does not link the moon runtime, yet `yue_sys_*` returns moon byte strings; a self-consistent layout suffices (the length sits in the **4 bytes before** the data pointer, matching the real `moonbit_object` header), with the real ABI interaction covered by the whitebox tests. (2) **The `stub.c` include order defeats `_CRT_SECURE_NO_WARNINGS`** — the probe includes `<windows.h>` first (which already pulls in `string.h`), and UCRT decides at that instant whether to warn about deprecated names, so the stub's two `wcscpy`/`wcscat` sites produce 2 C4996 warnings in the probe build. That is **an artifact of the probe's include order, not a code problem**: the real build has moon's native-stub include `sys_stub.c` first, so the macro takes effect and no such warning appears.
