- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe ends with **`AUTOSTART-BACKEND-OK`**, all **36 assertions passing**: `yue_sys_exe_path` matching an independent `GetModuleFileNameW` call character for character (including the `.exe` suffix and backslashes), ok=0 when unwritten, a write followed by the **probe's own** `RegQueryValueExW` read confirming it landed under `Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run` with type `REG_SZ` and a byte-identical value, overwrite / Chinese-path round trip / empty value (length 0 yet present) / a 1207-character long value, `NULL` arguments returning -1 from all three entry points, an independent read after removal showing `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`, and a second idempotent removal still succeeding. **The probe really writes the user's registry** (that is its point); it deletes its own exclusive value `com.moonbit.native-gui.autostart_cprobe` both on entry and on exit, cleaning up regardless of outcome (checked afterwards with `reg query`; no residue). `sys/autostart_wbtest.mbt` has **8 tests**: 6 pure (directory resolution in three states / Exec escaping / `.desktop` content and parsing in both states / Run value shape and its inverse / app_id validation / the error `Show`) + 1 "an invalid app_id is rejected first on every platform" + **1 real lifecycle** (cleanup → not enabled → enable → enabled → the destination reads back (the `.desktop` body on Linux, the Run value on Windows, inverted and compared against the exe) → repeated enable is idempotent → disable → not enabled → idempotent disable again), skipping rather than faking when `autostart_supported()` is false. `moon test ./gui/sys` runs **174, 173 pass, 1 red** — that one is the **pre-existing** `fsx_wbtest.mbt:109` (the `sys_make_dirs` case was already red on Windows: `pr_available()` is true there, yet the case used the POSIX `/tmp` plus `pr_run("rm"/"mkdir")`; re-checked with `git stash` on a pristine tree, same single red, unrelated to this batch). **Since fixed** - `sys_make_dirs` is now the C thin FFI `yue_sys_make_dirs` and the case itself is platform-portable; `sys` is 178/178. `examples/native-sys` gains "enable autostart / disable autostart" buttons and a status label, plus the exit readout `NATIVE-SYS-AUTOSTART supported=1 enabled=0 id=… exe=… on_hits=0 note=…` (`is_enabled` reads the disk/registry live, so it still works after the window closes, unlike window readouts); real-machine smoke: `exit 0` (no button clicked, so `enabled=0` and no registry residue). `moon check ./gui/sys ./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` is warning-free; the C side under MSVC `/W4` keeps only the 2 C4996 warnings explained above; whole-repo red dots hold at **287/68**.
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: macOS (a LaunchAgents plist, left as Unsupported), launch arguments and delayed start (Windows could use the `**` prefix or `schtasks` with a delay, Linux could add `X-GNOME-Autostart-Delay` — not written here), capability probing beyond `is_supported`, and the real-machine feel of autostart (tracked in `_人工测试清单.md`).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · user idle detection (W21e: `sys/idle.mbt`, read-only seconds, no binary state)

- **The interface reports seconds only, with no active/idle binary**: `idle_seconds() -> Result[Double, IdleError]` returns the **seconds since the last input**, and threshold decisions ("how long counts as idle") are left to the caller — screen-awake, lock-screen and power-saving each want very different thresholds, and a hardcoded number in the library would just be worked around. Clamping and conversion (negatives to zero) are pure MoonBit functions (`idle_clamp_ms` / `idle_ms_to_seconds`); the stub returns only the raw milliseconds.
- **Platform sources come in three tiers (all in the idle section of `sys/sys_stub.c`)**: Windows uses `GetLastInputInfo` + `GetTickCount`; Linux uses the X11 **XScreenSaver extension (XSS)**; macOS is not implemented this batch (always false / `Unsupported`).
- **Windows: the difference must be an unsigned modular subtraction, not an int64 subtraction** (this batch's key detail). `GetTickCount()` is 32-bit and wraps about every **49.7 days**. The correct form is `DWORD elapsed = GetTickCount() - li.dwTime;` — unsigned subtraction is inherently modulo 2³², so it stays correct across the wrap point; converting each side to int64 first yields a huge negative number at the wrap (a negative idle time once converted to seconds). The pure-C probe pins this semantic down with synthetic values (`0x10 - 0xFFFFFFF0 == 0x20`).
- **Linux: runtime dlopen, with no X link dependency**. `sys`'s Linux link line carries only pthread/dl/m/stdc++, and **a headless consumer should not be tied to X libraries**, so XSS goes entirely through `dlopen("libX11.so.6"/".so")` + `dlopen("libXss.so.1"/".so")` and `dlsym` for `XOpenDisplay` / `XDefaultRootWindow` / `XScreenSaverAllocInfo` / `XScreenSaverQueryInfo` / `XFree` / `XCloseDisplay`. The `XScreenSaverInfo` struct is **declared locally in the stub** (rather than including `X11/extensions/scrnsaver.h`, which would drag in X headers); symbols are fetched through a `void*` hop (`memcpy`) to dodge `-Wcast-function-type`. The result is cached in a static `g_idle_x11` (a `tried` flag guards against repeated probing).
- **A Wayland session gets "unsupported" rather than a wrong number**: with no X server (`DISPLAY` empty) `idle_supported()` is false outright. Under XWayland, input does not reach the X server, so XSS readings only ever **overstate** — better to report nothing than a value that would mislead the caller (exactly why "seconds only" pairs with `Result`: treating failure as 0 seconds reads as "just moved").
- **Supportability and this call's outcome are two separate tiers**: `idle_supported()` is a runtime capability probe (Windows always true; Linux needs `DISPLAY` non-empty and both libraries dlopened; macOS false). Inside `idle_seconds()`, `ok=1` gives `Ok`; otherwise it **consults `idle_supported()`** — true means `Err(Failed("idle query failed"))` (e.g. an X connection dropping mid-flight), false means `Err(Unsupported)`. Keeping them apart is what lets the caller distinguish "this machine has no such capability" from "this particular query failed".
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/idle_probe/idle_backend_probe.c` ends with **`IDLE-BACKEND-OK`**, all **13 assertions passing**: supportability always 1, the reading non-negative and within int32 (no truncation), matching an **independently recomputed** `GetTickCount() - li.dwTime` (within ±2000ms, tolerating the call gap and GetTickCount granularity), consecutive reads non-decreasing, the modular-subtraction semantic on synthetic values (wrap included), five consecutive reads all succeeding, and the `ok` out-parameter written every call. The probe only reads system state and writes nothing (one fewer self-provided artifact than W21d: the idle interface returns no moon byte string, so it needs no bundled `moonbit_make_bytes`). See the probe's header comment for the compile line (add `user32.lib` — `GetLastInputInfo` lives in user32; and because including `sys_stub.c` pulls in the registry functions, link `advapi32.lib` along with it). `sys/idle_wbtest.mbt` has **4 tests**: 3 pure (millisecond clamping / milliseconds-to-seconds / error `Show`) + 1 "supportability probe" (asserts true on Windows, merely prints elsewhere) + 1 "reading non-negative and two consecutive reads monotonic (a decrease can only mean an input zeroed it in between, and then the new value is necessarily <0.05s)". `moon test ./gui/sys` runs **178, 177 pass, 1 red** — that one is the **pre-existing** `fsx_wbtest.mbt:109` (unrelated to this batch; re-checked on a pristine tree earlier). **Since fixed** - see the revised C-stub policy line above. `examples/native-sys` gains a "read idle" button and status line, and an exit line `NATIVE-SYS-IDLE supported=1 seconds=2486.141 hits=0 note=…`; the real-machine smoke run ends `exit 0`, and `NATIVE-SYS-GEO` includes `idle=1786,368,110x32` (the button laid out). `moon check ./examples/native-sys` is warning-free; the repo-wide red count stays at **287/68**.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · cross-platform global-hotkey facade (W22: `core/hotkey.mbt`, the last of the three service facades)

- **W22's scope, tightened**: under "unify the tray / notification / hotkey facades", the tray (`core/tray.mbt`) and notifications (`core/notify.mbt`) already landed their own facades in W20c/W21a, and this batch **does not refactor their existing APIs** (changing error types and probe names would be a purely breaking change with zero caller benefit). What was genuinely missing was that **hotkeys had no cross-platform facade at all** — W20b landed only in the win package (free functions such as `register_global_shortcut` plus the `parse_accelerator` pure function), leaving Linux/macOS callers with no way to write isomorphic code. So W22 = add `core/hotkey.mbt`, shaped like the other two: **probe `hotkey_supported()` + structured error `HotkeyError{Unsupported, Failed(String)}` + pump hook `hotkey_poll()`**.
- **Current backends and the degradation contract**: Windows is a real implementation (`RegisterHotKey` + a thread queue, already verified in W20b); Linux's X11 `XGrabKey` and macOS's Carbon/AppKit hotkeys are **not wired**, and always return `Err(Unsupported)` — "not built" and "failed" are two separate tiers, so callers can hide UI accordingly, the same contract as the tray's absent SNI watcher and notifications' absent backend.
- **Accelerator strings in, id lookup in the facade**: registration takes a string (`"Ctrl+Alt+F9"` / `"CmdOrCtrl+Shift+M"`, where `CmdOrCtrl` maps to Ctrl/Cmd per platform — parsing delegates to win's pure function rather than reimplementing it in core); `hotkey_poll()` returns `Hotkey{id, spec}` on a hit — Win32's `poll` only returns an id, so **the accelerator-string lookup table is held by the facade itself** (process-level, same lifetime as win's registry; an id outside the table gets an empty `spec`, meaning it was not registered through this facade).
- **Platform probing goes through the runtime `@sys.sys_platform()`** (the C stub `ffi_sys_platform` returns 1/2/3 → "windows"/"macos"/"linux"), consistent with AGENTS rule 11 "runtime probe + match, no `#cfg`"; the `is_mac` criterion comes from the same source (it decides the `CmdOrCtrl` mapping).
- **Two small toolchain pitfalls (both already hit in the code)**: ① `Array` **has no `find`** — the lookup table's linear scan is a hand-written loop (`for h in arr { if … return Some(h) }`); appending uses `arr.push(x)`, **never `arr + [x]`** (the parser treats `+` as infix and reports `unexpected token infix 1`, an error message far from the root cause). ② `inspect(value, content="…")`'s `content` is a string **compared against the value's stringified form** — a boolean must be given `"true"`/`"false"`; passing a descriptive word like "supported" always fails (hit once in this batch and once in the taskbar batch, recorded here once).
- **Evidence**: `gui/core/hotkey_wbtest.mbt` has 2 tests (an unregistered id's lookup returns an empty string; "assert only when connected" — when the backend is available, an empty accelerator string must go through `Err(Failed)` rather than crashing or silently succeeding, other platforms only print skip); `moon test ./gui/core` **118/118** (the original 116 plus 2 new). `examples/native-app`'s hotkey section switched from calling `@win.*` directly to `@core.hotkey_*` (two sites: registration and polling; a poll hit now shows the spec directly), `--deny-warn` warning-free, and the smoke run survived 100 seconds with no panic and no CRITICAL.
- **Not done this batch, and not claimed as done**: the Linux `XGrabKey` and macOS hotkey backends (the facade is ready; landing the backends will not change callers); the unification refactor of the tray/notification APIs (explicitly out of scope, reason in the first bullet).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · taskbar progress (W20g: `win/taskbar.mbt`, the Windows half of the §6.3 seam item "taskbar progress / dock badge")

- **Write-only, no read-back**: Win32's `ITaskbarList3` has **no getter** — `TBPF_*` progress state cannot be read back (the same "mapping is not reversible; code review is the backstop" situation as W21a's kind→`NIIF_*` mapping). So the surface only has writers (`set_taskbar_progress` / `set_taskbar_state` / `clear_taskbar_progress`), and the probe (`taskbar_supported`) only reports whether the COM instance came up — it never returns a current progress value. Callers that need to display progress keep their own number (deliberately a different tier from `notify_title`, which *can* read back because `NIF_INFO`'s `szInfoTitle` is readable).
- **The COM instance is a process-level singleton; the target window is distinguished by hwnd**: the `ITaskbarList3` interface itself is window-agnostic (the `hwnd` argument of `SetProgressValue` is the target), so the stub keeps one static singleton (`wm_taskbar` plus a `wm_taskbar_tried` guard against repeated probing) and takes the window handle from the window slot table (an out-of-range `id` or a NULL `hwnd` returns 0, the same contract as every other `wm_*` entry). Initialization details: `CoInitializeEx(COINIT_APARTMENTTHREADED)` falls back to `CoInitialize` on `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE`; `S_FALSE` (this thread already initialized) counts as success; a failed `HrInit` must `Release` before reporting unavailable; there is no `CoUninitialize` at exit — the COM object is reclaimed with the process, and other COM users remain on the app exit path (W14 file dialogs, W20f WIC).
- **GUID symbols come from `uuid.lib`, not ole32**: C mode has no `__uuidof`, so the two constants `CLSID_TaskbarList` / `IID_ITaskbarList3` must be resolved at link time — **ole32.lib only exports the basic COM GUIDs; these two live in uuid.lib** (the same pitfall as W20f's `CLSID_WICImagingFactory` family living in windowscodecs.lib: which library a GUID constant lands in has to be confirmed one by one). Hence `gui/scripts/prebuild.py`'s Windows branch adds `uuid.lib` to the win entry (the probe's manual build line does the same).
- **Progress values go through `Int64`**: byte-count-style progress values overflow 32 bits, so the MoonBit side uses `Int64` for `value`/`total` and the C side passes them through as `ULONGLONG`; when `total <= 0` the `SetProgressValue` semantics are meaningless, and the documented fallback is "use the `Indeterminate` state for indeterminate progress".
- **Evidence**: the pure-C probe `experiment/taskbar_probe/taskbar_backend_probe.c` ends with **`TASKBAR-BACKEND-OK`** and **all 20 assertions pass** — `wm_init` succeeds, `supported == 1` and the singleton is non-NULL (these two together verify both the uuid.lib GUID symbol resolution and the `CoCreateInstance` + `HrInit` steps), a real window is created (`wm_window_new` + `wm_show`, closed with `wm_close` at the end), all five state ids and the `value`/`total` write paths succeed, a `4GB-1 / 8GB` large value succeeds, the guard paths (out-of-range `id=0` / `id=9999`, an unbuilt window whose slot exists but whose `hwnd` is NULL, illegal `state=5` / `state=-1`) all return 0 without crashing or writing, the teardown clears back to `NoProgress` before closing, and the same id returns 0 after the window closes (`hwnd` already NULL). `gui/win/taskbar_wbtest.mbt` has **4 tests**: ids aligned with `TBPF_*`, id round-trip, unknown ids falling to `NoProgress` (matching the C-side default branch), and "assert only when connected" — when the backend is available, a nonexistent window handle must return `false` rather than silently succeeding (other platforms only print skip). `moon test ./gui/win` **28/28** (the original 24 plus 4 new).
- **Example wiring**: `examples/native-app` gains a "taskbar progress (§6.3)" section — seven writer buttons (10% / 50% / 100% / error / paused / indeterminate / clear) plus a `live_counter` that reads the most recent operation's result label every frame (a failed write lands in the label rather than staying silent); the exit status line is `NATIVE-APP-TASKBAR supported=… last=…`. `moon check ./examples/native-app --deny-warn` is warning-free; the smoke run survives (110 seconds with no panic and no CRITICAL — note that native-app's self-destruct timer is **10 minutes**, so unattended runs always need a timeout, see the `_人工测试清单.md` header).
- **Not done this batch, and not claimed as done**: Linux's taskbar progress has its own Unity LauncherEntry (DBus `com.canonical.Unity.LauncherEntry`) path and macOS's is the dock badge (`NSDockTile`), both deferred to a later cross-platform facade batch — the GTK branch and the placeholder branch only provide compilable failing stubs with `supported` always 0; the **real look** of the taskbar button (green / red / yellow bar, indeterminate marquee) can only be judged by a human and belongs to section O of `_人工测试清单.md`.
- **The probe's two C4996 warnings are a pre-existing artifact**: `wcsncpy` / `wcsncat` in the `wm_im_*` section — the probe's own `#include <windows.h>` disables the stub's `_CRT_SECURE_NO_WARNINGS` (the same note as W21d); the real build (moon's native-stub) has no such warning.

### Media returns to its own module: module-level optionality restored (2026-10-12, architecture-boundary batch)

- **How it came up**: the `TODO.md` line "retire the old chain's `modules/gui-media`" was read as "ffmpeg is going away". Checking the tree confirmed ffmpeg is not going anywhere (the new-stack `yue/media` still imports `NoahLiu/ffmpeg-mbt/src` — it is the **decode backend**), but it surfaced a real problem: `yue/media` lived *inside the main module*, and for its import of ffmpeg-mbt to resolve the root `moon.mod` had to declare `NoahLiu/ffmpeg-mbt@0.1.0` — so **every consumer of the main module pulled ffmpeg into its dependency closure**, cancelling the original "media as a separate module = optional dependency" design.
- **The mechanism (a hard moon constraint)**: an import in `moon.pkg` requires the module dependency to be declared in `moon.mod` first (otherwise: `containing module is not imported`). Therefore "the media package lives in the main module" and "module-level optionality" are **mutually exclusive**: optionality can only be had by moving media wholly out of the main module. **Keep the two layers apart** — **package-level optionality** (a `moon.pkg` import is the compile/link boundary) was never lost: not importing `yue/media` means `-lav*` never reaches the link line. What was lost is the **dependency-closure / build-time** layer.
- **One measurement correction**: this repo is a moon workspace (`moon.work` lists `modules/ffmpeg-mbt`), so **building any in-repo package runs ffmpeg-mbt's prebuild** — measured: `moon build ./gui/core` and `moon build ./modules/yoga-mbt/src` both print the `ffmpeg(prebuild):` line, and neither has anything to do with media. That is **not** caused by the root `moon.mod`; workspace membership alone triggers it (`.mooncakes` holds no `NoahLiu/`, so every `NoahLiu/*` resolves from a local workspace member). **This batch's payoff is therefore confined to external consumers**: they sit outside this workspace and resolve only `moon.mod`, so once that line is gone ffmpeg-mbt never enters their closure. In-repo/CI still runs it (CI installs the ffmpeg dev packages anyway; no added burden).
- **What was done**: ①`git mv yue/media modules/gui-media/media` — the media package moves into the existing standalone module `NoahLiu/gui-media` (which was split out for optional media in the first place); ②the miniaudio system-library link flags (`-lpthread -ldl -lm -lstdc++ -latomic`) move out of the main repo's `scripts/prebuild.py` into the **new module's own** `modules/gui-media/prebuild.py`, keyed by package `NoahLiu/gui-media/media` (graceful empty link_configs off Linux, same stance as ffmpeg-mbt / the main prebuild); ③`modules/gui-media/moon.mod` gains `NoahLiu/yoga-mbt@0.1.0` (the media package takes layout nodes via `@yoga`) plus the prebuild option; ④the root `moon.mod` drops `NoahLiu/ffmpeg-mbt@0.1.0`.
- **The example has to move with it (the `b01d1dd` lesson)**: `examples/native-web` was the **only** package in the main module importing `yue/media`. Left there, the main module would have to depend back on the media module — recreating the "main ↔ media" publish cycle that once left both unpublishable and forced the examples into `modules/gui-examples`. So the whole example moves to `modules/gui-examples/native-web` (the module documented as "the standalone example module that depends on the media extension"); its `moon.pkg` changes `NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/media` to `NoahLiu/gui-media/media` (the alias still derives as `@media`, so **zero source changes**), and `modules/gui-examples/moon.mod` gains `NoahLiu/yoga-mbt@0.1.0`. **No new page**: the example's header states it is "the accumulating verification example for webview and media — no new page for the same family", which splitting would violate.
- **The consolidation policy follows**: the "verification example consolidation" notes in `TODO.md` now point the webview + media side at `modules/gui-examples/native-web` instead of `examples/native-web` (the historical evidence lines keep their original wording — that is what happened then).
- **Evidence**: ①`moon tree` for the root module **no longer lists `NoahLiu/ffmpeg-mbt`** (it was explicitly listed before; after the change the top level is just yoga-mbt / subproc / markdown); ②repo-wide `moon check` red count stays at **287/68** with every error still in the old-chain packages (`events.mbt`/`splitter.mbt`/`types.mbt`/`props.mbt`/`video_view.mbt`…), showing the move did not touch them; ③both moved packages re-check clean after a forced re-check (touching sources then `moon check`); ④the new module's prebuild run directly — `echo '{}' | python modules/gui-media/prebuild.py` → `{"link_configs": []}` (non-Linux degradation); ⑤`grep` shows no main-module package referencing `yue/media` and no `MEDIA_PACKAGE` left in the main prebuild. **One Windows-side gain (it was already broken)**: on Windows the root module could not build at all — the `native-web` → `yue/media` C stub needs `libavformat/avformat.h` and `audio_stub.c` uses `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (measured: `moon build ./examples/native-web` fails outright); after the move the root module contains no package that cannot build on Windows.
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: `miniaudio.h` is **still two copies** (`modules/gui-media/src/miniaudio.h` and `modules/gui-media/media/miniaudio.h`, same md5 `8d5bf2a0b46efa835bde4d0eac5a0cd8`) — folding them awaits retiring the old chain's `modules/gui-media/src`, which is blocked on migrating `systemprobe`'s media page; the media package is still unusable on Windows/macOS (POSIX clock + ffmpeg backend unwired); and media's real behaviour on Linux (does audio actually come out, how seek feels) cannot be verified on this host — it stays with the manual checklist.
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: macOS (would need `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` / IOKit HID, left as Unsupported), idle on a native Wayland session (would need `org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver` or `ext-idle-notify-v1`; this batch reports "unsupported" only), the **setter** side of "keep the screen awake" (`XScreenSaverSuspend` / `SetThreadExecutionState` — this batch only **reads** idle, it does not inhibit sleep), any notification mechanism for idle-threshold changes, and the real-machine feel (tracked in `_人工测试清单.md`).

### Media audio backend goes cross-platform: a three-way monotonic clock (2026-10-12)

- **How it came up**: the previous batch moved the media package into its own module and filed "media cannot build on Windows" under "POSIX clock + ffmpeg unwired". This batch takes care of the half that is **ours** — `audio_stub.c` is media's audio backend (the miniaudio device layer plus a PCM queue) with **zero ffmpeg dependency**, so whether it compiles and runs can be pinned down on its own, without waiting for the ffmpeg line.
- **Symptom (measured under MSVC)**: `audio_stub.c` reports `error C2065: "CLOCK_MONOTONIC": undeclared identifier`, plus `warning C4013: "clock_gettime" undefined`. `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` is a POSIX-only constant that MSVC's `<time.h>` does not provide (and `clock_gettime` itself only lands in some UCRT versions).
- **Fix (one stub.c, multiple branches, platform macros — precedent `win/stub.c`; AGENTS rule 11)**: `mbt_now_ms` now branches three ways — `_WIN32` uses `QueryPerformanceCounter` / `QueryPerformanceFrequency` (QPC is Windows' monotonic high-resolution counter; **not `GetTickCount`**, whose granularity is ~15.6 ms and which is nudged by system-time changes); `__APPLE__` uses `mach_absolute_time` + `mach_timebase_info` (not `clock_gettime`: `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` needs macOS 10.12+, and mach is both more conservative and equally monotonic); everything else POSIX keeps `clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC)` **byte-for-byte unchanged**. All three return "milliseconds since some arbitrary fixed epoch" — used only for differences (`elapsed = now - wall0`), where the absolute value is meaningless, so a platform-dependent epoch does not affect the playback clock.
- **Two overflow guards**: ①the Windows branch splits into `(ticks / freq) * 1000 + ((ticks % freq) * 1000) / freq` so `ticks * 1000` cannot overflow 64 bits (QPC counts from boot; at 10 MHz a year is ≈ 3.15e14, so ×1000 still has headroom, but the split is strictly safer); ②the macOS branch uses `__uint128_t` to bridge `t * numer / denom`, guarding the intermediate product when numer/denom is not 1:1 (as on Apple Silicon).
- **Both copies changed together**: `media/audio_stub.c` and `src/audio_stub.c` were byte-identical (md5 `2b807752…`). At one point the change left one extra comment line in the `src/` copy — which made them **no longer** byte-identical; that line was reverted on the spot and the two are identical again (both md5 `856a4c98e3879e77aa5108f7bb4dcfd8`). Reason: retiring the old chain promises a "fold to one copy" step, and silent drift between the two makes that impossible to decide mechanically.
- **Evidence (pure C probe `experiment/media_probe/audio_backend_probe.c`, which directly `#include`s the platform backend)**: last line **`AUDIO-BACKEND-OK`**, **48 assertions pass**, 0 failures — ①**clock**: consecutive/five-in-a-row reads are non-decreasing, the value is positive, and `Sleep(60)` advances it by 61 ms (within [40,400]); ②**open-argument validation**: `channels ∈ [1,8]` and `rate > 0` are accepted; `0`/`9`/`-1`/`rate=0`/negative rate all return NULL; ③**queue and volume semantics** (independent of whether a device exists): `push` appends, `queued_bytes` accumulates 400→600, an invalid push leaves the queue untouched, `clear` zeroes it and is idempotent on the second call, `played_bytes` is non-negative, volume clamps into [0,1] (0.5/1/0.25 read back exactly, -1→0, 2→1); ④**both device-lifecycle branches run** (no environment assumed): with a real device on this host → `start=0`/`is_started=1`/second start = -1/`played_bytes` really advances (measured 35280 bytes)/pause=0/second pause = -1; with no device (CI/headless) → start = -1 and the state stays 0; ⑤**NULL-argument defence across the board** (failure codes where they are due, no crashes where there should be none). The probe also **reads `QueryPerformanceFrequency` directly and prints 10000000 Hz**, proving the QPC high-resolution branch is the one taken, not a coarse-grained fallback. Build recipe in the probe header (`-I <moon>/include` for `moonbit.h`; add `ole32.lib uuid.lib` for miniaudio's WASAPI backend; `#pragma warning(disable:4244)` silences only miniaudio's own single assignment narrowing, not our warnings — the probe body is warning-free under /W4).
- **A first-hand measurement**: `miniaudio.h` (a 4.2 MB single header) **does compile under MSVC** — apart from one `ma_uint64 → ma_uint32` (C4244) on its own line 80532 it produces no warnings. The audio backend therefore has a real landing path on Windows; the clock was the only missing piece.
- **A boundary that must be stated**: after the change `audio_stub.c` **compiles and runs** on Windows (the probe is the proof), but `moon test ./modules/gui-media/media` **still cannot run** — the test executable links `ffmpeg_stub.c` as well, and that needs `libavformat/avformat.h` (the `ffmpeg-mbt` line is unwired off Linux). So this batch's Windows payoff is **a compilable, runnable audio backend**, not "the media package now works on Windows". The Linux branch is byte-for-byte untouched, so behaviour is unchanged (this host cannot run Linux — that is a code-level claim, not a measurement).
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: `ffmpeg-mbt`'s Windows/macOS backends (wiring a system decoder or shipping DLLs — a separate line); the media package therefore still cannot be built or tested as a whole off Linux; the macOS branch (`mach_absolute_time`) cannot be verified on this host and rests on a code-level argument only; audible output and seek feel still belong to `_人工测试清单.md`.
- **One measured conclusion along the way (so nobody "fixes" the wrong place)**: the media prebuild's "non-Linux → empty link_configs" is **correct** — do not add `ole32.lib`/`uuid.lib`, nor macOS frameworks. ①**Windows**: taking `audio_stub.c` (which `#include`s miniaudio) and **linking with no extra libraries at all** succeeds (measured: `/Fe:probe_nolibs.exe` links with zero libraries and still passes all 48 assertions at runtime), because miniaudio defaults to **runtime `LoadLibrary`** (`MA_NO_RUNTIME_LINKING` undefined) and emits no link-time symbols (the earlier probe carrying `ole32.lib uuid.lib` was that pair being **unnecessary**, not required). ②**macOS**: miniaudio's own wording is "the macOS build should compile cleanly without the need to download any dependencies nor link to any libraries or frameworks" — the default is runtime framework linking too (only an explicit `-DMA_NO_RUNTIME_LINKING` demands `-framework CoreFoundation -framework CoreAudio -framework AudioToolbox`, and that macro's automatic definition lives in the **DOS** branch, not the Apple one); the command line needs only `-lpthread -lm`, both of which macOS pulls in from libSystem by default. So of the three platforms, **only Linux needs explicit system libraries** (`-lpthread -ldl -lm -lstdc++ -latomic`).

### First example-migration batch: showcase skeleton + basic/feedback pages (G7, 2026-10-12)

- **Scope**: `examples/showcase` moves off the old chain (the `@yue` root package + `@declarative` + `@components`) onto the new stack (the `Node` layer in `core` + `ui` + `theme`); this batch lands the **skeleton + the basic-components page + the feedback page**. The other 13 of the old chain's 15 pages (icons / form / events & layout / nav / data display / charts / code & docs / store comparison / system integration / window / browser / canvas & rich text) were **removed from the working tree** (`git rm`; the sources are recoverable from history before `91204ca` at any time) and will come in page by page, **basics first, heavy controls later** — the browser page carries a WebKit heavyweight control and the icon-wall page builds 803 icon widgets, so neither is a good place to start.
- **Four mechanical differences (old chain → this stack; recorded once so no page has to rediscover them)**:
  ① **`style=[...]` does not exist**. New-stack components take explicit parameters only (`padding`/`row_gap`/`width`…); spacing is a property of the **layout container**, not of the control. The old `style=[("marginTop", 8.0)]` lines all go away and become the parent's `row_gap`.
  ② **Visibility and lazy mounting moved a layer**: `View::set_visible` → `Widget::set_visible` (internally the safe `display:none` yoga path) and `Node::mount_into(View)` → `Node::mount_into(Widget)`; `Node.mount`'s closure also returns a `Widget` instead of a `View`. Semantics unchanged.
  ③ **"Fill the remaining height" cannot be written on the control**: the old chain wrote `style=[("flexgrow", 1.0)]`; the new `scroll_v` takes **no** `grow` parameter, so the viewport widget is grown inside the `handle` callback (`node.set_flex_grow(1.0)` — `handle` receives the viewport itself).
  ④ **The nav page is ours to write**: `nav_page` was always an example-local helper (`examples/showcase/ui/section.mbt`) and this stack has no equivalent either, so it was reimplemented on the same mechanism in `shared/section.mbt` — `set_visible` for switching, **mount only on first selection** (mounting all 15 pages up front is not affordable at startup; that mechanism has to stay).
- **Pitfall 1: the local shared layer must not be called `ui/`**. On the old chain the component library was `@components` and the example's own shared layer was `@ui`, so there was no clash; on the new stack the component library **is** `ui`, and two packages both deriving to `@ui` is an outright ambiguity. Fix: `examples/showcase/ui/` → `examples/showcase/shared/` (deriving to `@shared`), with the `@ui.App`/`@ui.page`/`@ui.section` call sites updated. **Do not** paper over it with an explicit alias for the component library in `moon.pkg` — the example would then read wrong for the long run.
- **Pitfall 2: `popover_t`'s width/height are named parameters**. The old call was positional — `popover_t(trigger, content, 180.0, 60.0)`; the new signature is `popover_t(trigger, content, width?, height?, handle?)`, so two positional `Double`s fail to compile with **4080** (`which requires 2 positional arguments, but is given 4`). Whenever a new-stack signature has extra optional parameters, check whether they are named ones — copying old call sites is exactly how this bites.
- **Pitfall 3: the old chain's `@yue.VERSION` is unusable**. It lives in the **root package** (the old-chain package, i.e. exactly where the repo's 287 red dots are), and importing it drags that whole red package into the dependency closure. The example keeps a local `SHOWCASE_VERSION` constant instead, noting that the authoritative value stays `moon.mod` + `yue/version.mbt` (whose build-time consistency check is `scripts/prebuild.py`'s job).
- **Two component-layer consolidations (write the new shape; do not hunt for the old function)**: `alert_closeable` folded into `alert(closable=true, on_close=)`, and `result` takes only `(title, subtitle, semantic type)` — it **no longer** takes an actions array; the caller lays the actions out alongside it ("components give shape, content belongs to the caller" is a general stance of this component layer).
- **One API clarification worth carrying forward**: `toast_layer` on this stack **returns the push function only** — no "layer node + push function" pair. The strip opens on the **overlay layer** (created on the first push, taken down with the last), so a page no longer keeps an empty tree around and toasts always sit above dialogs and other overlays. Migrating code should **delete** the `toast_node` that the old `@components.toast_layer()` returned rather than mounting it.
- **Verification**: `moon check ./examples/showcase ./examples/showcase/shared ./examples/showcase/pages --deny-warn` is **warning-free**; repo-wide `moon check` red dots hold at **287 errors / 68 warnings** (the 14 deleted pages were already counted there); the real-machine smoke exits `0` with `SHOWCASE-READY title=组件库演示板 pages=2 follow=1` and `SHOWCASE-DONE page=基础组件`, the window title is right, it responds, and `CloseMainWindow` exits cleanly. The `libpng warning: iCCP: cHRM chunk does not match sRGB` line on exit is **pre-existing** (`examples/native-app` prints it too) and unrelated to this batch.
- **Not done this batch, not pretending otherwise**: the remaining 13 pages; the menubar / tray / single-instance wiring (both `dialog_t`s are mounted in the window root and openable from the feedback page, while menubar, tray and single instance are handled "as the system-integration and window pages come in"; `examples/native-app` already demonstrates them); and the real-machine feel (sidebar hover, scroll position surviving section switches, follow-vs-manual theme switching) which belongs to `_人工测试清单.md`.

### Declarative Layer and Self-Drawn Components

- Never call `set_background_color` inside mouse callbacks: runtime CSS rewrites swallow the immediately following press ("works on the second click"). Interactive states (hover / pressed) are expressed in on_draw; set_background_color is allowed only at mount time and in theme-subscription callbacks.
- A flex container's attach order is also its layout order and z-order: interleave widgets (handles / dividers) must attach in exact layout position and stay permanently visible — users find them by looking, not by hovering.
- **markdown_view span-offset accounting and mdast observed semantics (MD2, 2026-10)**: the old hand-written parsers (md_parse_inline / md_parse_blocks) are gone; `yue/markdown.mbt` now consumes mizchi/markdown (mdast). ①**Attributed-text range endpoints must be accumulated per rendered-text segment in UTF-16 code units (`md_utf16_len`) — never counted in code points, never derived from mdast Spans.** MoonBit's `for ch in str` iterates code points while `str.length()` counts UTF-16 code units (verified: "a👍b" iterates 3 times, length 4); AttributedText ranges are UTF-16-semantic, so styling after an emoji (non-BMP, 1 code point = 2 code units) shifts left when accumulated in code points. Span-based locating is not viable at all: rendered text ≠ source text (markers stripped, soft breaks turned into spaces, link URLs dropped) — ranges can only be measured over the rendered text. Inline nesting is flattened iteratively with an explicit style-carrying stack (`md_flatten_inline`), merging adjacent same-style segments to cut range count; rendering and tests share one accounting function `md_span_ranges` (wbtest asserts the bold range after an emoji is [4,6), not the code-point-misaligned [3,5)). ②Three mizchi/markdown 0.8.3 parsing semantics: `FencedCode.code` carries a trailing `\n` (line splitting must drop the trailing empty line); `parse_code_block_info` only understands `lang:file {meta}` and **does not split space-separated info strings** ("rust title=x" lands whole in lang), so the language must be taken as the first word; blank lines between paragraphs produce `BlankLines` blocks, so AST traversal cannot assume "block index = document order" (a test tripped on children[2] actually being a blank block). Rendering decisions: HtmlBlock / footnotes not rendered, tables degraded to row lines (cells joined by two spaces, bold header), task lists consume `ListItem.checked` (☑/☐), Blockquote children render recursively inside the accent bar, ordered lists honor `start`. Verification: `moon check` zero warnings + `moon test` 595 all green (19 new wbtests covering conversion / flattening / ranges / info / line splitting, plus the five-item CommonMark-deviation before/after acceptance: setext headings / ~~~ fences / info strings / indented code blocks / nested lists).

- **Subscriptions must be unsubscribable, or rebuild-style components accumulate dead subscriptions without bound (CORE2)**: Store/Signal subscription closures are held by the signal kernel and theme subscriptions by theme_box.subs, with no removal entry — every component that rebuilds rows or remounts a subtree leaks a whole generation per rebuild: transfer leaks one set per move; table_t leaks "row container + one themed_container per cell + bind_fg" per rows Store set (over a thousand per refresh on a 1000-row table); each snackbar push leaks two; each bind_node remount leaks the old subtree's bind_label Store subscription. Dead subscriptions cost little memory but every set / theme_apply then runs against destroyed views (set_color / schedule_paint on removed widgets), and the closures keep the captured view subtrees alive forever. Fix on both sides: (1) subscriptions carry ids — `Signal::subscribe` returns a handle and `Signal::remove(id)` removes it; on Store, `subscribe_id(f)` returns a handle and `remove(handle)` removes it (`Store::subscribe` keeps returning Unit: MoonBit forbids implicitly discarding non-Unit values, so changing its return type would touch a hundred-plus call sites repo-wide). (2) Theme subscriptions get the same treatment: `on_theme_change` returns a handle, `off_theme_change(handle)` removes it. (3) Deep or bulk subscriptions use the **subscription bag** `sub_bag_begin() / sub_bag_end() / sub_bag_discard(closure list)`: open a bag before a rebuild, and every subscription registered during it (Store + theme, including themed_container / bind_fg / bind_label at any depth of the subtree) pushes its unsubscribe closure into the bag; the old generation is discarded wholesale before rebuilding — transfer's render_all, table_t's rebuild, bind_node's rebuild and the snackbar's dismiss timer all use it. The bag is a stack (nesting-safe), the same "collection-time global context" pattern as the existing sig_collector in signals.mbt. Verified: moon check with zero warnings + moon test 657 all green (8 new cases: Store unsubscribe and idempotence, Signal handle removal, three bag generations and nesting, theme off and bag, empty-stack end, theme bag on component rebuild, carousel_stop).
- **Timers started at mount need a reclamation channel; the two timer APIs have different contracts (CORE2)**: a timer started when a component mounts has no "view was removed" notification, so without reclamation it keeps ticking against a destroyed subtree. The two libyue timer contracts differ: `set_timeout` returns an id that `clear_timeout(id)` cancels immediately (self-rescheduling carousel chains use this); `set_timer` is a periodic callback with **no cancellable id — the only way to stop it is returning false from the callback** (the shim's SetTimer does not hand back an id). Hence carousel_t gains a `rotation? : CarouselHandle` handle plus `carousel_stop(handle)` (stops auto-advance and cancels the pending timeout; manual arrows/dots are unaffected), while video_view_t's frame-advance timer follows a documented contract — `vid_stop(handle)` sets running=false so the next tick returns false and stops, and **must be called before unmounting** (at most one extra tick). Both "must stop before unmounting" contracts are written into the usage docs (components-ui / system-capabilities, zh and en in sync).

### Self-drawn canvas and chart rendering

- 8-digit hex colors have the alpha **first** (`#AARRGGBB` — a libyue `ParseHexColor` source-level convention, the opposite of CSS `#RRGGBBAA`; `yue/color.mbt`'s `parse_hex` agrees). Appending a tail neither errors nor changes opacity — the two hex digits land in the **blue channel**, showing up as color drift: instance one, the floating scrollbar fade changed the alpha string from "d9" to "73", which actually moved blue 0xd9→0x73 with red/green untouched, so the fading thumb turned yellow; instance two, chart area fill `color + "26"` prepends nothing — accent `#009688` + "26" → `#00968826` = alpha 0x00 (fully transparent) + RGB(96,88,38), so **the area fill had never rendered at all**. Fixed by routing everything through `with_alpha(hex, aa)` (prepends the alpha); any set_fill_color/set_stroke_color needing opacity must use it — no hand-appended suffixes.
- An offscreen `Canvas::new + get_painter` supports all geometry drawing (fill / stroke / arc / clip) without `initialize()`, so drawing benchmarks run on headless CI too; but text paths (`draw_text` / `AttributedText::get_bounds_for`) segfault outright — the GTK text stack needs initialize first. Benchmarks therefore come in two tiers: "pure pipeline + geometry-call mirror" (no display needed) and a real full-frame run (text included) that only executes when DISPLAY is present; both tiers print from `charts_wbtest.mbt`.
- `stroke()` under the painter's default stroke color is a no-op (draws nothing): a benchmark that forgets `set_stroke_color` shows stroke cost as ~0 and passes bogusly. Always set an explicit color before timing strokes.
- Under software rasterization (GTK/X11, no GPU) a path stroke costs ~1.8µs per segment, ~13µs for near-vertical segments (slope > 30); `fill_rect` ~1µs each (axis-aligned fast path), `draw_text` ~0.11ms per call, `line_to` itself ~12ns (path building only). A 1000-point × 4-series line chart drawn entirely with path strokes costs 45–60ms per frame — far over the 5ms acceptance line.
- Fix (shared by the whole chart family, see `yue/charts.mbt`): in dense mode (more points than pixel columns) decimate to columns (min/max preserving extremes) and switch to rect paths — area mode fills one rect per column up to the anchor (the fill's top edge *is* the line), line mode draws a min..max vertical bar per column; only sparse mode (points <= columns) uses a true polyline plus polygon area fill. Per-frame cost is decoupled from window size and grows only with plot width.
- libyue's Arc cannot express a counterclockwise arc: on GTK `PainterGtk::Arc` is a plain `cairo_arc`, and cairo normalizes `ea < sa` into a "plus 2π clockwise long arc"; the Win public API is hardwired clockwise too (the underlying ArcPixel has an `anticlockwise` parameter that is never exposed). The shim's "ccw as negative span" conversion therefore never takes effect on either backend — using ccw for a ring's inner arc wraps the hole into the long arc and fills a solid pie (verified by real-machine screenshot: donut and gauge centers were not hollow, with the total/percentage painted on the solid face). Fix: approximate the inner return arc with a polyline (32 segments per full circle, chord error < 0.4px, identical on all platforms); for pure strokes (icon arcs) simply swap the start/end angles, which is geometrically equivalent. Changing `PainterGtk::Arc` to `cairo_arc_negative` in the fork would be the root fix, but that needs a vendor-* release, so it is deferred.
- Chart acceptance benchmarks (release, Ubuntu 24.04 XFCE X11, offscreen canvas + initialize, real full frame incl. text): line 1000 points × 4 series (adversarial sawtooth) 3.08ms / smooth data 1.81ms; bar 200 categories 0.38ms; donut 50 sectors 0.70ms; gauge 0.15ms; scatter 10000 points 3.03ms. Pure-function pipeline (range / ticks / decimation / mapping) for the line chart: 0.028ms. Long-run push: 4800 pushes (10 min at 2Hz × 4 series) total 2.89ms with window length pinned at 1000 — live data is bounded at 4 × 1000 × 8B = 32KB; the increment is GC-reclaimed window garbage, which does not accumulate across continuous pushes.
- Line area anchor: the zero line when 0 is inside the range, the plot bottom for all-positive data, the plot top for all-negative (straddling columns emit two rects, above and below).
- `Painter::DrawText` (canvas `draw_text`) defaults to `wrap=true`: long text in a fixed-height row or narrow box gets wrapped by the platform layout engine and overflows the row bounds. Three symptoms verified on the real machine: process-table command lines (often 100+ chars) wrapped and bled into the next row; large chart y-axis values ("21414.7") stacked vertically as multi-line garbage; end-value labels of multiple line series overlapped when their last values were close. Fix: the shim gained `yue_mbt_painter_draw_text_ex` exposing TextAttributes' wrap/ellipsis (pure ABI translation), MoonBit's `draw_text` gained optional parameters, and table cells now draw with `wrap=false + ellipsis=true` (truncation is done by the platform layout, so no per-cell measurement); `fmt_axis` switches to k/M/G units from |v| >= 1e4 (one decimal, trailing zeros trimmed) so labels stay within 5 characters and never trigger wrapping; end labels are now collected, spread apart by y (14px minimum gap, clamped back into the canvas), then drawn.
- Chart / virtual-table responsiveness (`fill` switch): no fixed width — the container stretches along the cross axis of the column parent and follows window resizes. In the table's fill mode, column geometry is re-laid-out on every paint from the actual width: fixed columns keep their (possibly drag-resized) widths while elastic columns share the remainder (dragging swaps width between the two adjacent columns conservatively, so it never conflicts with the elastic re-layout). Self-drawn row containers that anchor to `w - offset` already followed their parent's width.
- Table header decorations (sort arrows, column-boundary lines, hover highlights) must be painted in the header container's `on_draw`, not in a per-cell container: on GTK, libyue Painter path fills (`begin_path` + `fill`) do not render at all inside a single header cell (about one column wide × 32px, with a Label child widget) — the code runs, nothing errors, but no pixels appear; the identical code renders fine on the whole-row header container or the table's big canvas container. `fill_rect` and path `stroke` render at every container size, so the symptom is easily misread as "wrong coordinates". Reproduce by painting `fill_rect` / path fill / path stroke side by side in one window. Fix: both table_t and table_v_t keep all header decoration in a single `on_draw` on the header container, leaving cells with interaction only. Root cause pending fork-level investigation (suspected to relate to the GTK draw region / clipping of small containers).
- Mouse events at header column boundaries: the pixels just right of a boundary line belong to the next cell, so pressing exactly on a visible boundary lands on the next cell's left edge (triggering a sort instead of a drag). Fix: the drag hot zone recognizes both edges (right 4px → boundary (j, j+1); left 4px → boundary (j-1, j)), with no handle on the last column's right edge.

### Layout Geometry (Yoga flexbox)

- Built-in layout assertions pass 16/16 (±1px); composition rules: content area = container − 2×padding; gap does not stack with margin; percentages resolve against the parent content width.
- Composite widgets (Tab / Scroll / Group) are measure-less leaf nodes in the yoga tree: their outer size must be given explicitly (e.g. `flex:1`) or they collapse (Tab freezes its minimum size at construction, driving the page area to zero).
- tabs_t once hard-coded the outer width to 360px and gave content pages no flex: any Scroll / Table placed inside collapsed to zero (measured: the sysmonitor overview page rendered completely blank except the tab strip). Fix: outer drops the fixed width in favor of `flex:1` (stretch gets the width in a column parent; fills when the parent has a definite height) and content pages get `flex:1` too. When the parent has no definite height flex does not grow, so embedded usage (the showcase section) is unchanged.
- After changing styles at runtime (`set_style`), calling `update_layout` on the root container does not refresh the subtree on GTK at all (yoga state has changed, bounds stay put), and calling it on a single node only re-lays-out that node and its siblings under the same parent — other subtrees (e.g. table body rows) are unaffected. A probe with four controlled variants (immediately after mount / 500ms later, update root / leaf, width / flexbasis) pinned the only reliable form: call it on every container whose style changed. table_t column dragging used to move the handle (bounds follow col_w) while column widths never changed (the style took no effect) because it updated the root; the splitter happened to have a single wrapper container to restyle, so the same pit existed but never surfaced. Fix: apply_col_widths calls update_layout on both header cells and both cells of every row individually.
- GUI automation: prefer keyboard (Tab focus + Space activate); `xdotool key --window` sends XSendEvent events that GTK drops — use XTEST (no --window); coordinate clicks are unreliable due to WM decoration offsets.

- **The Linux/macOS tabs_t page-switch collapse is cured (native ≥ v0.15.6-mbt.19)**: the lesion sits in yoga itself interacting with display:none, a three-layer mechanism — ① the `flex` shorthand (flex:1) resolves in this yoga vintage to grow=1 with a derived basis of 0 (`resolveFlexBasisPtr`); ② hiding a page goes through display:none, where `YGZeroOutLayoutRecursively` wipes the layout wholesale with `= {}`, knocking `computedFlexBasis` back to undefined; ③ after re-showing, the flex basis computation (Yoga.cpp `YGNodeComputeFlexBasisForChild`) rewrites the derived basis-0 as long as the resolved value is defined and the cache is undefined, and every later round then takes the "already computed" branch — a content measure never runs again, so under an auto-height owner chain (a scroll's content column) with no free space to distribute, the page container stays at zero height for good (probe measured the re-shown page at 1px, outer 65→36 collapsed to the head height). Pages that were never hidden stay healthy only by the coincidence that a content measure once entered the basis cache — not a stable guarantee. The fix (fork patch lb091188/yue 93078300: set a `zeroedByDisplayNone` flag in the zero-out pass, and for a flagged node the basis computation takes the content-measure branch for that one round; explicit flexBasis styles keep their behavior untouched) heals only the shorthand path; **the collapse of an explicit flexbasis 0 is inherent semantics** (every round rewrites 0 from the resolved value, the content-measure branch never runs), which is why tabs_t is platform-split — Windows keeps the explicit basis 0 (treating its visibility mid-state compression), Linux/macOS return to the plain flex shorthand. Regression tool: `moon run examples/probe-collapse` (four timed switch samples; the shorthand pages must hold a steady height). Pitfalls: the CI clang command line carries `-Wshadow -Werror` (the local `-Wall -Wextra` combo does not include shadow — patch code fully green locally, all red on CI; check shadow first), and the fork patch lives in `patches/` (the prebuilt workflow applies it with `git -C third_party/yoga apply` after Bootstrap) because yoga is a submodule and stays out of fork commits.
- **The boundary of the mbt.19 patch: showcase re-collapse and the shorthand ban on outer (closed out by next-day on-metal re-verification)**: after mbt.19 shipped, on-metal re-verification of showcase still collapsed — the patch only heals the node that was itself zeroed by display:none and re-shown; it cannot heal a flex-shorthand ancestor that was never hidden but gets dirty-invalidated by a sibling's visibility flip: tabs_t's own outer also carries the flex:1 shorthand, and once a page switch dirties it, the content-measure round under its auto-height owner (the scroll content column) resolves the derived basis-0 against the available size into 0 (not undefined), which the owner then takes as a zero-height content contribution — outer collapses to its margin height and the whole subtree goes to zero (gauge-measured 1px); everything looks fine initially only because the first natural-measure round sees an owner size of nan, where basis-0 resolves back to undefined and the content measure runs. A minimal pure-yoga repro (one availableHeight=undefined round, then a fixed-300 round plus switches) reproduces both shapes faithfully, and printf tracing in Yoga.cpp captured the three-step collapse chain: `outer mainOwnerSize 300→10→0` (10 being the margin height). The fix stays out of native: tabs_t's outer switches to **flexgrow + flexshrink written separately** — grow/shrink do not participate in the content measure and the basis takes auto semantics (CSS flex:1 1 auto): content height under an auto owner, fill/compress under a definite owner, the sole difference from the shorthand being basis auto. **Rule: any container that must live both ways — fill under a definite-height owner, content-size under an auto owner — must not use the flex shorthand; write grow/shrink separately. The shorthand is only fit where the owner height is always definite, or where the node itself is the visibility-switch target (protected by the mbt.19 patch).** Regression tool extended with `moon run examples/probe-collapse2` (full showcase-structure repro: sidebar + sibling pages toggling + scroll chain + dual-shape tabs; the gauge group must hold a steady 18). One probe-side note: never use button_t as a decorative sidebar — during window teardown the GTK focus-out callback can hit destructed state and segfault (gdb stack `OnFocusOut → Store<Responder>::get`), unrelated to the collapse; the probe uses labels instead, and the button_t focus-chain lifetime is tracked separately.


### MoonBit ↔ C ABI

- Trampolines must match the C function pointer prototype bit-for-bit, including arity: C calls `callback(closure, args...)` and the trampoline's first parameter receives the closure. Misalignment hides itself — row counts work, some callbacks fire; a callback is verified only once it has actually fired.
- `extern "c"` returning nullable types: older toolchains segfault outright (report success/failure via a `Ref[Int]` out-parameter); on moon 0.1.20260904 + moonc v0.10.12, `-> Bytes?` works — a C-side NULL maps to None correctly, verified in both debug and release against real missing-file and directory (EISDIR) paths (sysmonitor's read_text_file). Other nullable types (handles etc.) are untested; the out-parameter pattern remains the fallback.
- FFI pointer parameters need `#borrow` (compiler-enforced); widget parameters take the handle type `View`, never the MoonBit wrapper struct (otherwise handles are invalid at runtime and silently dropped).
- Closures across the ABI: capture-free top-level function literals compile to real C function pointers; capturing closures use the "function pointer + closure pointer" two-parameter form.
- Callback closures are kept alive process-wide by a registry and not reclaimed per window (negligible leak for single-window tools — settled).
- Toolbar / Vibrant have no Linux static-library symbols (link would fail) and are not exposed; the Browser empty-cookie-list crash is fixed since fork mbt.7.
- Changing a shim signature requires the same batch in `.cpp` / `yue_mbt.h` / ffi.mbt; both a missed signature sync and an entirely missed declaration manifest as a mangle split (`_Z`-prefixed symbol vs plain-C reference) — locate with nm, and run a ".cpp definitions × header declarations" cross-scan before packaging.
- **A standalone translation unit (e.g. `yue_accent_mac.mm`) that does not include `yue_mbt.h` must mark its function definitions `extern "C"` explicitly**: without it they export C++-mangled (`__Z25yue_mbt_system_accent_macv`) while `yue_mbt.cpp` references the plain-C name from the header — link-time undefined. Only macOS exposes this (Linux/Windows never compile the .mm), and neither does the vendor/CI prepare stage (a static archive never checks undefined) — only a real macOS link (moon test/build) hits it; with no local mac, CI is the only defense line. Actually fixed ahead of the 0.5.0 release (symbol re-verified via strings on the re-vendored archive as `_yue_mbt_system_accent_mac`).
- The XFCE panel prioritizes IconPixmap over IconName: set_icon_name and set_pixmap are mutually exclusive — setting one must clear the other.
- **Custom-protocol reject encoding and C-side payload pre-validation (CORE1, high severity)**: the None reject path of `Browser::register_protocol` used to encode `[1,0,0,0]` — the C side read the first 4 little-endian bytes as `ok=1` (success) and kept reading mime_len/content_len past the end of the 4-byte buffer (native heap over-read), then built `std::string(p+8, mime_len)` from a garbage length: undefined behavior. The reject never actually took effect either (libyue's WebKitGTK side rejects a request only when the handler returns nullptr, via `finish_error` — see OnProtocolRequest in the yue fork's `nativeui/gtk/browser_gtk.cc`). Fix: the MoonBit reject payload is now `[0,0,0,0]` (ok=0, so C returns nullptr without reading further bytes), with the encoding extracted into the in-package pure function `encode_protocol_payload` for white-box tests; the C side pre-validates against the MoonBit Bytes length before decoding segment by segment — ok==0 or a payload shorter than 12 bytes is rejected, as are negative or out-of-payload mime_len/content_len (length comparisons go through int64 to avoid `8+mime_len+4` overflow), and a nullptr payload is also defensively rejected. The Bytes length is the 4 bytes preceding the data pointer (the moonbit_object header's meta, which is the length for VAL_ARRAY; layout follows moonbit.h's `Moonbit_array_length` — moonbit.h itself is not included because its extern "C" memcpy declaration conflicts with glibc), and that assumption was empirically verified with a minimal C probe linking libmoonbitrun.o + the libruntime archive directly (make_bytes(23/4/0) all read back the correct length). Lesson: **for a self-encoded ABI payload with length fields, the C side must be able to obtain the total length itself and pre-validate first** — never trust fields after the peer "claims success"; cross-ABI contract tests must mirror the C decode logic (length pre-check, then segment reads) rather than only asserting the encoder's shape. Verification: `moon check` with zero warnings + `moon test` 636 all green (new yue/browser/browser_wbtest.mbt with 8 cases: None payload byte-exact as `[0,0,0,0]`; Some byte-exact across ok/mime_len/mime/content_len/content; UTF-8 multi-byte lengths counted in bytes; the 12-byte minimal payload for empty mime/content; the legacy `[1,0,0,0]` shape and an 8-byte ok=1 short payload both rejected; out-of-payload mime_len/content_len rejected; round-trip with long content containing quotes and newlines); the shim change went through prebuild's incremental rebuild, and after deleting the showcase exe under `_build`, `moon build examples/showcase` relinks with zero warnings (nm confirms the exe references yue_mbt_browser_register_protocol); showcase gains a demo-deny reject-path button and code snippet — the real-machine load-failure behavior is for the user to confirm under Linux WebKitGTK (Windows WebView2 silently ignores custom protocols anyway).

### Audio decoding capability boundary (miniaudio)

- AudioEngine's decodable formats are exactly miniaudio's built-in decoder set — **WAV / MP3 / FLAC only** (probe-verified 2026-10: generated mp3/flac/m4a/ogg samples and loaded each via a throwaway wbtest; mp3/flac/wav load fine, m4a and ogg both fail with `LoadFailed: Invalid file`). Root cause: miniaudio's Vorbis decoding needs an external stb_vorbis (this binding doesn't integrate one), and m4a/aac/opus/wma/aiff have no built-in decoder at all. The trap takes the form of "docs and filters claiming a superset": a file-dialog filter listing m4a/aac/ogg plus copy saying "WAV/MP3/FLAC/OGG etc." invites users straight into Invalid file (the ffmpeg-generated demo wav itself loads fine). Fix: filter only `wav,mp3,flac` (plus an "All files" entry), state the set boundary in the page copy and the yue/audio.mbt header comment, and append a "convert with ffmpeg first" hint to the load-failure message; no silent auto-transcoding (a demo board should show AudioEngine's real capability, and synchronous transcoding of large files would block the UI).

### VideoPlayer replay state machine (VpEnded / stop audio seek)

- Symptom: after a video plays to the end (EOF, non-looping), clicking play again does nothing — position pinned at duration, picture pinned on the last frame, audio queue already drained; after stop → play the picture does restart but sound is silent the whole way.
- Root cause 1 (play after ended does nothing): `vp_tick`'s EOF non-looping branch set state back to `VpIdle`, indistinguishable from the initial idle — `play()` on VpIdle always "resumes from the current position", and in the ended state pos=duration, so the target time is always past the end while the demuxer sits at EOF: the first tick hits EOF again and stops, so the click is a no-op.
- Root cause 2 (silent after stop → play): `stop()` only seeked the video demuxer; **the audio demuxer is a separate container instance opened on the same file** (independent read cursors), so it stayed at EOF — on replay `vp_fill_audio`'s `decode_pcm` returned None immediately, the queue stayed empty, silent throughout. Any "back to start" operation must seek both demuxers; missing one gives you a silent picture.
- Fix: ① an explicit `VpEnded` state (mirroring AudioPlayer's `ApEnded`), entered on non-looping EOF; `play()` on VpEnded first runs `vp_restart` (both demuxers seek(0) + pos/base_pts/frame_no reset + audio queue refill) before starting; ② `stop()` also seeks the audio demuxer to 0; ③ `seek()` migrates VpEnded → VpPaused, so scrubbing after the end and pressing play resumes from the scrub point instead of being mistaken for a replay (replay semantics belong only to "press play while parked at the end").
- Design trade-off: **an explicit state rather than a `pos>=duration` heuristic** — mid-playback, when the target time is past the duration but EOF has not been reached yet, pos is clamped to duration (heuristic false positive: "pause at the end, then play" would wrongly replay from 0); and with no container duration header (duration=0) the heuristic fails entirely. The explicit state is correct in both cases.
- Verification: white-box control — stashing video_player.mbt back to the pre-fix revision and running `moon test -p gui-media`, the two new cases fail precisely (play-after-ended's is_playing assertion, stop→play's audio decode_pcm assertion); with the fix restored, 11/11 pass. Whole repo: `moon check --deny-warn` zero warnings + `moon test` 641 all green (5 new cases vs the previous 636: loop EOF→vp_restart, ended→play, ended→seek→play, stop→play, volume clamping). Real-machine interaction feel (play-after-ended, replay after stop) is for the user to re-verify.

### Package split: single yue package -> core + six sub-packages (0.5.11)

- Environment: moon 0.1.20260920 / moonc v0.10.14, module NoahLiu/native-gui; prebuilt native libs in place.
- Symptom: before the split the single yue package held ~60k lines — FFI, native widgets, themed components, 20+ charts, markdown, the declarative layer and 30 system-capability files in one package. MoonBit compiles per package with no tree shaking inside a package, so webview-based or core-only consumers compiled the whole toolkit into their binary.
- Root cause (mechanism): MoonBit cross-package references must be qualified as `@alias.name` — evidenced twice: the official moonbitlang/async src/fs sub-package references root symbols as `@async.xxx`, and this repo's yue/media.mbt references traybus as `@traybus.xxx`. The split therefore means: one moon.pkg per sub-package, every in-package core reference qualified with `@yue.` (word-boundary codemod that must skip `.field` accesses, `::method` suffixes and string literals), and dependency declarations sinking to package level (mizchi/markdown -> yue/markdown; subproc and moonsqlitefile -> yue/system). No prebuild change was needed: link_configs concatenate flags across the dependency closure, so a sub-package importing only core automatically gets `-lyue_mbt`; webkit stays confined to the yue/browser entry.
- Fix (batch order is cycle avoidance): (1) markdown first — it calls components' code_view, so moving it later would cycle core->components->core; (2) components — pre-step moves the `hand()` cursor helper from components.mbt into core as pub (charts_interactive references it cross-package and charts moves later), and ~17 private theme getters plus bind_fg/bind_entry_theme/entry_ctrl_height referenced by 20+ files get pub-ified on demand; (3) icons; (4) charts (charts_wbtest.mbt carries its own extern "C", so its moon.pkg needs a native-only targets entry); (5) declarative — two pre-steps: the Node protocol (struct Node + attach) is extracted from declarative.mbt into core yue/node.mbt (otherwise four packages' unqualified Node references all break), and signals.mbt's trailing `bind()` (which calls bind_label) moves into the declarative package with it; (6) system — open_url (system.mbt) and tray.mbt stay in core (markdown's link clicks depend on open_url; procrun goes with the system layer); (7) version close-out 0.5.11 across moon.mod, yue/version.mbt and the gui-media pin (prebuild validates them at build time).
- Verification and the lesson: each batch ran moon check zero-warning + full moon test + moon build of examples/showcase and sysmonitor, plus a read-only acceptance review of the git diff. **Lesson: the gate missed systemprobe** — after the batches were committed, the pushed tree failed to build systemprobe with 15 link errors (its import adaptation stayed uncommitted in the working tree); hello/hello-themed were unaffected so the two-example gate didn't notice, and only a stash test on the committed state found it. Gate package-split work against every example, and verify each commit's scope with git status (another work stream's uncommitted files are easily swept in by whole-file commits).

### MoonBit native GUI stack G1 (paint contract) · toolchain syntax forms and buffer choice

Environment: Ubuntu 24.04 + X11 + XFCE, moon 0.1.20260920 / moonc v0.10.14. Adds `render` (pure MoonBit, zero FFI).

- `pub method name(self : T, ...)` fails to parse: `method` is a reserved keyword. Use `pub fn T::name(self : T, ...)`.
- `match expr with { }` is rejected (`unexpected token with`); write `match expr { }`. Record update `{ s with tx: 1.0 }` still uses `with` - the two constructs differ.
- `Bytes` is read-only (no `op_set`) and this version has no `ByteArray`. Mutable byte buffers use `FixedArray[Byte]` (already used by `yue/png_rgba.mbt`), which needs no `mut` binding for in-place writes. The pixel bitmap field is therefore `FixedArray[Byte]`; G2/G3 take its data address when handing off to Cairo/X11.
- `self.field[i] = v` silently promotes the whole method to requiring `T &mut`, forcing `mut` bindings at every call site - unacceptable for the ~10k lines of existing component draw code. Fix: copy the handle first (`let d = self.data; d[i] = v`), and keep mutable fields on the **inner** `PainterState` (`mut` fields) while the outer `state` field is never rebound, mirroring `yue/signals.mbt:247`.
- Structs have reference semantics, so pushing `self.state` onto the undo stack means `restore()` returns the same object and nothing reverts. Fix: copy field by field on save and on restore (covered by a test asserting pixels before/after `translate` + `restore`).
- Constructing with the type name (`Color { r: ... }`) does not parse here; use bare `{ r: .., g: .. }` with the type inferred from context.
- A test-only dependency across `moon.work` members cannot be declared only in `moon.pkg.json`'s `wbtest.import` (reports `Package ... not found`), and the `alias` entry field is silently ignored (default alias is the last path segment, hence `@src` in bench). Declaring it in the root `moon.mod` would change the published dependency graph, so the layout-to-pixel assertions move to the batch that actually wires yoga-mbt (G2/G7).
- Convention fork: 8-digit hex is parsed as CSS `#RRGGBBAA` (alpha last), while libyue's `Color` uses `#AARRGGBB` (see the `parse_hex` comment in `yue/color.mbt`; mixing them shows up as colour drift, not opacity). The new stack does not follow the latter.
- **Toolchain side effect (do not repeat)**: repo-wide `moon fmt` on this version rewrites ~80 unrelated files (the repo is not fmt-clean) and **migrates** `moon.pkg.json` into `moon.pkg`, where `warn_list` is unsupported (see `modules/yoga-mbt/HANDOFF.md` section 6.11) - the `-6` suppression in `yoga-mbt/src` gets moved out silently. Fix: run `moon fmt` only on this batch's files, then check `git status` and revert anything else with `git checkout -- <explicit paths>`.

- Verification: `moon check` with no warnings + repo-wide `moon test` 748/748 (8 new pixel-level assertions in `render`).

### MoonBit native GUI stack G2 (GTK3 window layer) · five pitfalls in the pump and present path

Environment: Ubuntu 24.04 + X11 + XFCE, moon 0.1.20260920 / moonc v0.10.14, GTK3 3.24.x. Adds `win` and `examples/native-window`.

- **Window never paints (frames=0)**: after `gtk_widget_show_all` the GDK map request still sits in the output buffer, so the server never sees the map and poll waits for an event that never comes. Fix: `gdk_display_flush(...)` plus one round of `gtk_main_iteration_do(FALSE)` after show (`gdk_flush` is deprecated).
- **Busy loop (dt=0, 100% CPU)**: calling `g_main_context_wakeup` right before `poll` makes GLib's internal wakeup pipe readable, so poll returns immediately forever. Fix: never call wakeup inside the wait; cross-thread wakeup only enters via the external `wm_wakeup()` (which is exactly what should make the current poll return early).
- **Hardened GLib signatures**: this machine's `g_main_context_query/check` take an extra `n_fds` argument (query's 5th is the buffer capacity; check's 4th is n_fds, not n_ready). Read `/usr/include/glib-2.0/glib/gmain.h` instead of copying examples.
- **Time base for relative delays**: `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` starts at boot (21405569 ms measured here). A Loop whose `last_now` starts at 0 fires every timer queued before the first `advance` immediately - the animation ran exactly one frame and the 5 s quit timer stopped the loop in the first tick. Fix: explicit `Loop::start(now_ms)`; unstarted loops use base 0 (covered by a test).
- **Known pitfall reproduced: moon does not relink on native static library changes**: after editing `win/gtk_stub.c`, `moon build` reports "no work to do" and runs the old exe. Fix: delete `build/libyue_win_stub.a` and the example's `_build` output.
- **Convention note**: the present path copies the caller's RGBA buffer into a backend scratch before swizzling to BGRx; it never modifies the caller's buffer in place, because offscreen snapshots share that buffer.
- Verification: `moon check gui/{core,win,render}` warning-free; `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` 9/9; `examples/native-window` smoke: 52 frames, exit code 0, no shutdown crash; yoga-mbt 79/79 unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack G3a (Cairo rasterizer) · bitmap format and parity between the two backends

- **Format decision (key)**: the `render` Bitmap now uses the exact `CAIRO_FORMAT_ARGB32` layout (little-endian B,G,R,A with **premultiplied** alpha). Cairo can then draw straight into our own buffer and the window present becomes a direct copy - one format end to end, no conversions. Consequences: the hand-written `Bitmap::paint` does source-over in premultiplied space (same arithmetic as Cairo) and `Bitmap::pixel` unpremultiplies, so the public semantics (independent RGBA components) and all G1 assertions stay unchanged.
- **Simpler present**: `wm_present` no longer swizzles RGBA to BGRx or copies into a scratch; it builds an ARGB32 surface over the caller's buffer (held by `#borrow` for the duration).
- **`stroke_rect` has one meaning on both sides**: Cairo's `stroke` is centered, which contradicts the border-box convention, so the C side reproduces inset semantics with four inner band fills.
- **Invariant under test**: the same rect and colour (including alpha) rendered through the pure-MoonBit rect painter and through Cairo are **pixel-for-pixel identical** (including `#00000080` over white = 127,127,127,255); a diagonal fill produces intermediate alpha, proving the anti-aliased path is actually used.
- Verification: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/render` 14/14 (6 new Cairo assertions); `moon check gui/{core,win,render}` warning-free; `examples/native-window` smoke 52 frames, exit code 0 (the demo now draws a Cairo circle and bezier arc end to end).

### MoonBit native GUI stack G3b (Pango text layer) · FFI out parameters and cross-package field visibility

- **SIGSEGV with no test output**: using `Array[Int]` as a C out-array (C writes `out[0..2]` through an `int32_t*`) crashes - MoonBit hands C the array *object*, so writing `out[0]` corrupts its length header and everything touching that array afterwards dies. The repo's established form is one `Ref[Int]` per out value (`ok : Ref[Int]`, `yue/ffi.mbt:452`); switching to three `Ref[Int]` params made it 7/7. **Rule**: never borrow an `Array[Int]` pointer to return several values.
- Constructing or reading struct fields across packages requires `pub(all) struct` (see `RectF` in `yue/geometry.mbt:20`); plain `pub struct` gives `Cannot create values of the read-only type`. Same story as `pub(all) enum`.
- Parameters with defaults must be labelled (`family? : String = ""`); `family : String = ""` errors with "Only labelled arguments can have default value".
- **Conventions**: font size is in **logical pixels** via `pango_font_description_set_absolute_size(desc, px * PANGO_SCALE)` (not points); `baseline` is the first baseline measured from the top of the layout box, matching yoga-mbt's `set_baseline` definition so wiring needs no conversion; ellipsize/wrap follow the teacher's `attributed_text_gtk.cc:82/128-132`.
- **Test philosophy**: no exact pixel widths/heights (they depend on the machine's font configuration - the G0 probe's "14px line height = 17px" is this machine's number); assert monotonicity, constraint behaviour under `max_width`/wrap, and painted-pixel counts instead, so another machine cannot produce a false red.
- Verification: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/text` 7/7; `moon check gui/{core,win,render,text}` warning-free; `examples/native-window` smoke 52 frames, exit code 0 (mixed CJK/Latin text drawn in the window); repo-wide red unchanged at 290 errors.

### MoonBit native GUI stack G3b addendum · line-height definition and the test binary that never relinks

- **Definition**: `line_height` is the **font's ascent + descent** from `pango_context_get_metrics`, and `line_ascent` is the ascent (= first baseline). Never derive line height from a sample string's box height - that turns "does the text have a descender" into a layout height difference.
- **Reconciliation with the G0 probe**: the probe's "14px measured 17" was actually the **first baseline**, which this layer reproduces as `line_ascent(14) = 17`; `line_height(14) = 22` here. Both numbers are right, the label was wrong; G5 should size text boxes from the 22 path.
- **Pitfall**: `PangoFontMetrics` is not a GObject - use `pango_font_metrics_unref`. Calling `g_object_unref` on it SIGSEGVs inside the test driver with no test output at all, which is easy to misread as an FFI argument problem.
- **Pitfall (new face of a known one)**: after changing a native static library, `moon test` executables are **not relinked either** - previously this was only recorded for `moon build` output, reproduced here under `_build/native/debug/test/<pkg>`: the fixed SIGSEGV kept happening because the test binary was still linked against the old archive. Reproduce with a plain main target first (a fresh target is always newly linked): "main fine, test crashes" points at stale artifacts. Delete `_build/native/debug/test/<pkg>` along with the library.
- Verification: after removing the artifacts, `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/text` 7/7; `moon check gui/{core,win,render,text}` warning-free; render 14/14, core 9/9, yoga-mbt 79/79 unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack G4a (editor kernel and key codes) · kernel semantics and toolchain syntax

- **Kernel semantics**: the text state lives only in `input` (the IME is one of its clients, not the owner).
