All indices are **UTF-8 byte offsets**, caret/anchor only land on code-point boundaries (back off over continuation bytes 0xC0..0xBF), matching the G0 probe; switching to code-point indices would break Pango width and IME preedit offsets at once. `get_text_in_range` aligns **start backwards, end forwards** - better to include one extra code point than return half a character. The kernel never touches the system clipboard (`cut`/`copy` return text, `paste_text` accepts it) so the whole thing is testable headless. `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` report "not consumed" for the upper focus stack; printable keys go through `keysym_to_text` (Latin-1 and UCS keysyms), other functional keys are left to G5's IME channel.
- **Toolchain syntax (all measured)**: (1) implicit-method form `fn helper(self : Editor, ..)` is not recognised (unbound) - private helpers also need `fn Editor::helper(self : Editor, ..)`; (2) top-level `pub name : Int = v` fails to parse, use `pub const NAME : Int = v` (uppercase); (3) `move` is a reserved keyword, renamed to `move_caret`; (4) `Int.to_char()` returns **`Char?`**, interpolating it warns "Option does not have a meaningful string representation" - match out the Char first; (5) variants of `pub enum X derive(Eq, Show) { A B }` cannot be constructed elsewhere; payload-less enums need `pub(all) enum`, and this batch went further and replaced the two-case enum with a plain `Bool`; (6) `} else match ... {` is illegal - a match used as a branch body must be wrapped in `{ }`.
- **Pitfall (bounds)**: `floor_boundary` accepts k == length (end is a legal boundary), so the loop must short-circuit on `k < n` before indexing, otherwise `Array::at` panics (SIGABRT observed).
- Verification: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/input` 10/10; `moon check gui/{core,input}` warning-free; render 14/14, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 79/79 unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack G4b (key/pointer events and focus wiring) · four GTK-side rules

- **Pitfall**: `gdk_keyval_to_lower(ev->keyval)` erases the shift case - X11 keyvals **already encode shift** ('a' and 'A' are different keysyms), so the kernel's `keysym_to_text` yields the right character when the raw keyval is kept.
- **Pitfall**: modifier bits must be remapped explicitly. GDK uses Shift=1/Control=4/Mod1=8/Mod2=64/Meta=16, this stack uses `MOD_SHIFT=1 / MOD_CTRL=2 / MOD_ALT=4 / MOD_META=8`; passing raw state makes every `Ctrl+Left` / `Shift+Tab` combination misfire.
- **Keys only arrive if all three are done**: `gtk_widget_set_can_focus(area, TRUE)`, `gtk_widget_add_events(... GDK_KEY_PRESS_MASK | ... | GDK_SCROLL_MASK)`, and `gtk_widget_grab_focus` after `show_all`. Missing any one gives a live window that reports **nothing** - the same class of failure as G2's "no flush, waiting on an event that never arrives": it shows as silence, not as an error.
- **Queue rule**: per-window 64-slot FIFO; when full it **drops the new event rather than overwriting**, so ordering is never corrupted. Events are read as "take returns the code, then `wm_ev_*` reads the last taken one" instead of many out parameters - MoonBit cannot hand C a writable array (see the G3b SIGSEGV entry).
- **Focus lives in MoonBit**: `core/focus.mbt` owns order and wrap-around (Tab from last to first, Shift+Tab from first to last, unregistering the focused item hands focus to its successor); the platform exposes a single action `Window::request_focus`. Logical focus in the toolkit mirrors libyue's `FocusManager`, where containers hold no system focus.
- **Caret placement**: hit-testing measures prefix widths code point by code point and picks the right-most boundary at or before the click; `Editor::boundary_after` was promoted from private to public so the upper layers advance the same way.
- Verification: all five packages warning-free; core 14/14, input 10/10, render 14/14, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 79/79; `examples/native-window` smoke on this machine: 49 frames, exit code 0, both boxes' text intact. **Pending real-machine checks** (user): typing letters/digits, backspace and arrows, Tab/Shift+Tab wrapping between the two boxes, click-to-place caret, 500 ms caret blink, CJK punctuation and compose keys (IME is G5).

### MoonBit native GUI stack G0 · real-machine evidence: `im_focus` is what makes the pure channel work; the preedit gap is in how the probe hooks it

Environment: Ubuntu 24.04 + X11 + XFCE, `GTK_IM_MODULE=fcitx` (fcitx5). Run by the collaborator on real hardware (AGENTS rule 6).

- **Symptom**: in mode B every keystroke landed as an immediate commit and preedit stayed 0 - the IME looked like it never took over. fcitx5 was fine; `GtkIMMulticontext` had silently fallen back to the built-in `GtkIMContextSimple` (Latin-1 only, no compose/candidates) because the context was never activated.
- **Root cause**: the probe only called `gtk_im_context_set_client_window` and never `gtk_im_context_focus_in`, so the context stayed inactive and `filter_keypress` consumed nothing.
- **Fix**: `gtk_im_context_focus_in` at realize, plus focus-in/out sync on the toplevel (losing focus must call `focus_out`, otherwise the IME keeps an unfinished compose string - checklist item 5). After this the **Chinese commit path works on real hardware**.
- **Remaining gap**: inline preedit (drawing the compose string ourselves) still does not show, localized to the probe's own wiring rather than the IME. Mode C's visible `GtkEntry` preview behaves like Electron, which is a useful baseline for the fallback shape. G5 should be designed from these two facts (commit reachable; preedit must be self-drawn with focus sync) and stop re-verifying that commit is reachable at all.
- Verification: `moon build experiment/ime_probe` and both modes exit 0 on this machine (assistant side); CJK typing itself was executed and reported by the collaborator per the README checklist.

- **Closed the same day**: inline preedit now works; the pure channel needs **four conditions** - (1) `focus_in`/`focus_out` synced with window focus, (2) key handling installed on the **focused widget**, not the window (that is exactly where our behaviour differed from `GtkEntry`), (3) `gtk_im_context_set_surrounding` fed every frame (committed text only, cursor in UTF-8 bytes), (4) `use_preedit=TRUE` - its meaning is "use the preedit string for inline feedback", not "the IM draws it itself" (reading it backwards cost two false negatives).
- **Another real-machine pitfall, unrelated to the IM**: the composition string may be assembled in **exactly one place**. The probe used to splice the preedit into the display string on the MoonBit side and splice it again while drawing in C, so the same text appeared twice; the fix moves both splicing and the underline into the draw side (plain text unlined, composition underlined, matching `GtkEntry`), with MoonBit pushing committed text only.
- **One misdiagnosis to remember**: blaming a frontend version mismatch was refuted by the mode-C control (a real `GtkEntry` previews fine in the same environment). Run a reference implementation before attributing to dependencies.
- **ibus not installed**: those two checklist rounds are marked "no ibus daemon in this environment, untested".
- **Verification**: `moon build experiment/ime_probe` plus smoke runs in both modes (exit code 0, assistant side); real machine (fcitx5) confirmed by the collaborator: one inline underlined composition, `[preedit] changed` in the stream, `[commit] 中文` intact, candidate window hugging the end of the composition.

### MoonBit native GUI stack G4 · why nothing could be typed: wrong handler layer, and NumLock read as Meta

Symptom: after G4 landed, the self-drawn box accepted no characters at all, while Tab seemed to "sort of work" (focus really was moving), animation and repaint were normal, and nothing logged an error. Objective verification by injecting `xdotool key` into the example window and printing `code/keysym/mods/focus/byte-length` per event pinned two causes:

- **(1) key handlers were on the toplevel window instead of the focus widget.** The G0 probe (`ec2849d`) had already settled this on real hardware: key handling belongs on the focused widget, because window-level wiring gets the ordering and the "consume the event" semantics wrong (fatal once an IME is involved). Now connected on `w->area`, same shape as GtkEntry.
- **(2) `GDK_MOD2_MASK` was being treated as Meta - fatal and easy to misread.** `wm_mods()` set MOD_META when `state & (GDK_META_MASK | GDK_MOD2_MASK)` matched, but on X11 MOD2 *is* NumLock, which is normally on, so **every** key event arrived with `mods=8`; `handle_key` sees meta/alt set, treats it as a chord and inserts nothing, silently dropping every printable key. Fix: Meta only from `GDK_META_MASK | GDK_SUPER_MASK`; lock bits (NumLock/CapsLock) never map into this stack's modifier set.
- **Technique worth keeping**: "did it reach C" - `fprintf(stderr, "[c] key ...")` in the C callback; "where did it die after MoonBit" - print `code/keysym/mods/focus/editor length` at the dispatch point. The single `mods=8` field settled cause (2); reasoning from behaviour alone kept pointing at "events never arrived".
- **Disproved hypothesis, recorded so nobody re-chases it**: after fixing the mapping the example briefly did not auto-exit, and I suspected `Loop::advance` starving later timers by breaking on a non-due head. Written as a headless case it **passes** (core 15/15). The stall was cause (3) - painting the GdkWindow outside the draw callback (see the G2/G4 lifetime entry) - plus too short a wait in my own test loop, not the scheduler.
- **Objective verification**: injecting `a b c Tab d e f` yields `NATIVE-WINDOW-OK ... a='helloabc' b='第二个框def'` - three keys into the first box, Tab moves focus, three into the second, with `mods=0`. All five packages warning-free; core 15/15, input 10/10, render 14/14, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 79/79; repo-wide red still 290 errors. **Still needs eyes**: click-to-place caret and the 500 ms blink appearance (xdotool can inject a click but cannot see the drawn caret).

### MoonBit native GUI stack G5a (Linux pure-channel IME) · the four probe conditions landing in win

The recipe came from the G0 probe (`ec2849d`, four conditions) and now lives in the new stack's own window layer: `win` creates a `GtkIMMulticontext` (`use_preedit=TRUE`), runs `filter_keypress` first on every key (when consumed the key is eaten and never forwarded to the kernel), reads commit text and the composition string back to MoonBit one field at a time, and feeds surrounding text plus the caret rectangle every frame; `input` gains `set_preedit`/`preedit_text` (the composition is display-only, **never merged into the text state** - there is still exactly one copy of the text); the example draws the composition after the committed text with an underline and adds the composition width to the candidate-window rectangle.

- **Pitfall 1 (pinyin goes in verbatim, the IM looks dead)**: calling `set_client_window` before `show` - the drawing area is not realized yet, `gtk_widget_get_window` returns NULL, the multicontext is attached to no IM module and `filter_keypress` never consumes. It **must run in the `realize` signal** (same as the probe), paired with `focus-in/out-event` for `focus_in`/`focus_out`.
- **Pitfall 2 (core dump, `free(): double free detected in tcache 2`)**: the UTF-8 string delivered by the `commit` signal is owned by GTK and freed after the handler returns; calling `g_free(text)` there is a double free. The probe's handler indeed does not free either.
- **Pitfall 3 (CJK never appears)**: feeding surrounding text every frame while also calling `gtk_im_context_reset` **breaks the composition** - reset means "discard the current composition". Feed only `gtk_im_context_set_surrounding(im, text, len, caret_bytes)` (the four-argument legacy call, same as the probe).
- **Pitfall 4**: the `preedit-changed` signature is `(GtkIMContext*, gpointer)`; the string must come from `gtk_im_context_get_preedit_string(ctx, &str, &attrs, &caret)` (connecting as `(ctx, text, data)` hands you a bogus pointer). `caret` is a **byte** offset inside the composition, the same unit as this stack's UTF-8 byte caret.
- **Toolchain addenda**: `FixedArray[Byte]` -> `String` goes through `@utf8.decode_lossy(FixedArray::unsafe_reinterpret_as_bytes(buf))` (neither `@utf8.decode_utf8` nor `buf.to_bytes()` exists in this version); `Double -> Int` is `x.floor().to_int()` (no `Int.floor_of_double`, no `+.` operator, and a **line-continuation operator must sit at the end of the previous line**).
- **Objective verification** (`xdotool` injection, `GTK_IM_MODULE=fcitx`): typing `nihao` + space yields `a='hello你哈哦'` in the exit line, the pinyin letters leave no residue, `seen=6` shows only the unconsumed keys (so filtering and "consume-and-eat" both behave), and the process exits cleanly. Five packages warning-free; core 15/15, input 10/10, render 14/14, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 79/79; repo-wide red still 290 errors.
- **Pending eyes/extensions**: the look of the preedit underline and whether the candidate window hugs the end of the composition (this machine can only prove the rectangle was pushed), the ibus round (probe-side conclusion: no inline preview under ibus is suspected to be a libpinyin display-style setting, unrelated to this layer), Windows `ImmAssociateContext` and macOS.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Painter contract completion, first batch (facade + blend modes + gradients)

- **Facade decision**: `Painter` is promoted from a "pure rectangle subset" to the **full contract facade** - `new(bitmap)` opens a Cairo session, and rectangles, paths, transforms, clips, blending and gradients all go through Cairo; when Cairo is unavailable (non-Linux placeholder stub, or all 16 session slots taken) it falls back to the pure rectangle implementation (path calls become no-ops). Rectangle drawing is **pixel-identical on both paths** (a new matrix-equivalence case runs one drawing sequence through both and compares).
- **Session discipline**: there are 16 Cairo session slots (`CR_MAX`) and the one-session-per-frame pattern requires `Painter::end()`; a leak silently downgrades later Painters to the fallback. A "20 open/close cycles still hold sessions" case guards this; nine older tests never called `end()` (14 leaked sessions in total) and were fixed.
- **Blend modes**: the 25 `BlendMode` values map **1:1** onto Cairo operators (16 W3C blends + 9 Porter-Duff). A standalone C probe on this machine (cairo 1.18 + pixman) confirmed **all 25 operators really work** on image surfaces, pigment and HSL families included, so no blend algorithm had to be reimplemented; a translucent source follows the W3C formula (`(1-α)·dst + α·Blend(dst,src)`; measured: 0.5-α multiply = 0.5·dst + 0.5·dst×src), and Multiply over a transparent destination degenerates to the source colour. The measured 25-row table became assertions (blend/HSL rows keep a ±2 tolerance against pixman rounding differences; Porter-Duff rows are exact).
- **Pitfall (snapping must happen in device space)**: snapping rectangle coordinates in **user space** leaves fractional device coordinates once the CTM carries a translate (e.g. `translate(1.3, 0.6)`), and Cairo antialiases those edges - diverging from the pure implementation. The facade-vs-fallback matrix case caught exactly this (red, then green). Fix: convert with `cairo_user_to_device`, then `floor(v + 0.5)` in **device space** and fill integer rectangles under a temporarily identity matrix; when the matrix has rotation/skew (non-zero off-diagonal) snapping is skipped and antialiasing is left in charge.
- **Pitfall (the clip region is graphics state)**: a temporary identity matrix plus `cairo_rectangle` + `cairo_clip` **cannot** be wrapped in `cairo_save`/`cairo_restore` - the clip is part of the saved state, so `restore` would undo the clip you just set. Swap the matrix manually with `cairo_get_matrix`/`cairo_set_matrix` instead (the clip is stored in device space, so later CTM changes do not move it).
- **Pitfall (default line width differs)**: a Cairo context starts at line width **2.0** while the contract defaults to **1.0**; not syncing in `Painter::new` makes strokes that never call `set_line_width` (such as the inner bands of `stroke_rect`) one pixel too thick - the "stroke one ring inside" case pinpoints it.
- **Pitfall (function that does not exist)**: `cairo_pattern_get_status` does not exist (the real name is `cairo_pattern_status`); the mistake surfaces as a **link-time** undefined reference, not a compile error.
- **Pitfall (artifact path is case-sensitive, an addendum to the G3b entry)**: the test artifact path here is `_build/native/debug/test/NoahLiu` (**capital N**); deleting the lowercase path silently does nothing and the tests keep running the old binary - this batch briefly chased a 20-pixel diff after the device-space fix that was in fact a stale artifact. Correct path after touching native libraries: `_build/native/debug/test/NoahLiu/native-gui/<pkg>`.
- **Gradients**: `Gradient::linear`/`radial` + `add_stop`; offsets clamp to [0,1], invalid hex is ignored, an empty-stop gradient is ignored entirely, and `set_fill_color` overrides a gradient. Implementation is a one-shot pattern (create, add stops, set as source, release the handle). Measured behaviour: linear gradients are sampled at pixel centres (`(x+0.5)/axis length`) and pad beyond the endpoints; radial ones are concentric and symmetric across the four corners.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/render` 25/25 (11 new cases: exact Porter-Duff values, the 25-mode table, the translucent formula, transparent-destination degeneracy, mode independent of colour order, linear gradient sampling and pad, radial symmetry, empty-gradient ignore plus solid-colour override, facade paths and antialiasing, save/restore restoring transform and style together, session slot recycling); five packages warning-free; core 15/15, input 10/10, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 80/80; `examples/native-window` smoke: 49 frames, exit code 0 (the example now uses the facade and exercises a gradient halo, a multiply square and a path circle); repo-wide red still 290 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · Painter second batch (offscreen Canvas and images) · sampling domain and archive rebuilds

- **Image decoding** goes through **GdkPixbuf** (already a GTK3 dependency - no new runtime dependency; `GdkPixbufLoader` sniffs the format), and the pixels are converted into this stack's `Bitmap` layout (premultiplied ARGB32, little-endian B,G,R,A). Premultiplication rounds like `Bitmap::paint` (`(c*a+127)/255`), so encode -> decode -> re-encode is byte-stable (translucent colours such as `#12345680` survive exactly). The decoder takes the **static first frame only** - the old `Image` was a `GdkPixbufAnimation` (animated GIF), and animation is a recorded gap, not faked.
- **Size semantics follow the teacher**: `get_width`/`get_height` return the **logical size** (pixels / scale) and `get_scale_factor` returns the recorded ratio, exactly like libyue's `image_gtk.cc:75-77` (`ScaleSize(.., 1.f / scale_factor_)`).
- **Pitfall (a blit must limit its sampling domain to the source sub-rect)**: building the pattern from the *whole* source bitmap and putting the source rect only into the pattern matrix lets bilinear sampling read colours **outside** the sub-rect - sprite-sheet and icon compositions bleed (the "top-left quadrant into 4x4" case went red first: the right edge sampled the neighbouring green block). Fix: create a **sub-surface** with `cairo_surface_create_for_rectangle(s, sx, sy, ssw, ssh)` so the sampling space follows the sub-rect origin, plus `PAD` extend so edges do not fade to transparent seams.
- **Pitfall (`ar rcs` appends renamed members)**: after splitting one C source into `cairo_stub.c` + `image_stub.c`, the stale `librender_cairo_stub.o` in the old archive and the new object both ended up in the library -> link-time `multiple definition`. `compile_stub` now deletes the archive before `ar rcs` and rebuilds when **any** source is newer (the cache used to compare a single source's mtime).
- **Fallout of moving an asset**: the PNG encoder moved from the root package (`yue/png_rgba.mbt`) into `render/png.mbt` (public entry renamed `png_encode_rgba`, internal `pngr_*` kept) and carried its six existing cases along. The root package's private `utf8_bytes` helper does not exist outside it, so the code now uses `@utf8.encode` with `moonbitlang/core/encoding/utf8` added to `moon.pkg`. The move took repo-wide red from 290 down to **287** (one fewer file and test in the root package).
- **File writing**: `Canvas`/`Image` `write_to_file` supports "png" only, landing via the C-side `fsw_write_file` stopgap (paths are taken as UTF-8 bytes) until the system layer exists.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/render` 40/40 (9 new cases: opaque and translucent PNG round trips, empty-image semantics and drawing nothing, logical size under scale, `resize` keeping a solid colour, `draw_canvas` 2x upscale across quadrants, `draw_canvas_from_rect` with no bleed, blit honouring a multiply blend mode, PNG round trip through the filesystem; plus the 6 PNG-encoder cases that moved along); five packages warning-free; core 15/15, input 10/10, text 7/7, yoga-mbt 80/80; `examples/native-window` smoke: 49 frames, exit code 0 (now drawing an offscreen canvas sprite at 4x); repo-wide red **287 errors / 68 warnings** (down from 290, never up).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · icon drawing surface ported (803 fixed-point paths)

- **Scope and fidelity**: the first 4197 lines of master's `icons/icons.mbt` (the 803-variant `IconKind`, the `path_num`/`fill_icon_path` interpreter, `draw_icon`, the `icon_path_data` block and the `kind_index`/`all_icons`/`icon_name` tables) moved wholesale into the new `icons` package. The only changes are two type references (`@yue.Painter` -> `@render.Painter`) and the header comment; the **data block is line-for-line identical** to the master copy (`diff` reports only the 7 header lines and the 2 type lines). The declarative wrappers `icon`/`icon_button_t` need the declarative layer and stay with the component-wiring batch (G7).
- **Semantics copied, not reinvented**: coordinates are integers in thousandths of the normalised value, the command set is M/L/C/Z, each subpath closes M..Z, the whole thing is a single `fill` (iconfont outline style), the safe margin is 0.88 (`v × s × 0.88 / 1000 + centre`), and parsing is read-and-dispatch with no cache (microseconds).
- **Test approach**: (1) the three tables correspond one-to-one - a "counts array where every slot is exactly 1" check instead of a hash set (no extra container for this), with pairwise name comparison for uniqueness (803 entries is small enough); (2) a **full sweep** of the path strings for charset closure (only M/L/C/Z, digits, `-`, `,`; starts with M, ends with Z); (3) `draw_icon` paints pixels whose bounding-box centre is within ±2px of the requested centre (centring semantics, not a pinned shape); (4) all 803 icons rasterised at 24px with **no empty result** (parse + rasterise, under 0.7s).
- **Environmental note (avoid misreading it)**: the example's `seen` counter includes **pointer motion** events (motion carries code 4 and shares the input queue), so with the mouse resting over the window a smoke run shows a non-zero `seen` while no text changes - expected, not stray key input.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/icons` 4/4; `moon check icons` warning-free; `examples/native-window` smoke: 49 frames, exit code 0 (now drawing a row of five vector icons).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · font and rich-text subsystem (Font / TextFormat / AttributedText)

- **Surface**: `Font` (family, logical-pixel size, nine weights, italic; the weight number *is* the CSS 100..900 value and is handed straight to Pango's `PangoWeight`), plus `TextAlign`/`TextFormat`/`TextAttributes`/`SizeF`/`AttributedText` (this batch covers **whole-object attributes**: font, colour, alignment, wrap, ellipsis, `get_bounds_for`, `set_text`/`get_text`/`clear`). Range attributes (`set_font_for`/`set_color_for`) need a Pango attribute list and stay for the next batch.
- **The drawing entries live in `text`** (`draw_in_box`/`draw_font`) rather than as `Painter::draw_text` methods: MoonBit cannot add methods to a type from another package (and render cannot depend on text either). When the component layer is wired, call sites move from the old contract's `p.draw_text(..)` to `@text.draw_in_box(p.bitmap, ..)` - a mechanical rewrite in G7.
- **Pitfall (semantic, caught by a probe)**: `wrap=false` was **not taking effect at all** - Pango wraps by default (`WRAP_WORD`) as soon as `pango_layout_set_width` is called (measured: a 120px width without a wrap flag still folded long text into 4 lines / 88px). Fix: the width is only handed to Pango when wrapping or ellipsizing is wanted; single-line non-ellipsized text is drawn at its natural width (allowed to overflow), and in-box horizontal alignment computes its own offset (the same shape as the vertical path). This also corrects the old claim that "long text in a fixed-height row overflows" - it was actually wrapping.
- **Pitfall (an FFI prototype change needs three places)**: adding weight/italic to `pt_font_line_height` updated the MoonBit extern and the portable placeholder but **not the real implementation** (the Pango branch of `text/stub.c`, still 4 parameters) - the extra arguments landed in the `out_h` pointer slot, whose symptom is not a crash but a "line-height assertion that mysteriously turns false". Rule: an FFI signature change means moon extern + real implementation + placeholder branch, all in one batch.
- **Pitfall (derived traits are a deprecated path in this toolchain)**: `pub(all) enum X { .. } derive(Eq)` raises the `implicit_impl_as_method` deprecation warning (`equal`/`not_equal` implicitly promoted to regular methods), and putting `derive(Eq)` after the enum name is a straight compile error (it belongs after the closing brace). To keep the zero-warning gate, this batch derives nothing and tests compare enums through in-package id helpers (`align_id`/`weight_value`).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/text` 14/14 (7 new: `wrap=false` single-line natural width versus `wrap=true` height, ellipsized width clamped, bold never narrower than regular, in-box horizontal/vertical alignment landing points and centring, `AttributedText` attributes and bounds, `new_with`/`format` round trip); `moon check text` warning-free; `examples/native-window` smoke: 49 frames, exit code 0 (the example now draws a centred bold title, exercising Font/TextAlign/AttributedText).

### yoga-mbt · corpus follow-up closed: a child's flex-basis was shadowed by the container's (Chrome comparison)

- **Origin**: the follow-up derived from corpus `93078300` - with explicit `flex-basis: 0` + `min-height: 0` inside an auto-height column container the engine returned 60 where CSS implies 0.
- **Reusable comparison technique**: a local HTML file plus the browser MCP's `getBoundingClientRect()` reads the real heights; for the spec text, extracting DOM from the drafts.csswg.org page (`document.querySelector('#anchor')`) works where WebFetch only returns the table of contents.
- **Finding 1 (Blink's rule)**: an auto main size equals the **sum of the items' hypothetical main sizes** (flex base clamped by min/max); growth never participates. Evidence rows: `basis 100 / content 300 / grow 1` -> **100** (the spec's Ideal algorithm would grow it and the Web-compatible one would use the max-content contribution, giving 300 - neither matches, and Blink is the alignment target); `basis 0 + min 0` -> 0; `basis 0 + min:auto` -> 60 (content floor); `basis auto + max-height 30` -> 30; `basis 40%` (indefinite container main axis, no reference) -> 60 (falls back to content).
- **Finding 2 (the root cause, a typo)**: while building flex items the base size was read from the **container's** `s.flex_basis` instead of `c.style.flex_basis`, so a child's flex-basis never took effect anywhere on the layout path - which also explains why any basis value gave the same result. The container's auto main size likewise ignored item bases; it is now handled by a new `main_contribution`: a definite basis wins and is clamped by the item's definite min/max, while auto / unresolvable percentages fall back to the intrinsic size. The `min: auto` content floor only applies during flex resolution (§4.5), not in the container's intrinsic estimate - otherwise every width/height query would recompute an intrinsic size per item.
- **Verification**: all 7 Chrome matrix rows (A/C/D/F/G/I/L) now match, captured as a permanent case in `patches_regression_wbtest.mbt`; yoga-mbt 81/81 (including the `display:none` corpus case and every pre-existing assertion); the benchmark is unchanged (1121 nodes x 500 rounds, release: 1.93s before, 1.95s after, measured A/B).

### MoonBit native GUI stack · theme system, first batch (palette derivation + change subscription)

- **Surface**: `Theme` (a 20-field palette) with the `default_theme`/`dark_theme` presets, `theme_from_accent` (the EP theme-workbench formula: keep the hue, saturation floor 0.45, lightness clamped per mode (light 0.34..0.52 / dark 0.55..0.72), hover -0.09 light / +0.08 dark, `*_light` variants mixed 12%/14% toward white in light mode and 30%/28% toward the panel background in dark mode, semantic colours on a fixed hue wheel 142/36/4/210 borrowing the accent's saturation (>= 0.4) with mode-dependent lightness, neutrals never following the accent), plus `theme_current`/`on_theme_change`/`off_theme_change`/`theme_apply`, 20 getters, `control_height` (32) and `entry_ctrl_height`.
- **Three deliberate omissions versus the old chain**: (1) native-widget CSS injection (the old `apply_native_theme_css`) - everything is self-drawn now, so the palette is the only colour source and there is no "native widgets follow the system theme" problem; (2) the `ffi_repaint_all` whole-window repaint fallback - repaint ownership arrives with the component batch through each control's subscription; (3) `theme_from_system` (system dark mode and accent) needs the system-integration layer, not wired yet. Batch unsubscription (`sub_bag_begin/end`) comes with the relink layer.
- **Where the colour math lives**: `theme/palette.mbt` (hex parsing, `rgb_to_hsl`, `hsl_to_rgb`, `mix_hex`, `lighten_hex`, `darken_hex`), with hex parsing reusing `@render.Color::parse` - i.e. the CSS convention where the alpha pair comes last, the recorded divergence from libyue's `#AARRGGBB` (see the G1 entry); theme strings only use the 6-digit form, so they are unaffected.
- **A pitfall inherited from master**: in `mix_hex(a, b, t)` the ratio `t` belongs to the **first** argument, and the endpoints (t=0/1) plus invalid inputs must return the input string **verbatim**, without a `hex6` round trip - `hex6` emits lowercase and would rewrite a theme's `#2D68C4` into `#2d68c4`, which no longer compares equal to the original. Assertions are written lowercase (`theme_from_accent("#FF0000").primary == "#ff0000"`).
- **Test approach**: no assertion on concrete derived colours (a new base palette would turn everything red); only **formula invariants** - a grey accent lifted to the saturation floor, a white accent clamped to the light-mode ceiling, dark mode raising a dark accent to its floor, the hover lightness direction, semantic hues within ±1°, the mixing direction of the light variants, and an invalid accent falling back to the base palette. Subscription tests assert that notification walks a **snapshot** (a subscriber registered inside a callback is not called in that same pass), that unsubscription is idempotent, that unknown handles do nothing, and that getters follow `theme_apply`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/theme` 7/7; `moon check theme --deny-warn` warning-free; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings; other packages and yoga-mbt untouched.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · widget host, first batch (layout tree <-> widget tree, hit-testing and routing)

- **Location**: `core`, per the package table in plan section 2 (the loop and focus stack already live there, the platform backend is `win`, the paint contract is `render`); this layer knows no platform handles. It is also the first time yoga-mbt becomes a dependency of the main package - `moon.mod` gained `"NoahLiu/yoga-mbt@0.1.0"` in its `import` block (without it moon reports "containing module is not imported", even though the workspace already contains the module).
- **Coordinate rule (one conversion point)**: yoga's `left/top` are offsets **relative to the parent's border box**; `relayout`/`relayout_at` fold them once (`collect_abs`) into absolute window-space rects stored on each widget. Hit-testing and the paint walk consume only those absolute rects, and events are translated into widget-local coordinates (local (0,0) = the widget's border-box corner) right before the handler runs. The paint walk layers `translate + clip_rect`, so an `on_draw` callback writes local coordinates only and never clips itself.
- **Propagation**: from the hit widget up the ancestor chain; the first handler returning `true` consumes the event (same rule for pointer, scroll and key). The host keeps **no parent pointers** - bubbling uses the hit path (`hit_path` / path-by-focus-id), so re-mounting subtrees cannot leave dangling parent references.
- **Pitfall 1 (widgets cannot be compared with `==`)**: `Widget` holds closure fields, so `Eq` cannot be derived, and `Option[Widget] == None` is a hard compile error (reported against the `impl Eq for Option` constraint). Fix: an auto-increment `id` per widget; identity comparisons and assertions go through it (helpers return -1 when nothing is hit).
- **Pitfall 2 (hidden subtrees kept stale rects)**: the layout engine stops recursing at `display:none`, so descendants retain the previous pass's `layout_*` values. Skipping them in `collect_abs` is not enough - a hidden descendant could still be hit or painted with an old rect. Fix: when a widget is invisible, zero the rects of the **whole subtree** before returning.
- **Pitfall 3 (`local` is a reserved word)**: `let local = ...` raises `Warning [0035] reserved_keyword` in this toolchain, which the zero-warning gate forbids - rename it.
- **Real-window proof (coordinate alignment is not paper reasoning)**: the example gained a 200x28 demo band (four 50x28 cells, each painting a colour block and counting only button-press hits). `xdotool mousemove --window <id> X 250` plus `click 1` at window x = 200/250/300/360 produced `NATIVE-WINDOW-OK band_h=1/1/1/1` - exactly one hit per cell, no misrouting and no double counting - alongside `frames=49`, `queue=0` and exit code 0.
- **Deliberately not in this batch (the remaining G6 item)**: dirty-region incremental relayout plus measurement/intrinsic caches persisting across layouts; the `App` host that translates raw `win` events into this layer's `PointerEvent`/`KeyEvent` and owns the present loop arrives with the declarative layer (G7).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` 22/22 (7 new: absolute-rect folding including parent padding; deepest-and-last-wins hit-testing plus out-of-bounds/zero-size/hidden misses; pointer localisation with three-level bubbling and consume-and-stop; offset tree mapping (12,45) -> (12,15); scroll bubbling to the container and not routing outside it; key routing by focus id with bubbling and unreachability once hidden; the paint walk's translate+clip - a child's out-of-bounds fill leaves no colour - and hidden subtrees not painted); `moon check` for the five packages and the example with `--deny-warn` warning-free; yoga-mbt 81/81; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · `sys` opened (JSON parser ported, package-local tests added)

- **Location**: the new stack's system-capability package follows plan section 2's name `sys`. First asset in: the dependency-free JSON parser, moved wholesale from master's `yue/vscjson.mbt` (public names `VscJson`/`vsc_json_parse`/`vsc_json_get`/`vsc_json_str`/`vsc_json_int64`/`vsc_hex_val`/`vsc_codepoint_at` unchanged - consumers only swap the `@yue.` prefix for `@sys.`). Removing it from the root package left the red count unchanged (that file had no errors to begin with).
- **Coverage filled in**: on master this parser had **no test of its own** (its coverage lives in the `vscode_history`/`browser_bookmarks` cases), so following the "every new-stack package writes its own wbtest" baseline it gained 7: scalars and type gates, Int64 millisecond-timestamp precision, chained lookups in nested structures, string escapes (plain / `\u` / surrogate pair / lone surrogate -> U+FFFD / unterminated / malformed `\u`), nine syntax-error inputs all rejected with a whitespace-loose positive control, the GeoJSON point path, and the hex plus code-point helpers.
- **Two measured semantics (found while writing the tests)**: (1) in this toolchain `String.unsafe_get(i)` yields a **UTF-16 code unit**, not a code point - `vsc_codepoint_at("😀", 0)` returns `(0x1F600, 2)`, which is exactly why that pairing helper exists; (2) the 2^53 gate for integral floats is tested against the **already-rounded double**, so the literal `9007199254740993.0` lands on 2^53 and passes; asserting "out of range rejected" needs a magnitude like `1e20`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` 7/7; `moon check sys --deny-warn` warning-free; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · rich-text range attributes (`rt_*` sessions) · three measured Pango facts

- **Shape**: `AttributedText` stores data only (text + format + base font/colour + `Array[TextRange]`); each measure or draw materialises a session on the C-side `rt_*` slot table (`RT_MAX 16`) and calls `rt_free` immediately - no long-lived handles, hence no "MoonBit object collected while C still holds the pointer". Text and family names are copied into owned memory at the entry point (the `Bytes` pointer MoonBit passes is only valid during the call).
- **Range units**: **UTF-8 byte offsets** `[start, end)` (same unit as the editing kernel; libyue used character ranges - a recorded divergence). Out-of-range endpoints clamp while building the layout, empty ranges are skipped, invalid hex never enters the list, and `set_text` keeps the range table as before. Public surface gains `range_count()` so the component layer can decide whether the rich-text path is needed.
- **Three measured Pango facts**: (1) `pango_layout_insert_attr` needs Pango 1.52+ and is not declared in this machine's headers - C then compiles it as an *implicit declaration returning int*, truncating the pointer, whose symptom would be a baffling segfault; switched to `PangoAttrList` + `pango_layout_set_attributes` (available since 1.44). (2) Foreground colour and foreground alpha are **two separate attributes** - `pango_attr_foreground_alpha_new` takes a single `guint16`, not four RGBA channels, so a translucent range needs `foreground_new` plus `foreground_alpha_new`. (3) `pango_attr_font_desc_new` copies the description, so the local one is freed straight after.
- **Override order**: attributes of the same kind apply in insertion order (later wins), verified with pixel bounding boxes for "dye the whole string red, then set the first character back to black" rather than by trusting documentation.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/text` 20/20 (6 new: the colour range lands on the right glyph while the rest keeps the base colour; multi-byte text proves the cut is on **bytes** not characters; a font-size range grows the box; later ranges override earlier ones; out-of-range/empty/invalid inputs paint nothing and do not crash; empty text plus range retention after `set_text`); `moon check text --deny-warn` warning-free; the example recolours the first 7 bytes of its title on screen, smoke: 49 frames, exit code 0; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings, yoga-mbt 81/81.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · App host and `TextEditorHost` closure (`core/app.mbt`)

- **Layering**: the host lives in `core` (plan §2 assigns App/Window and dirty-rect repaint there) and collects "window + `Loop` + widget tree + input-method channel + present" in one place, so examples and components stop re-typing the loop. The editor seam is **five callbacks on the focused widget** (`on_key` / `on_commit` / `on_preedit` / `im_context` / `im_cursor`) rather than a host object, because `input` already depends on `core` and a reverse import would cycle. The four G5b signatures (`process_key`/`commit`/`preedit`/`set_cursor_rect`) map one-to-one; the host just dispatches callbacks by focus instead of holding a host.
- **Raw event contract** (`code` from the C-side `WmEv`): `0/1` = key press/release, `2/3` = button press/release, `4` = pointer motion, `5` = wheel. The two payload slots keep their C names on the way in: the button number arrives in `button` (dx), vertical wheel deltas in `axis` (dy, positive = up), and `dx` on a wheel event is horizontal. Translation is split into `raw_to_event` so it is headless-testable; the rest of the host needs a real window.
- **Click-to-focus is a widget-tree function**: `Widget::focusable_at(x, y)` returns the first widget declaring a `focus_id` walking up from the deepest hit, and the host moves the focus stack on `Down` (Web semantics: clicking a form control focuses it) so widgets never touch the stack themselves. When the click is already on the focused widget **nothing** happens on the IM channel - otherwise clicking inside the same field would interrupt the composition in progress.
- **Two measured input-method facts (both "text landing in the wrong box")**: (1) one window owns a single `GtkIMContext`, so a logical focus change must `gtk_im_context_reset`, otherwise the composition pending in the previous field commits into the next one - injecting `a b Tab c` produced `t2='第二个框岸边'` although `岸`/`边` were typed in the first box. Added `Window::im_reset()` (extern + real stub + portable stub in the same batch); call it only at the instant focus really changes - calling it every pump keeps interrupting composition (the G5 "never reset each pump" rule stays). (2) reset alone is not enough: commit text is buffered C-side *while the key event is handled*, so with a burst of injected keys the Tab event is queued behind several commits and the next pump hands the old widget's commits to the new one. The switch point therefore drains commits against the **previous** id, clears the stale preedit, then resets (`App::flush_ime_for_switch(prev)` runs *before* the stack changes). After the fix the same injection yields `t1='hello岸边' c1=11 t2='第二个框曾'`.
- **Four toolchain notes**: (1) `loop` is a reserved word (the App scheduling field is `sched`; `local` was recorded last batch); (2) `PointerKind` cannot derive `Eq` (closure-bearing neighbours), so test it with `match`; (3) `FocusStack::focus(id)` **sets** focus - drawing code that asks "am I focused" must use the new read-only `is_focused(id)`; the example originally called `focus(id)` inside `on_draw`, so every repaint stole focus to the last widget that drew it (exit line `focus=3`, first box unreachable by keyboard); name the ambiguity away and provide the read-only helper; (4) scripted file edits with `str.replace(..., 1)` fail **silently** when the anchor misses (the 8 `Widget has no field` errors at the start of this batch came from that) - assert the replacement count.
- **Injection technique (carried over, two additions)**: keystrokes must go through XTEST, i.e. `xdotool key` **without** `--window` (with `--window` it is XSendEvent, which GTK drops - see the "GUI automation" note above); the mouse, by contrast, needs one atomic command `xdotool mousemove --window <id> X Y click 1` - splitting it into `mousemove --window` then a separate `click 1` yielded `clicks=0` in this run (same family as the recorded "XFCE click-to-focus eats synthetic clicks"). Also: when a test needs latin characters to reach the box verbatim, drop the IM bridge (`env -u GTK_IM_MODULE -u XMODIFIERS`), otherwise the local fcitx5 shuangpin engine turns `a b` into Chinese; when testing the cross-box leak, keep the default environment - that is the meaningful control.
- **Coordinate judgements must be read from the same frame as the layout under test** (2026-10-12, on this rule a false defect was withdrawn): a recorded defect said "the hit area is offset from what is painted - clicking a button's rendered centre triggers its neighbour, and the drift grows to the right". Re-measured it does not hold - taking the box boundaries from a screenshot of the very run being clicked, seven box centres all hit themselves. The error was in **when the coordinates were taken**: that screenshot came from another layout pass (the same example's button row moved by tens of pixels once the host gained whole-page scrolling), so clicking a new layout with old coordinates "drifts", and because the drift is proportional to the horizontal position it looks like a rightward-accumulating offset. The rule: read `xwininfo` for the window origin, screenshot that same window, measure the boxes from it, and only then click. Hit testing and painting are the same source in this stack (both read `Widget::rect`, and scrolling just makes the yoga layout carry the displacement), so "the two are a frame apart" has no code path - when this symptom appears, suspect the measurement first.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` 28/28 (6 new: raw event translation table; commit bubbles along the ancestor chain and stops when consumed; preedit goes only to the focused widget; `im_context`/`im_cursor` availability plus unreachability once hidden; widgets without `im_context` skip the IM round; `focusable_at` picks the nearest focusable on the hit path and ignores hidden ones); all eight stack packages (core/input/render/text/win/icons/theme/sys) `--deny-warn` warning-free; yoga-mbt 81/81; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. New example `examples/native-app` (the accumulating GUI page): exit code 0, `frames=49`; injection proof `a b Tab c` → `t1='helloab' c1=7 t2='第二个框c' focus=2` (typing plus Tab cycling), click at button centre (116,128) → `clicks=1` with focus taken over, `Return` while focused → `clicks=2`, click at the field's left edge (window x=17 → widget-local x=1) → caret back to 0 and typing `z` gives `t1='zhelloab' c1=1` - **click-to-place-caret, previously a eyes-only check, is now objectively verified**. Still awaiting the user's visual confirmation: caret blink feel, preedit underline, candidate window sitting at the end of the composition.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · font family enumeration, fallback and multi-font line height (`text`, third batch)

- **Shape of the enumeration**: the C side builds the family table once into a process-lifetime cache (`pango_font_map_list_families` -> **sort + adjacent dedupe** -> a `\n`-joined `GString`, handed over with `g_string_free(out, FALSE)`), and MoonBit reads it back as "length, then byte by byte" (this toolchain cannot hand C a writable buffer). Sorting is not fussiness: fontconfig's enumeration order follows load order and one family can appear several times under locale names, so without ordering no stable assertion is writable (682 entries on this machine).
- **Availability check lives in C**: `pt_family_available` walks the cached rows with `g_ascii_strncasecmp` - case-insensitive family matching *is* the fontconfig/Pango rule, and reimplementing ASCII folding on the MoonBit side is how you end up disagreeing with the backend.
- **Three fallback rules**: (1) the generic families `serif`/`sans-serif`/`monospace` always count as available (they are not in the enumeration; Pango resolves them to concrete families). (2) `resolve_family(candidates)` follows the CSS `font-family` list: first available wins, all-unavailable returns `""` (empty = "follow the system default", which measure and draw both honour). (3) `Font::resolve` falls back to the empty name **only when the name is unavailable** - available and generic names are kept, because keeping the requested name is what makes its metrics measurable.
- **One measurement corrected an expectation**: an unavailable family name does not make measurement fail - Pango silently substitutes and still returns numbers (`line_height_multi([base, Font::new("不存在", 13.0)])` equals the same call with `""`). So fallback earns its keep by **fixing the line-height basis and making the family diagnosable**, not by preventing a crash; writing the tests around "it should return None / blow up" would have produced fake-green assertions.
- **Multi-font line height, settled**: line box = **max ascent + max descent over the fonts present** (each inline box contributes with its own font, and the two extremes can come from different fonts) - **not** "the line height of the largest font size". Implementation splits the ascent/descent already returned by `pt_font_line_height` into `FontMetrics`, re-expresses `line_height_font`/`line_ascent_font` as projections of it (removing two duplicated C call assemblies), aggregates in `line_height_multi(fonts)`, and `AttributedText::line_height()` feeds in the base font plus every range font. Two hard properties are asserted: the line height **covers** the drawn height from `get_bounds_for`, and the base font's line height **does not** contain a 28px range (a short box clips glyphs - exactly what the component layer needs to know).
- **Six toolchain notes** (all hit while writing the tests): (1) **MoonBit `String` ordering is shortlex - length first, then code units** - so `<` and `compare` are *not* lexicographic (`"Agency FB" < "Aharoni"` is false, `"b" < "aa"` is true); use `String::lexical_compare` for dictionary order and `compare_ignore_ascii_case` for case-folded order. The family-table assertion tripped on this: the C table is properly lexicographic, yet `list[i-1] < list[i]` failed in bulk. (2) `Int::to_char()` returns `Char?` (use `unsafe_to_char` to force it); interpolating the Option silently yields `"Some('x')"` with only a deprecation warning at compile time. (3) `Buffer` in this toolchain exposes no `push`/`push_char` (writing a character is `write_char_utf8`), so `+=` on a short string is cheaper to get right. (4) `Array[Byte]` has no `freeze`/`to_fixed_array` path to `FixedArray[Byte]`; if the bytes go to C as `Bytes`, build with `FixedArray::make(n, b'\x00')` and index-assign from the start. (5) the `@utf8` package path is `moonbitlang/core/encoding/utf8`; `moonbitlang/core/utf8` fails with `Cannot find import`. (6) MoonBit has no single-quoted string literals (`''` is a lexer error).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/text` 28/28 (8 new: table non-empty/ordered/deduplicated/separator-free; availability with case flipped, generics and empty name; `resolve_family` first-available and all-unavailable -> empty; `Font::resolve` and `resolve_fonts` keeping size/weight/style; `font_metrics` agreeing with the two existing projections with 28px ascent above 13px; `line_height_multi` aggregation order-independent and idempotent; `AttributedText::line_height` counting font ranges but not colour ranges; line height covering the drawn height); `moon check text --deny-warn` warning-free and all eight stack packages clean; core 28 / input 10 / render 40 / icons 4 / theme 7 / sys 7 / yoga-mbt 91 all green; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. `examples/native-app` gained a font row (the same string in two boxes whose heights come from base and mixed `line_height()`, the right box visibly taller): smoke exit code 0 with status `fam=682 use='monospace' lhm=42`, and the same-round key/mouse injection still yields `clicks=2 t1='zhelloab' c1=1 t2='第二个框c' focus=1`.

### yoga-mbt · dirty-region incremental relayout + cross-layout cache persistence (YG3e, closing the 40% cost left by YG3d)

- **Three criteria, all required**: (1) **Change notification** - all 39 style setters plus `append_child`/`clear_children`/`set_measure`/`set_baseline`/`set_pixel_grid` start with `mark_changed(self)` (global clock +1, recorded into the node's `rev`); forgetting it raises no error, it just **silently returns stale results**, the hardest class of bug to hunt. (2) **Subtree version** - `calculate_layout` starts with `scan_subtree_rev(root)`, writing "largest rev inside this subtree" into every node, top-down; that is an O(nodes) integer comparison with no measuring and no math, and it makes "this subtree has not changed since its last layout" equal to `subtree_rev == laid_rev` **without parent pointers** (a parent pointer would cycle with `children`, which this toolchain's collector cannot reclaim - in a long-lived GUI that is a steady leak). (3) **Input fingerprint** - `layout_into` remembers the previous `(avail_w, avail_h, pinned_w, pinned_h)`; skipping needs version **and** fingerprint to match, because after the parent relayouts, the space it hands down or the size it pins can change, and then the subtree has to be recomputed even if untouched.
- **Two implementation traps**: (1) the reused value must be the **return value** of `layout_into_impl` (before pixel-grid snapping), not `layout_width/height` - the latter is rewritten by the `round_to_grid` post-pass, and reusing it makes a gridded tree drift every round (`round_to_grid` still walks the whole tree unconditionally; snapping is idempotent). (2) Cache invalidation moved from "clear the whole tree at entry" to a version gate: `cache_fresh(node)` clears that node's two caches only when `cache_rev != subtree_rev` - a deep leaf change must also invalidate its ancestors' **intrinsic-size** caches, and the subtree version does that without back-tracking; plus a `cache_slots = 16` cap, so continuous width changes "lose reuse, not correctness" (otherwise one entry per width grows forever).
- **Bench split into three scenarios** (same program, the pre-change engine taken via `git archive HEAD` as the control, same machine, release, us/round over 1121 nodes): `steady` same-arguments repeat **3652 -> 4**, `one_leaf` one leaf changed per round **3656 -> 78**, `resize` new root width per round (full relayout) **3664 -> 3602** (no regression on the full path). Debug: 7622->14 / 7570->176 / 7572->5940. **The checksums (sum of probe layout results) are identical before and after in all three scenarios** - a cross-engine, independent correctness signal, worth more than any self-asserted test. Timing comes from `moonbitlang/core/env`'s millisecond clock and lives only in the bench package, so the engine itself stays dependency-free.
- **Why the host cares**: `App::paint_frame` calls `root.relayout(w, h)` every frame, which is exactly the `steady` path (clean tree: 4 us per thousand nodes), so "relayout each frame" becomes viable in a GUI; once G7 wires `set_measure`/`set_baseline` from the text layer, `one_leaf` (one line of text changed) is the real shape.
- **Test philosophy and four toolchain notes**: the only correctness standard for incremental layout is **"node-by-node equal to a from-scratch full layout of the same state"**, and all 10 cases assert exactly that (reference tree built fresh with the same state, laid out once), never intermediate state. What was hit: (1) top-level identifiers must be unique within a package - the test's `snap` collided with the existing snapping helper `snap` in `algorithm.mbt`, reported as `toplevel identifier declared twice`; (2) the third clause of a C-style loop only accepts `i = i + 3`, `i += 3` is a syntax error; (3) under the warning gate `let a = []` triggers `unresolved_type_variable`, so write `let a : Array[(Double, Double, Double, Double)] = []`; (4) passing a `UInt64` duration through `.to_double()` into a function taking `UInt64` trips the type gate - do the unit conversion inside the callee. One more: the reparent case first asserted `left == 50` from the new parent's margin, but the value is **150** (row: `box_a` width 100 + 50 margin) - comparing against a tree built directly in the final state beats guessing numbers.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/yoga-mbt` **91/91** (81 pre-existing with zero regression + 10 incremental: same-argument reuse does not change results; a single-point change equals the full layout; untouched sibling subtrees are not re-measured; root width change matches the reference; pixel grid plus skip still lands on grid; hide/re-show leaves no stale coordinates; a node's own style change invalidates its intrinsic cache; structural changes are not hidden; subtree moved between parents; rounds beyond the cache-slot cap still equal the full layout); `moon check src --deny-warn` and `moon check bench --deny-warn` warning-free; the eight main-stack packages unaffected (core 28/28 etc., all `--deny-warn` clean), repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings.
### MoonBit native GUI stack · declarative foundation (`Node`/`mount`/Store/signals/relink on top of Widget)

- **Only one thing changes**: master's `Node { mount : (View) -> View }` becomes `Node { mount : (Widget) -> Widget }` - "constructors only build the tree, `mount` instantiates the control and attaches it to the parent" and the existential, naturally heterogeneous child type stay word-for-word the same. Foundation pieces: `node_of` (wrap an existing widget), `node_of_new` (create + configure callback, and the callback **returns the widget** so it can build sub-trees), `mount_children`, `App::mount(children)` (the content entry point; marks dirty once mounted).
- **Three relink decisions**: (1) **In-place replacement** - the new generation mounts at the tail of the parent, then `move_child` shifts it back to the old index, so siblings keep their order and coordinates no matter what got swapped; for that the engine gained `Node::insert_child(index)` / `Node::remove_child_at(index)` (clamped out-of-range insert; out-of-range remove returns None and changes nothing) and the widget layer gained `Widget::insert_child` / `remove_child` / `index_of` / `move_child` (widget array and node array kept in sync; removal matches by `Widget.id`, because a closure-bearing widget has no `==`). (2) **The old Windows workarounds are unnecessary**: master's `bind_node` defers the rebuild with `post_task` and calls `refresh_layout` after remounting, guarding against native remove+add leaving the yoga root un-recomputed so hit-testing kept hitting stale rects (field-proven: "one click and the whole window stops routing clicks, irreversibly"). This stack re-derives hit rects from the layout result every frame (`App::paint_frame` -> `relayout` -> `collect_abs`), so the hole does not exist - injection proof: after clicking the declarative row three times, the button still counts the next click. (3) **Each generation's subscriptions go into a bag**: `sub_bag_discard(gen)` before rebuilding, `sub_bag_begin/end` around mounting, so dead subscriptions do not accumulate with rebuilds; a free consequence is that **nested `bind_node` is correct too** - the inner node's `sig.subscribe` registers inside the outer bag and leaves with that generation.
- **Repaint path**: `set_repaint_hook` / `invalidate()` is a **process-level single slot**; `App::new` installs its own `schedule_paint` (mark dirty + `@win.wake()`). With no hook installed (headless tests) `invalidate()` is a no-op, so component code can call it unconditionally. Known limit: one host per process; multi-window needs "find the App owning this widget" at the host layer - not built now because there is no multi-window consumer.
- **Porting discipline**: the signal kernel and `Store` moved line by line from master's `yue/signals.mbt` / `yue/store.mbt` into `core/signal.mbt` / `store.mbt` (they were already platform-free; the public names `Signal`/`Store`/`batch`/`sub_bag_*` are unchanged), and 19 existing tests came along verbatim (automatic dependency collection, conditional branches depending only on the signals actually read, diamond convergence, batch coalescing, exact unsubscribe by handle, whole-generation bag discard plus no-bag degradation).
- **Two writing traps**: (1) several `_wbtest.mbt` files in one package **share top-level helper names**, and a duplicate does *not* raise `declared twice` - it silently makes the **earlier definition unused** (rewriting `sized` in `node_wbtest.mbt` immediately produced `Unused function 'sized'` in `widget_wbtest.mbt`); grep the package for existing helpers before writing tests. (2) Declaring `node_of_new`'s callback as returning `Unit` breaks any helper that returns a widget (`sized(w, ..)`) with a type mismatch; returning `Widget` avoids `let _ =` and lets the callback build children.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **53/53** (28 existing + 19 ported signal/Store + 6 declarative: `mount_children` order and coordinates; `bind_node` swaps the tree in place with siblings following; old-generation subscriptions fully dropped by bag - after 5 rebuilds the source still holds exactly 1 subscription; the parent never keeps a half tree (always exactly one child); `swap_node` pre-builds both and only toggles visibility with the hidden box zeroed; `invalidate` a no-op without a hook); yoga-mbt **92/92** (new positional add/remove case: removing the middle child and re-inserting it matches a reference tree node by node; out-of-range removal leaves the tree untouched); all eight stack packages `--deny-warn` clean; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a declarative row; clicking it does `Store.update` -> in-place rebuild): `decl=3 kids=6/6` (root child count constant - neither leaked nor double-mounted), same round `clicks=1` (button hit still fine after three rebuilds) and `t1='hellox' c1=6` (Tab cycling and typing still fine), exit code 0. Awaiting the user's eyes: the row's background flips on odd/even clicks, i.e. a genuinely new tree each time rather than just new text.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · widget interaction foundation (hover/press ownership, pointer capture, no self-capturing callbacks)

- **State lives in the host, widgets read it**: `Widget` gains `hovered`/`pressed`, maintained by the host from hit-testing and capture (`set_hover(id)` / `set_press(id)`, each walking the tree only when ownership actually changes); `on_draw` just reads them. The old stack polled hover state (`hover_group`, since libyue offers no motion events to poll against); this stack has full routing, so enter *and* leave are events: when the hovered widget changes, the previous one gets a `Leave` - the "enter with no exit" shape that tooltips get wrong is not available here.
- **A press opens a capture window**: the decision is a pure function `route_pointer(root, ev, hover_id, capture) -> (new_hover, new_capture, consumed, changed)`; `App` only stores and writes back the two states. Rules: the widget hit by `Down` becomes pressed and is the capture owner; every following `Move`/`Up` goes to that owner (along its path to the root, local coordinates still translated); `Up` clears the press, releases capture and re-evaluates hover **from the release position**; hover does not move while captured. Why it is required: without capture, once the pointer leaves the widget it never sees `Up` again - the stuck pressed look is the mild symptom (measured: the control stays highlighted), and drag-selection plus drag-the-caret simply have no basis. Equivalent to Web's `setPointerCapture`.
- **Callbacks must not capture their own widget (reference-cycle discipline)**: `on_draw` takes the `Widget` as first parameter and `im_cursor` now receives one, so a widget reads its own `rect`/`hovered`/`pressed` from arguments instead of a captured handle. Widgets *own* their callback tables, so a closure capturing the widget is a reference cycle that refcounting cannot break - invisible in a one-shot demo, a steady leak once `bind_node` drops a tree per rebuild. Every draw callback in the examples and tests moved with the signature.
- **One expectation corrected by measurement**: a `display:none` sibling **closes the gap** (a column's auto height counts visible items), so the region the hidden widget used to cover now hits the sibling that moved up, not the parent - my first assertion expected the parent and `route_pointer` returned the sibling's id. Practical meaning: components need no extra hover-state cleanup for hidden widgets; live siblings take the position anyway.
- **One more injection recipe**: write a drag as `xdotool mousemove --window <id> X Y mousedown 1` (atomic press at the point), then `xdotool mousemove --window <id> X2 Y2`, then `xdotool mouseup 1` (no `--window`; XTEST releases at the current point). Measured: pressing inside the band and releasing outside still delivers the up event to the band.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **59/59** (6 new: hover follows the pointer and `Leave` is delivered; during capture moves and the release go to the pressed widget while hover stays put; clicking empty space neither captures nor crashes; hidden widgets take no hover and the moved-up sibling wins the area; `by_id` with `dispatch_pointer_to` deliver by identity, coordinates not consulted; wheel keeps the same bubbling rule); all eight stack packages `--deny-warn` clean, yoga-mbt 92/92, repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a "capture band" whose drawing reads `w.pressed`/`w.hovered`): press inside, drag to (200,245), then to (300,100) and release -> `band=d1/u1/m3` (**the up event arrives despite releasing outside**), with `clicks=1` and `decl=2 kids=7/7` unchanged in the same round, exit code 0.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · first component batch (new package `ui`: text measurement feeding the layout engine, plus container / group / separator / image / icon / button)

- **Why not `yue/components`**: that directory holds the *old-link* component implementations (they reference the `@yue` root package and `@declarative`, and account for most of the repo's 287 red items). New-stack batches re-implement only the semantics worth keeping; sharing the package would make the per-package `moon check <pkg> --deny-warn` gate unrunnable, since the old files' errors come along. Hence a new `ui`; the old package gets replaced/deleted batch by batch and settled at G7.
- **Text measurement feeds the layout engine** (the piece G7 owed): `attach_text_measure(w, src)` installs both `set_measure` (size from `measure_font`) and `set_baseline` (from `line_ascent_font`), so text can finally take part in real `align-items: baseline` layout and a label's box is Pango's true size. Two assertions corrected by measurement: (1) **a column container's default `align-items: stretch` pulls the label's width to the whole line** - to see the measured width the parent must use `FlexStart` or the label needs an explicit width (my first attempt asserted 240 where the measurement said 70); (2) under baseline alignment **the top-edge difference equals the difference of the two ascents** (the larger font sits higher) - I had the sign flipped once. min-content probing (request axis `Some(0.0)`) is currently treated as unconstrained - a documented approximation, refined when ellipsis sizing lands.
- **Pointer callbacks also receive the widget**: `on_pointer`'s first parameter became `Widget` (same reference-cycle discipline as `on_draw`). A button needs `self.rect` to decide "did the release still land inside me" - capture guarantees the button *sees* a release that happened elsewhere, but only its own rectangle separates inside from outside.
- **Two naming divergences from master** (recorded, no compatibility shim): (1) `button(type="text")`'s `type` is a **reserved word** in MoonBit - as a parameter name it is a syntax error - replaced by `kind : ButtonKind` (`Primary`/`Border`/`Text`); (2) disabled styling temporarily uses `fill_hover` background with `text_secondary` glyphs, since the real disabled palette belongs to the theme batch (the current `Theme` has 20 fields and no disabled entries).
- **Theme following is one process-wide subscription**: mounting a component calls `watch_theme()` (idempotent), a theme change triggers one `invalidate()`. Per-widget subscriptions would be pointless - `invalidate` is global - and each would leave a dead subscription behind on every rebuild. Colours are read inside `on_draw` (never baked at construction), so the next frame after a theme switch is already recoloured.
- **The counterpart of `bind_label`**: `label_dyn(sig : Signal[String], ...)` updates text in place, re-installs the measure callback (marking the node dirty) and requests a repaint, without rebuilding the subtree; its unsubscribe goes through `@core.sub_bag_register`, which was promoted to public exactly for the component layer's per-generation cleanup.
- **Verification**: new package `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` 7/7 (label size comes from measurement and re-measures on signal change; two font sizes align on a shared baseline; button click fires only when the release lands inside; disabled consumes nothing and never calls back; hovered skin differs and the glyphs stay inside the box; container/group/separator geometry with the horizontal line spanning the parent; icon occupies its requested size and really paints, missing image files neither crash nor take space); **nine** packages (core/input/render/text/win/icons/theme/sys/ui) `--deny-warn` clean, core 59 / input 10 / render 40 / text 28 / icons 4 / theme 7 / sys 7 / yoga-mbt 92 all green, repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof: `examples/native-app` gained a bottom row built entirely from `@ui` constructors (label + vertical separator + icon + `加一` button + a disabled button), window raised to 420x372; two `mousemove --window 135 278 click 1` injections give `comp=2`, a further click over the disabled button changes nothing and leaves `decl=0` (disabled really consumes nothing), `kids=8/8` stays constant across rebuilds, exit code 0, with the earlier proofs (typing, Tab, click-to-caret, capture band) intact.
### MoonBit native GUI stack · first form-widget batch, plus two rendering defects only a screenshot could catch

- **Defect 1: text ignored the widget origin.** The text layer (`pt_draw*`/`rt_draw`) always drew by wrapping `Bitmap.data` in its *own* surface, so that path never went through the Painter's CTM or clip - the host's per-level `translate + clip_rect` had no effect on glyphs, and in the real window every control's text piled up in the top-left corner. Fix: hand over the session - the render stub gains `void *cr_session_context(int64_t id)` (the session's `cairo_t*`) and the text stub gains `pt_draw_box_cr` / `rt_draw_cr`, which do `cairo_save -> set_source_rgba -> move_to -> pango_cairo_show_layout -> cairo_restore` **on the caller's context** (save/restore keeps the caller's source and clip). MoonBit side: `Painter::cairo_id()`, `@text.draw_box_at / draw_at / draw_text_at`, `AttributedText::draw_at`. The old `draw_to/draw_in_box/draw(target,..)` stay for offscreen buffers (charts etc.).
- **Defect 2: nested painting accumulated offsets.** `Widget::paint` translated by `self.rect.x/y` at every level, but `rect` is the **absolute** rect, so from the second level down ancestors' offsets were added again (the second row's text fell off the window). Now `paint_at(p, ox, oy)` translates only the delta against the already-translated origin and passes its own absolute origin down.
- **Why earlier batches missed both**: in the batch-1 paint test the intermediate widget happened to sit at `(0,0)`, so absolute equalled relative and the assertions passed; the example's status line reports counters and text, never pixels. **Conclusion: "exit code 0 + right state" is not "right picture"** - the accumulating verification page now keeps two machine-checkable routes: a `NATIVE-APP-RECTS` line (each root child's `x,y,wxh`, so injection coordinates come from it instead of guessing off a screenshot) and `import -window <id> x.png` whenever pixels are the question.
- **Cross-archive linking**: the text stub now references a symbol defined in the render stub, so `moon test -p .../gui/text` failed at **link** time with `undefined reference to cr_session_context` (nothing at compile time). Per AGENTS rule 1 link flags live only in `scripts/prebuild.py`: text's `link_flags` gained `-lrender_cairo_stub` (consumer first).
- **Disabled must be a cell, not a bool**: the test caught a real bug in the stepper - with `disabled` baked into the closures at build time, `-`/`+` kept the enabledness of the moment they were mounted. Now `Enabled { mut off : Bool }` is read fresh by drawing and event handlers; composite widgets (checkbox row, switch, both stepper ends) share one cell and `set_disabled(cell, v)` changes it at any time.
- **Widgets own their drag state**: the slider keeps `dragging : Ref[Bool]` instead of reading the host's `pressed` - the host only sets `pressed` in the real event stream, and a component must complete a drag on **any** delivery path (otherwise dragging is untestable headless).
- **Two coordinate rules (four cases tripped on them at once)**: (1) `dispatch_pointer` / `dispatch_pointer_to` take **window** coordinates and translate to local only when delivering - passing local coordinates in a test silently misses (looks like "the click did nothing"); (2) coordinate-hit `dispatch_pointer` cannot deliver a move that has left the widget, so out-of-bounds dragging must go through `root.dispatch_pointer_to(w.id, ev)`, which is exactly what the host's capture window does.
- **Seven toolchain notes**: multi-subject `match` must be a tuple (`match (a, b) { (Some(x), y) => ... }`); `Double::clamp` takes labelled `min=/max=`; `Int::to_char()` returns `Char?` (use `unsafe_to_char`, otherwise you silently interpolate `"Some('x')"`); `String.split` yields `Iter[StringView]` and `StringView::to_string` is deprecated in favour of `to_owned`; `Array[Byte]` has no `freeze`/`to_fixed_array`, so bytes destined for C must start as `FixedArray::make`; dropping the `]` in `Option[(A, B) -> R]` produces the baffling `unexpected token infix`; line continuations cannot start a line with `+`/`&&` (MoonBit closes expressions at line end), and `type` is a reserved word so it cannot be a parameter name.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p .../gui/ui` 15/15 (7 new: checkbox click / toggle back / release-outside / disabled consumes nothing; radio group writes the picked index and re-clicking the selection keeps it; switch keeps its fixed pill size and repaints from the Store; slider value on press, drag beyond both ends, step snapping, disabled inert; progress fills by value; stepper clamps and auto-disables at the bounds; Store subscriptions leave with the bag - `sub_count = 0` after five rebuilds, one when unbuilt inside a bag), plus two rendering regressions (plain text lands at the widget origin and is clipped; rich text drawn through the session lands inside the translated box) and `text` 29/29. Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean; core 59 / render 40 / icons 4 / theme 7 / sys 7 / yoga-mbt 92 green; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window injection using `NATIVE-APP-RECTS` coordinates: `form=true/false/90/1` (checkbox on, switch off, slider dragged to 90 snapped by step 5, stepper +1), `decl=3 kids=9/9` (declarative in-place rebuild intact), `band=d3/u3/m3` (three releases outside the band all delivered), `clicks=2 t1='zhelloab' c1=1` (typing, Tab, click-to-caret), exit code 0; screenshot review: title, both fields, counter button, mixed line-height boxes, component row and form row all placed correctly.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · full single-line input semantics (`ui/entry.mbt`)

- **Shape**: `@input.Editor` stays the single authority over text - the component keeps no second copy; `Store[String]` is its outward projection (edits write back, and an external `store.set` flows into the kernel through a subscription registered with `subscribe_id` + bag teardown, so rebuilds leave no dead subscriptions). The seam with the host is still the five foundation callbacks; the four IME conditions are satisfied centrally by `App::pump_ime`, and the component only supplies `im_context` (committed text + byte caret) and `im_cursor` (candidate-window rectangle).
- **Focus highlight is synced by the host**: new `Widget.focused` plus `Widget::set_focused_by_focus(focus_id)`, called once per painted frame from `App::paint_frame`. Same reasoning as `hovered`/`pressed`: the focus stack only knows ids, while a widget needs "is that id me" - letting every component query the stack would both miss the stack and leak core's focus structure into the component layer.
- **A read-only whitelist must be written per keysym family**: the first version allowed "65288..65300 = navigation", which let **backspace (65288) through**, so a read-only box still deleted characters (the test caught it). Real mapping: arrows/Home/End/PageUp-Down live in **65360..65367**; backspace 65288 and delete 65535 are editing keys. Settled rule: allow Ctrl/Shift combinations, 65360..65367 and Tab; swallow backspace/delete/printable.
- **Double-click detection**: two `Down`s within 400ms and less than 4px apart (clock = `@core.now_ms()`, a re-export of `@win.now_ms()`). Side effect worth recording: rapid repeated clicks *are* double clicks - a headless case that pressed, released and pressed again to test dragging actually took the word-selection branch, so assertions must be written for that semantics.
- **Where CJK word selection is currently approximate**: `is_word_byte` counts everything `>= 0x80` as word content (same rule as the kernel), so a double-click inside Chinese text selects the **whole contiguous CJK run** - measured in the real window: double-clicking at the end of `组件框ab` selected all of it and typing `Q` left just `Q`. ASCII behaves normally (`组件框ab c` double-clicks select only `c`). Whether to converge on Blink's dictionary word segmentation is deferred to the text-side Blink comparison pass rather than guessed now.
- **Drag selection**: the press records an anchor; during capture `Move` calls `select_range(anchor, byte_at_x(x))`, so dragging past the box edges stays continuous (the second consumer of the host's capture window); `Up` ends the drag and later moves no longer follow (asserted by an unchanged caret).
- **Observation without extra getters**: the kernel handle is deliberately not exposed. Headless cases observe two public surfaces instead - `w.im_context()` (text + caret) and behaviour itself (a selection is visible through what typing replaces). Cleaner than adding getters purely for tests.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p .../gui/ui` **25/25** (10 new input cases: typing writes back to the Store with backspace and undo; commit lands in text while preedit only affects drawing; click at the far left/right places the caret; double-click word selection; drag selects all and stops following after release; Shift+arrow extends selection with Ctrl+A/C/V through the kernel's internal clipboard; read-only allows navigation and copy; password mask keeps plaintext in the Store; placeholder paints while the Store stays empty; external Store change flows into the kernel and `focused` syncs from the host). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean; repo-wide red back to 287 errors / **68** warnings (it briefly read 69 from one unused test binding).
