Real-window injection (`examples/native-app` gained an `@ui.entry` row with `focus_id=4`): click in, type `ab c`, double-click at the end, type `Q`, then `Home` + `Shift+End` + `Delete` + `Ctrl+Z` -> status `t3='组件框ab Q'`, `focus=4`, exit code 0; screenshot confirms the component box sits and strokes correctly. Awaiting the user's eyes: caret blink (the component's `caret_on?` is driven by the caller's clock), preedit underline, candidate window at the end of the composition.

### MoonBit native GUI stack · first display-family batch (`ui/display.mbt`: tag / badge / avatar / divider / alert / card / descriptions / empty / result / progress line)

- **One divergence from master** (recorded): the CSS-ish `style=[("marginRight", 8.0)]` channel is not wired yet (it belongs to the theme batch's style channel), so this batch exposes **typed** parameters only. The `SemanticType` enum (Primary/Success/Warning/Danger/Info) and `type_color`/`type_light_color` keep their names, with values read live from `theme`'s five main + five light slots (already in place from the theme batch - no new fields needed).
- **Two general rules for self-drawn components** (learned here, now in the code comments): (1) **drawing callbacks must not mutate layout** - `statistic` first called `set_width(measured)` inside `on_draw`, making paint a writer of layout (layout and drawing trigger each other within one frame, and the incremental engine's fingerprint bookkeeping gets churned by yourself); widths must be decided at mount. (2) **A widget must be tall enough for its own text** - the percentage column of the progress line initially took the bar's 8px height, so 11px glyphs were sliced in half (visible only in a screenshot); text nodes need at least their line height, and the container grows to the tallest child.
- **Closeable alert hot zone**: the whole bar consumes the press (so clicks do not fall through to what is underneath), but `on_close` fires only in the **top-right 24x24**, tested with local coordinates (`ev.x >= rect.w - 24 && ev.y <= 24`) - which still holds under capture, since the release always comes back to this widget.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **33/33** (8 new: five semantic colours distinct and all valid hex; tag and badge width follows content with overflow shown as "99+"; avatar paints its initial; text divider spans the full width with line on both sides; alert is taller with a description and only the hot zone closes (non-hot clicks do not count); a titled card adds two rows (title + separator); descriptions row count follows items; empty/result really paint; progress line has two columns and its fill follows the Store). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean; repo-wide red still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a display block, coordinates taken from the geometry line): click off the hot zone -> `closed=0`, two clicks in the top-right -> `closed=2`, with `comp=1` and `kids=11/11` in the same round, exit code 0; screenshot review: three tag colours, badges 7/99+/dot, avatar "刘", the "或" divider, the alert's accent bar and description, the progress bar with its percentage - all correct.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - display family finished (`timeline` + `carousel`, auto-rotation over the host's delay channel)

- **New channel: process-level delay (`set_timer_hooks` / `set_timeout` / `clear_timeout` in `core/loop.mbt`)**. A component only wants "run this once in a while" and should not know which host it belongs to, so this is the same process-wide single slot as `set_repaint_hook`: `App::new` forwards the two functions to its own `Loop` (`timer` / `cancel`) and components just call `@core.set_timeout(ms, f)`. **With no host installed, `set_timeout` returns 0 and does nothing** - the component still works, it just never advances on its own; a headless test can therefore install its own channel, take the callback and dispatch it by hand, which is what makes the carousel's self-rescheduling chain testable deterministically instead of waiting on a wall clock.
- **Three shape decisions for the carousel**: (1) all panels are **pre-built** and switching only flips `Widget::set_visible` - same reasoning as `swap_node`: not destroying a subtree leaves no dangling callbacks and needs no remount; (2) the arrow's hover background reads the **host-maintained** `Widget.hovered`, so master's two `on_mouse_enter` / `on_mouse_leave` handlers disappear in this stack - interaction state is owned by the host, components only read it; (3) hover-pause relies on the `Move` / `Leave` this widget sees **as an ancestor on the hit path** (events bubble from the deepest hit upwards and the first handler returning true consumes), so a page that swallows moves and leaves never delivers the pause signal. That is a consequence of the routing rule, not a guess, which is why `carousel_pause(handle, v)` exists for callers to take over.
- **Reclaiming the self-rescheduling chain**: one-shot timeout + next tick scheduled inside the callback, with the handle stored in `CarouselHandle.timer`; `carousel_stop` both clears `running` and cancels the pending timeout. The measured edge semantics: after `stop` **the tick already in the queue still runs once**, but the `running` flag makes it neither switch nor schedule again - so cancelling the handle cannot be the only safeguard, the flag has to exist too.
- **Timeline line rule** (as EP does): the first row's connector starts at its dot's centre, the last row's ends there, middle rows run through; row height is fixed and drawing uses local coordinates only (the host translates and clips per layer, so widgets never clip themselves).
- **Two toolchain/test measurements**: (1) payloadless enums with same-named constructors fail inside cross-type literals (`The constructor Primary is ambiguous` - this repo has `Primary` in both `ButtonKind` and `SemanticType`); annotate the array literal's element type rather than qualifying every assertion; (2) a dot is only 8x8, so aiming at "half the row height" (`y+12`) lands outside the hot zone, and the symptom is a silent "click did nothing" with **no diagnostic** - coordinate injection must read the widget's own `rect`, which is why the example's geometry output grew `NATIVE-APP-CAR` / `NATIVE-APP-AUTO` lines giving the arrows, the last dot and the auto-rotating panel.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **37/37** (4 new: timeline rows equal height with no line above the first dot, none below the last, dot colour different from line colour; carousel pre-builds three pages, arrows and dots switch, out-of-range wraps; auto-rotation runs over the host channel - pausing stops switching but keeps the chain alive, `stop` cancels the pending tick; empty input does not crash); `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **60/60** (1 new: uninstalled channel returns 0 and never runs, installed hooks forward `set_timeout` / `clear_timeout` in pairs, handle 0 is not forwarded). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, `yoga-mbt` 92/92, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained the "display family finished" block, window grown to 560x900): injecting "right arrow -> last dot -> left arrow" yields `car=2` - a unique index that shows both arrow directions and the dot each fired - baseline auto-rotation reads `auto=6`, parking the pointer on the auto carousel drops it to `auto=2` (hover pause measured); screenshot: three timeline dot colours with continuous connectors, both carousels' arrows and highlighted dot correct.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - date family first batch (value model + self-drawn calendar, "today" from the platform calendar clock)

- **New platform reading `wm_local_date`** (both `win` C stubs + the `ffi.mbt` extern + a `window.mbt` public API, forwarded by `core` as `local_ymd()`): a calendar needs **calendar time**, while the stack's existing `now_ms()` is `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (a monotonic clock used as the loop's time base) - deriving a date from it yields a stable but wrong number. The C side uses `time()` + `localtime_r` through the system timezone and packs `year*10000 + month*100 + day`; **failure and the portable stub return 0**, `core` turns that into `(0,0,0)`, and only then does the component's `today_date()` fall back to 1970-01-01. Layering rule: the platform reports, the upper layer decides the fallback - this layer never guesses a date.
- **`today` is an explicit optional argument** (`calendar_t(today? : DateYMD = today_date())`): the default reads the platform, tests pass a fixed date. Otherwise assertions like "which cell does 1 October land in" drift with the day the suite runs, which is the same as having no assertion.
- **The 42-cell grid math is extracted into a pure function** `month_grid(y, m) -> Array[(DateYMD, Bool)]` (one more than master): the panel only paints and clicks, so the grid rules (previous-month tail before the first cell, next-month head after the last, in-month count equals month length) are testable without widgets, and the date/range pickers will share this single copy instead of re-deriving it.
- **All 42 cells are built once; switching months only rewrites data**: the panel structure stays constant, so hit rectangles cannot drift out of sync between a press and its release (the host reads `hit_path` from the layout result every frame, but rebuilding a subtree still strands a press on a widget from the previous generation). `rebuild_grid` only writes each cell's `Ref[DateYMD]` / `Ref[Bool]` and calls `invalidate()`.
- **Selection is read live from the Store instead of cached**: `state_of` reads `value.get()` on every paint (only the Store-less form uses its own `sel`), so an external `v.set(...)` immediately moves the solid square and no "subscribe then refresh by hand" step is needed - master's `refresh()` callback chain is replaced by "painting reads live", with the subscription only requesting a repaint (`follow_store`).
- **Select on release, cross-month cells inert**: a cell consumes `Down` only when `in_month` (a tail/head cell does not even take the press, so the event keeps bubbling), and `Up` additionally requires the release to still be inside that cell (capture delivers the release back to the pressed cell; outside it counts as nothing).
- **Two toolchain measurements from writing these tests**: (1) `(x) -> Bool { ... }` is **not** a closure literal (that is a function-type annotation) - `Array::filter` wants `fn(x) { x.1 }`, and the wrong form surfaces as a confusing chain of "cannot infer / unbound / parse error"; (2) for value types this toolchain deprecates `derive(Show)` (use `Debug`) and additionally warns `implicit_impl_as_method` on `derive(Eq)` / `derive(Debug)` - under a zero-warning gate that is a bad trade, so **assertions observe strings** (`format()`, cell-state names), which adds no derives to components and makes failure output readable.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **44/44** (7 new date cases: leap years, month lengths, weekday via three Sakamoto anchors (2026-10-11 Sunday, 2000-01-01 Saturday), month shifting including ±12 and +15 months; zero-padded formatting, time clamping and range endpoints in either order; the 42-cell grid (October's first cell 2026-09-27, cell 5 = 10-01, last cell 11-07, 31 in-month; leap February 29; January's head crossing into the previous December); local "today" must be a real year, proving calendar time rather than the monotonic clock; the panel writes the Store on pick while cross-month cells and out-of-cell releases do nothing; switching months changes which dates are selectable and the selected cell paints its solid square (compared against the panel background, which is opaque); an external Store change moves the selection; range cell mapping for edges/middle/half-open). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, `yoga-mbt` 92/92, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` now lays 6b/6c out as three columns with the window changed to 900x700, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-CAL`): injecting "right arrow -> last dot -> left arrow" plus "click cell 14 -> right arrow into November -> click the same cell" yields `car=2 auto=6 date='2026-11-15'`, `kids=12/12`, exit code 0; screenshot review: three columns side by side, calendar header "2026 年 10 月", September tail greyed, 11 October (today) in the primary colour, indicator dots highlighted - all correct.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - navigation family first batch (segmented / breadcrumb / pagination / steps / collapse)

- **master's `stateful_item` becomes "paint reads live + host owns interaction state"**: the old helper kept its own `hover`/`focused` refs set from enter/leave/focus callbacks. Here hover and press are owned by the host from hit-testing (`Widget.hovered` / `pressed` / `focused`), so the background and foreground callbacks just receive this widget and read it at paint time; components no longer handle enter/leave. Two payoffs: a rebuilt subtree cannot be stuck in "pointer already left but hover still true", and the subscription count drops from two-per-item to one-per-group (`follow_store` only requests a repaint).
- **Selection always lives in a Store and is read while painting**: segmented / breadcrumb / pagination are "click sets, paint gets", so sharing one Store across several widgets keeps every highlight in sync without a registration table of "notify each control after a change".
- **Pagination clamping lives in the callback, not in disabled buttons**: the chevrons stay clickable at both ends and the value is clamped to `[1, pages]`. That differs from the web's disabled form but drops a piece of state (no recomputation when `pages` changes) and lets a headless case assert "eight clicks on the left chevron still rests at 1".
- **Collapsing a panel is `set_visible(false)` (display:none), not destroying content**: the body subtree is pre-built and stays resident; toggling only flips visibility and calls `invalidate()`. The test proves the collapsed panel really stops occupying space by watching the block's total height drop - asserting only visibility would miss "hidden but still taking height". Each panel's open flag is its own `Ref[Bool]`, so panels toggle independently.
- **One assertion I got wrong myself (recorded to prevent a repeat)**: steps interleave "connector before each square", so the child order is `square0 label0 connector1 square1 label1 ...`. Guessing indices from the visual order picks up a label widget - a label has no background, both samples match, and `l1 != l3` fails as `false`. **Pixel assertions on self-drawn components must read the actual child order**, not the order they appear on screen.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **49/49** (5 new navigation cases: segmented writes the Store on click with width following the text and the selected segment's background differing from the others; breadcrumb separators occupy slots, non-last crumbs write the Store while the last one does not; pagination jumps, steps with the chevrons and clamps at both ends after repeated clicks; steps' three states have mutually distinct backgrounds, connectors follow the current step, and moving the Store repaints the former todo square; collapse opens only the first panel, toggles panels independently and loses height once collapsed). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, `yoga-mbt` 92/92, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` adds this batch as a fourth column, window 1240x840, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-NAV` giving the four injection rectangles): injecting "second segment -> second crumb -> page 3 -> second panel head" yields `seg='分段乙' crumb='列表' page=3 step=1 fold='11'` (the `11` also proves the second panel opened without dragging the first along), with `kids=12/12`, the earlier `car`/`date` readings unchanged and exit code 0; screenshot review: segmented highlight, breadcrumb separators, solid current page, the three step states with connectors, and the fold arrow directions are all correct.
- **A harness trap (nearly misread as broken components)**: injecting all three batches' interactions as one 10-click sequence left the last three clicks dead - not because navigation was unwired, but because the example's 5-second auto-quit beat the injection. The tell is "the same coordinates pass when split into batches, and only the first N clicks pass when merged". The accumulating page's lifetime therefore grew from 5 to 9 seconds; from now on check the lifetime against "click count x per-click interval" whenever the page grows, and never read exit code 0 as proof that every interaction passed.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - navigation family second batch (side menu / grouped menu / tree / tabs)

- **Rounded corners live in the component layer rather than waiting on the Painter**: the Painter facade has no rounded rectangle, so `fill_round_rect` composes "four lines + four 90° arcs" inside `ui`, with angles strictly increasing (the `ccw` argument of `Painter::arc` is unavailable on both backends, so counter-clockwise needs swapped/increasing bounds). The menu's 4px radius and accent bar therefore match EP's look without extending the render contract.
- **Tree and collapse share one shape: pre-build the subtree and toggle `set_visible`** - collapsed means `display:none` (not measured, not occupying space, not painted, not hit-testable) and expanding only flips a flag. Node rows are appended in pairs ("row" + "children container"), so tests read `children()[2i]` for the row and `children()[2i+1]` for its children. A leaf row does not even consume `Down` (when it has no children), so the event keeps bubbling instead of silently swallowing the parent's hover.
- **Tab switching is driven by the Store subscription, showing the new page before hiding the old one**: that order was a Windows-only workaround in master (the reverse let yoga size the outer container by zero content in the intermediate state). This stack relayouts from the root every frame and has no such hole, but "show then hide" is harmless to keep and matches `swap_node`'s pre-built form. The tab header is appended **before** the pages so visual order equals child order (a test pins it with `head.rect.y < page.rect.y`).
- **Two ways I wrote the tests wrong (both belong to the "inject by coordinate" family, worth remembering)**:
  1. Pixel samples must use **each widget's own y**. The three menu items share width and x, so sampling the second item at the first row's y actually sampled the first row (the selected one) - "an unselected item matches the container background" then failed as "both are light blue". The symptom looks like a broken component; it was the wrong sample point.
  2. **A hidden widget's rect is zeroed** (an invisible subtree is cleared, per the G6 rule), so "click an item inside a collapsed group" does not hit that item - it hits whatever sits near the origin, here the group-one header, which reopened group one and looked like "nothing I did changed it by itself". Proving "collapsed means unclickable" needs an explicit `hit_path`/`hit_test` on window coordinates, not an offset from the widget's own rect.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **53/53** (4 new: side menu item width follows the container, clicking writes the Store, and the selected row's background differs from the unselected one when sampled per row; grouped menu headers affect only their own group, the column shifts up after a collapse so coordinates must be re-read, and items become clickable again after re-expanding; tree children are pre-built and collapsed, clicking a parent expands it, clicking a leaf does nothing, clicking again collapses; tabs pre-build three pages showing only the current one, both tab clicks and external Store changes switch pages, header sits above the page area). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, `yoga-mbt` 92/92, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a fifth column, window 1620x840, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-NAV2` with the second menu item, the tree parent row and the second tab): a merged 13-click run (three carousel + three calendar + four first-batch navigation + three of this batch - and the tab must be clicked **before** expanding the tree, since expansion pushes the tabs down) yields `car=2 auto=11 date='2026-11-15' seg='分段乙' crumb='列表' page=3 step=1 fold='11' menu='报表' tab=1 tree=1`, `kids=12/12`, exit code 0; screenshot review: menu selection with rounded light background, accent bar and the "报表" icon, tree caret directions, tab indicator and page content all correct.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - transfer closes the navigation family (10/10), plus two coordinate rules for rebuild-style lists

- **Selection becomes two component-owned tables; rows hold no subscriptions**: master's transfer subscribed once per row it built and relied on `sub_bag_begin/end/discard` to unsubscribe the whole bag before every rebuild, so dead subscriptions did not accumulate with the number of moves. Here a row only reads a self-owned `Ref[Array[String]]` (selection stored by value) and a rebuild is "clear the children and re-mount from the Store's current value", so there is no per-row subscription to leak at all - the bag is left with just the two column Stores. The trade is that the selection set is not itself a Store; what it buys is that rebuild count and subscription count stop being related.
- **`Store.set` does not check whether the value actually changed**: a no-op move still calls `from.set(remain)` and `to.set(merged)`, both Stores notify and the lists rebuild again. That is `Store`'s existing semantics (no deduplication, see `core/store.mbt`) and the component deliberately does not add an "skip set when equal" test, which would also block legitimate "refresh the view even for an equal value" uses. Tests must therefore be written to that semantics: re-read coordinates after every click that may trigger a set.
- **Two rules for "inject by coordinate" plus rebuild lists (both measured here)**: (1) a freshly rebuilt row **has no rectangle** until the next layout pass - in headless cases every click that may cause a rebuild must be followed by a manual `root.relayout(...)` (the host relayouts every frame, so a real window never notices). Missing it looks like "the click did nothing", because a zeroed rect plus an offset hits something else. (2) When batching clicks, **any click that changes layout must come after the widgets below it in the same column**: here expanding the tree first (+64px) made the two transfer clicks miss entirely (`xfer=3/1` unchanged); moving that tree click to the end gave `xfer=2/2`. The geometry line reports the *last* frame's rectangles, so the injection order has to match it or you are clicking new positions with stale coordinates.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **54/54** (1 new transfer case: click to select, chevrons move selections, selection clears after a move so a second click moves nothing, reverse move, and an external data change rebuilds the column - including the two relayout rules above). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, `examples/native-app` clean, `yoga-mbt` 92/92, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a transfer in the fifth column, window 1620x900, `NATIVE-APP-NAV2` extended with the `xrow`/`xbtn` injection points): after reordering the merged 15-click batch to "click before things move", the status line reads `car=2 auto=11 date='2026-11-15' seg='分段乙' crumb='列表' page=3 step=1 fold='11' menu='报表' tab=1 tree=1 xfer=2/2`, exit code 0; screenshot review: both columns' borders, the row marker squares and the `>`/`<` button directions correct.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - scroll container (relative positioning added to yoga, plus `ui/scroll.mbt`)

- **Scroll translation lives in the layout engine, not the widget layer**: `position: relative` + `top: -offset` moves the whole content block up, and a relative offset **only shifts the box without changing flow occupancy** - the container grows to the content's natural height and later siblings are unaffected, which is exactly what scrolling needs; hit-testing and painting both consume layout results (the host folds absolute rectangles via `collect_abs` every frame), so the component layer needs no second coordinate conversion. To that end yoga-mbt gained relative offsets: `top`/`left` win, the opposite edge is ignored when both are given, `auto` means no offset, and percentages resolve against the parent's content box; **absolutely positioned children ignore all this** (their `top` is an inset measured from the padding box). All three are pinned in `relative_wbtest.mbt`.
- **The content must set `flex-shrink: 0`**: in CSS a flex item can shrink by default, so a sole child would be squeezed to the viewport height and never scroll - a "container that should scroll neither scrolls nor deforms" trap only visible when you actually try to scroll, hence the test that pins the geometry first (20 rows x 20 = content 400, viewport 100, limit 300).
- **The scrollbar is an absolutely positioned child of the viewport, not a layer painted onto it**: pinned to the right edge and covering the content, it is the deepest widget on the hit path, so buttons inside content rows cannot steal its presses or drags (a bar painted in the viewport's `on_draw` gets eaten by content hit-testing - a "visible but unclickable" failure you only find by clicking). The thumb length follows the viewport/content ratio with a 16px floor for grabbing, and dragging maps "thumb centre follows the pointer": `offset = clamp((y - thumb/2) / (track - thumb)) * limit`; dragging to the middle measured `scr=171/320`, matching hand calculation exactly.
- **Geometry is copied into the state during the first paint**: viewport and content heights are only readable from layout results inside the paint callback (`self.rect.h` and the content child's `rect.h`), so `ScrollState.view_h/content_h` are refreshed by the viewport's `on_draw` each frame and both the thumb ratio and the clamping consume them. The cost is that **`max()` is still 0 before the first painted frame** (the wheel does nothing until then); a real window paints every frame so this never shows, and headless cases must `paint` once before asserting scroll behaviour - noted in the test comments.
- **A diagnostic mnemonic (this one cost ten minutes)**: a missing closing bracket in a type - `children : Array[@core.Node>` - reports as `Inline wasm syntax error: unknown operator ?STRING` plus `The type alias Array expects 1 argument(s), but is here given 5`, spanning the whole signature and pointing nowhere near the spot. When an error looks **semantically unrelated** (wrong argument count for `Array`, inline wasm), re-read the bracket pairing in the signature instead of guessing at language features (the control experiment: emptying the function body reproduces the same error, so the fault is in the signature).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **58/58** (4 new scroll cases: viewport/content geometry from layout with the wheel changing the offset and clamping at both ends; content shifts with the offset and the bar sits above the content (proved by hit-testing); clicking the track jumps and dragging the thumb follows, stopping after release; content shorter than the viewport neither scrolls nor draws a bar (proved by the pixel showing the row colour there)). `moon test -p NoahLiu/yoga-mbt` **96/96** (4 new relative-positioning cases: `top` only shifts without moving siblings or container size; `left` may be negative and `auto` does not offset; giving both ends makes `top`/`left` win while only `bottom`/`right` shifts the other way; percentages resolve against the parent content box and absolute children ignore offsets). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a sixth column "scroll container": a 190x120 viewport holding 20 rows of 22, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-SCR`): injecting three wheel-down notches yields `scr=90/320` with the geometry line showing `row0=1182,437` (the first row moved up by 90 = 527-90), dragging the bar from top to middle yields `scr=171/320` (matching hand calculation), exit code 0; screenshot review: rows 7-11 visible, rows clipped at the viewport edge, and the thumb position tracking the offset.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - overlay foundation (`core/overlay.mbt`) and the first three popups (popover / dropdown / tooltip)

- **Three decisions for the host's overlay layer**: (1) **an empty layer is invisible** - a `display:none` widget is on no hit path, so clicks reach the content normally while nothing is open; the first popup makes it visible and triggers an **immediate relayout** (the layer was invisible, so its rectangle was zeroed and its own origin cannot be derived otherwise), letting popups be placed by "window coordinate minus layer origin". (2) **Dismiss-on-outside fires on release** - both press and release are consumed by the layer (so the click does not leak into the content), but the dismissal happens on release: dismissing on press makes the release of the same click re-hit the trigger and immediately reopen what was just closed (measured: the second click closed and reopened the menu, looking like "the click did nothing"). (3) **Popup content is created on open and dropped on close** - it never enters the content tree, takes no space and has no hidden state to keep in sync; that is a different road from the "pre-build + toggle visibility" switching components, because popups are instantaneous.
- **`popover_t` / `dropdown_menu` wrap the trigger and watch for the release**: inner widgets run first (buttons consume the press), and the release bubbles up to the wrapper, which toggles - so wrapping a button or a label both work. "Clicking the trigger while open" is in practice consumed by the layer; the component's `overlay_alive` branch is a fallback (programmatic dispatch, or a form where the layer was removed).
- **`tooltip_t` follows the host-maintained hover state instead of bubbling move events**: a child may consume moves itself (this repo's button does `Move => !is_off()`), after which a wrapping component never hears about it - that is exactly why the real window showed "no bubble after 300ms of hovering". `Widget::set_hover` therefore gained **ancestor semantics** (hovering a child marks the parent, as CSS `:hover` does) plus an `on_hover_change` callback fired by the host only on real transitions; the tooltip starts and stops its timer there and works around anything. A core case pins "all three widgets on the hit path count as hovered, siblings do not, and re-setting the same state does not re-fire".
- **One divergence from master (recorded)**: the old `tooltip_t` used the native GTK tooltip (`ffi_view_set_tooltip`); a self-drawn stack has no native tooltip channel, so it becomes "show a self-drawn bubble after a hover delay" - fully themeable (rounded, themed), at the cost of managing the delay and dismissal itself (via `@core.set_timeout`, a no-op without a host).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **62/62** (4 new overlay cases: an empty layer does not block content, clicking the trigger opens and clicking again closes, the popup sits 2px below the trigger; clicking outside dismisses it and that click does not leak into the content; the dropdown has 4 rows including a 1px separator with selection reporting its index and closing; the tooltip appears only after the delay, closes on leave, and never appears if the pointer leaves first). `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **61/61** (1 new: ancestor hover semantics plus `on_hover_change` firing on transitions without duplicates). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained an overlay column, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-OV`): injecting "open the dropdown -> pick item 2 -> open the popover -> click outside -> hover the tooltip anchor" yields `pick=2 ov=1` (the `ov=1` meaning the tooltip bubble is still open at exit), with all earlier readings unchanged (`car=2 auto=14 date='2026-11-15' ... xfer=2/2`) and exit code 0; screenshot review: the dropdown chevron points up, the pane shows four rows with a separator, and the popup sits above the content.
- **An environment note worth recording**: this machine's display changed to portrait (1440x2560) mid-session, so the example's seven side-by-side columns (~1800px) extended past the screen and the rightmost columns could not be clicked. The accumulating page is now split into two rows (4 columns + 3 columns, window 1400x1260). **When the requested window size exceeds the screen the WM silently clamps it**, and injecting purely from the geometry line then targets invisible areas - the symptom being "some columns just do not react".

### MoonBit native GUI stack - overlay family finished (dialog / toast / context menu) with three measured rules

- **The dialog opens on the overlay layer** (a divergence from master, recorded): the old chain made the scrim an **in-page absolutely positioned layer** (one `Node` resident in the page tree, shown and hidden via `set_visible`); here it opens on the layer - a dialog naturally has to sit above all content and other popups, which is exactly what the layer means, and no hidden tree has to stay resident. The scrim uses `overlay_show_full` (a wrapper with all insets zero) plus **an explicit `width/height: 100%`**: depending on inner `align-items: center` to fill is unreliable - when the content is wider than the window the scrim follows the content (measured: with a 420 panel in a 400 window the scrim was only 420 wide and failed to cover the window).
- **`node_of_new`'s finish runs at construction time** - any initialization that needs the host to already exist (like "open according to the Store immediately") must use the **explicit `mount` form** (`{ mount: fn(parent) { ... } }`). A test constructing `dialog_t(visible=true)` before the host was installed silently lost the open call (`overlay_count == 0`). In a real window `App::new` installs the host before anything is built, so only tests hit this - but treating "construction depends on a process-global slot" as normal is fragile, so it is now mount-time.
- **Right-clicks dispatch ancestor-first** (`dispatch_on_path` walks root-to-deepest when `button == 3`): a context menu is a container-level affordance that must be reachable by a wrapper outside inner widgets - otherwise wrapping a button fails, because the button consumes the press first (this repo's buttons consume every `Down` regardless of button). All other buttons keep "deepest wins" unchanged.
- **Toasts open on the layer, created by the first push** (a divergence from master, recorded): the old chain returned "layer node + push function" for the caller to mount; here the top strip is a layer entry (opened on push, closed when the last bar goes), so **toasts always paint above dialogs** and no empty tree stays resident. The entry point is just a push function, a no-op without a host, and the stacking order "later entry on top" is asserted directly by handle order in a test.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **67/67** (5 new: the dialog opens from its Store with confirm/cancel closing first and then calling back and the Store returning false; the ✕ closes and mask clicks obey `close_on_mask`; a toast appears on push with width following the text, auto-removes on time and takes the layer down with the last bar; a right-click opens at the pointer and reports the picked item; a toast stacks above an open dialog). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained an overlay-second-batch column in row two, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-OV2`): injecting "open dialog -> confirm -> push toast -> right-click -> pick item" yields `dlg=1 ctx=1 toast=1` with `ov=0` at exit (the toast auto-removed after 2s), exit code 0; screenshot review: the dialog's translucent scrim with a centred panel (title / close / body / cancel+confirm) and the centred top toast with a green check.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - the four date pickers (`ui/datepicker.mbt`, date family 5/5)

- **Popups always open on the overlay layer** (the freshly landed overlay foundation gets its first consumers): both the calendar panel and the time panel are one `overlay_show_at` entry, so dismissing by clicking outside or by clicking the field again is handled by the layer and the field respectively - components never track "somebody closed it outside".
- **The calendar panel and grid are reused straight from `datetime.mbt`**: `build_calendar` (42 cells built once, month switching only rewrites data) and `range_state_of` (solid edges, light band inside) are shared by all four pickers, which makes `date_range_picker` a dozen lines of "two-phase click plus write the Store" - the payoff of extracting the grid math into pure functions.
- **Field interaction decisions**: the whole field consumes the press (entering capture); on release the **local x** distinguishes three things - clicking the ✕ clears (hot zone only when `clearable`, hovered and filled, at `width-40..width-20`), an open popup closes, otherwise one opens. Clearing does not fire `on_change` (as in master: the callback takes a non-null value).
- **Two divergences from master (recorded)**: (1) the old popup was a native Popover; here it is a self-drawn panel on the overlay layer; (2) the old time input was two `input_number` fields, here it is a **stepper** (hours ±1, minutes ±5, wrapping) because a self-drawn stack has no native input-field semantics and keyboard editing would need its own focus and IME wiring (for keyboard-editable multi-line text use `entry` or the upcoming `textarea_t`).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **71/71** (4 new: the date picker shows its placeholder, opens the calendar, fills and closes on pick, closes when the field is clicked again, and clears via the hovered ✕ without firing the callback; the date-range picker writes a whole range with its two-phase click and closes; the time range stepper changes values with confirm writing back and cancel not; the datetime range writes each of its four fields independently, dates through the calendar and times through the stepper). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a date-picker column in row two, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-DP`): injecting "click the date field -> click the 1 October cell" yields `dpk='2026-10-01'`, "click the range field -> 3 October -> 7 October" yields `drg='2026-10-03~2026-10-07'`, and "click the time field -> plus -> confirm" yields `trg='10:00'`, exit code 0; screenshot review: the calendar popup sits below its field and the time popup shows its hour/minute steppers with cancel/confirm.
- **One injection-coordinate rule (hit again this batch)**: both time-popup clicks missed on the first attempt because I derived the centre from "container height" and was off by one row (the step landed on the minutes row, the confirm click on empty space to the right of the button). **Coordinates inside a popup must be read back from a screenshot or printed from the widget's own rect**, not inferred; recomputing as "panel padding + row index x row height + half" hit on the first retry.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - table first batch (`ui/table.mbt`: model + `table_t`)

- **Sort state belongs to the component, the sort order belongs to the caller**: the component toggles `Store[TableSort]` from header clicks and draws the arrow (new column ascending -> same column flips -> third click clears with `column = -1`) but **never sorts the data itself** - whether a numeric column wants numeric order, lexicographic order or a custom comparison is a business decision, and sorting behind the caller's back gets it wrong (master documents the same rule, followed here). The indicator has two forms: a solid triangle for the active column (up for ascending, down for descending) and a grey up/down hint on hover for sortable columns.
- **Column widths are a pure function, `table_widths`**: fixed columns take their declared width, elastic ones (`<= 0`) share the remainder equally, and when the total is too small elastic columns drop to zero (overflow rather than squeezing fixed columns). A unit test pins all three cases without painting a table.
- **Rows form a rebuild-style list** (same as `transfer`): a data Store change clears the children and re-mounts from the new value, with no per-row subscriptions; selection and sorting only affect **painting** (read live in `on_draw`), so clicking or sorting never rebuilds. Selection stores **source-data indices** rather than display positions, so a selected row follows its data across re-sorts - steadier than master's display-position ref.
- **Four cell types**: plain text (aligned per column), semantic tag (light background plus main-colour text, matching the display family's `tag_of_type`), colour box (swatch plus label) and multi-line (at most two lines within the row height).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **75/75** (4 new: the three column-width cases; header cell count, zebra striping, clicking a row writing the selection Store with a left accent bar, clicking again deselecting, and the row callback carrying the source index; the four-step header sort state machine including a non-sortable column ignoring clicks; tag and colour-box cells producing pixels plus a data change rebuilding the whole table). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a table section, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-TB`): injecting "click the header twice (asc -> desc) -> click row 2" yields `tsort=0/false tsel=1`, exit code 0; screenshot review: the descending sort triangle, zebra stripes, and the selected row's light-blue background with its left accent bar.
- **One rule (three instances of the same typo this batch)**: a missing closing bracket in nested generics (`@core.Store[Array[TableRow]>` instead of `]]`) reports as `The type constructor Store expects 1 argument(s), but is here given N` plus `Expected upper case identifier for type name`, spanning the signature and unrelated to the real fault. Grepping for `]>` after writing nested generics is now a fixed self-check; two of the three instances were localised by the control experiment "empty the function body and the same error appears, so the fault is in the signature".

### MoonBit native GUI stack - virtual list and virtual table (the ten-thousand-row form)

- **Only the visible window is mounted**: the viewport and the row height are fixed, so a change in the scroll offset re-mounts the window as "first row = offset / row height - overscan", with a spacer above and below pushing the inner height to "count x row height". Hit-testing and painting only involve the mounted rows: a 200-row store measures at 10-13 mounted row widgets.
- **A spacer is not a translation - both are required** (real-machine defect: the virtual table in `examples/native-app` "went blank from the top downwards" after scrolling): the first version only inserted a `first x row height` spacer when `first > 0` and left the inner block un-translated, with the rationale written as "the spacer also places the visible rows at the correct scroll position". **That rationale is wrong**: without a translation, row `i` always sits `i x row height` below the inner origin, i.e. **exactly one `offset` too low**, and the host clips every widget to its own rect (`clip_rect` in `widget.mbt`), so the larger the offset the further the visible rows fall out of the bottom of the viewport while the top empties out - precisely the reported symptom. The spacer only covers the "window's first row -> content origin" gap; the offset itself has to be carried by `position: relative` + `top: -offset` on the content block, the same mechanism `scroll_view` and `sticky_list` use. The old form looked fine only while the offset was a small exact multiple of the row height.
- **Geometry regressions must assert rects, not mount counts**: this defect survived because all three existing tests asserted "which rows got mounted" and "is the inner height right", and none asserted "does a row's absolute rect fall inside the viewport". The new pinning test scrolls **1.5 rows** (a non-integer multiple exposes the bug fastest), asserts that the content block moved up by exactly the offset and that the first row's top is half a row above the viewport top, then scrolls to the bottom and asserts 5 rows are still visible inside the viewport. Any component whose contract is geometric gets `rect` comparisons.
- **The scrollbar was extracted from `scroll_v` for reuse** (`scroll_bar(state)`): track/thumb ratio, track-click jump, press-and-drag, stop-on-release are written once and shared by `scroll_v` and `virt_list`. The extraction itself hit a scripting trap: slicing by line numbers duplicated the setup lines (the span from `let bar` to `append` already contained them), and `--deny-warn` immediately reported `unused dragging` - **re-run the gates after any extraction**, duplicated blocks and unused bindings cannot hide.
- **Never rebuild the window inside `on_draw`**: the paint walk is iterating `kids`, and mutating children would trip the iterator; so `on_draw` only **copies** the viewport height into the state, and window rebuilds happen exclusively in event callbacks (`ScrollState.apply`) and the data subscription. On the first frame, when no real viewport height is known yet, the declared `height` is used as a fallback - otherwise the first frame would mount only a few overscan rows and look empty.
- **When data shrinks, clamp the offset before rebuilding**: the subscription first writes `content_h` from the new count, pulls an out-of-range offset back to the maximum, and only then rebuilds the window; skipping that leaves the view stranded on blank space after a deletion.
- **The virtual table is a fixed header plus `virt_list`**: the header does not scroll, and its rows share the same construction as `table_t` (the header and row builders were extracted into `table_header` / `table_row_widget`, used by both, leaving `table_t` itself as just "assemble header + rebuild-style rows"). The header width subtracts the 8px scrollbar and the side borders so its column widths match the data rows.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **78/78** (3 new: a thousand rows mount only the visible window and after scrolling four notches the window's first row is offset / row height - overscan = 4 while the inner height stays count x row height; dragging the scrollbar changes the offset and shrinking the data clamps it back; the virtual table keeps its header fixed, mounts only visible rows, and its header sorting and row selection match `table_t`). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a virtual-table section, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-VT`): injecting "three wheel notches down -> click the middle of the list" yields `vt=1/1` with `NATIVE-APP-VT mounted=13 total=200` (**a mounted count far below the total is the machine-checkable evidence that virtualisation is active**), exit code 0; screenshot review: rows 1-6 after scrolling, the selected row's light-blue background with its left accent bar, and the scrollbar thumb tracking the offset.
- **Fix batch verification**: `moon test ui` **133/133** (including the new pinning test), `moon test core ui render text theme icons sys input win dbus chart mdview web` **865/865**, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean. Real-machine items still pending the user's check: `moon run examples/native-app` and the process page of `moon run examples/sysmonitor` - after a dozen wheel notches the row content must keep tracking the scrollbar with no blank band at the top, and row clicks plus thumb position must agree with the offset.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - table column resizing and the sticky list (table 2/2, scroll extras wrapped up)

- **Column widths are live**: `table_t` and `table_v_t` each hold a `Ref[Array[Double]]` of widths; header dragging mutates it and the two header cells plus **already-mounted row cells are re-widened on the spot** (no tree rebuild). The virtual table uses a `rev : Store[Int]` to make `virt_list` re-mount the window (only a dozen visible rows, so rebuilding is cheap). The drag makes **adjacent columns trade width** and clamps to `[MIN_COL_W, total - MIN_COL_W]` (a 40px floor), with no handle on the last column's right edge.
- **A seam only a real window exposes**: header cells consume the press themselves (clicking the title sorts), so **boundary dragging on a sortable column was dead** - the parent (the header) never received the press. The fix is for the cell to *not* consume presses within 3px of its left/right edge, handing boundaries to the header; the unit test therefore deliberately uses **sortable** columns (it was first written with non-sortable ones, where the test passed but the real window refused to drag).
- **The sticky list's "current group" is derived from layout results**, not computed from the offset: group heights depend on content (row counts, wrapping), so each frame reads the rendered group rectangles and writes the last group whose start is <= the offset into `StickyList.cur_group`, with the floating bar only painting it. The bar is an absolutely positioned strip inside the viewport (the same relative-offset content translation as `scroll_v`), and scrolling plus the scrollbar reuse the shared pieces.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **80/80** (2 new: dragging a header boundary trades width between neighbouring columns, clamps at the minimum, re-widens mounted row cells, and stops after release - using sortable columns; the sticky list switches its pinned title when scrolling past a group boundary, shifts the content block, and reports a scroll limit of content height minus viewport). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a sticky-list section plus the table drag): injecting "press the header's 140px boundary and drag 80px right" yields `head0=24,521,200x40` (= total 240 minus the 40px floor, matching hand calculation) and "five wheel notches down" yields `sticky='组二'` (`NATIVE-APP-STK max=238`), exit code 0.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - split layouts and the first text-family batch (`hsplit`/`vsplit`, `TextRole`/`label_t`/`measure_text_height`)

- **Dragging a split handle only changes styles**: the old chain (master's `yue/splitter.mbt`) wrote `set_bounds` directly **and** duplicated the width into the style (a GTK-era workaround where runtime layout-property changes did not stick, see its probe-splitter note). This stack relayouts from the root every frame, so a style change takes effect on the next frame and nothing else is needed; during a drag the host's **pointer capture** keeps delivering moves to the handle even outside its bounds, with displacement measured in window coordinates.
- **The total is read from the three widgets' rectangles rather than capturing the parent**: clamping needs "the combined length of both panes", and the parent container must not be captured by a child's callback - a parent/child mutual reference is a cycle. Using `first.rect + handle width + second.rect` is equivalent and cycle-free.
- **The first pane starts at a percentage and becomes fixed once dragged**: `percent(ratio*100)` lets it follow container resizes without "measure once then compute", and the first drag switches it to `fixed(px)` (user-controlled from then on); `min_first`/`min_second` clamp during the drag.
- **Text roles (`TextRole`) and `measure_text_height`**: a role bundles size and colour (Title/Section/Body/Secondary/Accent) with `role_color` read live from the theme; `measure_text_height(text, role, width)` lets dynamically-growing labels set an explicit height - the natural height a container measures and the height produced by wrapping at the box width need not agree, and an explicit height is the stable choice (a trap recorded by the old chain, kept here).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **85/85** (5 new: measuring height grows noticeably when a width is given and taller roles measure taller; role sizes and colours (Accent uses the primary); a themed label wraps at `max_width` and a fixed colour overrides the role colour (pixel-checked); an `hsplit` handle drags the first pane's width, clamps at both extremes, and stops after release; the same for `vsplit`). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a split/text section, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-SPL`): injecting "press the handle and drag 100px right" yields `first=24,1236,788x116` / `split=788` (688 + 100, matching hand calculation), exit code 0.
- **A window-size check worth repeating (hit again this batch)**: the example page has grown past 1400px, so with a 1260-tall window **the lower half sits outside the window** and injections land on invisible ground and do nothing; raising the window to 1500 made it work. **Whenever a section is added to the accumulating page, check the window height against the last entry of `NATIVE-APP-RECTS`** - a coordinate in the geometry line does not mean it is clickable.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - first five form-family items (`button_t`/`link`/`form_item`/`form`/`rate_t`)

- **Which pixel column the focus ring lands in**: `stroke_rect(0.5, 0.5, w-1, h-1)` with line width 1 strokes a band centred at 0.5, i.e. the **second** cell of the border column - sampling at `ox` reads the background colour and looks like "the ring was never drawn" (the first test in this batch failed exactly that way). Sample at `ox+1`, or scan a column first to find where the ring actually is.
- **Out-of-bounds clicks never reach the widget, so they cannot test clamping**: `dispatch_pointer` hit-tests first (`rect.contains`), so an event whose x exceeds the widget width is not delivered to it at all and the `clamp` inside the handler is untested. "Clicking the far right still yields the last star" must use the rightmost point **inside** the bounds (x=120 for a 126-wide rate); to exercise the outside path use `dispatch_pointer_to(id, ev)` (which is what the host's pointer capture does).
- **`on_change` fires at the interaction point only**: master hooks the callback into `value.subscribe`, so external writes also call back; this stack standardises on "the callback is the user's action" (same as the date pickers) - external writes only repaint. Both semantics are defensible, mixing them makes callers think their own write came back as a callback, hence the note.
- **A form item's error line reserves its height permanently**: the error row is a fixed 18px (present even when empty), so appearing/vanishing text causes no layout jump - proved in the real window by the `NATIVE-APP-FORM item=` rectangle being identical before and after clicking the "danger and report" button. The text itself is `label_dyn(err.signal())`: the Store's signal feeds the label directly and `relabel` re-measures in place, no subtree rebuild.
- **Button width comes from a different place than in master**: master measures the text at mount (`get_bounds_for`) and sets `width = text + 32`, then centres manually; here text measurement already feeds the layout engine (`attach_text_measure` + 12px side padding), so the width is a layout result and painting only centres inside the box - that manual measurement is gone.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **90/90** (5 new: button skin resolution (four variants x hover, plus explicit colour swallowing the hover state); themed button width follows the text, a release outside the box is not a click, the focus ring is painted only while focused, disabled consumes nothing; link width equals text width, hover shows the underline, the callback fires on release; form item's fixed-width label, the reserved 18px error row, and an empty string measuring 0 wide that re-measures in place once set; rate star vertex geometry, click writes the value and calls back, hover only previews without writing, leaving clears the preview). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a form-family section, geometry output gained `NATIVE-APP-FORM`): injecting "click the solid button, the link, the danger button (writes the error text), then the 4th star" yields `btn=2 lnk=1 rate=4 ferr=1`, with `item=24,1475,245x39` identical to the run without clicks, exit code 0; the screenshot shows the four button variants (white on primary / primary on light / primary without a background / white on danger), the hovered link with its underline, right-aligned labels and five outlined stars.
- **Check the window height against the last geometry entry again**: this section sits at the very bottom and the page now reaches 1542, so with the 1500-tall window the rate row was outside (clicks did nothing); raising the window to 1660 hit it first try. Also mind the race between the 12s auto-quit and injection: sleeping until the timer expires leaves a destroyed window id and xdotool reports `BadWindow`.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - dropdown select and colour picker (`select_t`/`color_picker_t`, form family 13/15)

- **Overlay handles must be asked for liveness, not for non-zero**: `overlay_show_at` returns monotonically increasing ids that are **never reused**, and "click outside dismisses" is done by the layer - the component's own `open_id` is not reset along with it. So testing the open state as `open_id.val != 0` treats a **stale handle** as still open, which shows up as "after dismissing by clicking outside, the field needs two clicks to reopen" (the first one only clears the stale handle). Use `overlay_alive(open_id.val)` (internally: does `overlay_rect_of(id)` still exist); keeping the `!= 0` guard inside `close_pop` is harmless (hiding a stale id is a no-op). `popover_t`/`dropdown_menu` were written that way from the start; the four date pickers and this batch's two widgets used the weaker test, now unified, each with a "dismiss outside, then one click reopens" regression.
- **Painting one widget into a tight bitmap must cancel its absolute placement**: `w.paint(p)` translates by `rect.x/rect.y`, while the bitmap is only w×h - a popup row sits tens or hundreds of pixels down, so the drawing lands outside and every pixel assertion comes out equal (symptom: "the selected row looks identical to an unselected one"). Use `w.paint_at(p, w.rect.x, w.rect.y)`, which zeroes the translation and makes bitmap coordinates equal widget-local coordinates.
- **Wrap long popup lists in `scroll_v`**: the pane height follows the formula (`rows*28+8`, capped at 300), so any number of options stays inside the window; scroll geometry is still copied into the `ScrollState` on the first paint (see the scroll-container note), so headless tests must `relayout` once before clicking.
- **`container`'s `padding` only sets left/top**: that is the `Widget::set_padding(left, top)` contract, not CSS's four sides - the swatch container measures 176x72 rather than 176x80 and the bottom band comes from the pane's own fixed height. Reading it as four-sided padding misplaces a geometry assertion.
- **Three deliberate differences from master**: (i) the popup is a self-drawn pane on the overlay layer, not the native Popover, so "dismiss outside" is the layer's job and the component tracks nothing; (ii) master closes on field focus-out, here whether the field is focusable depends on the caller passing `focus_id`, and dismissal only happens via "outside click / clicking the field again"; (iii) the dropdown's clear button **does** call `on_change("")` (like master) while the date pickers' clear does not (also like master) - both kept, this is not an internal inconsistency.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **93/93** (3 new: dropdown geometry (pane 2px below the field, same width, 28px rows), picking writes the value and closes, the selected row's light background and its check mark appear in pixels, clearing calls back; dropdown "dismiss outside then one click reopens"; colour picker grid geometry (rows of 5+1, pane 176x80), the selected cell differing from an unselected one by more than 20 pixels (inner stroke + white check), clicking a cell writes the value and closes). The date-picker test gained the same stale-handle regression. Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (the form section in `examples/native-app` gained a select + colour row, geometry line gained `sel=`/`col=`, status line gained `sel= col= ovmax=`): injecting "open the dropdown, click the 2nd option, open the colour field, click the 2nd swatch, click the dropdown's x" yields `sel='选项乙/'` (picked then cleared), `col='#2E9E5B'`, `ovmax=1` (the overlay count is back to zero at exit, so the peak is sampled every tick to prove a layer really opened), exit code 0; the screenshot shows the 5x3 swatch grid below the field, painted over the content.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - multi-line text area (`ui/textarea.mbt`, form family 15/15)

- **`focus_id` is only a declaration; the host's focus stack must register it**: a widget carrying `focus_id` merely says "focusable", and `App` **does not change focus on press if that id is not in the stack** (that is what the comment in `core/app.mbt` means). The symptom is misleading: **clicking into the box works and the caret is painted (clicks go through the pointer path, independent of focus), yet typing does nothing**. The four new widgets in the example (solid button / dropdown / colour picker / text area, ids 21..24) all needed `app.focus.register(id)` before they accepted the keyboard.
- **The right way to inject keys with xdotool**: `xdotool key --window WID ...` uses XSendEvent, which GTK drops - producing the illusion "mouse injection lands, keyboard injection does nothing". Do `xdotool windowactivate --sync WID` + `xdotool windowfocus WID` first, then call `key`/`type` **without `--window`** (XTEST delivers to the focused window) and the events arrive.
- **A Chinese input method swallows letters and arrow keys**: in the real window `type "cd"` never reached the box (the letters went to fcitx to build a preedit) while `Return` got through. The tell is whether a preedit appears; to prove ASCII typing, switch the IM to English mode first (query it read-only with `fcitx5-remote -n` - **never `-t`, which toggles**). The text area's keyboard semantics are therefore covered mainly by white-box tests (`dispatch_key` straight in), with the real window proving click-to-line, Enter-inserts-newline and wheel scrolling.
- **Vertical motion picks "the rightmost codepoint boundary not past x"**: written as a strict `<` at first, moving up between two equal-length lines landed one character short (measured caret=1 instead of 2). Matching the single-line box's `byte_at_x` is what is correct.
- **Guard the end of text when walking back to a codepoint start**: `while at > ln.start && (bs[at] & 0xc0) == 0x80` reads out of bounds when `at == text length`, and the symptom is the test driver reporting only `SIGABRT (core dumped)` with no assertion in the trace. Add an `at < n` check.
- **Do not use `j = hi` as a `break` inside the unit loop**: in `wrap_units` the inner while "escaped" by pushing the cursor to the end, which swallowed the whole line into one unit - externally: **nothing ever wraps**. Use an explicit `let mut go = true` flag.
- **Caret following looks like clipping**: when the caret lands on the last half-visible line the whole block moves up (measured 16px) and the first line is cut in half - that is `ensure_visible` working, not a paint bug (do not report it as one when reviewing screenshots).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **98/98** (5 new: wrap units (spaces / latin words / CJK per codepoint, and a range cut short); display-line layout (split on newlines, empty text still yields one line, greedy wrap that leaves no leading space, lines that abut, CJK wrapping into three lines, an over-long word that overflows instead of breaking); typing plus Enter, up/down moving across display lines while remembering x, Home/End at line bounds, replacing a Shift+Home selection, backspace at line start joining lines; click landing on the right line (CJK byte offsets), drag-select across lines deleting everything, read-only refusing edits; scrollbar appearing only when content exceeds the viewport, thumb and track swapping at the bottom, and the top band changing once the caret is scrolled into view). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (the form section in `examples/native-app` gained a text area, geometry line gained `ta=`, status line gained `ta=`/`tacur=`, example content raised to 8 lines): injecting "click the 3rd line inside the box (window y=1644)" yields `tacur=12` (that line's first byte, matching hand calculation), injecting `Return` yields `ta='行 1/行 2//行 3/…'` with `tacur=13` (one extra empty line), wheel-up returns to the top; the screenshot shows three full lines, the thumb back at the top of the track, and the 16px caret-following shift in the intermediate frame.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - declarative layer L1 constructors (`ui/declarative.mbt`: vbox/hbox/bind_label/hover_group/mount_window…)

- **`hover_group` needs no polling machinery in this stack**: the old chain (`yue/declarative/hovergroup.mbt`) ran a shared 100ms pointer-poll because GTK pointer events do not bubble - a child's GdkWindow owns them and the ancestor container never sees enter. Here the host's hover state is already **parent-chain** semantics (a parent counts as hovered while the pointer is on a descendant, i.e. CSS `:hover`; see the tooltip note), so the component just reads `Widget.hovered`: no timer, no pointer query, no "skip work when nothing flipped" optimisation to build. Recorded as a **mechanism difference**, not as "same feature implemented".
- **`bind_label`'s forced re-layout is free on the new stack**: master's `set_text` only dirties paint, so a growing label got clipped unless `update_layout` was called; here text changes go through `relabel` (reset the measure callback → node dirty) and the next layout pass follows the new text. Real-window proof: one button click takes the label from 36px to 87px (`lblw=36/87`) with no subtree rebuilt.
- **`mount_window` offers no `center` / `on_close`**: those ride native window channels. This stack lets the WM place the window and closing merely returns from `App::run()`, so post-run cleanup belongs to the caller. Fewer honest parameters beat two fake ones.
- **No second wrapper around same-named components**: `label`/`button`/`entry`/`checkbox`/`container`/`group`/`separator`/`image`/`icon`/`radio_group`/`slider`/`progress` already are declarative constructors returning `@core.Node` in `ui`; aliasing them again would only add signatures to maintain. This batch adds three kinds only: renames (`vbox`/`hbox`/`text_edit`/`picker`/`scroll`/`tab`/`date_picker`), foundation entries (`mount_window`, `bind`/`bind_label`) and one new capability (`hover_group`). `overlay_scroll` is simply `scroll_v` here (its scrollbar has always been an absolutely positioned child of the viewport), `gif` comes with the icon/animation batch, and `cursor_group` needs a window-level cursor channel (new FFI) so it is its own batch.
- **Three toolchain spellings (measured this batch)**: (i) `test "name"` and its opening brace **must be on the same line** - split across lines and the parser reads the body as a separate top-level block, producing error sites dozens of lines away that make no sense (`The value identifier tabs is unbound` + `Parse error, unexpected token id`); (ii) `Option::is_none()` is deprecated in this toolchain (it suggests `x is None`); (iii) match guards are not `v when cond` - MoonBit writes `| cond`, and a simple two-way branch is clearer as `if/else`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **103/103** (5 new: vbox/hbox take direction and gaps from parameters; bind_label re-measures in place when the Store changes and paints the new text; bind follows a derived signal and unsubscribes with the bag; hover_group flips the whole card's background when a child is hovered and does not count a release outside it; the renamed constructors really land on the new components - picker opens a popup and writes back, scroll produces a scrollbar with max>0, tab switches pages through the Store). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a declarative section, geometry line `NATIVE-APP-DC`, status line `dcl=`/`lblw=`, window raised 1760→1900 as the page grew again): injecting "click the 加一 button in the card" yields `dcl=1 lblw=36/87` (one Store write moves both text and width) and `label=34,1377,87x19`; parking the pointer on the card's blank area and screenshotting shows the whole card in its hover background.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - window-level cursor channel (`wm_set_cursor` + `Widget.cursor` + `cursor_group`)

- **In a fully self-drawn stack the cursor is a window-level thing**: with no native widgets there is no "every widget carries its own cursor"; the shape can only be set on the drawing area's `GdkWindow`. So the chain is: a component declares a **request** on its widget (`Widget.cursor`, codes `@core.CURSOR_*`: 0 default / 1 hand / 2 text I-beam / 3 resize-horizontal / 4 resize-vertical) → the host, on `Move`, takes **the deepest non-zero request along the hover path** → and only calls the FFI (`Window::set_cursor`) when that differs from what is already applied. "Deepest non-zero" rather than "deepest" is what makes a wrapping `cursor_group` fall back naturally: children that request nothing inherit the group's shape, children that do request win.
- **One mechanism difference from master**: master's `cursor_group` had to apply the cursor **recursively to the group root and every descendant**, because under GTK the cursor follows the native event window and a container alone cannot cover children that own one. Here the group sets itself only; keeping a distinct shape for a child means that child makes its own request (and then it wins).
- **Two guards on the C side**: `gtk_widget_get_window(area)` is NULL before realization (nothing to attach the cursor to - skip it, the next hover retries), and `WmWindow.cursor_kind` remembers the applied shape so repeated requests do not allocate another `GdkCursor` (creating one per hover flicker is pure waste). An unknown name (theme missing the glyph) falls back to `default`.
- **Two toolchain notes**: (i) `pub(all) enum PointerKind` does **not** come with `Eq`, so `pev.kind == Move` fails to compile (`Type PointerKind does not implement trait Eq`) - write a `match`; (ii) fields of a `pub(all) struct Rect` are public too, so tests can read `w.rect.x` directly without a getter.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **62/62** (1 new `cursor_at`: inside a group it falls back to the group, a child's own request wins, outside the group and on empty space both return the default); `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **104/104** (1 new: button / themed button / hover group ask for the hand, `entry`/`textarea` for text, `hsplit`/`vsplit` handles for the two resize shapes, `cursor_group` can override the shape while its children stay default). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` samples the shape the host applied on every tick, status line gained `cur=hand/text/horiz/vert`): injecting "park over the solid button, then the text area, then the split handle, then blank ground" yields `cur=8/8/8/0` (the first three shapes each sampled for 8 ticks; the vertical one is 0 because the example has no `vsplit`), exit code 0. **The actual cursor appearance (does the hand look right, is the text shape an I-beam) is for the user to confirm on the machine** - injection only proves the host dispatched different codes per region.
- **A disabled widget's cursor request must be computed at hit time, never frozen at build time (real-machine feedback: hovering a disabled button still showed the hand)**: `Widget.cursor` is a **one-shot `Int` written while building the tree**, whereas disabled state here is a **cell** (`Enabled` - the established rule in `state.mbt`: encoding it as a bool and freezing it at build time makes later `set_disabled` calls unable to reach the already-fixed field). `button()`/`icon_button_t()` set `CURSOR_HAND` unconditionally, so enabled and disabled were indistinguishable on the cursor face. The fix is not "give every component a way to recompute its cursor" but making the request itself computable on demand: `Widget` gains `cursor_fn : Option[(Widget) -> Int]`, which **takes precedence over the static `cursor`**; `cursor_at` now evaluates each node through the new public method `Widget::cursor_request()` (returning 0 still means "this widget makes no request", so the query keeps walking up and the `cursor_group` group request keeps working). `ui/state.mbt` adds the private helper `enabled_cursor(cell, on)`: `CURSOR_DEFAULT` when disabled, `on` when enabled. Touched: `button`/`icon_button_t` in `ui/basic.mbt`, and `checkbox`/`switch_t`/`slider`/`button_t` in `ui/form.mbt` (the stepper's two sides go through `button()`, so they are covered automatically).
- **Lesson: when changing a field's semantics, grep the repo for assertions on that field, not just implementation-side call sites** - `ui/declarative_wbtest.mbt:151` asserts on the button's **static** `cursor` and went red immediately (`0 != 1`); the user caught it by running `moon test core ui`. A grep over implementation-side call sites cannot find it (the assertion writes `.cursor`, unrelated to where the component is constructed). Checklist for this kind of change: write sites -> assertions reading the field -> the field's public accessor (adding `cursor_request()` here is exactly about giving assertions a semantically correct entry point).
- **Verification**: `moon test core ui` **243/243** (1 new in core: "with `cursor_fn` present the computed value wins over the static `cursor`, and flipping state on the same tree takes effect on the very next query"; 1 new in ui: "a button's cursor follows the disabled cell - disabled asks for no hand, re-enabling restores it immediately"; 1 pre-existing `declarative_wbtest` assertion corrected). Real-machine re-check: hovering a disabled button shows the plain arrow, and it turns into the hand the moment the widget becomes usable again.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - editable combo box (`combo`, closing out the declarative L1 set)

- **Composition: all editing semantics live in the inner `entry`; this component only adds the "candidates" layer.** Filtering follows the text (the `entry`'s `on_change` re-opens the popup), Enter takes the highlighted row via the `entry`'s `on_enter` hook, while Up/Down/Escape arrive through **key bubbling along the ancestor chain**: the `entry` does not consume those three function keys (the kernel's `handle_key` falls through to `keysym_to_text`, gets None, leaves `handled=false`, and the host keeps asking upwards), so the wrapping `combo.on_key` is the one that sees them. This is the first place the stack really uses "ask the widget first, then the ancestors" as a division of labour, worth recording: a component that wants keys its child ignores needs no new callback parameter on the child - the parent's `on_key` already is that channel.
- **Opens on release (same pattern as popover/dropdown)**: the `entry` consumes the press (it must place the caret) and only the release bubbles to the wrapper, so clicking anywhere in the field means "place caret + show candidates" and no separate arrow hot zone is needed.
- **Highlighting does not rebuild the popup**: a row's selected state is a live closure (`fn() { hi.val == idx }`), so Up/Down only move `hi` and `invalidate()`; only a changed filter result re-opens the layer (`close()` + `overlay_show_at`).
- **Popup ids increase monotonically - miscounting them in a test crashes it**: every `overlay_show_at` mints a fresh id (closing does not recycle), so an assertion about "the Nth opening" must count openings, not current layers. Count wrong and `overlay_root_of(missing id)` returns None, the helper hands back an empty widget, and the next `children()[0]` is out of bounds - the symptom is the test driver reporting only `SIGABRT (core dumped)` with no assertion in the trace (hit once this batch).
- **One difference from master**: master's combo was the native editable combobox (GTK `ComboBoxEntry`); here it is fully self-drawn - the popup lives on the overlay layer and outside-click dismissal is again the layer's job.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **105/105** (1 new: clicking the field opens the full list -> `dispatch_commit` of "甲" filters to two rows and re-opens -> the highlighted row's light background and check mark appear in pixels and Down moves the highlight to the other row -> Enter writes the highlighted value, closes and calls back -> reopening leaves a single row and Escape closes without changing the value -> an empty filter result opens nothing). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app`'s dropdown row gained a `combo`, geometry line `cmb=`, status line `cmb='…'`, focus id 25 registered): injecting "click the field, click the 3rd option" yields `cmb='组件丙'`, exit code 0; a four-click run "open, pick 2nd, click the field again, click where the old 3rd row was" stays at `cmb='组件乙'` - on the second opening the current text is already 组件乙, the filter leaves one row, so a click at the old row-3 position falls outside the popup by design (that is the filtering working, not a lost click).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - scaled drawing fix and GIF animation (`cr_blit` rewrite + `Animation` + `gif`/`icon_button_t`)
