- **The scaling path through `cairo_pattern_set_matrix` was wrong (a real bug fixed this batch)**: the old code put the "source pixel → device pixel" map into the pattern matrix (`translate(d0,d1)` + `scale(k)`), but Cairo's semantics are the opposite of that intuition - with an opaque 80×80 box at (20,20)-(99,99) inside a 120×120 source scaled to 72, the pattern-matrix route produced (33,33)-(71,71) (pushed to the bottom-right of the target and clipped away) while the CTM route (`identity` → `translate` → `scale` → `set_source_surface` → fill the size of the sub-rect) produced the correct (12,12)-(59,59). **At equal source/destination size the two agree**, which is why the bug survived: no test ever covered the scaled branch of `draw_image`/`draw_canvas`. The diagnostic order is worth copying: first prove with a **fully opaque synthetic image** that something is drawn (2304 cells), then switch to a source **with transparent regions** to reproduce the misplacement, then lift the whole blit into a standalone C program and compare three formulations (parent + matrix / CTM / sub-surface + matrix) - located in one pass.
- **`moon run` artifacts are not relinked after a stub-library change either** (this trap was previously recorded only for test executables): `build/librender_cairo_stub.a` was 13:50 while `native-app.exe` was still 13:39 - MoonBit sources were untouched, so moon considered the product current, and the result was green white-box tests with a blank GIF in the real window. Delete `_build/native/debug/build/NoahLiu/native-gui/examples/<name>` (note that `moon run`'s executable lives under `build/`, not `execbin/`). **The check**: compare the mtimes of the `.a` and the `.exe`; "it just ran" does not mean it is new.
- **The GdkPixbuf animation iterator starts lazily and only honours absolute time**: the first `gdk_pixbuf_animation_iter_advance(iter, t)` merely anchors the animation's start at `t` and always reports "no change"; afterwards whether a frame changes is entirely the loader's decision against accumulated time. So this layer's virtual clock advances **only "current frame duration +1ms" per call** (keeping pace with real time) and the component keeps asking on its `delay_ms` rhythm. An earlier version pushed four steps per call, which made the animation run four times too fast.
- **This machine's gif loader exposes no `tDelay`** (measured NULL, though PIL reports 400ms) → the layer falls back to a 100ms poll; because the loader decides when to flip, frames still change every 400ms (the real window counted `gif=30` frames in 12 seconds ≈ 2.5 fps, matching the asset).
- **`loop` and `static` are both MoonBit reserved words**: a `loop~` parameter fails to parse (renamed `looping~`) and a `static` struct field triggers the `reserved_keyword` warning (renamed `is_static`).
- **Where to sample an icon button's hover background**: a `fill_round_rect` with radius 4 clips the corner cell, so pixel assertions must sample the top-middle (`(w/2, 2)`) - `(1,1)` reads "nothing changed".
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/render` **42/42** (1 new scaling regression: the same source's opaque bounding box at target sizes 120/72/48 must be (20,20)-(99,99)/(12,12)-(59,59)/(8,8)-(39,39), plus `draw_image_from_rect` not bleeding outside its sampling rect); `moon test -p .../gui/ui` **107/107** (3 new: the gif component advances on its own, pauses break the chain, `play` resumes, static images never schedule - and the 48×48 scaled instance really has ink; icon button size, hover background, release-outside-is-not-a-click, disabled consumes nothing); `core` 62/62, `yoga-mbt` 96/96, nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained an icon/GIF section, geometry line `NATIVE-APP-MEDIA`, status fields `gif=`/`ib=`): injecting "click the icon button" yields `ib=1` and `gif=30` self-advanced frames, exit code 0; the screenshot shows the theme-coloured icon, the ✕ icon button, and the 48×48 GIF dot actually painted (previously blank).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - following the system theme (`theme_from_system` + `App::follow_system_theme`) and the style-channel decision

- **The theme package stays platform-free; the following logic lives in the window layer**: `theme` takes the two readings as parameters (`theme_from_system(sys_color_scheme, sys_accent)`), the probe is in `win`, and the polling plus `theme_apply` are in `core/app.mbt` (`App::follow_system_theme(interval_ms)` returns the stop function). The theme package therefore keeps zero platform dependencies (so `core` depending on it cannot cycle), and the "follow" policy is testable head-lessly with fake readers in four states. Same reasoning as `local_ymd`: platform probes are re-exported through core so examples and the integration layer never import `win`.
- **What is read is the desktop's gsettings, not a native widget stylesheet**: `org.gnome.desktop.interface`'s `color-scheme` first (a value containing dark/light counts), falling back to the `gtk-theme` name (case-normalised, searching dark/light); the accent comes from `org.gnome.desktop.wm.preferences button-settings` (when it literally holds `#rrggbb`) or `accent-color` (GNOME stores a **name** like blue/teal, translated through a table). **`g_settings_schema_has_key` must gate the read**: this machine's schema has no `accent-color` key at all, and calling `g_settings_get_string` on a missing key emits a GLib critical. GSettings objects are built once and kept (recreating them per poll would hammer dconf).
- **Measured on this machine (Ubuntu 24.04 + XFCE)**: the GNOME schemas are installed (38 `org.gnome.desktop.*`), `color-scheme='default'` (neither dark nor light → falls to `gtk-theme='Orchis-Teal-Light-Compact'` → contains light after normalising → reports "light"), and there is no `accent-color` → empty accent. So `theme_from_system` lands on "light + built-in primary", identical to the default palette; the real-window status line confirms `sys='light/'` and `fing=#2D68C4/#F5F6F7`. **The rule**: the follow loop only calls `theme_apply` when the **fingerprint** (`primary/bg_page`) changes, so a desktop that reports nothing computes the same theme every tick and repaints nothing.
- **Style-channel decision: no CSS-shaped style table in the new stack**. The five master helpers (`themed_container`/`set_panel_bg`/`bind_bg`/`bind_fg`/`bind_popover_bg`) exist because native widgets hold imperatively-set colours that do not follow a theme switch; here components read the palette live inside `on_draw` and a theme switch repaints through one process-level subscription (`watch_theme`), so those helpers are **structurally unnecessary**. Callers override geometry or colours through two more direct routes: typed parameters (`width=`/`bg=`/`padding=`…) or `handle=fn(w){ ... }` to reach the underlying widget's yoga style - the latter is this stack's style escape hatch, and it is type-safer than maintaining a string table. `sub_bag` batch unsubscription already exists in `core/signal.mbt` and is exercised by the component batches (rebuild-style lists unsubscribe a whole generation).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/theme` **8/8** (1 new: the fingerprint relations across the four scheme/accent states - unreadable equals light, dark differs from light, a given accent goes through the derivation and matches `theme_from_accent`, and after `theme_apply_system` both the getters and the fingerprint follow the new snapshot); `ui` 107/107, `core` 62/62, `render` 42/42, `yoga-mbt` 96/96, nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` starts `follow_system_theme(interval_ms=1000)` and stops it at 3s to check the stop function's ownership): status line gained `sys='light/'` and `fing=#2D68C4/#F5F6F7`, `frames=118` with all other readings unchanged, exit code 0. **Whether the UI actually recolours when the system theme flips is for the user to verify** (e.g. temporarily `gsettings set org.gnome.desktop.interface color-scheme prefer-dark`); the assistant does not change desktop settings on its own.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - first chart batch (`chart`: algorithm layer + line/bar/donut/gauge/scatter)

- **A new package rather than editing `yue/charts` in place**: the old directory references the `@yue` root package and the old Painter contract - it belongs to the repo-wide red-point backlog, and the per-package `--deny-warn` gate is meaningless if the two are mixed (same reasoning as `yue/components` → `ui`). Three layers in the new package: `algo.mbt` (pure functions, same source as master: rolling window, coordinate mapping, 1-2-5 ticks, unit scaling, bar slots and hit-testing, sector angles, gauge arcs, least squares, range padding, truncation), `draw.mbt` (painting, consuming only the pure results), `components.mbt` (`@core.Node` components).
- **The pixel-assertion yardstick changes**: chart components paint a panel background first, so "ink pixels (alpha>0)" always equals the whole area - all five new tests failed for exactly this reason on the first run. The correct check is **pixel-by-pixel difference between two bitmaps** (`diff_of`), used both for "the picture changed after new data" and for "it restored after leaving" (the latter asserts the difference is exactly 0). This applies to every component that fills a background before drawing, not just charts.
- **Dense line cost is decoupled from window size** (keeping master's decision): decimate per pixel column, each column keeping (x, y_min, y_max); in area mode fill one rectangle per column down to the anchor (the fill's top edge *is* the line, no separate stroke), in line mode draw a min..max vertical bar. Only the sparse case (points <= columns) draws a real polyline plus polygon area. Rationale: about 1.8µs per stroked segment under software rasterisation, so 1000 points × 4 series exceeds the 5ms acceptance line.
- **Donut hit-testing is new (`donut_hit`)**: polar test against the same centre/radius formulas as `draw_donut`, with the hub and everything outside the ring returning -1; bar hit-testing still uses master's `bar_hit` (shared slot formula with the drawing, otherwise highlight and bars disagree).
- **Reuse `ui`'s foundations instead of copying**: `follow_store`/`watch_theme`/`text_width`/`fill_round_rect`/`default_handle` were made `pub` for the chart package. Duplicating them would have meant re-copying `watch_theme`'s "subscribe exactly once per process", which is precisely the part that must not be duplicated.
- **Four toolchain spellings (measured this batch)**: (i) `pub const` names **must be upper case** (`pub const pi` fails with "Expected upper case identifier for name of const... Did you mean let?") - lower-case constants use `pub let`; (ii) `for i = 1..<n { }` is invalid - ranges only appear in `for x in a..<b`, so a C-style loop needs `for i = 1; i < n; i = i + 1`; (iii) `StringBuilder` writes through `write_string`/`write_char`, there is no `add`/`append`; (iv) the `Ordering` type cannot be referenced in this toolchain (a comparator function fails with "The type Ordering is undefined"), so sorting a handful of items falls back to insertion sort.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **11/11** (6 pure-function tests: window eviction and out-of-range no-ops, degenerate ranges and padding ratios, 1-2-5 steps and negative starting tick, k/M/G unit scaling, bar slots and both hit orientations, sectors accumulating only positives with the 0.0002 rad gap for a single slice, gauge band colours and ring step counts, regression degeneracies, 8-digit colour strings, label truncation; 5 component tests: line repaint on new data and dense decimation, bar hover highlight and restore, donut pixels plus four hit cases, gauge both smoothing paths, scatter with a trend line). Nine packages `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a chart section - line + bar in one row, donut + gauge + scatter + a push button in the next, geometry line `NATIVE-APP-CH`, status fields `ln=`/`chp=`): injecting "click the push button three times" yields `ln=27/23 chp=3` (window 24→27 points, last value 7→23, matching `pushes*7%29+2` exactly), exit code 0; the screenshot shows all five charts - area-filled polyline with the latest value labelled at the right, bars with positive/negative colours and category labels, donut with centre total and a percentage legend, gauge at 68%, scatter with a least-squares trend line.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - sys batch 3 (hardware family: brightness / volume / nightlight / printer / powerprofile / monitor)

- **Dependency injection instead of direct DBus coupling**: brightness's write path (logind availability probe + SetBrightness) went through `@traybus` on master; the new stack turns both into **process-level injection slots** - `brightness_set_logind_probe(()->Bool)` and `brightness_set_action((subsystem,name,value)->String)` (empty string on success, error text otherwise), with the DBus line injecting the real implementation once traybus lands. The read path (device enumeration, current/max values, pure sysfs) does not touch the slots. Same inversion as `ci_tooltip`'s callbacks: **the semantic layer defines the slot, the platform layer injects**.
- **monitor moved up from the session family**: nightlight's display enumeration depends on monitor (xrandr), and monitor itself only needs platform + pr_run with no DBus - ordered by dependency, it joins this batch.
- **Three files deferred to the DBus line**: `sensor` (iio-sensor-proxy) and `bluetooth` (org.bluez) read properties through `@traybus`'s generic D-Bus layer; `disk` (UDisks2) has fifty `@traybus.gdbus_call_system` call sites - their core is bus calls, not command lines, so they were not forcibly split.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **41/41** (11 new: monitor's xrandr parsing matrix plus the six files' pr_run command surfaces and parsing pure functions migrated same-source; brightness's set path reports Unsupported before injection while the read path hits real sysfs on this machine); `moon check sys --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. **Real-machine checklist**: volume steps and mute, brightness current-value read, powerprofile list and switch, printer enumeration, nightlight colour temperature taking effect, monitor mode list.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - sys batch 2 (`sys/dialog.mbt`: file dialogs)

- **GTK side lives in the win stub, semantics in the sys package**: the `GtkFileChooserDialog` wrapper goes into `win/gtk_stub.c` (`wm_file_*` prefix, slot table matching the window stub - the dialog needs a GTK parent window, and that file is already compiled into the GTK link unit by prebuild); `sys/dialog.mbt` is a thin semantic shell (filters string passthrough, option bits, run/result/destroy). The sys package **does not import win** - `run_for_window`'s parent is a plain handle id (Int64) with no MoonBit-level symbol dependency, but linking still needs `-lyue_win_stub`, which `scripts/prebuild.py` now **emits separately for the sys package** (same GTK library list as win).
- **Querying an un-run chooser segfaults (measured)**: `gtk_file_chooser_get_uri` crashes with SIGSEGV while the chooser's internal file-system model is uninitialised - the stub keeps a `wm_dlg_accepted` flag and only allows the query after `gtk_dialog_run` returned ACCEPT, returning an empty string otherwise. Same-family GTK assertion: `set_current_name` (set_filename) is only legal on a SAVE action; calling it on an OPEN dialog raises Gtk-CRITICAL.
- **Headless degradation path**: `wm_file_dialog_new` runs `gtk_init_check` first and returns a 0 handle on failure (wbtests have no X connection; CI) - every interface no-ops safely on handle 0; with a display (this machine) real objects are built and the test asserts both paths.
- **Third crime scene for "stub changes don't relink"**: after editing gtk_stub.c, `moon test` reported undefined references - `build/libyue_win_stub.a`'s mtime was newer than the source (and the test artifacts weren't relinked either); the criterion is comparing `build/*.a` vs the test exe mtime and deleting both `_build/native/debug/test/.../gui/sys` and the stub library to force a rebuild. Same root as the G-batch stub lessons: moon's incremental build simply cannot be trusted for C source changes.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **30/30** (1 new: dialog build/configure/destroy on both paths - with a display, real objects configured then destroyed; headless, all interfaces no-op; the modal run and real file picking are left for real-machine verification); `moon check sys --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. **Real-machine checklist**: open a dialog in-app → pick a file → the result path is correct; the save dialog honours the initial name; the filters dropdown groups correctly.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - sys batch 1 (`sys`: fsx / envx / procrun)

- **The sys stub goes straight through native-stub**: directory listing (dirent), environment variables (setenv/unsetenv), text I/O (fopen) and file removal need only standard headers - **unlike** the win/render/text stubs' "prebuild static library plus portable placeholder" routine, which exists because those need GTK headers (native-stub sees no pkg-config paths). A single `sys_stub.c` branches per platform at the top via `#if` (the Windows branch is a placeholder; the FindFirstFileW wide-char routing waits for M2), and `moonbit_make_bytes` links against the runtime symbol via a bare `extern` declaration (same as ime_probe_stub).
- **`sys_getenv` goes through the C-layer getenv, not core/env**: "readable immediately after an in-process setenv" is master's test contract, and core/env's table is a startup snapshot in some implementations - reads needing live semantics go through the stub, while plain reads can still use core/env. The stub also carries `sys_read/write_text_file`, `sys_remove_file` (idempotent) and `sys_platform()` (compile-time `#if`, the equivalent of master's `@yue.platform()`).
- **procrun only swapped its dependency surface**: `@yue.read_text_file/ffi_remove_file/platform` became this package's `sys_*` trio; `@subproc` (chensuiyi/subproc) was already in the main module's dependencies, and `pr_run`'s spawn → timed reaping → temp-file capture → C locale pipeline moved over verbatim.
- **Migration pitfalls**: the old wbtests' `utf8_bytes/from_utf8_bytes` (root-package helpers) became `@utf8.encode/decode_lossy`; after replacing the `ffi_getenv_bytes` out-parameter `ok` Ref assertion pattern with the Option-returning `sys_getenv`, the neighbouring `inspect(ok.val)` lines must go too (changing a signature without cleaning its assertions explodes at compile time).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **29/29** (fsx: real directory listing with temp files visible, `..` excluded, open-failure branch, flat-text decoding, in-process write-read-modify-delete round trip with overwrite=false preservation, idempotent unset, `a=b` rejection; envx: point reads cross-checked against core/env, the common set only collecting what exists with a full XDG_* sweep, error branches; procrun: `pr_available`/`pr_run` real subprocess round trips - `echo`, non-zero-exit stderr capture, timeout kill, the `/bin/sh` script path). `moon check sys --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - Markdown view (new package `mdview`; component library complete)

- **A new package, not the old directory**: master's `yue/markdown` depends on the `@yue` root package (red-point area), so the new stack lands at `mdview` per the one-folder-one-package convention (`mizchi/markdown@0.8.3` is already in the main module's dependencies - the new package just imports it). The pure-function layer (flattening/range accounting/footnote scanning/line splitting) migrated with zero diffs; the rendering layer switched component surfaces (`@ui.hbox/vbox/label/separator/checkbox/code_view`, `@render.Image`, `@text.AttributedText`).
- **Link opening became an `on_link` callback**: master's rich-text line click called `@yue.open_url` directly; in the new stack open_url belongs to the system-capability line (desktop-integration family pending), so `markdown_view` gained an `on_link? : (String) -> Unit` parameter threaded through `MDCtx` into the rich-text-line closure - **the first draft used a process-wide single-slot forwarder, changed to ctx threading because multiple instances would overwrite each other** (applying a struct's function field needs `(ctx.on_link)(u)`; plain `ctx.on_link(u)` fails with "has no method").
- **The rich-text line follows the theme via fingerprint caching**: rebuilding the AttributedText in every on_draw is unacceptable (animation frames rebuild endlessly), while building once at construction leaves link/code colours stale - `on_draw` compares `@theme.theme_fingerprint_of(theme_current())` and rebuilds only when the fingerprint changed.
- **Block-spacing strategy changed**: master's blocks each carried marginBottom (style strings); the new stack's `@ui.hbox/vbox` only take four-side padding, no padding_bottom - inter-block spacing is delegated to the outermost `vbox`'s `row_gap`, with individual bottom margins going through `node.set_margin_bottom(@yoga.fixed(...))`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/mdview` **26/26** (24 pure-function/flattening/range/GFM assertions migrated same-source - UTF-16 accounting/emoji, footnote numbering order, reference-link definitions, strikethrough/superscript ranges, fence info first word, line splitting, `file://` stripping, table alignment mapping rewritten as `is`-pattern assertions (yoga enums lack Eq); 2 mounted components - a full-GFM document (headings/bold-italic-strike/links/task lists/ordered/quote/code block/table/rule/definition list/footnotes) yields >5 child nodes with content-driven height, and the link-click callback does not crash); `moon check mdview --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. The real-window geometry line confirms layout (`md=24,2745,420x403`); visual feel left for the user to verify on a real machine. **With this the ten component families plus Markdown are fully closed out.**

### MoonBit native GUI stack - code_view syntax highlighting (`ui/codeview.mbt`)

- **Tokenizer migrated verbatim, component reshaped**: `Token`/`lang_keywords` (eight-language keyword tables)/`tokenize_lang`/`classify_word` moved over from master's `components_text.mbt` with zero changes (the spellings were already on the new toolchain); `code_view` went from "AttributedText pre-layout + draw_attributed_text" to "per-token `@ui.text_width` measurement at construction time → per-segment `@text.draw_box_at` inside on_draw" - the old whole-run colouring trick existed to dodge the Windows AttributedText range-colour defect, which the self-drawn stack structurally never hits; line height comes from `@text.line_height_font`.
- **Per-pure-colour pixel counting is a fragile assertion**: a small 13px monospace glyph (e.g. the keyword "fn") can end up with **not a single pure-colour pixel** after sub-pixel antialiasing (this batch saw `purple=0` while a six-character CJK string scored `green=22`) - component pixel assertions now count "ink outside the background"; per-colour assertions remain reliable only for large colour areas (chart sectors/bands).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/ui` **110/110** (3 new: the tokenizer assertions migrated same-source - keywords/types/qualified names/calls/ranges/strings/comments/multi-language; mounted component - dark base at ninety percent, ink > 150, height linear in line count); `moon check ui --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. The real-window geometry line confirms layout (`code=24,2745,400x153`, moonbit with line numbers plus a python block); visual feel left for the user to verify on a real machine.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - specialised chart batch 4 (`chart`: effectscatter / themeriver / geo; chart line complete)

- **Two conflicting 8-digit colour semantics (measured this batch)**: the old chart convention is `#AARRGGBB` (alpha first, see the colour pitfalls entry), while the new stack's `@render.Color::parse` reads 8 digits as **RGBA (alpha last)**. Feeding an 8-digit input straight into `@theme.parse_hex` reads the alpha byte as red (`#ff009688` yields r=255 - the exact cause of `"#ffff0096" != "#ff009688"`); fix: **strip the leading two alpha digits and parse the trailing six** (preserving the old "RGB untouched" semantics), then re-synthesise alpha from the parameter.
- **The ripple animation moved from "callback returns false to unregister" to a real handle**: the old `set_timer` had no cancel id, so stopping relied on the callback returning false; the new `@core.set_timeout`/`clear_timeout` have real handles - `EffAnim` carries `running + timer`, and `eff_stop` cancels the pending callback immediately (the same re-arm pattern as the carousel: the callback schedules the next tick).
- **GeoJSON parsing goes through `@sys`** (`vsc_json_parse` already migrated into sys), `RectF` became a local `GeoRect` (same shape as treemap's `TmRect`, each self-defined); the old `argb_hex` lives in the red-point root package, so the chart package implements it locally (`Int::unsafe_to_char` for hex digits - `Char::from_int` is deprecated).
- **Three testing lessons (all hit this batch)**: (i) `ink_of` on a component that paints a panel background always equals the full area (the first batch's pitfall; the headless default theme's bg_panel is **pure white** `#FFFFFF`, and a white base still counts as ink) - assert content via **signature-colour sampling** or `diff_of`; (ii) themeriver reserves 240px for its legend, so the canvas must exceed 298 to have any plot area (component default width 560) - a narrow-canvas test silently draws nothing; (iii) **hand-written JSON in tests should be validated with an external parser first** - one extra `]` in a coordinate array made the parser honestly return None, which was first misread as a parser bug (when every fragment passes but the full string fails, suspect the string itself).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **189/189** (43 new: the three same-source wbtest files, 40 tests, plus 3 mounted components - ripple changes on new data and is_running goes false after eff_stop, the river changes on new series at width 560, the map parses GeoJSON and renders region plus flight then changes on clear); package `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. The real-window geometry line confirms layout (`eff=24,2542,300x170`); visual feel left for the user to verify on a real machine. **With this, the chart line - fifteen specialised charts, five general charts and the interaction layer - is fully closed out.**

### MoonBit native GUI stack - specialised chart batch 3 (`chart`: sunburst / graph / parallel / pictorialbar)

- **Same playbook as batch 2**: the conversion script handles dependency renames, with three classes of hand-written spots - the "no data" blocks, conditional-align `draw_text` calls (pictorialbar's four omit the `wrap=` parameter, which the regex demanded, so they were missed; calls that omit defaulted parameters also need hand-fixing), and the component `mount` blocks. The sunburst centre total (20px SemiBold plus the "总计" caption) uses the same `@text.draw_box_at` spelling as `draw_donut_core`.
- **Literal construction of `SunItem`/`TreeItem`/`TmItem` inside the package fails with "Mutiple possible record types"**: the three share the `{name, value, children}` shape so a literal cannot disambiguate - uniformly go through `make` plus `children.push` (array-field contents are mutable); same applies across packages (batch 2's pitfall recurred here and is now the standing rule).
- **Optional parameters cannot be passed positionally**: `GraphNode::make(name, value? : Double)` and `GraphEdge::make(s, t, weight? : Double)` reject `make("甲", 30.0)` with "requires 1 positional arguments" - always pass `value=30.0` / `weight=5.0` by name. `SunItem::make`'s `value? : Double?` is a double Option, so numbers need `Some(v)`.
- **graph's layout cache moved onto the recycle bag**: the old chain cleared the cache inside `subscribe` plus `schedule_paint`; in the new stack `follow_store` only requests repaints, so cache invalidation gets its own `subscribe_id` plus `sub_bag_register` unsubscribe (same pattern as gauge_t).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **146/146** (48 new: the four same-source wbtest files, 44 tests, plus 4 mounted components - sunburst changes on new data, the relation graph's layout cache invalidates on data change and the picture changes, parallel coordinates change on new rows, pictorial bars render in both orientations); package `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. The real-window geometry line confirms layout (`gph=282,2346,260x190`); visual feel left for the user to verify on a real machine.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - specialised chart batch 2 (`chart`: kline / sankey / treemap / tree)

- **Mechanical migration plus hand-rewritten spots**: the four files went through a conversion script for dependency renames (`@yue.*` → `@theme`/`@render`/`@core`/package-local functions), while the "no data" blocks, the conditional-align `draw_text`, and the component `mount` blocks were rewritten by hand - `draw_text`'s `align=` accepts arbitrary expressions (`align=if first_col { @text.End } else { @text.Start }`), which a literal-enum-matching converter misses; such passages are always hand-fixed.
- **treemap's `RectF` became a local `TmRect`**: the old chain reused the root package's `RectF` (x/y/width/height), while the new stack's core `Rect` spells its fields w/h and means a layout rect - the chart package carries its own same-shaped `TmRect` (15 replacements) instead of a cross-semantics dependency. `from_hsl` exists in `@theme.palette`.
- **`tree_chart_t`'s `orientation : String` became `vertical : Bool`**: the old string parameter ("horizontal"/"vertical") was a historical API shape; new-stack component parameters are always typed (matching `bar_chart_t`'s `horizontal?`).
- **TreeItem/TmItem construction across packages must go through `make`**: literal construction of a `pub(all) struct` outside its defining package fails with "There is no record definition with the fields" (hit while building tree data in native-app) - cross-package construction is uniformly `TreeItem::make(name, value=…)` plus `children.push(…)` (children is an array field; contents are mutable).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **98/98** (34 new: the four same-source wbtest files, 29 tests, plus 5 mounted-component tests - candlestick shows both up-red and down-green pixels, sankey renders and changes on new data, treemap hover highlights and restores via the flat hit ledger, tree renders in both orientations); package `--deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287/68. The real-window geometry line confirms layout (`NATIVE-APP-CHSP sankey=332,2190,300x150`); screenshot re-check was blocked: a 2500-tall window exceeds what XFCE lets you move (`windowmove -1346` got clamped to -1278, the target row's bottom stays off-screen and along the clamped path GTK loses the expose so the capture is an empty backing) - **visual feel left for the user to verify on a real machine** (second row of the native-app specialised-charts section).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - specialised chart batch 1 (`chart`: boxplot / funnel / heatmap / radar)

- **One file per chart, same-source migration**: master's `charts_boxplot.mbt`/`charts_funnel.mbt`/`charts_heatmap.mbt`/`charts_radar.mbt` each moved over as `boxplot.mbt`/`funnel.mbt`/`heatmap.mbt`/`radar.mbt` (pure-function layers verbatim + draw layers switched to `@render.Painter`/`@text.draw_box_at`/live `@theme` colours + component layers in the `node_of_new` shape), the four wbtest files moved with them plus one **mounted-pixel assertion each** (`ink_of > N` + data-change `diff_of > 50` + empty/degenerate input does not crash) - a "pure functions pass but the component never wired up" regression gets caught on the first run. The old `AttributedText` "no data" placeholders uniformly became the fixed-box `@text.draw_box_at` spelling (same as `draw.mbt`).
- **Every dependency already exists in the new stack**: `mix_hex`/`lighten_hex`/`parse_hex`/`rgb_to_hsl` in `@theme.palette`; `bar_slot`/`draw_hgrid`/`series_color`/`fmt_axis`/`truncate_to` reused from the package; `mut_view().sort_by(fn(a,b){ -1/0/1 })` works on this toolchain (an Int-returning comparator - unrelated to the un-referenceable `Ordering` pitfall).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **64/64** (27 new: boxplot quantile interpolation/five numbers/double-ended outliers/zero IQR, funnel stable sort/band geometry/trapezoid widths/percentages, heatmap range/colour-band interpolation/cell rects, radar angles/polygons/per-axis normalisation/layout + four mounted components); `moon check chart --deny-warn` clean, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained a specialised-charts section, window grown to 1400×2300, geometry line `NATIVE-APP-CHSP`): exit code 0; screenshot colour sampling finds all four charts - boxplot success box 68px + danger outlier dots 28px, funnel primary first band 3508px, heatmap light-primary 5773px + deep-primary 1944px (band gradient), radar both series 169/168px. **Visual feel for the user to verify on a real machine.**
- **Screenshot practice note**: when the window is moved too far off-screen, X clears the off-screen backing store (`import -window` captures uniform grey 242) - move the target section into view while **keeping most of the window visible** (client origin around -1200 for a 2300-tall window here) for pixel sampling to work.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - chart interaction layer (`chart`: tooltip + DataZoom/VisualMap/mark/export + clickable legend + three interactive `charts_it`)

- **Migration surface, file by file**: master's `charts_tooltip.mbt`/`charts_interactive.mbt`/`charts_it.mbt` moved over as `tooltip.mbt` (`CiTip` state with edge-clamped positioning, the `CiPlot` plot rect, nearest-point `ci_nearest_idx` and sector `ci_sector_at` hit tests, the `ci_tooltip` interactive canvas), `interactive.mbt` (the full `CiZoom` window math, `CiVisualMap` colour bands, `ci_mark_line`/`ci_mark_area`, `ci_save` offscreen export, legend hit/visibility tooling, the `ci_legend` component) and `charts_it.mbt` (`line_chart_it`/`bar_chart_it`/`donut_chart_it`); the three wbtest files moved with them.
- **Legend colours changed shape: store (name, original index) plus a `color_at` closure instead of colour strings.** Master freezes (name, colour-string) pairs into the Store, so a theme switch leaves every swatch stale until `on_theme_change` rebuilds the whole items list; the new `ci_legend` takes `color_at : (Int) -> String` and resolves colours inside `on_draw` (series colours, donut colours and bar positive/negative colours are all live theme lookups), so the process-wide `watch_theme` repaint picks up new colours automatically - **the `on_theme_change` rebuild chain is structurally unnecessary**. The bar sign-dependent colour closure captures the data Store and reads its current value.
- **The `on_scroll` callback signature has no widget self parameter** (`Option[(ScrollEvent) -> Bool]`, unlike `on_draw`/`on_pointer` where the self parameter exists precisely to avoid capture cycles). Mapping a wheel event to a zoom anchor needs the canvas size; the solution is a **size Ref created in the constructor closure, refreshed every frame by `on_draw` (which does get self) and read by `on_scroll`** - a visible widget has always painted at least one frame, so there is no window where (0, 0) could be read.
- **`ci_tooltip` gained an `on_leave` callback**: hiding the tooltip only clears `CiTip`, but the caller's own hover state (the line-chart crosshair/dots, the donut's expanded sector) is draw-only state outside `hit` and would linger - the first run of the "restore on leave" assertions failed (diff=276/1012) exactly because the crosshair and expansion kept painting. All three interactive components pass `on_leave=fn(){ hover.val = -1 }`.
- **Drag capture is no longer the component's business**: master's `set_capture`/`release_capture` is structurally unnecessary here - the host's `route_pointer` makes the hit widget the captor on `Down` and routes all subsequent `Move`/`Up` to it (no lost release when the pointer leaves).
- **`draw_donut` split into `draw_donut_core`** (sectors + centre total, colours via a `color_at` parameter): the interactive versions get their legend from `ci_legend` (clickable, hideable) and cannot reuse the embedded-legend `draw_donut`; `draw_donut` itself becomes a thin shell (core + embedded legend). The general `ci_sector_at` (thickness-parameterised) coexists with the first batch's fixed-thickness `donut_hit` - interactive donuts take a configurable thickness, and hit geometry must follow the draw geometry.
- **PNG export actually works on Linux in this stack** (`render/png.mbt` is a pure implementation, no platform shim): master's `ci_save` always failed off Windows (the shim never exposed an export API) so its wbtest could only cover format validation without touching disk; here the wbtest writes a real `/tmp` file and asserts `Ok`, and the offscreen `Painter` in `ci_save_chart` must be `end()`-ed when done (Cairo session slots are limited).
- **xdotool's XTest wheel injection does not reach GTK3 (measured this batch - a tooling limitation, not a code bug)**: `xdotool click 4/5` produces proper button 4/5 events at the X layer (`xev` sees them, synthetic NO), but GTK3's X11 backend goes through XInput2 and **never turns XTest core button events into scroll-events** - reproduced with a 20-line minimal GTK probe (scroll-event + fprintf only, nothing printed). The component chain for wheel zoom is covered by the wbtest's direct `dispatch_scroll` call (inject `dy:1` → window narrows → pixels change); **real-wheel feel is left for the user to verify on a real machine**. Programmatic wheel injection would need uinput (`/dev/uinput` exists, ydotool/evemu not installed).
- **Triple alignment for real-window screenshots (stepped on with 10px targets)**: (i) `xdotool mousemove --window` takes **client-relative coordinates** (same frame as GTK event coordinates, so injections need no conversion); (ii) when the window is taller than the screen, `import -window` captures only the **visible screen portion** with the screen - not the window - as origin (here 2080-tall window vs 1080-tall screen, window moved to negative Y, so screenshot y = client y − 866); (iii) frame and client origins differ by one title bar (34px on XFCE). The first sampling pass with a wrong offset concluded "legend swatches not painted" - a 10px swatch offset by 34px is missed entirely; **align first (back out the client origin from `getmouselocation` after a move) before declaring something unpainted**. Likewise, with a window taller than the screen the injection point must first be moved into view via `xdotool windowmove` to negative coordinates (XTest clamps to the screen's bottom edge - a point outside the screen is equivalent to no injection).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/chart` **37/37** (26 new: the three migrated wbtest files - tooltip sizing/edge-clamped positioning/nearest-point inverse of map_idx/sector quadrants and ring tolerance/`CiPlot` fractions and degenerates; the full zoom math - normalisation, set-window, the wheel anchor invariant, pan bounds, visible interval; VisualMap two/three-band with invalid-colour fallback; legend accumulate-and-hit with the width gate; visibility flag fit/toggle/filter (incl. offset addressing); slice narrowing; `ci_save_chart` size and format validation plus a real PNG write; the three interactive components mounted and injected - line hover shows crosshair and tooltip then restores on leave, legend click hides a series and changes pixels, bar `dispatch_scroll` direct call narrows the window, donut hover expands a sector and restores; plus the pure helpers - plot paddings matching the draw code, donut geometry with thickness clamping, items original-index and palette cycling, window sizing by longest series). All nine packages green (core 62/input 10/render 42/text 29/icons 4/theme 8/sys 7/ui 107/chart 37), ten packages `--deny-warn` clean, `yoga-mbt` 96/96, repo-wide red unchanged at 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window proof (`examples/native-app` gained an interactive chart section, window grown to 1400×2080, geometry line `NATIVE-APP-CHIT`): exit code 0; after injecting "bar-canvas wheel (ineffective, see above) → donut legend first item click → donut sector hover → line canvas hover", pixel sampling matches item by item - legend swatches all present (including the bar's negative item in red `(214,69,80)`), **the hidden item's swatch turned border-grey `(216,220,225)`**, the 12-o'clock first sector (primary blue) vanished with the remaining sectors redistributed (that spot is now success green), the tooltip's dark base `(49,49,51)` sits right of the hover point, and the line hover shows dot colours. **Wheel zoom, tooltip follow feel and legend click feel** are for the user to verify on a real machine.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - desktop integration, first half (`sys`: desktop detection + app lookup + open-external)

- **Migration surface, file by file**: master's `yue/system.mbt` (`open_url`/`reveal_in_file_manager`) + `yue/system/appfind.mbt` + `yue/traybus/detect.mbt` (desktop environment detection) land as `sys/launcher.mbt` + `sys/appfind.mbt` + `sys/desktop.mbt`. The desktop detection is **carried as its own pure functions inside `sys`** rather than importing `@traybus`: the old-chain traybus package is part of the repo-wide red-dot backlog, and sys feeds it (wallpaper/default apps dispatch on the desktop) so importing it would create a cycle. When the DBus line lands, reuse this one copy instead of writing a second `detect_desktop`.
- **Detached spawn gets its own stub**: master used the shim's `g_spawn_async`, while `sys/sys_stub.c` only uses standard headers, so this is `fork → setsid → fork → execvp` plus a **CLOEXEC pipe** reporting back whether exec succeeded. **Not going through [pr_run] and waiting** is deliberate: `xdg-open` usually stays alive after handing the window off, so waiting would pin the calling thread; the double fork also guarantees no zombie (this function `waitpid`s the intermediate child, the real grandchild is reparented to init).
- **Real bug found and fixed this batch (write end of the pipe closed too early)**: the intermediate child used to `close(fds[1])` **before** forking the grandchild, so the grandchild had no write end to report through when `execvp` failed → the intermediate child read EOF (all write ends closed) and called it a success → "command does not exist" came back as `Ok`. The assertion "a nonexistent command must be Err" is exactly what caught it (red on the first run). Correct order: the intermediate child forks the grandchild first (it inherits the write end), **then** closes its own write end and keeps only the read end; because the write end is CLOEXEC, a successful exec closes it automatically and the intermediate reads EOF, while a failed exec falls through to `write("E")`.
- **`Ok` from `open_url`/`reveal_in_file_manager` means only "handed to the system"**; whether something actually opens is the platform's business. Arguments never go through a shell (argv passed directly), so shell metacharacters in URLs and paths are not an injection surface.
- **The "select" half of `reveal` is an acknowledged gap**: master first asks the FileManager1 bus for `ShowItems` and only falls back to opening the parent directory when the service is absent; this stack has no bus channel yet, so **every call currently takes the fallback path** (parent directory opens, selection lost). `ShowItems` comes with the DBus line.
- **appfind's ordering keeps master's `mut_view().sort_by` shape**: in this toolchain a comparator that returns the `-1/0/1` literals works fine - the earlier "`Ordering` is undefined" note only applies when the type name `Ordering` (or `Ordering::Less`) is written out explicitly. Ordering semantics unchanged: compare `name` by codepoint, then `id`; `appfind_str_cmp` compares codepoint by codepoint instead of String's default shortlex order (which would put `"New"` before `"Beta"`).
- **Platform surface**: the Windows start-menu (`.lnk`) and macOS (`.app`) branches did not come with this batch — the Windows side of `sys_stub.c` is still a placeholder, and `open_url`/`reveal`/`appfind_*` return `Unsupported` off Linux; the platform-dispatch batch can add master's pure functions (`appfind_win_*`/`appfind_mac_*`) directly.
- **Two toolchain findings (measured this batch)**: (i) a Uint64 literal suffix like `500u64` **does not parse** (`unexpected token id (lowercase start)`) — convert the monotonic-clock difference with `.to_int()` before comparing; (ii) **a payload-free `pub(all) enum` does not get `Eq` either** (the earlier note was about `PointerKind` with a closure field; this extends it to plain enums) — `desktop_detect() == before` will not compile, so compare with `match` or via `desktop_name()`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **59/59** (18 new: desktop detection 3 — single-value mapping, colon-separated multi-value first-recognised, and a real-environment save/change/assert/restore round trip; app lookup 10 — the INI subset parser, entry filtering with the zh_CN fallback, dedup merge and ordering, directory assembly's three states, real-disk collection with silent skips, `NoScanDir`, a live `appfind_installed` scan of this host, and `appfind_executable` hitting `/usr/bin/sh` plus argument guards; open-external 5 — `parent_dir`/relative-path normalisation, the real cwd, detached spawn's success/failure/non-blocking cases (`sleep 3` returns in under 500 ms), and the two error enums' `Show` plus the platform gate). `core` 62/62, `ui` 110/110, `render` 42/42, `mdview` 26/26, `theme` 8/8; twelve packages clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged. **Left for real-machine re-verification** (observable once `examples/native-sys` lands): whether `open_url` really raises the default browser, whether `reveal_in_file_manager` opens the parent directory window, and the app count reported by `appfind_installed`.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - platform input mapping layer (input line #19, first batch: `core/input_map.mbt`)

- **Why the tables come before the backends**: the Windows and macOS backends cannot be verified on this machine (no hardware), and the rule is "do not write code that cannot be verified", so the **platform-agnostic口径** lands first as pure functions plus regressions: modifier-bit folding for three platforms, two keycode tables (Win32 virtual keys / macOS `kVK_*`), shift application, pointer button numbers, and the "who gets this key first" routing table. Backends then fill in the table instead of each re-deciding the semantics - and drifting into three versions of it.
- **Three rules**: ① keysyms use **X11 values** (the Linux backend already reports GDK keyvals; the other two align to it) and printable ASCII is simply its character code; ② letters and digits yield the **unshifted base**, with shift/caps folded by `win_apply_shift` - X11 keyvals already carry shift semantics while Windows virtual-key codes do not, so without this step "Shift+A" would read as 'a'; ③ the OEM symbol row is **not** hardcoded into a layout table: Windows uses `WM_CHAR`/Unicode and macOS `charactersIgnoringModifiers`, because a hand-rolled layout table is both wrong and unmaintainable.
- **One measured semantic correction (now in the table)**: cursor and selection keys must be treated as kernel keys **even with Ctrl** (Ctrl+Left/Right = word jump, Ctrl+Home/End = document start/end - the most used editor actions) but **not with Alt** (Alt+arrows is a host action like Back). The order is the semantics: composing → cursor keys → Tab/Shift+Tab (focus traversal) → shortcut combos → printable keys (through the IM first) → everything else to the host.
- **Authority is written into both comments**: the Linux modifier fold currently lives in `wm_mods` in `win/stub.c` (C has to do it itself; it cannot call back into MoonBit) and is **bit-identical** to `gtk_mods_to_mods` - both comments name the other, so a change to one must change the other. The executable definition of the table is the MoonBit side (with regressions pinning it).
- **macOS `kVK_*` covers special keys only**: Return/Tab/Space/Backspace/ForwardDelete/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown/arrows/F1-F12/keypad - letters and digits are **deliberately unmapped** (on mac the character comes from the Unicode text the event carries; the OS resolves the layout). Also `kVK_Delete`(51) is actually **backspace** and `kVK_ForwardDelete`(117) is the delete key - the easiest thing to get backwards, so both are asserted.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **78/78** (7 new: three-platform modifiers, including "GDK's lock bit is not a modifier" and "Windows button bits do not leak into modifiers"; the Win VK table (letters/digits/control/function/keypad/unknown → 0); shift application (letters via shift XOR caps, digits and symbols via the table, everything else unchanged); the mac VK table with backspace and delete pinned separately; button numbers (Win `MK_*` and mac `buttonNumber` both folding to 1 left / 2 middle / 3 right); the six routing rules; the reset and surrounding triggers). `moon check core` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. **No real-machine evidence in this batch** (it is all pure functions); backend wiring follows the Windows foundation and a mac device.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - the native tier for overlays (batch E remainder: `core/overlay_native.mbt`)

- **The tier check looks at exactly one thing**: whether the host window has a mounted child surface (`Window::has_child_surfaces()`, reading the C-side slot table via `wm_window_surface_count`). With a webview area present the whole overlay moves to a real window; otherwise it stays on the layer. Reading the C table instead of keeping a MoonBit-side counter removes a class of "forgot to decrement" bugs. All six overlays go through `overlay_show_at`/`overlay_show_full`, so the tier switch takes effect in one place.
- **The native table does not live in `OverlayHost`**: the host belongs to an `App`, and a host holding an `App` is a reference cycle; the popup table is a process-level `Map[handle, App]`, used by `overlay_hide`, `overlay_clear` and the exit cleanup.
- **"Open a frame, measure, then shrink to fit" hit two GTK facts**: ① the drawing area's `gtk_widget_set_size_request` is the window's **minimum content size** (set from the initial size at creation), so calling `gtk_window_resize` without updating it cannot shrink below the initial size - the overlay keeps a dead margin after measuring; ② `set_resizable(FALSE)` makes programmatic `gtk_window_resize` ignored entirely (an undecorated window cannot be drag-resized anyway, so TRUE has no side effect). `wm_window_set_size` now handles both.
- **"Dismiss on blur" misfires during mapping, so it defaults to off** (control experiment): with it on, the popup closed itself about two seconds after opening while its own "collapse" button had never been clicked (`hides=0` proves we did not close it); with the switch off, the same sequence keeps the popup alive. The cause is the transient + keep-above window emitting focus-out repeatedly while it maps. Until there is a "only after it actually held focus" predicate, the overlay stays open and is dismissed by its own content - "opens then vanishes by itself" is worse than "does not dismiss on outside click". The `overlay_set_dismiss_on_blur(on)` switch stays in place for when the predicate lands.
- **A new event constructor reaches even the "frozen" baseline page**: adding `LostFocus` to `@win.Event` moved the whole-repo backlog from 287 to 288 - `examples/native-window` (the G2-G5 baseline, no new features) matches events exhaustively. The method is to watch the backlog number, find who grew, and add the empty arm; **"frozen" does not mean exempt from口径 changes**.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **81/81** (3 new: without child surfaces the layer tier still serves show/hide/count; measurement only counts visible children's right/bottom edges; with no native overlays the blur and fit passes are both no-ops). `moon check core` / `win` / the four examples clean under `--deny-warn`; ui 110/110, web 11/11, media 18/18 unchanged; backlog back to 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence (clicking "浮层盖网页" **inside the bridge window that has a webview pane**): `NATIVE-WEB-OVL opens=1 tier=1 native=1 layer=0`, and the screenshot shows self-drawn content (a title plus a "收起" button) sitting on top of the WebKit-rendered body text - precisely what the layer tier cannot do.
- **Not done in this batch (not pretended)**: a usable outside-click dismissal predicate (the switch above is off), Esc to close, the popup anchor following the owner's scroll/move, and per-component size hints (currently three fit passes).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - native popup windows (batch E: `win/popup.mbt` + `core/popup.mbt`)

- **Why it has to be a real window**: self-drawn pixels always sit **below** a native child surface (the webview area) - the z-order is on the native side and Painter cannot cover it. So "dropdowns / context menus / tooltips / toasts / dialogs must be able to sit on top of the page" has exactly one route: the window system (the overlay discipline in plan §4.5). That is what this batch adds, not yet another self-drawn layer.
- **Five GTK settings must all be applied, and before `show`**: `set_decorated(FALSE)` + `set_type_hint(GDK_WINDOW_TYPE_HINT_POPUP_MENU)` + `set_transient_for(owner)` + `set_skip_taskbar_hint/skip_pager_hint(TRUE)` + `set_keep_above(TRUE)` (plus `set_resizable(FALSE)`). The type hint and the transient relationship only take effect **at mapping**, so setting them after show is too late.
- **One hard measured finding: `gtk_window_get_position` always returns (0,0) under a reparenting window manager.** It reports the position *relative to the parent window*, and the WM wraps a top-level inside its decoration frame, so relative-to-frame is naturally 0,0. The screen origin has to come from the GdkWindow: `gdk_window_get_origin(gtk_widget_get_window(w), &x, &y)`. The symptom is not an error but **the popup landing exactly at the requested local coordinates regardless of where the owner is** - with several windows every menu would land at the same screen point, which reads as "the popup does not follow the button". After the fix: `owner_origin=2,34`, requested local (420,300) → landed at (422,334), matching xwininfo digit for digit.
- **Placement is a pure function**: `popup_place(owner_x, owner_y, x, y, w, h, screen_w, screen_h)` flips to the other side of the anchor when it would pass the right/bottom screen edge (edge-hugging menus open inward), clamps to 0 when flipping is still negative, and treats a zero screen size as "clamp, do not flip". All three boundaries are asserted, including the easy-to-get-wrong "if the flipped position is still on screen, use it rather than clamping to 0".
- **A popup is just a secondary window - no second lifetime**: `App::open_popup` goes through batch A's registry, so it shares the loop and pump, `close_window()` removes only itself without affecting the host, and `App::shutdown` tears everything down. Evidence: after choosing a menu item `xdotool search --name '^popup$'` returns nothing while the main window is still there, `NATIVE-WEB-POP opens=1 closed=1 choice='乙' wins=1 owner_origin=2,34 win_size=1100x700`, and the screenshot shows the undecorated popup sitting on top of WebKit-rendered page text.
- **This batch ships the channel plus one self-drawn popup example only**: the six overlays in `core/overlay.mbt` (tooltip / dropdown / context menu / dialog / toast / popover) still use the self-drawn layer. Switching them to the native variant is the next step, with the rule: use a real window only when the host window has a mounted pane - otherwise keep the layer, since with no webview there is no reason to open an extra window.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **71/71** (one new test with seven placement boundaries); `moon check win` / `core` / the three examples clean under `--deny-warn`; ui 110/110, web 11/11, media 18/18 unchanged; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence as above (240x150 popup at (422,334), IsViewable, closing itself on selection, main window unaffected). **Left for the user's eyes**: the look of the undecorated popup and how keep-above behaves across multi-monitor / window switching.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - media player (batch D: new package `yue/media`, CPU feedback + software decode)

- **The engine is a port, not a rewrite**: `audio_player.mbt` (416 lines) and `video_player.mbt` (476 lines) from `modules/gui-media` turned out to have **zero @yue dependencies** (only the component wrappers touch UI), so both engines plus their 15 existing regression tests moved into the new package `yue/media` unchanged apart from fixture paths and one public entry point. The pitfalls the old chain paid for (A/V drift, progress restarting from zero after seek, crashes on reload, ended-state versus idle) came along with those tests - **half the value of this batch is catching what the teacher already got right**.
- **Fixture path basis changed**: native test executables run with cwd = **workspace root**, not the package directory (the old package used `../ffmpeg-mbt/src/testdata/` from `modules/gui-media/src`). The new package must say `modules/ffmpeg-mbt/src/testdata/av_1s.mp4`; the tell is the relative path printed verbatim in the open error.
- **Frame byte order**: ffmpeg's sws target is `AV_PIX_FMT_RGBA` (R,G,B,A) while `render`'s `Bitmap` is Cairo `ARGB32` little-endian (B,G,R,A, premultiplied). Feeding frames therefore swaps R and B; for opaque pixels premultiplied equals straight, so alpha is written as 255. `rgba_to_bitmap` pins this byte by byte (the test asserts `10 20 30 00 → 30 20 10 ff`) and the real-window evidence is testsrc's colour bars showing no channel inversion.
- **This toolchain has no `FixedArray::of_list` / `Array::freeze` / `Bytes::of_list`**: test bytes must be built with `FixedArray::make(n, b'\x00')` + indexed writes + `Bytes::from_array`. Related shape surprises: `String::split` yields a **`StringView` iterator** (`to_owned()` for a `String`), `replace` takes labelled arguments (`old=`/`new=`), the builder is `StringBuilder()` (there is no `Buffer::new`), and unused parameters must be written `_y`.
- **UI/engine layering**: `player_t` lives in `yue/media` (importing `@core`/`@render`/`@text`/`@theme`), **not in `ui`** - otherwise every app that only uses a button would link ffmpeg and miniaudio. The engine files (`audio.mbt`/`video.mbt`/`subtitle.mbt`) stay UI-free; media does not depend on webview and both depend only on the window/layout/event layers (building-block rule 1).
- **Clock and repaint**: the engine's `tick()` (formerly `vp_tick`, made public in this batch, plus a new `frames_decoded()`) is driven by a self-rescheduling `@core.set_timeout(33, ...)` and **never advanced inside painting**; each tick calls the `on_tick(position, frames, playing)` observer, which is how the example proves "frames advance and the clock runs" rather than a stuck image. The Image object is rebuilt only when `frames_decoded()` changes (rebuilding per frame would churn cairo surfaces).
- **Seek debouncing became a partitioning pure function**: `player_bar_hit` splits the bar into "leftmost 34 px = play button / rightmost 150 px = time text / middle = progress track"; during a drag only the displayed position moves and the real seek happens **on release** - the old chain's bug was seeking on every drag step, which stalls the picture for a whole tick when the keyframe distance is long. Two boundary facts worth keeping from the assertions: the track ends clamp rather than overflow (at 449 px the fraction is >0.99, not exactly 1.0), and negative x falls into the play-button zone (partition first, then compute the ratio).
- **Failure to open is not silent**: when `make` fails, `player_t` draws an explanation panel and hands the reason out through `on_error` (probe + semantic degradation); `err=''` in the example is the evidence that no degradation happened.
- **Not in this batch (recorded in TODO, not pretended done)**: playback speed (audio needs time stretching; speeding up video alone breaks A/V sync - exactly the failure this document keeps warning about), embedded subtitle tracks (mov_text/ass; this batch takes external SRT/VTT text), fullscreen, a volume slider UI (the engine has `set_volume`, the control bar does not draw one), frame stepping.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/media` **18/18** (15 ported: audio play/pump/seek/loop, video decode/clock/duration/frame size/silent advance without an audio track; 3 new: four subtitle cases - both timestamp spellings, SRT/VTT in one parser, inline tag stripping, hit-test boundaries; frame byte order plus the short-packet tail pixel; control-bar partitioning). `moon check yue/media` and `./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`; core 70/70, ui 110/110, web 11/11, render 43/43, text 29/29 unchanged; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, "开一扇播放器窗" = a third window playing `av_1s.mp4` with an inline SRT): `NATIVE-WEB-MEDIA frames=10 pos_ms=1000 playing=0 err=''` (a one-second clip decoded all 10 frames, the clock reached 1000 ms, playback stopped by itself, no degradation), and the screenshot shows the colour-bar frame without channel inversion, the white overlay "第二条字幕（0.6~1.0 秒）", and the control bar full with `0:01 / 0:01`. **Left for the user on a real machine**: whether audio actually comes out (this host has a miniaudio device), the feel of dragging the progress bar, and speed/stepping on a long clip (not in this batch).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - typing into a native entry stops working after visiting the webview (two independent root causes)

- **Symptom (user, real machine)**: after clicking into the webview page and clicking back into a native entry, typing did nothing.
- Reproduced with xdotool injection plus temporary C-side probes, which split it into **two independent defects**:
- **① Our drawing area never got the GTK keyboard focus back**: we call `gtk_widget_grab_focus` once at window show, then the WebKit child takes focus on click - and a `GtkDrawingArea` does not take focus back just because it was clicked (GTK does not do that for it). The probe showed it plainly: after clicking back, not a single `[focus] in` and not a single `[key]` - every key went to the page (even Ctrl+Z, so "some shortcuts still seem to work" was an illusion). Fix: the host now requests focus **unconditionally** on pointer press - previously it only asked when the focus stack actually changed, and that change is exactly what never happens (the stack still holds the same entry).
- **② An empty entry (placeholder showing) never draws the composition at all**: `ui/entry.mbt` draws "placeholder" and "text + composition" as one if/else, so an empty box takes the placeholder branch and **the composition branch is skipped entirely**. The input method was working fine (the probe kept printing `[preedit] '爱'`), yet nothing appeared on screen - which reads exactly like "typing does not work". Fix: draw the placeholder only when there is **no text and no composition**, and give the composition its own branch. This one has nothing to do with the webview - an empty box reproduces it alone (the multi-line `textarea` is fine: it draws its composition outside the placeholder loop).
- **One tightening in the same batch**: when the keyboard focus is taken away (`Event::LostFocus`), hand the IME session back to the system - pending commits go to the current widget, the composition display is cleared, `im_reset` breaks any engine-side composition and the dedupe caches are reset. The focus stack is left alone (returning to the window keeps the same widget focused).
- **Probe methodology (learned the hard way)**: application-side `println` is **block-buffered** when stdout is redirected to a file, so killing the process loses every line - my first reading of "the application logs nothing at all" was wrong for exactly that reason; `stdbuf -oL` revealed the real data. C-side stderr is unbuffered, so an asymmetry between the two logs should make you suspect buffering before drawing conclusions.
- **Verification**: `[focus] in` appears after clicking back and `[key]` follows (neither existed before the fix); the empty-box screenshot shows the composition "爱" with its underline; the full round trip (clear -> click into the page -> click back -> type) shows the composition "阿宝". `core` 82/82, `ui` 110/110, `input` 10/10, `web` 15/15, every package clean under `--deny-warn`, whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. **Left for the user to re-verify**: the reported scenario (navigate inside the page, then type in the native entry).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - ownership of the presented frame (fix: garbled picture / flicker when a second large window opens)

- **Symptom (reported by the user on a real machine)**: opening a second window such as the bridge window or the local-video window produced a garbled picture and a flickering self-drawn area; a single main window looked fine.
- **Root cause**: `wm_present` pointed `w->pending` **straight at the MoonBit-side `Bitmap`**, while the actual blit happens in the **next** draw callback (GTK only lets you touch the GdkWindow's cairo inside that callback - there is no way around it). Once the callback is queued, nothing on the MoonBit side references that Bitmap any more - in `App::paint_frame` it is a local, replaced by the next frame - so when the GC collects it is entirely up to allocation pressure. Reading that address from an expose afterwards reads **freed memory**, which is the garbled picture; every expose re-reads it, which is what looks like flicker.
- **Why exactly a second large window**: with one small window the buffer usually gets re-blitted before the GC touches it; add a 1000x620 animated window and the allocator sees dozens of fresh buffers per second, so the window lands in the "already collected, still being exposed" interval. Covering it and uncovering it (forcing an expose of the old region) reproduces it best.
- **Fix**: the backend **owns** the frame buffer - `WmWindow` gains `backing/backing_cap`, `wm_present` goes through `wm_store_frame` (realloc on demand + one memcpy) and `pending` points at the backend's own buffer; `wm_close` frees it (slots get reused by the next window, so a bare `memset` leaks *and* corrupts). Both the GTK and the Win32 branches changed identically to avoid two semantics (the Windows side cannot run here - code-reviewed only, to be re-verified with the W line on real hardware).
- **One policy line**: any buffer that must survive "until the next callback" across the FFI boundary has to be owned by **the side that gets called back**. A comment is not a fix - the old comment already said "ownership stays with MoonBit"; that was accurate and useless, because nothing kept it alive until that moment. The same shape was checked in `web`'s JS results and `yue/media`'s frame feedback: both `malloc`/`g_free` on the C side, so neither carries this hazard.
- **Verification**: after the change `moon build ./examples/native-web` relinked (`build/libyue_win_stub.a` rebuilt), and the real window ran "open the local-video window → screenshot → cover it with the main window and move away to force one expose → screenshot again": the 23-minute clip decoded continuously to `NATIVE-WEB-LOCAL present=1 frames=1369 pos_ms=56397 speed=1.25x err=''` (a 1000x620 animated window allocating dozens of buffers per second is precisely the shape that used to fall into the collected-but-still-exposed interval), zero X errors in the log, and both screenshots match the video with no striped garbage; **the user re-verified on the real machine: the bridge window and the local-video window no longer garble and the self-drawn area no longer flickers**. Whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - the lifetime of a handle across FFI (fix: the log fills with GTK/WebKit assertions after closing a window)

- **Symptom (a control experiment located it, not a guess)**: same example - closing **only the bridge window** leaves the log clean, while closing the **main window** (the one with a live webview pane) always prints 5 CRITICAL lines at exit: `webkit_web_view_get_title/get_uri` failing `WEBKIT_IS_WEB_VIEW(webView)`, followed by `gtk_widget_get_parent`/`gtk_widget_destroy` failing `GTK_IS_WIDGET`. The assertions do not kill the process, which is why they had been shrugged off.
- **Root cause**: window teardown goes through the window layer's `wm_close`, which zeroes the child-surface slots belonging to that window and destroys the GtkWidget tree (the WebKitWebView is a grandchild and dies with it) - **but nobody tells the capability layer**. So the `Webview` still holds its old slot number as `handle` and `pumping == true`: the scheduled pump and the example's status polling keep calling `wb_get_title/wb_is_loading` with it, and the stub's `wb_get(id)` only asks "does this slot have a view", never "is that view still alive", so it hands out a dangling pointer. Finally `wb_destroy` (from `close` at exit) uses it once more for `gtk_widget_get_parent`/`destroy` - those are the two `GTK_IS_WIDGET` lines.
- **Fix (one choke point, both branches shaped the same)**: put the liveness test on **the side that knows when the teardown happens** - in the stub, `wb_view_alive(v)` means "the slot has a view **and** `wm_surface_container(v->surface_id) != NULL`" (the container comes from win's slot table, becomes NULL the moment the window is destroyed, and is the only reliable liveness signal here). `wb_get` now goes through that gate, so **every** engine query and action turns into a no-op after the view dies, instead of twenty methods each re-deriving a condition; `wb_destroy` uses the same test - with the container gone it only `g_free`s and `memset`s its own slot and never touches the pointer again. MoonBit gains the public `Webview::is_alive()`, and the pump's next tick, finding the view gone, sets `pumping = false`, zeroes `handle` and drops `eval_waiters` (stop scheduling, rather than spin).
- **One policy line (same family as "on-screen buffer ownership")**: a handle across FFI and a buffer across FFI are the same problem - only the side that performs the teardown knows when the thing stops existing, so the invalidation test has to come from it. `handle != 0L` on the MoonBit side can only ever mean "it was created", never "it is still there"; writing the liveness test into the stub's single accessor (`wb_get`) beats repeating it in front of every method. Checked the same shape elsewhere (the filter is "does it build another C slot table indexed by id"): `win`'s `wm_windows[]` is memset on close, and every accessor was counted - anything touching `w->window`/`w->area` already returns on NULL, the rest only read counters like `ev_count`/`commit_len` (which are 0 after a memset, so inherently safe); `yue/media`'s FFI passes an opaque pointer (`MbtAdev`) rather than a slot number, with the lifetime owned by MoonBit's own `close` - a different shape, not in this class.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/web` **17/17** (1 new: with a non-zero handle whose view is gone, `is_alive()` says false and after `retire_if_dead` the handle is zeroed, `pumping` is false and `eval_waiters` is empty); `web` and the example clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window judgement (the same control experiment re-run): open the bridge window → close it → close the main window gives **0 CRITICAL lines** (5 before the fix), the process exits by itself, and the exit readout is intact (`last='标题 本地桥接页'`). **Re-verified by the user (2026-10-12) on the manual path**: closing windows one by one from the title-bar button, down to the main window, still logs zero CRITICAL. Two regressions confirm the liveness gate does not block live views: the bridge page still loads from the protocol and its Chinese title reads back, and the main window's protocol row gives `reg=1 loads=1 files=2 unreg=1 fails=1 fail='WebKitNetworkError/399 yuedemo://local/index.html'`, field for field identical to before the fix.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - embedded subtitle track (batch D2c: ffmpeg-mbt exposes the stream, plus a lying duration)

- **The subtitle stream simply did not exist before**: `modules/ffmpeg-mbt` located only video and audio (two `av_find_best_stream` calls in `vf_open`), and subtitle packets were dropped with the "not my stream" branch. This batch adds the third: `VfFmt.sstream` plus a standalone `VfSDec` (`vf_sub_open`/`vf_sub_next`/`vf_sub_pts_s`/`vf_sub_end_s`/`vf_sub_close`), and on the MoonBit side `subtitle_cues(path)` reads the whole track in one pass into `Array[SubCue]`.
- **It must read from its own container**: if the subtitle decoder shares the `AVFormatContext` with video/audio, both sides advance the same `av_read_frame` cursor - after draining subtitles the video starts mid-file or reports EOF. So `subtitle_cues` opens and closes its own handle; the cost is one extra container open (subtitles are sparse, one pass beats a per-frame lookup), and what it buys is **calling it mid-playback still does not disturb the main timeline** - which is now a headless assertion (open a fresh demuxer after the drain and check the frame pts still advances).
- **A duration lie caught from real data, not guessed**: mov_text reports `AVSubtitle.end_display_time` as **2000 ms always** (the default when the codec does not fill a per-cue duration). Reading it literally makes the first cue cover the second and linger a second past its end. The judgement was the printed table: `0.2..2.2 / 0.6..2.6` (wrong) against `0.2..0.6 / 0.6..1.0` (what the source says). The fix takes the **shorter of the packet duration and `end_display_time`** - and the packet duration has to be read *before* `av_packet_unref`, which zeroes it. In mp4 the sample duration is authoritative, and the measured values fall back to the source's intent.
- **The ASS record prefix is stripped at the FFI layer**: some text subtitle decoders (mov_text among them) leave `rect->text` empty and only give the whole ASS dialogue line `ReadOrder,Layer,Style,Name,MarL,MarR,MarV,Effect,Text` - that is the decoder's output *shape*, not the content, so normalising it belongs to the搬运 (`subtitle_ass_text`). The test is deliberately conservative: at least eight commas, `ReadOrder` a non-empty number, and the three margin fields numeric-or-empty. Style/name/effect are free text and cannot serve as a test, otherwise a cue whose body happens to contain eight commas gets cut in half (positive and negative cases pin this). Layout tags (`{\an8}`, `<c.xxx>`) still go through `yue/media`'s `strip_cue_tags`, the same cleaning path external subtitles take - so both sources are identical by the time anything renders.
- **Two sources in the player plus one observation port**: `player_t` uses the `subtitle` text when given (an explicit intent wins) and only then tries the container's embedded track; `on_subtitle(count, embedded)` joins the existing `on_tick`/`on_speed` ports, and the example writes both into its exit line (`NATIVE-WEB-MEDIA ... subs=2 emb=1`, `NATIVE-WEB-LOCAL ... subs=0`) so "this clip has no subtitle track" and "the track could not be decoded" stay distinguishable - the latter reports its reason through `on_error` instead of silently returning an empty table.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/ffmpeg-mbt/src` **6/6** (2 new: `subtitle_ass_text` stripping plus three "must not strip" counter-cases; the real container `av_1s_subs.mp4` drained with correct start/end seconds, Chinese text, main timeline undisturbed, and "no track = Ok([])" separated from "cannot open = Err"); `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/media` **24/24** (2 new: embedded-to-`Cue`搬运 with the same cleaning contract, and end-to-end hit lookup by position); `yue/media`/`web`/`core`/`ui`/`ffmpeg-mbt` and `examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. The fixture is a 13 KB three-stream file kept in the repo (`av_1s.mp4` stream-copied plus two mov_text cues: `ffmpeg -map 0:v -map 0:a? -map 1:0 -c copy -c:s mov_text`). **Re-verified by the user on real hardware (2026-10-12)**: the embedded-subtitle window shows the first cue 0.2-0.6 and the second 0.6-1.0, the external-text window is unchanged, and the long-clip window's `subs=0` raises no error.
- **Not in this batch (not pretending otherwise)**: ASS style rendering (colours/positioning/track selection - cues are overlaid as text only), multi-track listing and switching (`av_find_best_stream` picks one), and the playback-speed listening re-check.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - player playback speed (batch D2b: pure-MoonBit resampling, one shared clock)

- **Speed with zero new C**: the audio side needs no stub - decoded interleaved s16 PCM is linearly resampled by `1/speed` before being pushed to the device (`media_resample_s16`, pure MoonBit); the device still consumes at the original sample rate, so half the samples walk twice as fast. The cost is **pitch moves with rate** (this is "speed change", not "time stretch", which would need a phase vocoder). The video clock changes in exactly one place: `vp_target_pts(base, elapsed_ms, speed) = base + elapsed × speed`, so both sides share one axis - "picture fast, voice slow", the very desync this document keeps warning about, cannot happen.
- **An existing defect the speed work exposed**: `tick` used to decode at least one frame per tick (it compared `pts >= target` only *after* decoding), so low-frame-rate video - and **any slow speed** - ran ahead of the clock (picture stepping 30 frames/s while the clock advanced at 0.5×). The fix moves the gate *before* the decode: check the already-decoded frame's pts first and do not decode when the picture is ahead. This also finally delivers what tick's own comment always claimed ("低帧率视频画面跨 tick 保持").
- **Changing speed must re-pin the clock base**: `set_speed` reads the current position, sets the speed, then writes that position into `base_pts`/`pos_base` and the present instant into `wall0`/`played_base` - without re-pinning, the position jumps by the old/new speed ratio.
