The audio position formula follows: the queue holds resampled samples, so "consuming N samples = walking N×speed source samples", and that factor belongs in `AudioPlayer::current()`.
- **Streams without timestamps are not pretended to work**: the fallback path (one frame per tick, stepping by stream fps) has no pts to chase, so speed affects only the clock, not the picture - stated in `tick`'s doc comment rather than hidden in the code.
- **Gears come from a table, not from in-place arithmetic**: `bar_speeds = [1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 0.75]` and a click only advances an index (`player_speed_at(i)`, wrap-around and negative included), so there is no way to drift out of the gears the way `+= 0.25` would allow; clamping lives in the engine (`media_clamp_speed`, [0.25,4.0], NaN → 1.0 rather than the fastest/slowest gear). The control bar gained its sixth zone (`speed`), the track start moved from 114 px to 154 px and the volume fallback threshold from 400 to 440 - the "drawing and hit-testing read the same constants" rule paid for itself a second time in this line of work.
- **An observer instead of guessing**: `player_t` gained `on_speed(v)` (same shape as the existing `on_tick`/`on_error`), and the example writes the gear into its exit line `NATIVE-WEB-MEDIA ... speed=1.5x`, so the evidence does not require reading engine internals.
- **Not in this batch (not pretended done)**: embedded subtitle tracks (mov_text/ass need `ffmpeg-mbt` to expose a subtitle stream first), real-audio re-verification (whether sync still holds under speed and whether the pitch shift is acceptable by ear).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/media` **22/22** (3 new: the gear table with wrap-around and negative indices plus the short label spellings; `media_clamp_speed` and the three `vp_target_pts` arithmetic cases; `media_resample_s16`'s half/double sample counts, interpolation midpoint, stereo non-crosstalk and the three pass-through cases; plus `get_speed` clamping added to the real-decode case, and the bar-partition test rewritten for six zones); `moon check yue/media`/`./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, "开一扇播放器窗" then two clicks on the bar's speed zone: 1x→1.25x→1.5x): `NATIVE-WEB-MEDIA frames=10 pos_ms=1000 playing=0 speed=1.5x err=''` - the gear is reported through the `on_speed` observer (not by reading engine internals), the one-second clip still decoded all 10 frames at 1.5× and stopped by itself at 1000 ms with no degradation, and the screenshot shows the bar as `▶ |< >| 1.5x` with a full progress track, the volume bar and `0:01 / 0:01`. **Re-verified by the user (2026-10-12)**: A/V stays in sync under speed change, the pitch shift is acceptable, and the volume bar really changes loudness.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - player control surface (batch D2a: frame stepping, volume bar, fullscreen)

- **Frame stepping must land on the target frame immediately**: `seek` clears the current frame to `None` and waits for the next tick, but **`tick()` returns straight away while paused** - so after a step the picture stayed on a black frame and it looked like the button did nothing. The fix extracts the "decode forward from the current position up to the target time, capped at 8 frames" part of `tick` into `vp_settle`, and stepping becomes: pause first → move the position by `1/fps` → `seek` → `settle`. All four judgements live in the headless test: after a step `frames_decoded()` grew, `current()` advanced, `current_frame()` is non-empty, and `is_playing()` is still false (stepping must not drag the player into the playing state).
- **The control bar went from three zones to five** (play / step back / step forward / progress track / volume / time text), and drawing plus hit-testing read the **same** `BAR_*` constants - zones written in two places drift eventually. One more rule: below `w < 400` the volume bar neither draws nor responds (the track's right end is then computed from the time text), otherwise you get "visible but unclickable" or the reverse. The track ends still **clamp** rather than overflow (114 px → 0.0, 354 px → >0.99).
- **Volume and seek debounce in opposite directions**: seek only happens on release (every real seek rewinds to a keyframe, that is expensive), while volume applies on **every step** - hearing it change while dragging is the expectation, and it triggers no decoding. The volume bar draws `engine.get_volume()` (the clamped value inside the engine) rather than a local copy - a local copy disagrees with the display whenever `set_volume` clamps. For this, `VideoPlayer` gained `get_volume` (`AudioPlayer` already had one; the two now agree).
- **Fullscreen is a window-level action**: widgets cannot reach the window, so the chain is `Window::set_fullscreen` → `App::set_fullscreen`, requested by the host (the example wires the button). The C side is two lines - `gtk_window_fullscreen`/`unfullscreen` (ABI-only, per the "C stubs on demand and minimal" policy line). After the switch the size change flows through the ordinary allocate events; the host does not recompute rectangles.
- **Not in this batch (not pretended done)**: playback speed (audio would need time stretching or miniaudio's pitch path, both of which touch the device thread - separate batch, with the pitch-shift cost stated), embedded subtitle tracks (mov_text/ass need `ffmpeg-mbt` to expose a subtitle stream first), real-audio re-verification.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/media` **19/19** (1 new frame-stepping case: `n=0` does nothing, and after a step the frame count / position / frame present / still-paused four judgements hold, both ends clamp, `get_volume` returns the clamped value; the bar-partition case rewritten for five zones plus the narrow-bar fallback); `moon check win`/`core`/`yue/media`/`./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, "开一扇播放器窗" then injected `>| ×2`, `|< ×1`, a click mid-volume-bar, and the fullscreen button): `NATIVE-WEB-MEDIA frames=21 pos_ms=1000 playing=0 err=''` (the one-second clip decoded its 10 playback frames and then stepped up to 21, which proves stepping really decodes; `err=''` means no degradation), and after clicking fullscreen `xdotool getwindowgeometry` reports `Position: 2560,0 Geometry: 2560x1440` (the child window really filled the second monitor). The screenshot shows the bar with `▶ |< >|`, the progress track, the **volume bar** (its fill visible after the mid-bar click) and `0:00 / 0:01`. **Left for the user's eyes**: how frame stepping looks, the feel/audibility of the volume drag, and subtitle/control placement in fullscreen.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - local content channel and fine-grained navigation reasons (batch C remainder: `web/scheme.mbt`)

- **Schemes register per context, not per view**: the callback signature of `webkit_web_context_register_uri_scheme` is `(WebKitURISchemeRequest*, gpointer)` - **there is no view argument**, so scheme ownership is inherently context-level. The old chain registered statically per process, which stops making sense once profiles hang off panes. The slot table here dedupes on `(context, scheme)`: the engine is asked once per pair, and re-registering only swaps the root directory.
- **"Unregister" is a substitution, not a revocation**: WebKit has no unregister-uri-scheme. Unregistering clears that scheme's root, so every later request goes through `webkit_uri_scheme_request_finish_error` and the page sees "the scheme exists but yields nothing". That is safer than keeping the old directory around (a switched profile could still read the previous profile's files) and more honest than pretending the registration can be undone - the return value only says the substitution succeeded.
- **Static files under a root, with traversal blocked in the stub**: a request path containing `..` is rejected outright - no canonicalisation, no symlink escape. MIME comes from an extension table (html/htm/css/js/mjs/json/svg/png/jpg/jpeg/gif/woff2/txt), everything else `application/octet-stream`.
- **Writing a file does not create its parent directory, and the symptom shows up somewhere else** (hit on the real window in this batch): the page area just said `not found`, which reads like the protocol is broken, while the actual cause was that `/tmp/native-web-scheme` did not exist and both `sys_write_text_file` calls had failed. The fix is `sys_make_dirs`, but the **judgement has to be split first**: the example's exit line gained a `files=` counter (registration ok / load issued / files actually present) - without that segment you are guessing which link in the chain broke.
- **One policy line (settled 2026-10-12)**: C stubs are **on-demand and minimal** - written only where MoonBit cannot reach (the GTK3 window layer, Cairo, Pango, WebKitGTK, miniaudio/ffmpeg, dirent/getenv/spawn, sockets); C does ABI translation and slot tables, while decisions, parsing and degradation semantics stay in MoonBit. Before adding a function, check whether an existing MoonBit-level channel already composes it. This batch is one real trade-off: directory creation gained no C - `sys_make_dirs` = `pr_run("mkdir", ["-p", path])`, the same route `defaultapps_wbtest` already uses. The cost is that it only holds where `pr_available()` is true, so the function doc says "the probe is the contract" instead of letting callers assume it works everywhere.
- **Revised later the same day - the rule is about capability, not a blanket ban on C in `sys`**: where the composing route is genuinely equal, take it; **where it is not, add the C.** Directory creation was written the composing way first, and the cost showed up immediately in measurement: it never worked outside POSIX, because Windows has no `mkdir` on `PATH`, so `fsx_wbtest.mbt:109` had been red there from the day it landed. `sys_make_dirs` is now the thin FFI `yue_sys_make_dirs` (recursive `CreateDirectoryW` on Windows, `mkdir(2)` on POSIX) - the same family as `sys_read_text_file` / `sys_remove_file` / `fsx_list_dir`, which were C all along. So this was not a new exception to the rule but the removal of its one inconsistent member, and it dropped a subprocess dependency (no `pr_available()` requirement, no fork + two temp files per call).
- **`CreateDirectoryW` does not report "already exists" for a drive prefix** (measured on the real machine this batch): building a path level by level, `"C:"` as a segment returns **`ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED(5)`**, not `ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS(183)` - matching only 183 reads the drive root as a failure and the whole call returns 0. The pattern is **probe first, create second**: if `GetFileAttributesW` succeeds, that level already exists and is skipped; `ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS` is still tolerated for create-time races. POSIX has no such trap - `mkdir` gives `EEXIST` for an existing directory.
- **Two fine-grained navigation facts**: ① `WEBKIT_LOAD_REDIRECTED` yields kind 15 - callers must be able to tell "the address changed" from "it reloaded"; ② the load-failed payload became structured `uri|domain|code|message` (`domain` via `g_quark_to_string`), parsed on the MoonBit side into `NavError`; error text can itself contain `|`, so the first three fields are taken positionally and everything left over belongs to `message`, and a short payload gives `None` rather than guessed fields.
- **A seam bug that only the real window caught**: `WebEvent` already had `LoadFailed`, but the kind table in `Webview::dispatch` had no arm for 14, so every failure arrived as `Unknown(14, raw)` and callers could not read the reason at all. The headless suite had "kind -> event, one by one" and still missed it, because the list it walked skipped 14. Both halves were tested; the mapping between them was not. Now 2/3/4/14/15 are all pinned in that test, plus one end-to-end case (dispatch 14 -> `LoadFailed` -> `nav_error_parse` gives uri/domain/code/message).
- **A `load_html` page does not enter the back/forward history** (measured on the real window - do not mistake this for broken back/forward): the bridge page is installed with `load_html`, and clicking a non-existent domain afterwards (the example's `a.example` - `.example` is the RFC 2606 reserved domain, DNS must fail) read `can_back=0 can_fwd=0` with `go_back()` a no-op. **But the cause is not "a failed navigation wipes the history"** - separating the four steps shows it:
  ```
  after-load_html  url=https://local.test/  can_back=0   ← load_html adds no entry
  after-ok-nav     url=https://example.com/ can_back=1   ← a real navigation does
  after-fail-nav   url=https://a.example/1  can_back=1   ← the failure wipes nothing
  after-go_back    url=https://example.com/ can_fwd=1    ← and it really goes back
  ```
  So a failed navigation leaves a perfectly navigable state; that reading was 0 only because the bridge page's history held nothing besides the `load_html` document. **Cross-platform agreement**: Microsoft's docs state for WebView2's `NavigateToString` that "the back or forward history will not be modified" - same shape as the Linux measurement; Apple makes no promise about `loadHTMLString:baseURL:`, to be checked on real mac hardware (recorded in TODO). **Two policy lines**: ① an engine-semantic no-op must be stated in the UI (the example's buttons now log either "后退 → url" or "后退：无处可退（can_go_back=0；load_html 不入历史）"), otherwise it reads as a bug; ② to give a local page a real history, hand it a real URL through the custom protocol above and use `load_url`, not `load_html`.
- **The public face of custom protocols is now "declare the name up front"** (2026-10-12, taking the strictest of the three): Linux may register a new scheme at any time per context, but WebView2 keeps its list on the **environment options** (before the environment is created, once per process) and WKWebView keeps it on the configuration that must be fixed **before the view exists** - treating "add a name at runtime" as public semantics means writing code that silently fails on Windows/macOS. Now: `webview_predeclare_schemes([...])` (before the first webview) → `register_protocol(scheme, root)` only accepts declared names (the root may change at any time, `unregister_protocol` still substitutes an error handler); `webview_seal_schemes()` seals the list when a view is created, and an empty list gets the default name `app` so the zero-config case works. Linux's dynamic ability stays inside the stub, not in the API. **Verification**: `web` 16/16 (one new case: creating a view seals the list, an undeclared name is refused, declaring after the seal returns false and leaves the list unchanged), real window `NATIVE-WEB-SCHEME reg=1 loads=1 files=2` (the example declares `yuedemo` before creating any view and the protocol page still works).
- **Protocol registration must accept "before mount" too** (2026-10-12, caught while moving the bridge window onto the protocol): the pane's view is created in the frame where it first gets a real rectangle, and `register_protocol` used to gate on `handle != 0L`, so anything called before the mount returned false and left the caller to schedule its own retry - while `load_html` had already solved the same problem with `pending_html`. Two rules for one seam is how bugs get written. Now `pending_protos` records the queued `(scheme, root)` pairs and `mount_at` applies them right after creating the view; `unregister_protocol` first cancels a queued entry (true if it removed one, still false if nothing was ever registered - "not mounted yet" must not degrade into "always succeeds"). **The tell**: the example's first run fell into the `load_html` fallback and logged "协议未就绪", which is exactly this seam.
- **A protocol channel answers with a MIME type only, so text types without an explicit charset come out garbled** (same batch, real-window screenshot): `wb_mime_of` returned bare `text/html` for `.html`, WebKit had no other way to guess the encoding and decoded as ISO-8859-1, so the whole Chinese page rendered as mojibake. Fixed in two places - text extensions in the stub now carry `charset=utf-8` (html/css/js/json/txt) and the page itself emits `<meta charset="utf-8">` - but over a protocol **only the header is dependable**: a `<meta>` tag takes effect only once the decoder reaches those bytes, and the first screen has already been decoded with the wrong table. **Verification**: `web` 16/16, `web` and `examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`; the real-window screenshot shows the protocol page rendering Chinese correctly, and the event area logging `后退 → yuedemo://local/bridge.html` proves the protocol page **is in history** and really can be stepped back from (contrast: the same document loaded via `load_html` makes 后退 a no-op), with the exit line `navs=5 … dom_ok=1 text_bytes=68 … last='标题 本地桥接页'`.
- **The protocol channel only hands over a MIME type, so text types must carry `charset=utf-8` themselves** (the mojibake caught on the real window): the very same bridge HTML renders Chinese fine through `load_html` and turns into `æ¡¥æŽ¥é¡µ` through `yuedemo://`. The two channels have different encoding contracts - `webkit_web_view_load_html` takes a UTF-8 string, so the engine decodes UTF-8; the protocol feed hands over only a content type, and with a bare `text/html` WebKit falls back to ISO-8859-1 (an in-page `<meta charset>` is only visible once the tags have been decoded, and they are already mangled by then). Fixed once on the stub side: `wb_mime_of` returns `...; charset=utf-8` for html/css/js/mjs/json/txt and leaves binary/image types alone. **The rule**: over a protocol feed the MIME is the only encoding contract - never rely on the in-page declaration; keep writing it anyway (the file may be opened by another browser straight from disk).
- **Registrations queued before mounting must be accepted, otherwise the natural "register then load" order is the caller's problem**: the view is created in the frame that first gets a real rectangle, so calling `register_protocol` right after `win.mount(...)` used to return false - exactly the bridge window's path, which silently fell back to `load_html` and left the "后退" button a permanent no-op. Now, same shape as `pending_html`: before mounting it is stored in `pending_protos` and `mount_at` applies each entry once the view exists; `unregister_protocol` can withdraw a not-yet-applied registration (returns true if it removed one, false only when there was nothing to remove) - "not mounted" is not the same as "silently succeeds". **The example's judgement moved with it**: the bridge page now goes through the protocol (a real URL is the only way to get a real history), and a failed registration is stated out loud in the event area ("协议未就绪，退回 load_html（此页不入历史）") instead of degrading quietly.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/web` **16/16** (4 new: four-field payload split with a `|` inside `message` and short-payload `None`; `nav_parse_int` sign and stop-at-nondigit; unmounted registration accepted, withdrawal works, a second withdrawal returns false; the kind-14 seam above); `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **164/164** (1 new `sys_make_dirs`: create nested -> write -> read back, idempotent, empty path rejected, write into a missing parent fails); `moon check sys`/`web`/`./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, the "批 C 余项" row, two separate judgements): ① loading the protocol page gives `NATIVE-WEB-SCHEME reg=1 loads=1 files=2 unreg=0 title='本地协议页'` plus `NATIVE-WEB-STAT ... title='本地协议页' url='yuedemo://local/index.html'`, and the screenshot shows two hard facts - the page `<title>` is the protocol page's, and the body paragraph has a blue background (so `style.css` also came through `yuedemo://`, not inline); ② unregistering then reloading the same URL gives `unreg=1 fails=1 raw=0 fail='WebKitNetworkError/399 yuedemo://local/index.html' title=''`, and the page area paints the very text passed to `finish_error` (the screen literally shows `scheme unregistered`) - which proves "unregister = substitute an error handler" really takes effect, and that the failure reason arrives as a **structure** rather than a blob. One enum fact on the side: `WEBKIT_NETWORK_ERROR_FAILED = 399`, not the intuitive -1. ③ Four readings after the bridge page moved onto the protocol (injected clicks, event area read from the screenshot): `导航开始 yuedemo://local/bridge.html` → clicking link A gives `加载失败 g-resolver-error-quark/0 https://a.example/1` → clicking "后退" gives `后退 → yuedemo://local/bridge.html` (**it really goes back now**, where the `load_html` version was always a no-op) → the page area shows correct Chinese headings/body/links and `抓正文 ok=1 title=本地桥接页 链接=0` (DOM extraction runs against the protocol page and the title comes back as UTF-8). **Re-verified by the user (2026-10-12)**: the protocol page renders Chinese correctly, clicking the reserved-domain link reports a structured reason, and "后退" really returns to the bridge page. One policy line stays for whoever comes next: two windows without their own data directories share one context, so the main window's "注销该协议" also clears the bridge window's root (re-registering restores it - the example's comment says so).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - JS bridge / DOM capture / navigation surface (batch C: the webkit stub and contract of `web`)

- **Asynchronous events all go "queue in C, drain from MoonBit" - no C-to-MoonBit trampolines**: navigation, title, JS results, page `invoke`, permission, download and process termination are all engine-side async signals; giving each a closure trampoline means managing 12 callback lifetimes and thread affinities by hand. The shape here is one 32-slot ring per view (`wb_next_event` yields the kind, `wb_event_num`/`wb_event_text` read the payload), and a **full queue drops the oldest** - events are hints, not a ledger, and blocking on them would stall the main loop.
- **The pump is not hooked into painting**: a result callback may mount or unmount widgets (e.g. redraw a list after capturing links), and mutating the tree mid-paint is exactly what this stack avoids (`Widget::paint_at` is translating and clipping layer by layer). So mounting starts a self-rescheduling `@core.set_timeout(30, ...)` pump, and `close` stops it.
- **`load_html` must not be dropped before mounting** (hit on the real window in this batch): a pane mounts on the frame where it first gets a real rectangle, so calling `load_html` right after `win.mount(...)` found handle 0 and the page area stayed at `about:blank`. The tell: `navs=1` with only about:blank in the events and every capture empty. Same fix as `load_url`: record `pending_html` and apply it in `mount_at` right after the view is created.
- **Brace balance in the capture scripts must be pinned**: the four variants each appended their own `}})` and one extra closing brace slipped in, so the page replied `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '}'`. Marking that `ok=false` in the parser is correct (no silent success), but the generator needs a lock: a single wrapper `dom_wrap(fields)` (variants only write their own fields) plus assertions that `{`/`}` and `(`/`)` counts match and the script ends with `})`.
- **2.52 has exactly one permission entry point**: the `decide-policy` enum has **no** `WEBKIT_POLICY_DECISION_TYPE_PERMISSION_REQUEST` (only NAVIGATION_ACTION / NEW_WINDOW_ACTION / RESPONSE), so permissions must come from the `"permission-request"` signal; and `webkit_permission_request_allow(request)` **takes one argument** (not the old `(request, gboolean)`). Returning TRUE from the handler means "we dealt with it", so default-deny is "report the event + return TRUE", and allowing is "explicitly allow, then still return TRUE".
- **`g_signal_lookup` before connecting optional signals**, and expose the result as a mask (`wb_signal_mask()`: 1 download / 2 process-terminated / 4 permission / 8 new-window). Whichever bit is missing is a missing piece of evidence - never let "the callback never fired" and "it is not implemented" collapse into the same symptom.
- **Two API shapes that read backwards**: ① under webkit2gtk-4.1 (this build links libsoup-3.0, whose headers and libraries come along with `pkg-config --cflags/--libs webkit2gtk-4.1`) `webkit_cookie_manager_get_cookies_finish` returns a **`GList`, not a `GSList`**, whose elements are `SoupCookie`; ② from 2.40 `webkit_web_view_evaluate_javascript_finish` hands back a **`JSCValue*`** directly (only the old `run_javascript_finish` gave a `WebKitJavascriptResult*`), and `jsc_value_to_string` on a JS string returns the content **without quotes** - so the structured payload is the JSON text itself, with no extra layer to strip.
- **Profile hangs off the pane**: a non-empty `data_dir` in `wb_create` goes through `webkit_website_data_manager_new(base-data-directory, base-cache-directory)` + `webkit_web_context_new_with_website_data_manager` + `webkit_web_view_new_with_context`; empty uses the default context with its own user content manager. `browser_profile_persistence()` is always `Persistent` on Linux (the "macOS 14+ plus a bundle id" boundary is Apple's), but the probe exists so callers never branch on platform.
- **Two example-side counting pitfalls**: ① with one shared counter set for four capture variants, the last variant (structure) has no body text or links, so plain assignment erased the earlier evidence - take maxima instead; ② grabbing a top-level window that owns a child X window (WebKit) with `import -window <id>` yields striped garbage; capture the composited screen with `import -window root -crop WxH+X+Y`.
- **Contract names follow the old chain** (`load_url`/`get_url`/`get_title`/`reload`/`stop`/`go_back`/`go_forward`/`can_go_back`/`can_go_forward`/`is_loading`/`load_html`/`set_user_agent`/`execute_javascript`/`execute_javascript_with_result`/`add_user_script`/`add_raw_binding`/`remove_binding`/`has_bindings`/`get_cookies_for_url`), with `current_url` as an alias of `get_url`. `remove_binding`/`remove_all_user_scripts` always return false with the reason written down: WebKit has no unregister entry point, and that must not read as a defect of this stack. `window.open` is intercepted and reported by default, with an opt-in "navigate in this pane" - this stack does not do one-window-many-webviews.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/web` **11/11** (9 new headless: capture-script shape and brace balance; structured parsing of all four JSON variants; parse failure keeps `ok=false` plus the raw text; every call safe while unmounted with `capture_dom`/`add_raw_binding`/result-carrying eval returning false/-1; kind→`WebEvent` mapping item by item; JS results paired by sequence and delivered exactly once; invoke on both paths (`on_invoke` plus the `on_event` side channel); persistence probe consistent with engine availability). `moon check web` and `./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`; core 70/70 and ui 110/110 unchanged; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, "开一扇桥接窗" = a second window hosting a `load_html` local page, no network needed): `NATIVE-WEB-JS binding=1 evals=1 dom_ok=4 dom_bad=0 links=3 text_bytes=68 meta=1 headings=2 elements=14 invokes=1 navs=2 perms=1 wins=1 crashes=0 cookie_bytes=2 last='invoke {"msg":"来自页面","n":7}'`; the composited screenshot carries three hard facts - the page body reads "已被原生改过" (native→page `execute_javascript` really mutated the DOM), the native event area lists "页面回话 {...} / 权限请求（默认拒绝）WebKitGeolocationPermissionRequest / 新窗请求已拦下 https://b.example/2", and the bridge window count stays 1 (the new window was intercepted rather than spawning a second webview). **Re-verified by the user (2026-10-12)**: in-page scrolling and link clicks work, and our pure-channel IME yields while the webview holds focus and comes back after clicking into a native entry.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - panes and the webview channel (batch B: `win/surface.mbt` + new package `web`)

- **Window content became a `GtkFixed` holding "drawing area + positionable children"**, not a `GtkOverlay`: an overlay needs `pass_through` to let events through the container, and event ownership for a container that itself has children is unreliable there (the container being transparent does not imply its children behave). `GtkFixed` is an ordinary container - a pointer over a child belongs to that child, anywhere else to the drawing area, **with no extra mechanism**. The price is tracking the size ourselves: `set_size_request` on the drawing area from the fixed's `size-allocate`, gated by "only when the size actually changed" - `set_size_request` triggers another allocation round, so unconditionally setting it self-oscillates.
- **The surface table must record its owning window**: `WmSurface{container, window_id, kind, x,y,w,h}`, and `wm_close(window)` clears every surface belonging to that window. Otherwise the window is torn down first and the capability layer (the webkit stub) still calls `gtk_fixed_put` with a dangling `GtkWidget*` - segfault. Surface slots are recycled the same way as the window slots in batch A (scan for `container == NULL`).
- **Pointers travel C-to-C, MoonBit only carries integers**: win exports `wm_surface_container(int64) -> GtkWidget*` and `web/webkit_stub.c` declares it `extern` (same executable, static link). That keeps `web`'s MoonBit layer from importing `win`'s MoonBit layer, so the dependency rule "webview depends only on the window/layout/event layers" holds.
- **A missing webkit SDK is a degradation, not an error**: `scripts/prebuild.py` probes `webkit2gtk-4.1`; found -> compile with `-DWB_HAVE_WEBKIT` and hand `-lwebkit2gtk-4.1` to `web`; not found -> compile the **macro-less** placeholder branch (`wb_available()` returns 0). Either way the `wb_*` symbols must exist, otherwise it is a link-time failure rather than a run-time degradation. The stub's branch shape mirrors win: `#if defined(WB_HAVE_WEBKIT)` / `#elif !defined(__linux__)` / otherwise an empty translation unit, so nothing is defined twice against prebuild's `.a`. Link order puts `-lyue_web` before `-lyue_win_stub` (the former references the latter's symbol).
- **Sub-pixel values must be rounded, not truncated**: pane rectangles come from yoga as Doubles and are synced to the surface every frame. With truncation a 0.4 px drift makes "the rectangle changed" permanently true, so every frame pays an extra FFI call plus a native relayout. The rule lives in the pure function `webview_rect_key` (`round()`, same treatment for negative coordinates) and the dedup compares the rounded key.
- **`ChildKind` is a register, not an open set**: only `Webview = 1` today. The child surface keeps exactly the "embed a native control" meaning (plan §4.5); allowing custom values would turn "not a general sub-surface" back into an empty phrase.
- **Verification**: new package `web` headless 2/2 (rounding and dedup key; probe/constructor agreement with zero handles before mounting; `close` safe when never mounted); `moon check win` / `web` / `./examples/native-web` clean under `--deny-warn`; core 70/70 and ui 110/110 unchanged; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-web`, this host X11/XFCE + WebKitGTK 2.52.6): split layout gives `NATIVE-WEB-STAT supported=1 loading=0 title='Example Domain' url='https://example.com/'` with `NATIVE-WEB-GEO pane=262,71,848x605 sidebar=12,71,240x605` (two non-overlapping panes); injecting "toggle full/split + load + one click inside the page area" yields `loads=1 toggles=1 mode=full pane=12,71,1098x605`, and the screenshots confirm WebKit really renders the page and reflows to the new width after switching to full - **one pane mechanism producing both layouts**. **Left for the user's eyes**: scrolling and link clicks inside the page, and whether our pure-channel IME really yields while the webview holds focus (and comes back after).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - multi-window host (`core/host.mbt`: one host, N windows)

- **Shape**: one window = one `App` (its own platform window + widget tree + `FocusStack` + IME/hover/capture state). Windows share exactly two things - the `Loop` (one process-wide time base, built once by `host_loop()`) and the platform pump (`@win.wait`/`wake` were already process-level). The registry only answers "which windows exist, which is current, does closing one quit the host"; `App::step` now drains events per window, pumps the IME per window and paints the dirty ones, always iterating a `host_windows()` **snapshot** (a window can close mid-drain and mutate the table).
- **The closing verdict**: the first window in the registry is the primary one; only closing the primary - or the last remaining - quits the host (`host_close_action(primary, remaining)` is a pure function, headless-testable). Closing a secondary window just removes it. The teardown order stays "stop dispatch first, then destroy": `running=false` -> deregister -> `window.close()`. `App::shutdown` destroys **every** window still registered (secondaries may be open); handles already recycled to -1 make `wm_close` idempotent.
- **Two process-wide single slots had to be upgraded or multi-window draws/queries wrong**: ① the `invalidate()` repaint hook became a **broadcast** (mark every live window dirty + one wake) - a Store shared by two windows then repaints both, and an over-marked window only wastes one frame; ② the overlay host slot became a **resolver** (`set_overlay_provider(() -> OverlayHost?)`) so popups open on the **current window** (a new window becomes current on creation, whichever window receives input becomes current, and closing the current one falls back to the last registered). `set_overlay_host(h)` survives as the static form for headless tests.
- **A latent design flaw this batch exposed**: overlay handles used to restart at 1 per host, so "handle 1" meant different layers in different windows. Handles are now process-wide increasing (`overlay_cid()`) plus `overlay_ids()`/`overlay_top_id()`. The symptom was not a compile error but a **test crash**: a dozen call sites in `ui` hardcoded `overlay_root_of(1)`, and once ids accumulated the lookup returned `None` and `children()[0]` aborted with `Array::at` out of bounds (SIGABRT). Rule: **handles are allocated at run time - use the return value or `top_id()`, never a literal**.
- **`raise` is a MoonBit keyword**: `pub fn OverlayHost::raise(...)` does not report "reserved word"; the whole function header fails to parse and the errors cascade into `The value identifier self is unbound` plus `unexpected token if, you may expect pub|fn|...`, which looks like a broken body. Renamed to `raise_layer`; the tell is that the first error points at the header line.
- **The GTK stub must recycle window slots**: `wm_window_new` used to only `wm_next_id++`, so opening and closing secondary windows would exhaust the 32 slots (and `App::open_window` would silently return None). It now takes the first slot with `area == NULL` - `area` is always set at creation and `wm_close` memsets the record, so "closed" and "never used" are equally free.
- **Cross-window injection pitfall: clicking raises another window** (XFCE defaults to click-to-raise). In the first round the "close this window" click seemed dead (`closes=0`), looking like a broken button; in fact the earlier click on the main window had raised it over the About window, so that click landed on the main window. Rule: **`xdotool windowactivate --sync <target>` before every cross-window click**, and judge by an observable state (does `xdotool search --name 关于` still list it) rather than by a counter alone.
- **Two example-side rules**: ① each About window owns its **own Store** (sharing one makes "which window got the typing" meaningless and the exit readouts overwrite each other); ② the smoke self-quit timer moved from 12 s to 25 s - the multi-window flow (open -> click and type in the second window -> close -> reopen) does not fit in 12 s.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/core` **70/70** (8 new headless registry tests: `host_loop` returning the same Loop - proved with `pending()` since `started()` cannot prove identity; primary/secondary bookkeeping and active fallback; the three closing verdicts; `close_window` idempotence and a secondary not stopping the primary; `invalidate` broadcasting; overlays landing on the current window with independent stacks and handles per window; `mount` raising only its own layer; `shutdown` destroying all windows and resetting the registry). `ui` 110/110, `chart` 189/189, `mdview` 26/26, `render` 42/42, `text` 29/29, `input` 10/10, `theme` 8/8, `sys` 163/163, `dbus` 64/64, `yoga-mbt` 96/96 all green; `moon check ./examples/native-app` and `./examples/native-sys` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings. Real-window evidence (`examples/native-app`, new "多窗口（批 A）" panel at the top plus the "关于（第二窗）" window): `NATIVE-APP-MW wins=1 opened=1 closes=1 main_clicks=1 sub_clicks=2 sub_entry_bytes=2 sub_focus=101` and the geometry line `NATIVE-APP-MW-GEO open=688,262,160x32 sub_btn=16,53,464x32 sub_entry=16,85,280x32 sub_close=16,117,464x32`; the injected sequence was "open -> two clicks in the second window -> type and commit in its entry -> the main window's control click -> close the second window -> open another", with `xdotool search --name 关于` going from present to absent while the main window id stayed alive the whole time (**closing one window does not affect the host**), and the About window screenshot showing "本窗点击 2 / 在窗 2" plus the committed text in its entry (**one tree per window, focus stack per window**). **Left for the user's eyes**: repaint while dragging/occluding two windows, and the preedit underline inside the About window.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - single instance and the logind brightness bridge (`dbus`: instance closing + `bridge.mbt`)

- **Single instance = bus-name claiming plus a Wake inbound call**: the first instance takes `RequestName(app_id, DO_NOT_QUEUE)` and gets `PrimaryOwner` (a repeated claim yields the idempotent `AlreadyOwner`); a second instance that gets `AlreadyExists`/`InQueue` instead calls the first instance's `org.moonbitlibyue.Instance.Wake('as' argv)` and exits. The first instance answers the Wake before firing its callback (`is_wake_call` is checked first in `bus.mbt`'s inbound branch - the second instance's `call_sync` has a deadline, so slow work like raising a window must not make it wait). Example evidence: launching a second copy while the first runs prints "已有实例，已唤起并退出" with `exit=0` in the second, and "收到第二实例的唤起" in the first.
- **The brightness write path is wired one way only**: `dbus` depends on `sys` (environment, subprocesses, sysfs reads) and not vice versa, so `bridge.mbt` **injects** the logind capability into sys's two slots at startup (`brightness_set_logind_probe` for the probe, `brightness_set_action` for the `(subsystem, name, value) -> error string` action) and sys never imports D-Bus. Measured here: after injection `@sys.brightness_supported()` flips from the fake default to true (`logind_available=true`), while `brightness_devices()` still reads only sysfs (0 devices on this desktop) - the injection line cleanly separates the read path's and the write path's dependencies.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/dbus` **64/64** (2 new: the brightness gate matching logind's availability after injection, and a repeated same-process name claim yielding `AlreadyOwner`); `moon check ./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` clean; real-window evidence as above (`pool=:1.483`, second instance `exit=0`). **Left for the user on a real machine**: `brightness_set_percent` actually changing the screen needs a machine with a backlight (this host is a desktop with no `/sys/class/backlight` device).

### MoonBit native GUI stack - SNI tray and DBusMenu (`dbus`: sni + icon + instance + filemanager)

- **Migration surface**: the old chain's `traybus` files `sni.mbt` (731 lines, the StatusNotifierItem object side plus a DBusMenu server), `icon.mbt` (75, the program-generated default icon bitmap), `instance.mbt` (221, single-instance name claiming/waking) and `filemanager.mbt` (98, FileManager1 `ShowItems`) ported line by line into `dbus`. **The tray hooks previously stripped from `bus.mbt` came back in the same batch**: the `Conn.items`/`menus` registries, re-registration of every item after a `NameOwnerChanged(arg0=watcher)` watcher restart, carrying tray items along during reconnect migration, and the three-way inbound dispatch (single-instance `Wake` replies first and fires after -> a path hitting `items`/`menus` goes to SNI's `handle_call` -> the `CallReq` fallback last). This is the "foundation strips semantics, capability modules put them back" closing move.
- **Tests came along, `inspect` became `assert`**: `sni_wbtest` (212) / `instance_wbtest` (93) / `filemanager_wbtest` (24), with 40-odd old `inspect(x, content=…)` calls replaced this batch.
- **Real-bus evidence (protocol half of rule 5)**: on this host the XFCE panel (`xfce4-panel`) *is* the StatusNotifierWatcher. After clicking "托盘图标" in the example, the watcher's `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` went from **9 to 10 entries** (the extra one being this process's item), and it is unregistered symmetrically before exit; the `sni_wbtest` live cases create several tray items, verify their paths and menu objects never collide, and confirm both registries are clean after unregistration.
- **A test-environment pitfall (judgement rule first)**: running the create-unregister tray tests repeatedly occasionally yields `托盘注册失败: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.NoReply`, or `NameHasOwner` returning something other than true. The cause is not the code: **a test process has no main loop, so the panel's synchronous property queries to our items can only be serviced incidentally inside a `call_sync` pump**; after several rounds a single xfce4-panel query wedges and subsequent registrations time out. The judgement rule is "a re-run is clean, and `dbus-send` still lists `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems`"; the fix is not to leave the test process unpumped for long (the example's host pumps every 200 ms and never sees this). A temporary debug print was used to pin the branch and then removed.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/dbus` **62/62** (12 new: sni 6 / instance 4 / file manager 2), three consecutive runs with zero skips; `moon check ./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` clean, `moon build` passes; real-window evidence `pool=:1.479`, five-button geometry line `tray=747,391,110x32`, exit code 0. **Left for the user's eyes**: after clicking "托盘图标", does an icon appear in the XFCE notification area, does right-clicking it show the "刷新探测 / 退出" menu, and does clicking the icon increment the "托盘点击" counter in the window.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - D-Bus capability modules, first batch (`dbus`: UPower/logind/network/screensaver/inhibit/MPRIS + notifications)

- **Migration surface**: the old chain's `traybus` files `upower` (197 lines) / `logind` (105) / `netmon` (191) / `screensaver` (94) / `inhibit` (146), plus `yue/system/media.mbt` (707 lines, MPRIS), ported line by line into `dbus` (package prefixes swapped only; media's `pr_run`/`pr_available` now point at `@sys`). **Their tests came along in the same batch, and every old `inspect(x, content=…)` became `assert_eq/assert_true`** - those inspects asserted nothing on the native backend (recorded twice before), so porting them unchanged would have meant "all green, zero coverage"; this batch alone contained 54 of them.
- **MPRIS polling moved to an injected schedule channel**: master's `media_watch_status` hooked `@yue.set_timer` directly (its callback returned a Bool to decide whether to keep ticking); the new stack gives it a `schedule : (Int, () -> Unit) -> Unit` parameter (the GUI layer passes `@core.set_timeout`, headless tests pass a fake channel that collects the callbacks) and re-arms internally via `arm()` - the same layering reason as `clipboard_watch` and the theme probe: a capability package does not know about an event loop.
- **Notifications are new work** (master's implementation lives in the C++ libyue with only a thin MoonBit wrapper, and the TODO lists it as "must be rewritten"): `notify_caps` uses `GetCapabilities` (**no side effects**; the capability list is not translated into an enum, the raw entries stay available), while `notify_send`/`notify_close` use `Notify`/`CloseNotification`. **Progress is not a method of its own** - per the freedesktop spec it means re-sending `Notify` with `replaces_id` set to the same notification and a standard `value` hint (INT32, 0..100; this layer clamps above 100 and treats negatives as "no progress"), which desktop daemons render as a progress bar; `notify_progress` has exactly that shape, and the argument order plus hint shape are pinned by the pure function `nf_notify_args`.
- **Setting-class discipline**: sending a notification really pops a bubble on the desktop, so tests stop at "capability probe + argument construction + error normalisation (unknown method / no service)"; the actual send is left to the button in the example for the user to click (the same rule as open_url/reveal).
- **Example wiring and real-window evidence**: `examples/native-sys` gained a "窗口 / 剪贴板 / D-Bus" panel (session unique name / notification capability count / system bus "not connected (connects on first use)"), a "发通知(带进度)" button, and the host pump (`dbus_set_post_delayed` wrapped around `app.timer`, plus `dbus_pump()` every 200 ms). A full run ends with `pool=:1.434` (the connection is alive the whole time, proving the injected pump really feeds the bus), notification capabilities = 7, exit code 0, and a screenshot confirms all five rows.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/dbus` **50/50** (27 new: UPower 4 / logind 2 / network 3 / screensaver 2 / MPRIS 12 / notifications 4), `moon check ./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` clean. **Left for the user on a real machine**: clicking "发通知(带进度)" should pop a bubble whose progress grows with each click (this host's xfce4-notifyd already reports 7 capabilities over the real bus); and the four monitors (UPower/logind/network/screensaver) need real-world events - plugging/unplugging power, locking the screen, dropping the network - to observe.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - D-Bus foundation (new package `dbus`: wire + dbus + bus + socket stub)

- **Migration surface**: the old chain's `yue/traybus/` `wire.mbt` (833 lines of wire-format codec) and `dbus.mbt` (419 lines, the `GDBusValue` generic call layer) are ported **line by line** (only `sys_f64_to_bits/from_bits` gain new FFI names), while `bus.mbt` (784 lines) is adapted: connect / SASL / Hello / send / receive / dispatch / reconnect all stay, but the **SNI and single-instance semantics embedded in the generic layer are stripped out** (`items`/`menus` fields, the watcher special case, the inbound `Wake` branch) - those come back with their own capability modules.
- **The biggest layering difference: no glib fd watch.** The old chain used `g_unix_fd_add` to hang the bus fd on the GTK main loop and libyue's `PostDelayedTask` for reconnect backoff; this stack bans glib and does not depend on the GUI layer either, so there are two injection points instead: the host calls `dbus_pump()` periodically (default `timeout_ms=0`, a non-blocking poll, fine on every frame/tick), and reconnect backoff goes through `dbus_set_post_delayed(fn(ms, f){...})`. **With neither injected, synchronous calls still work** - `call_sync` and `gdbus_call_*` carry their own `pump_until` pump (handshake, Hello and registration all use it), so a pure CLI scenario needs no event loop at all; the price is that "nobody pumps, no async signals", which the package header states outright.
- **The socket stub uses only standard headers** (`dbus_stub.c`, compiled through native-stub, no link flags written): `socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_STREAM|SOCK_NONBLOCK|SOCK_CLOEXEC)` + `connect` (tolerating `EINPROGRESS`) / `read` / `write` / single-fd `poll` / `close` / `getuid` / Double bit patterns (`memcpy` reinterpretation guarded by a `static_assert` on 8 bytes). Its error-code conventions are **bit-for-bit identical** to the old shim (read: >0 bytes / 0 peer-closed / -1 EAGAIN·EINTR / -2 otherwise; write: >=0 written / -1 not writable yet / -2 otherwise; poll: revents bits 1=IN 4=OUT 8=ERR 16=HUP), so none of the upper-layer branching changed.
- **SASL EXTERNAL needs no explicit credential passing**: the kernel attaches `SCM_CREDENTIALS` at connect time and the server reads `SO_PEERCRED`; the client only sends `AUTH EXTERNAL <hex(uid)>` (the old shim has no `sendmsg` either - this was verified, not assumed).
- **One place the public surface is cleaner than the old chain**: inbound method calls used to require a handler with same-package access to the priv `Conn`/`Msg` in order to reply; the new package exposes `CallReq{bus, sender, path, iface, action, args, reply, reply_error}`, where `reply` **derives its signature from the argument values** (`gdbus_args_wire`), and signal subscribers receive decoded `GDBusValue` arrays only - wire-format value types never leak, so capability modules and outside callers see the same shape.
- **Three toolchain findings (measured this batch)**: (i) struct literal fields use `field: value`; writing `=` gives `Unexpected '=' in struct expression`; (ii) **a function-typed struct field is not a method** - `req.reply_error(...)` fails with `Type CallReq has no method reply_error`, so bind it first (`let f = req.reply_error`) and call that; (iii) `;` is not a comment (nor is `;;`); using it inline triggers a cascade of parse errors.
- **Real-bus evidence (the bus half of rule 5)**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/dbus` **23/23** - session handshake succeeds (local unique name `:1.392`), `ListNames` really returns and contains `org.freedesktop.DBus`, an unknown-method call really yields the D-Bus error name and text, signal `AddMatch` really registers, `dbus_pump` idles without blocking or declaring death, and the system bus `ListNames` returns 96 names. **The panel half (a tray icon actually appearing in the XFCE notification area) and notification popups remain for the user on a real machine**, to be covered by the SNI/notify batches.
- **Later batches**: SNI tray + DBusMenu (with real-panel verification), notifications (`org.freedesktop.Notifications` and `set_progress`), UPower/logind/NetworkManager/screensaver/MPRIS monitors, single instance, and the real logind `SetBrightness` injection for `brightness`.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - cumulative system-capability page (`examples/native-sys`)

- **One page for all of `sys`**: five panels (system & machine / desktop & hardware / apps & file types / windows & clipboard / recent & history) are all **read-only** probes; of the three buttons only "重新探测" (re-probe) is read-only - that is the one injection clicks - while "打开链接" and "打开目录" are setting-class and only act once clicked. Exit prints two lines: `NATIVE-SYS-GEO probe=… open=… reveal=…` (injection points) and `NATIVE-SYS-STAT os=… de=… mon=… wp=… vol=… prof=… mime=… def=… apps=… recent=… hist=… dl=… bm=… ffx=… vsc=… wins=… clip=… probes=… opens=… reveals=… changes=…`, matching the on-screen readings one by one. A run on this host: `os=ubuntu de=XFCE mon=9 wp=ok vol=65 mime=text/plain def=sublime_text.desktop apps=179 recent=156 hist=5 dl=5 bm=166 vsc=1 wins=9 probes=3`, exit code 0.
- **`@core.App`'s root container defaults to Row**: calling only `app.mount([...])` without `app.root.set_column()` lays the panels side by side and pushes them out of the window (the first version of this page had the button's rect at x=1442 in a 1200-wide window, so every injected click missed). `examples/native-app` starts with `root.set_column()`; a new page must do the same, and a panel row that really is horizontal needs an explicit `direction=@yoga.Row`.
- **An injection count lower than the click count is not a lost event**: one re-probe re-reads whole databases (Chrome's `History` is 37 MB here), the UI thread blocks while doing it, and a click arriving too soon is split - press before the block, release during/after it - so it does not count. Judgement rule: three clicks spaced 1.6 s give `probes=3`, clicks inside 1 s give 1 - **injection scripts must leave a beat for heavy work**.
- **How the clipboard watcher is wired**: `@sys.cbw_start_watch(fn(ms, f) { let _ = app.timer(ms, f) }, cb, interval_ms=1000)` - the host supplies the scheduling channel and `sys` never learns about an event loop (headless tests drive it with a fake channel, see the previous entry). This host has no xclip, so the page honestly shows `ClipboardError::Unsupported`; that is not a page defect.
- **Verification**: `moon check ./examples/native-sys --deny-warn` clean, `moon build ./examples/native-sys` passes; `sys` 163/163, `core` 62/62, `ui` 110/110, `chart` 189/189, `mdview` 26/26, `render` 42/42 all green; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged. **Left for the user's eyes**: whether "打开链接" raises the default browser and whether "打开目录" opens `/tmp` in the file manager - injection only proves the button really handed the action to the system call, not the window on the other side.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - system integration, bus-free status trio (`sys`: sysinfo + windowctl + clipboard_watch)

- **The file-reading stub must not size buffers from `ftell` alone (a real bug fixed this batch; it affected every read that went through this stub)**: pseudo-files under `/proc/meminfo`, `/proc/uptime` and `/sys/class/dmi/id/*` report **0 bytes** for `lseek(END)` - their content only appears when actually read. The old shape (`fseek(END)` → `ftell` → allocate that size → `fread`) hit exactly this: pseudo-files returned `Ok("")` (**not an error**), so callers saw a parse failure and blamed the data format. `yue_sys_read_text_file` now uses ftell only as a hint and loops `fread` to EOF (one path for regular files and for tens-of-megabyte SQLite databases), and the two live assertions (MemTotal/MemAvailable, uptime seconds) flipped from red to green. **Judgement rule**: a pseudo-file read that "succeeds but is empty" points at how the length was taken, not at permissions.
- **Window / clipboard / clock capabilities go through commands**: `windowctl` parses `wmctrl -l -p` (list), `-i -a` (activate by id) and `-c` (close by title); `clipboard_watch` polls `xclip -selection clipboard -o` read-only - a missing xclip surfaces as exit code 127 and is normalised to `Unsupported` (matching "tool absent" rather than "command failed"); `sysinfo` only reads `/etc/os-release`, `/proc/meminfo`, `/proc/uptime` and `/sys/class/dmi/id/`. Activating, closing and watching are setting-class: white-box tests cover command construction and output parsing only, the actions stay with the caller.
- **The clipboard watcher's timer is injected by the caller**: master's `cbw_start_watch` hooked libyue's `set_timer` directly (no cancel id; it stops via a parking flag). `sys` does not import the GUI core layer, so scheduling became a parameter `schedule : (Int, () -> Unit) -> Unit`; the GUI layer passes `fn(ms, f) { let _ = @core.set_timeout(ms, f) }` while a headless test passes a fake channel that collects each tick's callback - which makes re-arming and "stop prevents the next arm" testable tick by tick (same layering rationale as the theme batch's "probe passed in as a function").
- **Six bus-dependent modules stay on the DBus line**: `power` (UPower), `session` (logind/screensaver), `online` (NetworkManager), `keepawake` (logind inhibit), `powerctl` (`gdbus_call_system`) and locale_sys's localed half (the `timedatectl` half is bus-free).
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **163/163** (36 new: sysinfo 14 including two live `/proc` assertions; windowctl 14 with a real `wmctrl -l -p` list of 8 windows; clipboard_watch 8 covering fake-channel tick dispatch, stop preventing the next arm, and the missing-xclip normalisation). `sys` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - system integration, history family (`sys`: browser history / bookmarks / Firefox / VS Code)

- **The SQLite dependency must be declared at package level**: `prowk/moonsqlitefile` is already in `moon.mod`'s `import`, yet using `@moonsqlitefile.*` from `sys` failed with `Package "moonsqlitefile" not found in the loaded packages` - **a module-level import does not substitute for a package-level one**, so `sys/moon.pkg` needs `"prowk/moonsqlitefile" @moonsqlitefile`. The old-chain `yue/moon.pkg` has no such line (those files sit inside the repo-wide red-dot backlog), so copying the old chain's package config walks off a cliff.
- **Reading SQLite needs a raw-bytes entry**: added `sys_read_bytes_file`, sharing the same stub call as `sys_read_text_file` (`yue_sys_read_text_file` already slurps the file into `Bytes`; the difference is only whether MoonBit decodes it). The text version cannot feed SQLite: decoding with `decode_lossy` and re-encoding is not byte-identical (invalid UTF-8 sequences get eaten by replacement characters), and the database page header fails immediately.
- **The time bases differ per source**: Chrome/Edge/Brave `History` uses **microseconds since 1601-01-01 UTC** (constant `11644473600000`, `<=0` means unknown → 0, sub-microsecond truncated); bookmark `date_added` uses the same base but stored as a **numeric string**; VS Code entries use Unix milliseconds. Mixing them yields dates in 1601 or 50 millennia ahead.
- **Profile probing is "collect only what exists, without reading the database"**: directory enumeration only looks for an entry named `History`/`Bookmarks`, and only the data path reads the matched database in full - listing profiles no longer slurps tens of megabytes just to test existence. Known boundary: this layer has no way to enumerate profile directories, so it probes fixed candidate names (`Default` + `Profile 1..24`) and misses custom-named profiles.
- **Live end-to-end actually runs now** (new strict smoke for `bh_browsers`/`bh_history`/`bh_downloads`): Chrome's 37 MB `History` is really read, two profiles are hit, returned timestamps land in 2026, the ordering invariant (time descending, ties by url ascending) holds, and the `downloads` table reads; VS Code's `state.vscdb` plus recents run end to end; this host has no Firefox (`~/.mozilla/firefox/*/places.sqlite` absent) so it takes the legitimate skip branch. **Same discipline as the previous batch**: a detected database that fails to read must `fail`; `Err(_) => ()` is only allowed when the host genuinely has no such data.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **127/127** (41 new: browser history 12, Firefox 10, bookmarks 9, VS Code 10, each combining pure-function assertions with a live smoke). `sys` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - desktop integration, third part (`sys`: recent files / recently-used.xbel)

- **Data surface**: the GTK family (Thunar / Nautilus / GIO, xdg-desktop-portal-gtk) all append to `$XDG_DATA_HOME/recently-used.xbel` (default `~/.local/share/`); this layer **only reads, never writes**. The parser is a hand-written thin scanner (it only takes attributes off `bookmark` plus `bookmark:application` / `mime:mime-type`), and everything error-prone lives in three pure functions: XML entity decoding (named entities plus `&#NN;`/`&#xHH;`, with bad codepoints and surrogate values kept literally), URI percent-decoding (consecutive `%XX` groups as UTF-8, while `100%` / `%E` / `%zz` stay literal) and ISO 8601 → Unix milliseconds (first three fractional digits, no timezone means UTC, leap-day validation). This host really yields **156 items**.
- **`inspect(x, content="…")` in the old wbtests asserts nothing on the native backend** (recorded once before; this batch is the second scene): ported as-is the suite goes "all green, zero coverage". Conversion rules: booleans → `assert_true`/`assert_false`; numbers → `assert_eq`, and **Int64 literals need the `L` suffix** (XBEL millisecond timestamps exceed Int's width, `assert_eq(items[0].added, 1776757238510)` does not typecheck); a debug array text like `[1, 2, 3]` becomes an array literal; `content="<should parse ok>"` is not an assertion at all but the caption of an error branch, so it becomes a `println` plus an explicit failing assertion.
- **A smoke case's error branch must not skip silently**: the old `rf_recent` / `rf_by_app` shape was `Err(_) => ()` ("no data on this host, so skip"), which also hides the real regression of "the file exists but cannot be read". It is now `Err(e) => { assert_false(rf_supported()); println(...) }` - the Err branch is only legal when this host genuinely has no such file.
- **Two XBEL tolerance semantics pinned by tests**: a fake `<bookmark>` hidden inside a comment is not collected (`skip_declaration` walks to `-->`); when a bookmark carries two `mime:mime-type` nodes **the first wins**; an XBEL truncated mid-write only collects the already-closed entries.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **86/86** (13 new: entity decoding, ISO conversion with leap days, calendar day counts, percent decoding, URI restoration, the full XBEL sample's six cases, non-XBEL and truncated input, mapping and tie-ordering, exact per-application filtering, `rf_take` bounds, `rf_data_dir`'s three states, and two live smoke reads). `sys` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - desktop integration, second half (`sys`: wallpaper + default apps, plus a child-process environment fix)

- **`pr_env_blob` was not passing DISPLAY / DBus, so desktop-side commands could only fail in a child process (this single line is why the previous batch's monitor tests had to skip)**: [pr_run] builds the child environment from a whitelist (previously only `PATH` / `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` / `HOME` plus `LC_ALL=C`); `xrandr --query` exits 1 without `DISPLAY`, and `xfconf-query` / `gsettings` cannot start without `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`. After passing through `DISPLAY` / `XAUTHORITY` / `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` / `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, on **the same host** the monitor cases really read DP-0..5 plus HDMI-0 (primary, 1920x1080) and HDMI-2, and the read-only wallpaper smoke test reads `/usr/share/xfce4/backdrops/Dolomites_by_Felix_Hartlieb.jpg`. **Judgement rule**: if a command works in the shell but exits non-zero through `pr_run`, the whitelist is missing a variable — do not blame the tool version first.
- **Wallpaper property selection is tied to connected outputs**: XFCE's `last-image` is picked in three passes ("workspace0 on a connected output → first workspace0 → first parseable"), the first using [mon_list]'s connected set to discard stale entries left by unplugged monitors; when xrandr is unavailable it degrades to "first workspace0". The write path picks its target property by the same rule (the same "drawing and hit-testing share one source" discipline as the chart batch) and creates the `monitorNone-1` fallback entry when nothing exists. The third pass **no longer considers connection**, it just takes the first parseable entry in list order.
- **The default-app write path really writes and reads back in tests**: `da_set_default` lands in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/mimeapps.list`, so the test points `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` at `/tmp/da_wbtest_config` (save / change / assert / restore) and asserts three states (missing file → new section; same mime re-set → the original line is replaced with no second line left; another mime → appended inside the section). The user's real configuration is untouched while the whole read-write chain is exercised.
- **Two toolchain findings (measured this batch)**: (i) **same-named constructors across several enums in one package need disambiguation** — `Unsupported`/`CommandFailed`/`ParseFailed` collide between `WallpaperError`/`MonitorError`/`VolumeError`/`FileManagerError`/`AppFindError`; writing them bare in a test gives 4124 (`The constructor ... is ambiguous`), so tests must use `WallpaperError::ParseFailed(...)`. Inside the implementation files `match self { Unsupported => ... }` infers the type and needs no change. (ii) **A payload-free enum does not get `Show` either**: `println("\{de}")` does not compile (`Type DesktopEnv does not implement trait Show`), so diagnostics go through the dedicated `desktop_name()`.
- **Verification**: `moon test -p NoahLiu/native-gui/sys` **73/73** (14 new: wallpaper 9 — the three-pass selection over this host's real xfconf property list, the `monitorNone-1` fallback, gsettings/KDE output parsing and argument construction, JS single-quote escaping, text helpers, argument guards and `Show` texts, read-only smoke on the live desktop; default apps 5 — mimeapps section parsing with non-deduplicated repeated keys, the four merge cases, association merge/dedup/ordering, a real write/read round trip against a temporary `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and the live xdg-mime probe). `sys` clean under `--deny-warn`; whole-repo backlog still 287 errors / 68 warnings, unchanged. **`wp_set` is a setting-class call**: this batch only verifies argument construction and does not change the desktop appearance (same discipline as master); changing the wallpaper is left for the user on a real machine.

## Linux

### Distributions

#### Ubuntu 24.04 ✅ mainline

- System dependencies: `build-essential cmake pkg-config libgtk-3-dev libpango1.0-dev libfontconfig1-dev libx11-dev libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev`.
- AppIndicator runtime is gone, so libyue's built-in tray is unusable → replaced by `yue/traybus/` (pure-MoonBit SNI direct to the panel).
- The webkit2gtk package name varies by distribution (4.0 / 4.1); prepare.py probes with pkg-config and accepts either.

#### Other distributions ❓ untested

- First porting step: check each dependency's pkg-config name, then run prepare.py.

### Desktop environments (tray / menu behavior)

#### XFCE ✅

- Autostart .desktop findings: the Exec path comes from readlink on /proc/self/exe (during development it points into the _build output, so the entry breaks as soon as the artifact moves — only a fixed install path is reliable; AppImage likewise points at its mount point); the full file passes desktop-file-validate with zero warnings; the enabled check honors both system-side disable switches (Hidden=true and X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false), and disable is idempotent (a missing entry counts as success).

- Context menus are rendered by the panel mirroring DBusMenu; the app is not asked to draw via SNI ContextMenu.
- xfce4-panel 4.18 sends only the batch `EventGroup` / `AboutToShowGroup`, not the singular versions: implementing only the singular forms gets silently rejected by UnknownMethod — the menu opens but clicks do nothing.
- Desktop notifications must go through `Notification::Show()`; `NotificationCenter::AddNotification` never issues the DBus Notify call on Linux and fails silently.
- Global shortcuts are exclusive XGrabKey registrations; a taken key combination makes Register return -1 (no crash, silent failure) — callers must check and offer another key.
- With desktop focus, xfwm4 captures the keyboard and global shortcuts do not fire (they work when an application window has focus).

#### GNOME ✅ (X11 and Wayland sessions)

- The AppIndicator extension reads the Menu property at registration time to build its proxy: an empty menu returning `/` permanently kills the menu client (clicking the icon does nothing). traybus always returns the real `/MenuBar`, exporting it even when empty, filled via LayoutUpdated.
- With `ItemIsMenu=false`, both left and right clicks emit Activate.
- Global shortcuts segfault on Wayland sessions (upstream casts the root window through a GDK X11 macro): a `GDK_IS_X11_DISPLAY` guard is in place; non-X11 sessions get Register = -1.
- Mouse button semantics are yue-unified 1=left 2=right 3=middle (GDK's 2/3 are swapped); components judge by yue semantics.
- Environment variables for launching GUI apps over SSH: X11 session `XAUTHORITY=/run/user/1000/gdm/Xauthority`; Wayland session `XAUTHORITY=/run/user/1000/.mutter-Xwaylandauth.*` and `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-0`; both need `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS=unix:path=/run/user/1000/bus`.

#### KDE ✅ (Plasma 5.27)

- The panel consumes SNI directly; new icons go straight into the visible tray area; tray / menu / quit all pass.
- Protocol behavior is the opposite of XFCE: only singular `Event` / `AboutToShow`, no batch versions; traybus implements both.

#### Deepin ✅ (23 / 25, DDE)

- dde-dock implements StatusNotifierWatcher; SNI direct connection works; new icons land in the collapsed tray area by default and can be dragged out.
- libyue's Popover (transparent window + pointer grab) does not render on DDE and slows the pointer: the shim checks `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` (23=DDE, 25=Deepin) and falls back to a borderless plain window.
- Binaries built on the host (Ubuntu 24.04) run directly: the glibc symbol ceiling is 2.38 and deepin ships the webkit2gtk-4.1 runtime; for cross-distribution shipping, check the glibc symbol requirement first (`objdump -T | grep GLIBC_`).
- `TextEdit::Delete()` deletes the selection, it does not clear; use `set_text("")` to clear.
- DDE clipboard-manager interactions produce occasional CHECK / CRITICAL log noise; functionality is unaffected.

#### KDE / MATE / Cinnamon / Budgie / LXQt ❓ untested

- All support SNI at the protocol level and traybus implements the protocol; awaiting per-environment verification.

### DBus wire protocol (traybus)

- The DBus array length prefix excludes alignment padding before the first element; counting it makes dbus-daemon disconnect as a protocol violation.
- The variant signature in the header SIGNATURE field is "g" (u8 length-encoded); encoding it as "s" passes unit tests but real buses reject it.
- The SNI Menu property must always return the real menu object path — never `/`, even when empty.
- Unit-test self-consistency ≠ interop: protocol issues are located with dbus-monitor on the real bus; GNOME panel-side exceptions show up in journalctl.
- Single-instance RequestName must carry flags=4 (DO_NOT_QUEUE): the default 0 queues the request, so the second instance's already-running check hangs until the first instance exits — semantics fully broken. Real-bus findings (Ubuntu 24.04 XFCE): reply 3 = held by another connection (instance exists → wake it and exit); after SIGKILL of the first instance the bus reclaims the name automatically and a new connection gets reply 1 (becomes the primary); reply 4 = this connection already owns it (idempotent). The full chain (RequestName flags=4 → EXISTS → Wake('as') → RETURN) was verified by dbus-monitor capture on the real bus: Wake to RETURN in 39µs; after SIGKILL of the first instance a third instance claims normally.

- The second-instance wake dispatch must hook Conn::handle's kind==1 branch in bus.mbt before sni.mbt's handle_call: all inbound calls funnel into handle_call, and without the interception Wake lands in the UnknownMethod fallback so the first instance never sees it. Wake naming convention: interface org.moonbitlibyue.Instance, object path /org/moonbitlibyue/Instance, member Wake ('as' = the second instance's command line, passed through moonbitlang/core/env args(), including argv[0]; callers decide what to keep).
- FileManager1 (reveal-in-file-manager) findings on XFCE: the NameHasOwner probe works; ShowItems wire format is "ass" (array of file URIs + an empty startup id); file URI percent-hex uses uppercase (RFC 3986 allows either case, uppercase is the convention), unreserved characters (A-Za-z0-9-._~) and '/' stay raw, everything else is %XX-encoded per UTF-8 byte — locked by unit tests. When the service is offline or the call fails, the fallback is xdg-open on the parent directory — selection is lost, a semantic downgrade that the usage doc must state.
- External-open spawns go through g_spawn_async (G_SPAWN_SEARCH_PATH + stdout/stderr to DEV_NULL); glib reaps the child automatically, no zombies; no shell involved, argv passed directly. xdg-open's behavior for nonexistent paths or unopenable URLs varies by desktop, so the library's Ok only means "handed to the system" — the platform-side outcome does not travel back.
- Screen-saver inhibition (Inhibit/UnInhibit) picks the service by real NameHasOwner presence, not by desktop-name guessing: the candidate list is [org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver, org.xfce.ScreenSaver]; measured on Ubuntu 24.04 XFCE only org.xfce.ScreenSaver is online (held by xfce4-screensaver), org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver is unheld. The two are interface-isomorphic: object path and interface name derive from the service name by dot→slash, Inhibit("ss" = app name + reason) -> u cookie, UnInhibit("u" = the original cookie) must match bit for bit (real-bus capture: Inhibit returned cookie 1516211641, UnInhibit 2 seconds later carried the same value, empty successful reply). GNOME / KDE service holdings pending real-machine notes.
- System bus (measured Ubuntu 24.04): socket is /run/dbus/system_bus_socket — connecting by this default with DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS unset works; when the variable is explicitly set, take the first unix:path= among the comma-separated entries, and if none matches (e.g. a tcp: address) return an explicit Err carrying the original text — silently falling back to the default socket would reconnect to a bus that really has UPower, turning the degradation test into a false pass. BecomeMonitor is rejected on the system bus (both dbus-monitor and busctl monitor), so capture is unavailable; verification goes through the probe's own round trips plus busctl call to cross-check reply shapes.
- Multi-bus connections coexisting (B5 infrastructure): the shim's fd watch went from a process-wide single slot (each watch overwriting the previous) to an fd→callback dispatch table plus unwatch; the MoonBit side routes fd→Conn through a registry. Dual connections (session SNI + system UPower) verified to coexist without clobbering. When a glib source callback returns 0, glib destroys the source itself — the shim table entry must be erased in step (otherwise a later unwatch calls g_source_remove on a dead id and warns).
- Wire-level 'd' (DOUBLE) and 't' (UINT64) must land as a set: in a real UPower GetAll reply UpdateTime is 't' and Percentage/Energy are 'd'; missing 't' sent the variant's unknown-signature old defensive branch ("return empty string but leave the read position alone"), misaligning every subsequent element and aborting inside the read_string slice (unit tests stayed green — self-crafted shapes never hit it). The defense is now two layers: unknown variant signatures push pos to the message end (parsing degrades to garbage values instead of misalignment), and read_string/read_sig gained bounds checks. Adding a type is an eight-site whole-chain change: DVal/Sig/sig_align/parse_one_sig/sig_of/sig_char/encode/decode_in.
- Double↔IEEE 754 bit conversion lives in the shim (moonbitlang/core has no Double::to_bits/from_bits — confirmed): yue_mbt_sys_f64_to_bits/from_bits are pure bit reinterpretations (static_assert sizeof(double)==8), and the wire layer's 'd' piggybacks on the Int64 8-byte little-endian read/write. Note: once traybus's whitebox test target references such externs, its link needs -lyue_mbt — but link_configs propagate to "targets depending on the package", and traybus does not depend on yue (it's the reverse), so prebuild.py must carry a separate identical entry for NoahLiu/native-gui/yue/traybus (the single static-lib member references the whole gtk set, so trimmed flags won't link).
- UPower reading path (measured Ubuntu 24.04, desktop machine): DisplayDevice (/org/freedesktop/UPower/devices/DisplayDevice, interface org.freedesktop.UPower.Device) aggregates the main battery — IsPresent=false → Ok(None); mind the interface-name split: devices are …UPower.Device, the toplevel is …UPower, and PropertiesChanged's arg0 filter naturally keeps device-level signals out of toplevel subscriptions. OnBattery lives on the toplevel object; power-source events subscribe to toplevel PropertiesChanged and parse the changed dict directly in the callback (signal dispatch sits on the drain stack — call_sync inside a callback would re-enter the receive path; forbidden).
- Disconnect self-healing (B5, not yet exercised by a real disconnection): mark_dead is idempotent (keyed on the fd registry), reconnects via the shim's post_delayed_task (extern'd directly, bypassing the traybus→yue reverse dependency) after 500ms, at most 3 attempts; on success it replays AddMatch rules, migrates the subscription table and tray items, and re-registers them (during the disconnect the watcher already dropped the old registrations when our unique name vanished). In a pure-CLI context (main loop not running) the reconnect callback never fires and gives up naturally.
- **Multi-item DBusMenu object paths must be derived from the item path (CORE2, whitebox + real-bus regression)**: the SNI Menu property used to return `/MenuBar` unconditionally, and the menus registry (menu object path → Item) collided on that key — creating a second tray item did `c.menus.set("/MenuBar", item2)`, silently replacing the first item's menu route, so the panel only ever saw the last registered item's menu (the first item's layout queries landed on the second item or hit UnknownObject); unregistering could not clean the stale key either. Fix: `menu_path_of(item_path)` derives the path (`/StatusNotifierItem` → `/MenuBar`, `/StatusNotifierItem_N` → `/MenuBar_N`, the first item keeps the ksni convention), unregister and the create-failure path clear both the items and menus tables symmetrically, and `Conn::next_item_path` now scans for the first free path (the old length()+1 numbering reused an occupied path after a middle item was unregistered, colliding with its menu route too). Real-bus regression (sni_wbtest, auto-skips without a panel watcher): two items get distinct paths/menu paths, `gdbus_call_session(own unique name, each menu path, GetLayout)` returns each item's own labels (never the other's), both tables are empty after unregistering, and a fresh item restarts from `/StatusNotifierItem`; with the pre-fix code stashed in (menu_path_of always `/MenuBar`) the related cases fail precisely. **The panel-side behavior of two icons each opening their own menu still needs on-machine visual review** (AGENTS rules 5/6).
- **Disconnect self-healing must not unconditionally open a new connection (CORE2, whitebox + real-bus regression)**: `try_reconnect` used to call connect_session/connect_system and overwrite g_conn/g_sys_conn as soon as it found a saved connection — if an on-demand path (sni_available / Item::create / system_conn) had already rebuilt a live connection after the disconnect, that connection plus its fd and glib fd watch leaked (the g_conns registry routes by fd, and after the overwrite nothing could unwatch/close it), with subscriptions and tray items split across two connections so signals only reached the orphaned one.
