- **Key mapping aligned with GDK**: the character face is taken in `WM_KEYDOWN` via `ToUnicode` (ASCII/Latin-1 verbatim, otherwise `0x01000000|unicode`, matching `gdk_unicode_to_keyval`, so both of `keysym_to_text`'s Latin-1/UCS paths accept it); **for Ctrl combos the `VK_CONTROL` bit must be removed from the `GetKeyboardState` snapshot before conversion** — with the control bit present ToUnicode yields control characters (`\x01`) and the kernel's Ctrl+A check (`keysym=='a'`) breaks; cleared, it matches GDK's "keyval is the base character, modifiers ride in mods" semantics. AltGr (Ctrl+Alt) therefore cannot reach special characters — deferred to the IME/later batches. Dead keys (`ToUnicode` returning negative) push no event; composition happens naturally on the next key via layout state. Alt+F4 is handed back to `DefWindowProc` to produce `WM_CLOSE` (same semantics as "the window manager requests close"); other system keys (F10 menu, Alt+Space) are swallowed.
- **Blitting needs no byte swap**: `render`'s Bitmap is cairo ARGB32 (little-endian bytes B,G,R,A premultiplied), which is byte-compatible with GDI's 32bpp `BI_RGB` top-down DIB (negative biHeight) — `StretchDIBits` blits directly; present only records the pointer + `InvalidateRect`, the actual blit happens in `WM_PAINT` (same shape as the GTK stub's "blit only in the draw callback", avoiding the dead-window window). `WM_ERASEBKGND` returns 1 to prevent flicker.
- **Loop recipe**: wait with `MsgWaitForMultipleObjectsEx(0,NULL,timeout,QS_ALLINPUT,MWMO_INPUTAVAILABLE)` (returns immediately when undispatched messages exist, negative timeout = INFINITE); cross-thread wakeup is an `HWND_MESSAGE` message-only window + `PostMessageW(WM_APP+1)`; the pump drains with `PeekMessageW` + `DispatchMessageW` and deliberately does **no TranslateMessage** (WM_CHAR is not consumed; the character face is taken in WM_KEYDOWN).
- **Slot and creation ordering**: `WM_SIZE` arrives during `CreateWindowW`, so the slot id must land in `WM_CREATE` via `CREATESTRUCT.lpCreateParams` (setting `GWLP_USERDATA` only after the call drops the first WM_SIZE); client-area semantics via `AdjustWindowRect` (frame stretched so the client area is exactly width×height). DPI via `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext` per-monitor v2 (through GetProcAddress so old systems missing the export don't fail to load), whole stack in physical pixels, `WM_DPICHANGED` repositions by the suggested rect with WM_SIZE doing the bookkeeping.
- **Verification** (2026-10-11, this machine): `moon build examples/native-window` links the full chain; smoke `NATIVE-WINDOW-OK frames=45` (5s timer exit, exit 0, 45 presented frames ⇒ present→WM_PAINT→StretchDIBits works); the core.App host shape verified by a delete-after-run probe printing `WIN-SMOKE-OK` (App::new + declarative mount + timer + clean exit); core 62/62 still green (tests never call App::new, so no real windows pop up), whole-repo check 287/68 unchanged. band_h=0/0/0/0 is the placeholder text backend (W5).

### WebView2 webview stub and ffmpeg real linking on Windows (platform batch, 2026-10-13)

- **WebView2 custom schemes must be pre-registered at Environment creation**: navigating to a custom scheme not registered via `ICoreWebView2EnvironmentOptions4::SetCustomSchemeRegistrations` fails outright (failure code 9 on a real machine, later 8). The MoonBit-side `webview_predeclare_schemes` list flows through a new extern into a global list in the C stub; `wb_create` passes a hand-written `WbOptions` (four nested sub-interface COM object) and the engine collects the list while creating the Environment.
- **A hand-written COM propget/propput missing the `self` parameter silently corrupts the vtable on x64**: declaring `get_bool(BOOL *value)` with one parameter — the engine calls it with `(self, &value)` per the COM contract; on x64 RCX (=self) is read as value, so `*value = FALSE` actually zeroes the first 8 bytes of the object (lpVtbl!), and every later call on that interface jumps a NULL vtable = segfault. put_str/put_bool share the disease (missing this read, harmless on x64). **C does no signature checking on vtable slots (only a C4113 warning)** — count the parameter list of every hand-written COM method against the header.
- **The array returned from `GetCustomSchemeRegistrations` must be `CoTaskMemAlloc`-allocated**: the contract is that the caller (loader) frees it with `CoTaskMemFree` — handing over a static array pointer means the loader frees it and the heap manager raises `0xC0000374` (heap corruption), crashing inside the engine right after get_regs returns with nothing on the stack pointing at registrations. Debugging used AddVectoredExceptionHandler (exception code/address/module) plus HeapWalk full-heap validation (stderr is unbuffered; stdout buffers are lost on segfault).
- **MSVC reads BOM-less UTF-8 sources using the system code page (GBK) by default** (C4819): UTF-8 bytes of Chinese comments and ffmpeg headers get misread — when the last byte of a comment line pairs with the newline into one GBK double-byte char, the next line is swallowed into the comment, cascading into hundreds of "illegal storage class / undefined identifier" errors. Fix: add `/utf-8` to `stub-cc-flags` (the vendored ffmpeg headers hit this too — rational.h reported C4819).
- **moon prebuild `vars` + moon.pkg `${build.VAR}` substitution works** (verified on 0.1.20260920): prebuild emits `{"vars": {"FFMPEG_INC": "..."}, "link_configs": [...]}` and moon.pkg declares `link: { "native": { "stub-cc-flags": "/utf-8 -I${build.FFMPEG_INC}" } }` — vendor header paths get injected into stub compilation without static hard-coding; `stub-cc-flags` only affects native-stub compilation. Platforms that do not emit the variable must still provide a placeholder value (`/usr/include` or `.`) so `${...}` never resolves to nothing.
- **ffmpeg dev-content vendor policy**: from BtbN `ffmpeg-n9.0-latest-win64-lgpl-shared-9.0`, the include headers + 7 import libraries + LGPLv3 LICENSE total 2.5MB committed under `modules/ffmpeg-mbt/vendor/` (**choose lgpl-shared over gpl-shared** — LGPL obligations are minimal for dynamic linking); runtime DLLs (150MB) stay out of the repo — place next to the exe or on PATH. `ffmpeg_stub.c` drops the `_WIN32` all-failure stub branch; all three platforms share one implementation (the ffmpeg API is cross-platform and the original Linux branch had no POSIX-specific calls).
- **miniaudio needs zero extra link libraries on Windows**: WASAPI/COM/dsound/winmm are all runtime LoadLibrary'd — `gui-media/prebuild.py` emitting empty link_configs is the correct shape (not a fallback). gui-media passes 24/24 including the AudioPlayer smoke (pump 33 ticks playing a 1-second asset — the WASAPI device chain actually produced sound); ffmpeg-mbt passes 6/6 (real ogg/m4a/wav + video decoding).
- **Unattended shutdown for GUI-subsystem examples**: `native-web` has no self-exit timer (manual acceptance walks through every section), so automation must use `taskkill /IM` (sends WM_CLOSE; statistics print after app.run() returns and get flushed) — `kill -9` loses the stdout buffer too.

### WebView2 cross-HWND focus and wheel forwarding (focus batch, 2026-10-13)

- **GotFocus/LostFocus live on the Controller, not on ICoreWebView2**: `add_GotFocus/add_LostFocus` are methods of `ICoreWebView2Controller`; and for this event pair the `Invoke` sender parameter is `ICoreWebView2Controller*` (the event-handler macro hard-codes sender as `ICoreWebView2*`, which on x64 means real crashes beyond C4113) — these two handlers are hand-written, not macro-expanded.
- **Wheel messages are delivered to the focused window regardless of mouse position**, so once WebView2 takes focus the host's scroll areas go dead. Fix: two halves. (1) The stub subclasses the engine input window (`Chrome_WidgetWin_1`, found by class name under the panel HWND via FindWindowEx right after the controller create completes; not finding it only loses half the forwarding and is not an error) — when focus is in the page but the mouse is elsewhere, wheel messages are SendMessage'd back to the host root window; (2) the host's `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` branch first asks via a wheel probe (registered by the web stub at create time through the `wm_set_wheel_probe` function-pointer slot, avoiding a reverse win→web symbol dependency) whether the screen coordinate lands on a web page, and forwards the message verbatim to the Chrome window if so. Together the two halves reproduce the GTK "hover scrolls" behavior. Subclassing stores the original proc and self HWND via SetProp; destroy restores and RemoveProps.
- **Host-driven focus handover**: `MoveFocus(COREWEBVIEW2_MOVE_FOCUS_REASON_PROGRAMMATIC)` (WebKit branch: `gtk_widget_grab_focus`); the GotFocus event fires afterwards, so the host can react with UI state.
- **Focus events are exposed as kinds 16/17 (WebView2-only)**: WebKitGTK is same-tree widgets where the host perceives focus natively, so it does not emit them.
- **Debugging incident (lesson)**: a one-line `python -c` script passing a bad `newline` value to `io.open(p,'w')` on an existing large file raises ValueError during TextIOWrapper init — but the file is already truncated empty by the 'w' open, losing hundreds of lines (recovered via git checkout and redone with the Edit tool). Never rewrite large files with inline python one-liners; use the Edit tool or write the script to a file first.

### MoonBit native GUI stack - image decode backend (W9: WIC on Windows)

- **Why this is the single exception to the platform matrix**: Windows rendering is a **pure MoonBit rasterizer** (zero third-party deps, per the plan matrix), but a rasterizer does not decode — image decoding has to borrow a system capability, **WIC** (`windowscodecs.lib`, an OS component, not a third-party package). The non-Linux branch previously stubbed the whole `img_*` set as no-ops, so on Windows `Image::new_from_file` always returned an empty image and the animated-GIF widget collapsed to zero size; the test layer skipped those cases behind `decode_available()` / `ui_decode_guard()` probes (so they were never red). This batch implements the `_WIN32` branch of `gui/render/stub.c`.
- **Three measured WIC facts** (proven by the pure-C probe `experiment/img_probe`, no MoonBit runtime involved):
  1. **WIC's GIF decoder hands back raw sub-rectangle frames and does not composite for you** — `IWICBitmapFrameDecode::GetSize` reports the **frame rect** (12x12 in the probe), with the offset in `/imgdesc/Left|Top` (14/14 measured). GdkPixbuf's `GdkPixbufAnimationIter` on Linux does that compositing for us, which is why this difference is new here: full-canvas compositing is our job — the canvas is the logical screen from `/logscrdesc/Width|Height`, and each frame is closed out by its disposal method (0/1 keep, 2 clear the frame rect, 3 restore the snapshot taken *before* that frame was drawn).
  2. **The transparent index is already applied by WIC** (frame alphas measured at only 0 and 255). GIF transparency means "keep the previous frame here" rather than blending, so the compositing rule is simply "skip alpha==0, otherwise overwrite the whole pixel" — no alpha blending involved.
  3. **`/grctlext/Delay` is in centiseconds** (per the GIF spec), so multiply by 10 for milliseconds; missing/zero falls back to 100 ms, same rule as the Linux branch.
- **Premultiplication matches the Linux branch word for word**: after converting to `GUID_WICPixelFormat32bppBGRA` (straight), this layer premultiplies with `(c*a+127)/255` before handing pixels to MoonBit — the same formula as the Linux branch, which keeps "encode -> decode -> re-encode" byte-stable (the semi-transparent round-trip assertion in `image_wbtest` depends on it). The static first-frame size rule also follows Linux: **a GIF is composited at logical-screen size**, so a sub-rect first frame is not cropped down to the frame rect.
- **Memory strategy for animation**: pixels are decoded **on demand** (N frames are not all cached) and the composited result is cached in a canvas, so repeatedly fetching the same frame (once per repaint tick) does not re-decode; advancing one frame is incremental (only the previous frame's disposal is closed out), while wrapping or jumping replays from frame 0. The 4096-frame cap only guards against pathological files (memory is independent of frame count, but the wrap tick is O(n)).
- **Frame numbering keeps Linux's monotonic rule**: `img_anim_frame()` only ever increases (the displayed frame is `frame % count`). As a result the widget-side `gif(looping=false)` stop condition ("frame number back to 0") **never fires on either platform** — that is pre-existing behavior and `image_wbtest`'s "frame number equals number of successful advances" assertion depends on it, so this batch does not change it. One asset fact worth noting: the four frames of `examples/showcase/assets/dot.gif` (used by showcase) have **byte-identical** compressed data and differ only in their local color tables (so it reads as "the same dot changing color" rather than moving).
- **Link surface**: the Windows branch of `gui/scripts/prebuild.py` gains a **RENDER_PACKAGE** entry (`windowscodecs.lib ole32.lib user32.lib`) — previously the render package had no link_configs at all on Windows (every stub was a no-op needing no external symbols), so `render.whitebox_test.exe` would hit unresolved symbols the moment it calls WIC. The GUID constants (`CLSID_WICImagingFactory1` / `IID_IWICImagingFactory` / `GUID_WICPixelFormat32bppBGRA`) come from the same place as the W20f icon loading; C mode cannot use `__uuidof`, so they are resolved at link time.
- **No `/utf-8` needed**: under MSVC's GBK misreading of the Chinese comments, the render stub emits only 2 C4819 warnings (byte-for-byte the same as before the change; `win/stub.c` emits 6), with no swallowed lines and no syntax errors — so no `stub-cc-flags` is introduced (the ffmpeg-mbt one exists because its vendored headers genuinely trip).
- **Verification**: the pure-C probe `experiment/img_probe/img_backend_probe.c` (independent of the moon toolchain, compiled directly with `cl /W4 /utf-8`, including `render/stub.c` so it can call the `img_*` entry points) ends with **`IMG-BACKEND-OK`** across 60 assertions: PNG file/memory entry points agree on size and premultiplication never overflows, garbage/NULL/zero-length/out-of-range handle/missing file all return 0, `dot.gif` static first frame is 120x120, the animation reports 4 frames with a 400 ms delay, advancing is monotonic, different frames really do differ, and after wrapping the displayed frame is byte-identical to frame 0; free is idempotent and everything is a no-op afterwards; a PNG opened through the animation entry reports `is_static=1` and never advances; and **sub-rectangle compositing** (`experiment/img_probe/assets/subrect.gif`, frame 1 is 12x12 at (14,14)) keeps the canvas at 40x40 with (0,0) still holding frame 0's black and the box tinted red. To run it: compile the exe with `cl`, then execute `experiment/img_probe/img_backend_probe.exe .` from the repo root (the asset rebuild script `make_asset.py` regenerates subrect.gif with ffmpeg). `moon check` on the library module is warning-free.
- **Not done in this batch, and not pretended otherwise**: the macOS backend (still a stub); the "frame number wraps to 0" semantics for `looping=false` (pre-existing cross-platform behavior; changing it would require touching both `ui/gif.mbt` and the `image_wbtest` assertion); JPEG/BMP/TIFF were not verified format by format (they go through the same `CreateDecoderFromFilename` sniffing path, with PNG/GIF proven); real-machine appearance (the image areas of showcase's card/media pages, whether the GIF animates) belongs to `_人工测试清单.md`.

### C-side text layout object cache (Pango / DirectWrite, 2026-10-14, data-driven)

- **Background and evidence**: the frame-stage ledger (`gui/core/prof.mbt`) read on the native-app real machine `frames=190 layout=220ms paint=34487ms present=78ms total=34785ms` — **paint is 99.1% of the frame cost while layout is only 0.6%** (yoga's internal cache works; "full-tree relayout every frame" is not the bottleneck). The cost is that every full-window repaint re-creates a layout object per visible text widget on the C side (Pango `PangoLayout` / DirectWrite `IDWriteTextLayout`: itemize + shaping + font resolution, all redone).
- **Fix (same shape on both branches)**: cache layout objects keyed by path marker (`"m"` = measure / unconstrained draw, `"b"` = box-aligned draw) + full text bytes + font quadruple (family/size/weight/style) + width constraint/wrap/ellipsize/alignment — same key means identical layout configuration, so reuse is read-only. Cap 1024 entries per backend, whole-table clear on overflow (same contract as the MoonBit-side metrics cache). Measure and unconstrained draw share the `"m"` key (identical config path); box draws use `"b"` (width normalized by wrapped_axis, alignment only participates when width-constrained).
- **Read-only-reuse preconditions (hold on each platform)**: Pango — color is set via `cairo_set_source_rgba` and geometry offsets are computed per call, neither touches the layout; DWrite — `ptw_render` does not mutate the layout when there are no ranges (range fonts/colors belong to the rich-text rt_* path only, which still builds layouts per call and skips the cache), text color goes through `SetColor` on the shared brush.
- **Windows-side free precondition**: `img_anim_advance` (WIC) is one frame per call and the component asks once per `delay_ms` beat, so layout objects naturally reuse across beats; the canvas resources (WIC bitmap + D2D render target) were already process-cached — this batch covers the remaining big chunk, `CreateTextLayout` (with full shaping).
- **Measured**: `experiment/anim_cost_bench` (30-row typical page of "button + input + text", 1400×900, Windows pure-raster backend) full-window repaint **21ms/frame**, 90×32 dirty rect **0.8ms/frame**, relayout 0ms — the P5 gate "local change under 16ms per frame" is met. The after number for the full native-app page awaits a re-run of `NATIVE-APP-PROF` (the ledger now lands in `native-app-prof.txt`).
- **Not done, not pretending**: rich text rt_* (AttributedText) still builds layouts per call (small share on the demo pages; benefits to be estimated separately); when the MoonBit-side measure cache hits, the C side is never reached — this C cache mainly serves the draw path and cold starts after the MoonBit cache clears.

### Raster fill fast paths: direct-write fill_rect + analytic rounded-rect fill (2026-10-14, probe-located)

- **How it was located**: the NATIVE-APP-RENDER three-stage stats proved text accounts for only ~15ms of a ~232ms full frame — the culprit is non-text drawing. `experiment/paint_probe` (component-level isolated timing) broke it down: 60 cards of 400×120 rounded rect = **432ms/frame (7.2ms each)**, straight rects 110ms, three charts 56ms, virtual table 22ms — **rounded rects going through generic path scan (flattening + polygon AA) is the single largest item, 20ms per card**.
- **Fix (pure-raster backend, render package)**: (1) `composite_solid_span` — whole-span direct write for **opaque solid** source + Normal op (no per-pixel source/premul/blend calls; premultiplied BGRA, opaque source = raw color); (2) `Raster::fill_device_rect` tries the fast path before the generic cov composite; (3) `Raster::fill_round_rect` **analytic fill** — no path scanning: per-row ink spans (middle rows via the fast path), corner-strip pixels get coverage from "pixel-center distance to the corner circle center" (0 outside / AA at the rim / 256 inside). Applies only when axis-aligned and uniformly scaled (corners stay circular); otherwise returns false and the Painter falls back to `trace_round_rect+fill`.
- **Trap (hit in practice)**: the fast-path entry **must call `apply_fill()` first** — calling `raster.fill_round_rect` directly skips Painter::fill's source setup, so the shape gets painted with the previous source color (the table color-box and overlay-panel pixel asserts went red instantly for exactly this reason).
- **Measured (same probe, re-run)**: single rounded card 20ms→**1ms (20×)**, 60 cards 432→**23ms (19×)**, 60 straight rects 110→**10ms (11×)**, virtual table 22→**5ms (4×)**; chart row 56→36ms (vector paths/strokes are the next big chunk). Package-by-package suite green (only the virtlist style-slot red remains — a pre-existing failure from the frontend session).
- **Follow-up batch · frame-level text batching (DWrite submission merge)** (2026-10-14): NATIVE-APP-RENDER shows the d2d stage at 132us/segment (62 segments/frame = 8.2ms) — the fixed per-segment BeginDraw→EndDraw submission overhead dominates. `dwrite_text.cpp` gains a batch queue: consecutive pt_draw/pt_draw_box calls on the same target bitmap accumulate (cap 128); flushing does one BeginDraw → Clear(**union**) → per-segment DrawTextLayout → EndDraw → one read-back of the union + one per-pixel blend (overlapping segments composite inside D2D in queue order). **Triple z-order safety**: (1) flush on target/dimension change (multi-window alternation); (2) render gains a process-level "raster gate" — each of the 11 pixel-writing entries (fill/stroke/rounded/box_shadow/draw_image/draw_canvas) calls `pt_flush_if_overlap` with the current clip rect first, flushing only when the op's clip intersects the pending batch union (non-intersecting ops keep the batch alive, so fill-text interleaves on card walls still form long batches); (3) core `paint_frame` calls `flush_pending` before present. Layout-cache eviction can happen between enqueue and flush, so queued layouts are AddRef'd and Released after flush (no dangling). **Switch isolation**: batching is enabled only on the production path (core `text_batch_init`); tests/probes keep the old path bit-for-bit — zero suite disturbance. Measured (paint_probe): 200 segments/frame, immediate **21ms → batched 2ms (10×)**; the immediate path (rt_* rich text) flushes the batch at entry to preserve order. Pango/mac branches have no batch (stub no-ops); stub.c's common tail uses `#ifndef _WIN32` to avoid double definitions with dwrite_text.cpp.
- **Follow-up batch · incremental active edge table (AET)** (second fill_polys rewrite, pixman-isomorphic): the GET (global edge table) is sorted by y_lo once; scanline advancement only does "deactivation compaction / edge activation (monotone pointer) / exact-precision x recompute + nearly-sorted insertion sort" per subsample — no more full edge rescan per subsample. **Two measured iron rules**: (1) x must be **bit-identical** to the old crossing formula `ax + (bx-ax)×t` (store the original traversal-direction endpoints ax/ay/bx2/by2; incremental stepping drift, and mistakenly using bx2 as by2 in the t denominator, both flip boundary-pixel coverage quantization — caught by box_shadow/star/icon asserts in practice); (2) a sub-1px span straddling two pixels (e.g., [10.6,11.4)) covers `[floor(xa), ceil(xb))`, not one pixel (star tips / thin rings caught it). **Diff-harness methodology**: extract the old algorithm from git into a temp wbtest, render identical shapes through both, pixel-diff (this round caught a whole triangle rendering zero ink = activation pointer indexing the unsorted array). Measured (paint_probe): 80-segment polyline 11→**4ms**, arcs 15→**2ms**, chart row 28→**17ms**; the icon full-scan crash was a debug print's cov OOB (not the AET). Known rendering change: under-coverage artifacts at self-intersecting-polygon (pentagram) inner-boundary pixels (old 239/207/159 dim seams) corrected to full coverage 255 — nonzero winding has nonzero on both sides of a wind 1↔2 boundary, so full coverage is correct. **Addendum (2026-10-14 evening)**: the AET batch carried the actual crash culprit — the y1 clamp mistakenly wrote `cx1` (clip right bound) instead of `cy1` (bottom bound); when a bitmap is wider than tall and geometry extends past the bottom edge, the row loop runs past the bitmap height and composite_span writes out of bounds (0xc0000409, caught by graph_wbtest). The batch had initially mis-attributed this to the frontend session; a git-worktree bisect (3be0d9b 189 green → e2c7c14 crash) corrected the record.
- **Follow-up batch · scanline engine, pixman-style** (fill_polys rewrite, 2026-10-14): ported four proven pixman/Cairo scanline patterns — (1) **full-coverage runs set directly**: pixels whose four samples all fall inside a crossing span are set to 256 in one go (the old code evaluated all 16 samples per pixel inside polygons); (2) **sampling only at span boundary pixels** (at most one per end, capped at 256); (3) **composite only the inked range** (per-row ink_l/ink_r — usually ~90% of the bbox width is empty coverage); (4) `composite_span` gained a **direct-write fast path for fully-covered runs** under opaque solid + Normal (partial-coverage pixels expand to the identical formulas, bit-for-bit); (5) crossing sort switched to `sort_by` index sort (old insertion sort was O(n²)) plus **zero-allocation scratch arrays** (tuple push boxed once per crossing — measured 57k allocations/frame of GC pressure). Measured (paint_probe): 80-segment polyline 18→**11ms**, arcs 15→**12ms**, chart row 39→**28ms**. The remaining structural item = incremental active edge table (edges walked once per row, x stepped incrementally, no per-subsample full edge rescan + sort) — a larger rewrite.
- **Follow-up batch · analytic stroke**: after adding stroke benchmarks to `paint_probe` — 60 card borders of 400×120 **stroke = 49ms/frame** (0.82ms each, the largest remaining item on card-heavy pages). `Raster::stroke_round_rect` analytic stroke: ring pixels partitioned uniquely by center (straight bands via row/column overlap with `[edge-hw, edge+hw]`, corner quadrants per-pixel `|dist-rr|≤hw` SDF, inner hole vanishes naturally when line width ≥ 2×radius), measured **49→6ms (8×)**; also adds a public `Painter::stroke_round_rect` API. Polylines (80 segments, 18ms) and arcs (3 rings, 15ms) remain generic — chart-primitive analytics queued as the next candidate batch.
- **Final measurement (native-app real machine, all five batches in)**: `frames=4421 paint=144299ms` — **full-frame paint 232ms→32.6ms (7.1×)**, max_frame 344→188ms; text is ~28% of paint (d2d 9.3ms/frame), the rest is chart vector paths and strokes. Layout 0.23ms/frame and present 0.5ms/frame are negligible.
- **Not done**: stroke_round_rect / chart vector paths (stroke_polys) still generic — chart pages and card borders are the remaining chunk; rounded corners under rotation or non-uniform scale still take the generic path; per-frame batching of DWrite's per-segment BeginDraw/EndDraw (next step for the 9.3ms/frame of text).

### Self-sustaining repaint loop: present and expose igniting each other (2026-10-13, real-machine feedback)

- **Symptom**: on real hardware, `examples/native-app` felt "laggy overall - scrolling, clicking and hovering all have 300-500 ms of latency", together with "the chart's push-point button does nothing, clicking a table header does not sort, and the virtual table will not scroll". In the code these look like three unrelated bugs; in fact it is **one root cause plus one example-side wiring gap**.
- **Root cause (identical shape in both branches)**: `wm_present` asked the system to redraw on every frame (`InvalidateRect(NULL,FALSE)` on `_WIN32`, `gtk_widget_queue_draw` on GTK), while the system's repaint callback (`WM_PAINT` / `wm_on_draw`) set `need_draw = 1` again - so MoonBit's `poll_event` returned `NeedsDraw`, `dirty` became true, a full frame was painted, `present` ran, and the cycle repeated: an **endless loop**. Two consequences: (1) the app was **never idle** - `wm_wait` always returned immediately because a paint message was always queued, every tick repainted 1400x3520 ~ 4.9M pixels, and input could only be serviced after a whole frame, which is exactly the 300-500 ms latency; (2) the mouse-move events accumulated during one frame (easily 100+ with a high-polling-rate mouse) filled the 64-slot event queue, and `wm_push`'s "drop when full" rule then **threw away the button-up and wheel events that followed** - that is the "button does nothing / header does not sort / wheel does not scroll". Note that all three symptoms hold **simultaneously within one frame**: the `Down` made it into the queue, the `Up` was dropped, the widget stayed in its pressed state, and the button simply looks broken.
- **Fix (both branches, kept symmetric)**: the repaint callback now only **blits the cached frame** and no longer requests a repaint (`WM_PAINT` sets `need_draw` only when there is no frame to blit yet; `wm_on_draw` likewise). The sole source of repaint requests is back to the MoonBit side's `schedule_paint` (`@core.invalidate` / `App::schedule_paint`), which is the stack's documented contract anyway: **paint only when content changed**. The first frame is unaffected - `wm_show` already sets `need_draw = 1` (`wm_mark_draw`).
- **The other half: coalescing consecutive moves in the queue.** In `wm_push`, if the last entry in the queue is also an **unconsumed** move, it is replaced by the new one (a pointer position is state, not a stream). Replacement only happens when the last entry is a move, so a Down/Up in between is never disturbed and FIFO semantics survive. This one must stay even with the loop fixed: any single frame (still on the order of a hundred milliseconds) can accumulate dozens of moves.
- **Window height 3520 -> 900 (example side)**: viewport culling (`widget_cull_skip`) paints only the part inside the clip, so **the window height directly determines the per-frame pixel count**; now that `enable_root_scroll` exists, the tall column whose only purpose was "show the whole page in one screen" is pure cost. It is now the `APP_WIN_H` constant - set it back to 3520 for a full-page view.
- **"Sorting does not work" is an example wiring gap, not a component bug**: a header click only flips the `TableSort` store (the component deliberately **does not sort for the caller** - that rule is documented on `TableSort`), and the example never subscribed to it to write the reordered rows back. A `@ui.sort_rows` reference implementation was added (**leading digits compared numerically first** - the demo data is mixed "12 chu / 6 chu" style, where plain lexicographic order puts 12 before 6 and looks like nothing happened; plus a **stable** sort so blocks of equal keys do not shuffle), and the example wires it up in two or three lines.
- **Lesson**: "never idle" bugs show up on real hardware as **a pile of unrelated broken features**, not as a performance problem. Next time "clicking does nothing + lag + cannot scroll" appear together, check the **enqueue rate versus consume rate of the event queue** first, then look for a self-sustaining repaint loop - both silently drop events, and dropping leaves no log at all.
- **Verification**: `moon check --deny-warn` is clean for both the library module and the `examples` module (two injected-error probes confirmed the gate really covers those modules); `gui/win/stub.c` compiles standalone under MSVC (`cl /c /W4 /utf-8`) with a warning count byte-for-byte identical to before the change (only the 2 pre-existing C4996); `table_wbtest.mbt` gains 2 tests (sort rule + stability). **The real-machine behavior (whether latency really drops to "one frame", whether clicks stop being dropped) belongs to `_Windows真机测试清单.md` for re-verification.**

### Three long-red library tests on Windows closed out (2026-10-13, W9 / table-sort follow-up)

After the W9 and table-sort batches landed, the Windows local library suite carried 3 permanent reds (`moon test` as one combined run also sporadically times out on this machine; **run it package-by-package** to stay stable). Root causes and fixes:

- **`moon test` runs test processes with cwd = module root (`gui/`), regardless of the invocation directory** (probed via `yue_sys_getcwd`: `moon test gui/sys` from the repo root and `moon test sys` from `gui/` both report cwd `gui/`). So the repo-root-relative path `examples/showcase/assets/dot.gif` used by wbtests **never exists in tests**; the GIF decode case hit `None => assert_true(false)` permanently, and `ui_decode_guard()` could never find the image, so the ui GIF component assertions were silently skipping. Fix: tests now probe two candidates (`examples/...` and `../examples/...`, whichever decodes wins — `test_asset` in `image_wbtest`, `gif_asset` in `basic_wbtest`); the guard and the component tests now really run on Windows.
- **`Image::resize` only opened a Cairo session; Windows has no Cairo backend, so the whole frame came back empty** — upscaled pixels read `(0,0,0,0)`. `Cairo::over` returns `None` on backend-less platforms and the old implementation just gave up. Fix: route through `Painter::draw_image` (stretching blit implemented on both the Cairo and pure-raster backends), platform-free.
- **`compare_cells` only read a leading integer — not natural sort** — `"虚拟行 3"` starts with a non-digit and fell back to lexical order, putting `"虚拟行 12"` before it (a platform-independent plain bug; Linux should have failed too — the case simply never met a green CI run since landing). Fix: rewritten as **natural order** (char-by-char; when both sides sit on a digit run, compare the whole run numerically; equal runs continue; the string that runs out first sorts first); `leading_int` removed.
- **Per-tick animation cadence differs by platform (test contract)**: Windows (WIC) `img_anim_advance` is **one frame per call** (the component asks once per `delay_ms` beat; no internal clock); Linux (io-gif) anchors on a virtual clock, so frames advanced per beat differ. The `basic_wbtest` window "12 beats → 2..6 frames" was calibrated on Linux; replaced with the platform-neutral invariant "12 beats advance at least 2 frames and at most 12 (guard against multi-frames-per-beat runaway)".
- **Verification**: all green on Windows, package-by-package — core 132, ui 125, render 43, text/theme/icons 41, sys/input/win/dbus 280, chart/mdview/web 233, **854/854**; `moon check` zero warnings. The combined run's sporadic SIGTERM timeout is a local-machine phenomenon; CI (Linux) and the per-package gate are unaffected.

## macOS (CI build chain verified, GUI pending real-machine)

- The libyue v0.15.6 release ships ARC / no-ARC dual libraries: Darwin link flags = main library + `-lyue_mbt_noarc` (no-ARC symbols are referenced by the main library, so it goes after) + frameworks AppKit / Carbon / IOKit / Security / WebKit / OpenDirectory + `-lobjc -lc++ -lpthread -lbsm -Wl,-dead_strip`; the prebuild Darwin branch is pre-configured accordingly.
- CI (macos runner) covers build + tests; headless runners have no WindowServer, so no GUI smoke.
- `#else` fallbacks in shim platform branches swallow macOS: borderless needs `#elif defined(OS_WIN)`; CurrentDirForDrag splits into three branches (mac uses `getcwd`); `Window::SetSkipTaskbar` / `SetIcon` / `App::SetID` have no macOS declarations — guard the calls as no-ops.
- **AppleClang 17 (after the macos-15 image update) parses the return of `getRed:green:blue:alpha:` as void**, making `![...]` a compile error; the value is guaranteed after the nil check and sRGB conversion, so discarding the return compiles under both BOOL/void parses (69136b7 — once lost to a full-tree rollback and picked back up; when a rollback baseline contains a sick file, later fixes vanish with it, so cross-check that file's history before re-pushing vendor).
- **Three root causes of the CI red streak before the 0.5.0 release (since 9-22; Linux/macOS red, Windows green)**: ① the AppleClang compile error above dead-locking prepare; ② the missing extern "C" (see ABI section) dead-locking the link; ③ sysmonitor's S4 hardware-sampling test asserting "coretemp always has a Package sensor" — virtual-machine runners lack the hardware, so environmental gaps (no sensors / no DISPLAY) must skip, not assert. Also: the CI native-layer cache key must include the shim source hash (with only prepare.py in the key, a shim change keeps the old key and the restored build/ cache holds a stale shim archive); and with no Actions log access, slicing the failing output into `::error` annotations (the check-runs annotations API is anonymously readable) is the only forensics channel.

### Store built-in dedup and `T : Eq` bounded impls (2026-10-13, frontend P3 step 0)

- Policy: `Store::set` / `Store::update` get `pub fn[T : Eq]` bounded implementations (same value: store without notify, via `Signal::set_eq`); `Signal::set` and the notifier are untouched, and `Store::signal()` still reaches `Signal::set` for forced delivery. Use `set_always` / `update_always` for unconditional re-notify.
- Pit 1: **a same-name bounded impl shadows the generic one** — calling `self.set(...)` from inside the unconstrained `Store::update` fails with `Type T does not implement Eq`, because the name resolves only to the bounded impl. Cross-constraint logic must call the low level directly (`sig.set_eq` / `sig.update`).
- Pit 2: dedup requires **element Eq**: `Array[T]` Eq derives from `T : Eq`. Composite value types need `pub impl Eq for X with fn equal` plus `pub extend X with Eq::{not_equal, equal}` (without extend you get the `implicit_impl_as_method` deprecation, same precedent as W21c); re-implementing Eq for 2-tuples errors as "already defined" in core.
- Pit 3: chart data windows (`Array[LineSeries]`, `TreeItem`, `GeoRect`...) have no Eq on elements and full-window comparison costs more than it saves - chart and examples use `set_always` throughout; 33 call sites were pinned one by one by the compiler.
- Verification: core 121/121 (new dedup regression: same value silent, changed value fires once, `set_always` forces, `update` follows the same policy); repo-wide `moon check --deny-warn` clean; library tests 859/861 (2 reds are the pre-existing W9/sort baseline).

### Animation go/no-go: under pure software raster a transition is a full-window repaint per frame (2026-10-13, sets P5's schedule)

- Question: should P5 (`transition` = state-bucket interpolation + frame driver) follow P4 immediately? Measured instead of guessed: `experiment/anim_cost_bench` (a typical page of 30 rows of button+input+text, 1400x900, `page.paint` x20) gives `full=16ms/20 frames`, `relayout=0ms/20 frames`, a 90x32 rounded fill `<1ms/frame` on this machine.
- **But this box is Linux: `Painter::new` picks the cairo backend** (see the backend probe in `render/painter.mbt`, `Cairo::over` first), so 0.8ms/frame is the **host-side submit cost into system cairo/X**, not the CPU raster cost. The real per-frame price lives on the Windows/mac `PureBackend(Raster)` path: the performance batch measured 300-500ms per full-window frame on device (still awaiting per-tier timing), and `text_bench` on Windows is ~0.35ms per boxed text draw - which a full repaint pays for **every on-screen text node**.
- Conclusion / policy: **animation is continuous dirty-repaint**. Without damage rects, a 150ms hover transition on Windows is ~9 full-window repaints - visibly stuttery, and it would undo the "typing repaints only that box" granularity promise. So **P5 moves behind §6.6's "damage-rect repaint + text layout cache"**, with the gate stated as: a local change (hovering one button) must cost < 16ms per frame. P4's `opacity` / `box_shadow` are **static properties** (one frame suffices) and are unaffected - already shipped.
- Toolchain pit found on the way: `local` is a **reserved keyword** (`[0035] The word 'local' is reserved`), so `let local = ...` breaks; renamed to `part` in the probe. Treat words like `alias` / `abbrev` / `local` as reserved until probed (same family as design.md §1.6's table).
- Next number needs the device (user runs it): on Windows, `moon run examples/native-app`, quit, and read the `NATIVE-APP-PROF` line (layout / paint / present accumulated + worst frame, always-on via `core/prof.mbt`) - that sets both "how much damage rects buy" and P5's go gate.

### Declarative frontend P4: Painter global alpha and hard shadow (2026-10-13)

- Global alpha lives in the **facade state machine** (`PainterState.global_alpha`, save/restore stack): fills/strokes (incl. gradient stops) scale color alpha in `apply_fill/apply_stroke`; text goes through `Painter::paint_color` (parse -> scale -> re-emit hex at draw time); images/canvases run `dim_bitmap` (per-pixel alpha scale) before blit when `global_alpha < 1`. Both backends (cairo / pure raster) get full-path coverage with zero backend changes because the facade is the only entry.
- Pit 1: **asserting on the wrong channel** - layering 50% red over an opaque target keeps pixel alpha at 255 (alpha compositing changes color, not coverage); `alpha == 0` style checks must run on a **transparent** target, and gradients must be compared `full.a` vs `0.25.a` on blank bitmaps.
- Pit 2: `round_rect_path` calling `begin_path` internally wiped the outer ring when the shadow traced outer+inner subpaths - split into `trace_round_rect` / `round_rect_path_ccw` (no begin_path), so a ring = one outer loop + one reversed inner loop, nonzero winding paints only the annulus with no overlap darkening.
- `Style.opacity` channel: `apply` writes `Widget.alpha_paint`; the paint traversal sets `p.set_global_alpha(inherited * own)` on entry and restores after the subtree (CSS-like box-tree accumulation). `box_shadow` ships the **hard** tier (offset rounded-rect rings, `layers` equal steps); the blur tier waits for a blur capability.
- Verification: 3 new render tests (full-path scaling / rounded rect / shadow ring), render 45/46 (1 red is the pre-existing W9 baseline); ui's `fill_round_rect` moved into the facade with behavior unchanged; repo-wide `moon check --deny-warn` clean; library tests 880/882 (2 reds baseline). Visual checks (opacity demo, shadowed cards) go to `_人工测试清单.md`.

## Declarative frontend P2-e: frame-cost A/B and the idle-repaint gate for self-driving widgets (2026-10-14)

Real-hardware report: "page switching in showcase lags, section widgets render late". A controlled experiment came first (probe `experiment/showcase_cost_bench`: 14 pages x 120 text lines, 1835 drawn widgets, 20 full-page repaints, same machine and same tree, only the revision changes):

- **Readings**: `380610a` without the C-side text-layout cache = 9.9 / 11.95 ms per frame (warm/cold); `323b537` with it = 3.35 / 3.25; `HEAD` after the four P2-e batches = 3.4 / 3.35. So **the text cache made full-page repaint roughly 3x faster**, and **per-frame style-slot reads (P2-e) sit inside the noise** - they are not the lag. The cold variant (1680 distinct strings, above the cache's 1024 cap) did not regress on the local Pango path, but the **"clear the whole table on overflow" policy has never been measured on Windows/DWrite** - the A/B harness is in place and the on-device ledger comes from `SHOWCASE-PROF` / `showcase-prof.txt`.
- **The gate reads "was I painted this frame"**: carousel rotation and gif advance ride the process-level `@core.set_timeout`, and previously they only checked `rotation.paused` - so a carousel whose **page is hidden still switched panels and called `invalidate()`, repainting the whole window for nothing**. The widget layer has **no reference to its parent** (parent<->child is a reference cycle the counting collector cannot break - see the splitter batch), so "is some ancestor visible?" cannot be asked. Instead core keeps a process-level paint epoch: `bump_paint_epoch()` once per frame before the host's traversal, `paint_at` records it into `Widget.paint_epoch` only after the `visible` test and the viewport cull pass, and `Widget::is_painted()` answers "painted this frame". The self-driving callback becomes `if !w.is_painted() { re-arm only - no page switch, no invalidate }`: hidden pages stop all idle repaints and resume on their own once visible again.
- **Related pit (order-sensitive tests)**: the epoch is **process-level** and shared by every test in the package. The pre-existing "fire a tick without painting" tests passed by accident on `0 == 0`; once a new test bumps the epoch they turn falsely red. Rule: **a test that drives self-animation must paint one frame first** (same shape as the real host), otherwise it asserts on an initial zero rather than on "was painted".
- **Verification**: 1 new whitebox test (hide the block -> fire: no panel switch, the `invalidate` counter does not move; show it again -> rotation continues), `moon test ui` 140/140, library-wide 872/872, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean; showcase prints `SHOWCASE-PROF` (frames / layout / paint / present / worst frame) at exit. If `frames` is still abnormally high something else is spinning, and that is the next check.

## Damage-rect narrowing: section/page switches did not repaint (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Environment**: `examples/showcase` walked on device after damage-rect repaint landed (frame bitmap reuse + painting clipped to the dirty region); the ledger is the `SHOWCASE-PROF` line printed at exit.
- **Symptom**: switching a section or a page left the screen unchanged - the new content only appeared once the pointer hovered some widget. `SHOWCASE-PROF` (615 frames / 1323 ms, 2.2 ms average, 134 ms worst) looked healthy: frames were being produced, just painting a tiny patch.
- **Root cause**: `App.dirty_rect : Rect?` used `None` for **two different meanings** - "whole window is dirty" and "nothing is dirty yet". In one batch, a whole-window request (`invalidate()` / `schedule_paint` / `host_repaint_all`, all of which only write `dirty_rect = None`) could be followed by the pointer router marking the hover change through `mark_widget_rect`, whose shape is `Some(rect_union(dirty_rect, w.rect))`, and `rect_union(None, r) = r`. **The whole-window request was therefore narrowed to the small rectangle under the cursor**, and the rest of the frame kept the previous pixels. "It refreshes when I hover" is exactly that narrowed rectangle repainting the cell under the pointer.
- **Fix**: `App` gains `mut dirty_full : Bool`, set by every whole-window request (`schedule_paint`, `host_repaint_all`). Local marking now goes through the pure helper `accept_widget_rect(full, cur, r)` - with `full` set it **holds the whole-window state instead of narrowing**; `paint_frame` takes the frame's clip through `dirty_clip(full, rect)` (whole-window -> `None`, i.e. no clip) and resets both afterwards. The two decisions are pure functions so they can be pinned: the host `App` needs a real window, these judgements do not.
- **General policy**: an `Option` standing for "two different kinds of nothing" invites exactly this bug - when one `None` means "everything" and another means "empty", any "merge one more entry in" operation overwrites the strong meaning with the weak one. Either add a flag (this batch) or use an explicit sum (`Whole | Region(Rect)`).
- **Verification**: 1 new core regression on the pure helpers (without a whole-window request the rectangles still union; with one, a local mark must not become a small rectangle; the clip prefers whole-window), `moon test core` 133/133, all 13 library packages run individually 882/882, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean. Device re-check (user): switch sections/pages in showcase **without moving the mouse** and the new page appears; re-run prints a `SHOWCASE-PROF` line whose `frames` does not grow from this change (it only preserves whole-window semantics within a batch that already asked for it).

## Windows-only `GUID` helpers sat outside the guard, so the library would not compile on non-Windows (2026-10-14)

- **Symptom**: `moon run examples/showcase` / `moon test ui` died with `sys/sys_stub.c:2023:45: error: unknown type name 'GUID'` (the power-scheme code).
- **Root cause**: the two exported functions (`yue_sys_pp_current_guid`, `yue_sys_pp_set`) do have `#if defined(_WIN32)` bodies, but the two `static` helpers they share (`sys_pp_guid_string` / `sys_pp_parse_guid`) take `GUID` in their signatures and sit **outside every guard** - a `static` function still has to type-check even when never called, and on Linux/mac `windows.h` is not included.
- **Fix**: both helpers wrapped in `#if defined(_WIN32) ... #endif` (they are only used from the Windows branch, so Windows behaviour is unchanged).
- **General rule**: in platform stubs, anything whose **types come from a platform header** (GUID, HINSTANCE, PangoLayout, ...) must be inside the guard wholesale - guarding only the body while exposing the signature does not count. After touching C, run a compile on a *non-target* platform (`cd gui && moon check`) - that is what reveals a missing guard immediately.
- **Verification**: `moon check sys ui --deny-warn` passes, `moon build showcase --target native` links, library-wide 924/924.
- **Same trap, second time (2026-10-14 windowctl batch)**: `sys_win_find_ctx_t` / `sys_win_find` / `sys_win_find_cb` / `sys_mon_find_t` / `sys_mon_find_cb` / `sys_monitor_index` / `sys_win_enum_cb` / `sys_wcs_icontains` — a set of `static` helpers whose signatures carry `HWND` / `HMONITOR` / `BOOL CALLBACK` — again sat outside every guard, and `moon test sys` on Linux died with `sys_stub.c:2338: error: expected ‘=’, … before ‘sys_win_find_cb’`. The fix is the same: two whole blocks (the enum helpers, and find + title matching) wrapped in `#if defined(_WIN32)`; `sys_win_blob_t` / `sys_win_blob_append` / `sys_win_enum_ctx_t` only use stdlib and stay outside.
- **Closing action (what keeps it from recurring)**: whenever C is added for one platform, that same batch must run `cd gui && moon test <that package> --target native` **on the opposite platform** — exported functions never break, because their bodies carry an `#else` branch; only helpers outside the guard break, and only the opposite compiler sees them. Windows-only `sys` batches are therefore compiled on Linux, and `win` / `dbus` the other way round. Readings for this batch on Linux: `moon test sys` 182/182, library-wide 928/928, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links.
- **A third shape of the same trap (2026-10-14, platform batch 4, volume)**: the guard was drawn **too wide** — the whole section, including the two exported functions `yue_sys_vol_get` / `yue_sys_vol_write`, was wrapped in `#if defined(_WIN32)`, even though each of them already carries its own `#else` fallback. On Linux this does not surface as a type error but as a **link error** (`sys.whitebox_test.o: undefined reference to yue_sys_vol_write`), because the MoonBit-side declaration is still compiled while the C symbol vanished. One more rule: **exported function bodies always stay outside the guard** (they carry both branches); the guard wraps only platform-specific helpers, constants and GUID tables, and its `#endif` must close at the end of the helper block, never over an exported function. Readings for this batch on Linux: sys 185/185, library-wide 932/932, `moon build showcase --target native` links.


## Table hover lit only one cell, and "add a row" did nothing: damage covered only the deepest widget, and a dedup Store held the array it mutates (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom ①**: hovering a single-selection table lit only the cell under the pointer instead of the whole row (the user confirmed every table variant behaves this way).
- **Root cause ①**: not a painting problem - a headless probe showed that after `set_hover(cell.id)` the whole 440 px row is `fill_hover` from x=10 to x=400. What was wrong is the **damage region**: hover is parent-chain semantics (like CSS `:hover`; `set_hover` flags the whole ancestor chain), but after pointer routing the host marked only the "old/new hovered widget's" rect. The row paints a row-wide background while the repaint window was one cell, so it looked like "only the cell changes".
- **Fix ①**: `App::mark_state_chain(id)` walks the hit path - the deepest widget is always marked, an ancestor only when **its own painting depends on state**. "Depends on state" is recorded by `@style.apply` as `Widget.paints_state` (true when the style carries a `hover`/`active`/`focus` bucket); core does not know the style layer and just reads the boolean. With no state-painting ancestor the cost stays one cell rect - switching to chain marking never degrades back to whole-window.
- **Symptom ②**: showcase's 添加一行 button grew nothing.
- **Root cause ②**: `Store`'s built-in dedup compares **by value** (`set_eq` when `T : Eq`), and the example stored the `lang_base` **array object itself**. "Add a row" then does `lang_base.push(...)` in place and `table_rows.set(lang_base)` - old and new are the same object (whose content changed too), so they are necessarily equal, the notification is swallowed, and the rebuild in the subscription never runs. `set(rows.copy())` gets swallowed the same way, because the stored object already holds the new content.
- **Fix ②**: seed the store with a snapshot (`Store::new(lang_base.copy())`) so the stored value is not the object being mutated in place - then push + set really do compare unequal and the notification fires (sorting benefits too: notify only when the order changes, skip when it does not, which is exactly what dedup is for).
- **General rules**: ① any parent-chain state (hover / press / focus) must extend the damage region to **ancestors that paint that state**, otherwise local repaints show a half-updated frame; ② "mutate an array in place, then set the same array" is a dead end on a deduplicating Store - hand it a fresh snapshot, or call `set_always` deliberately.
- **Verification**: `moon test core` 136/136 with 1 new pin (a state-painting ancestor widens the dirty rect to >=200; with no such ancestor the rect stays <=50.5), library-wide 922/922, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean (`App::mark_id_rect`, now unreferenced, removed). Device check: hovering lights the whole row, and 添加一行 appends a row with the read-along label updating.

## The timeline connector came out broken: row gaps were left to `row_gap`, so no widget owned them (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on showcase "数据展示 -> 时间线" the vertical line between the three nodes was cut - an empty 12 px at every gap.
- **Root cause**: each row's marker cell drew its line only inside its own box (`fill_rect(9, top, 2, bottom - top)` with top/bottom clamped to the dot centre on the first/last rows), while the 12 px between rows came from the container's `row_gap` - **that band belongs to no widget**, so nobody painted it and the line broke at every gap.
- **Fix**: the row spacing now lives **inside the row** (a non-last marker and row box grow to `height + gap`; the last row carries no extra), and `timeline` no longer passes `row_gap`. The dot centre still comes from the content band (`cy = height / 2`), so dot positions and the first/last clamps are unchanged - each row just owns one more segment of line. Measured row heights are 68 / 68 / 56 at y = 0 / 68 / 136 (total height identical to before).
- **General rule**: anything that must run continuously across rows (connector lines, timeline spines, table separators, scrollbar tracks) cannot live in container gaps - the gap is not a paintable unit. Either fold the spacing into the row (this batch) or have the container itself draw the continuous line.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 156/156 - the timeline case now asserts row heights `(68, 68, 56)` and adds two pixel checks that the line must exist inside the gaps (`pixel(10,60)`, `pixel(10,130)`); control experiment: putting `row_gap` back with no in-row spacing turns it red at `(56,56,56) != (68,68,56)`. Library-wide 921/921, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean. Device check: the spine runs unbroken from the first dot to the last.

## Breadcrumb clicks did not navigate: "current item" was decided by position, not by the bound store (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on showcase "导航组件 -> 面包屑", clicking the last crumb ("基础组件") did nothing.
- **Root cause**: the component's own doc says "the **current** item is dark and not clickable", but the code decided "current" by position - `is_last` drove both the dark colour and the `on_click` guard. The demo passes a static three-item list `["数据展示", "导航组件", "基础组件"]` while the current page is the middle one, so the last crumb was neither the current page nor clickable. The click plumbing itself was fine (non-last crumbs did write the store); what was wrong is the **definition of "current"** - the bound navigation state is the only authority.
- **Fix**: both colour and clickability now key off `selected.get() == mine`: the crumb equal to the navigation state is dark and does not change the value, every other crumb navigates (including the last one). The `/` separators stay non-clickable.
- **Test-side**: the nav_wbtest case asserted "the last item does not change the value" - it had been written against the wrong rule - and now asserts "the last item navigates too + the current item does not + separators do not". Control experiment: putting the positional predicate back (`i < n - 1`) turns it red at `"列表" != "详情"`, reverting makes it green. The showcase description was fixed too (it promised "no highlight for the current item, every item navigates", which matched neither the old nor the new code).
- **General rule**: "which item is current" has exactly one source - the bound Store. Any positional stand-in (last/first) for state shows up as a dead-but-clickable-looking corner as soon as the demo hierarchy differs from the real one.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 156/156, library-wide 921/921, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean. Device check: each of the three crumbs switches page, and only the one matching the current page stays dark and inert.

## The time popup drew its title over the panel instead of occupying a slot (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: in the time picker popup the "00:00" title overlapped the first `[-] hour [+]` row.
- **Root cause**: the title was not a widget - the panel's own `on_draw` painted it into `(0, 0, w, 28)`, while the stepper and button rows are real children laid out from the `top=10` padding, so the first row landed at y=10..42 under the 0..28 title. **Painting something "as a header" does not reserve a layout slot** - the same trap was already recorded for `md_marker` (a marker cell with no measure got height 0 and the number dropped onto the text baseline).
- **Fix**: the title became a **real child** (height 28, width 100%) and the panel's `on_draw` keeps only the background and border; padding was completed on all four sides (it only had `top`/`left`, so the button row sat flush against the bottom edge). Measured after the fix, the popup children run `y=10 h=28` (title) -> `38/32` -> `70/32` -> `102/28` with the panel 140 tall and no overlap.
- **Test-side follow-through**: three existing cases indexed the popup as `panel.children()[0]` / `[2]` (stepper row / button row); with the title now child 0 they shift by one, and their comment states that the title occupies a slot. A new layout pin asserts: 4 children, first is the 28-high title, consecutive children do not overlap, and the panel is tall enough to contain them.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 156/156, library-wide 921/921, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean. Device check: the popup's time sits on its own row, the two stepper rows and the cancel/confirm row follow in order with even padding.

## `stroke_rect` strokes inward while callers passed centered coordinates: the ring sat 1 px off to the bottom-right (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: in the colour picker each swatch's border looked displaced - a 1 px strip of the fill survived on the left/top while the right/bottom edge was flush.
- **Root cause (measured pixel by pixel)**: the facade contract says `stroke_rect` draws **inside** the rectangle (both backends implement it that way: the cairo stub `cr_stroke_rect` and the pure raster `Raster::stroke_rect` both draw inward by `line_width` and snap to pixels), yet **28 call sites used the Canvas/CSS centered-stroke habit**: `stroke_rect(0.5, 0.5, w - 1.0, h - 1.0)`. Under inner semantics that ring lands in `[0.5, 1.5]` - snapped to column 1 - while the right/bottom edges land on the last column, so the whole ring shifts 1 px down-right. Probe on a 26x26 swatch, middle row: before `F B F...F B` (column 0 is fill), after `B F...F B` (columns 0 and 25 are border).
- **Conversion (centered -> inner)**: `(x, y, w, h)` at line width `lw` becomes `(x - lw/2, y - lw/2, w + lw, h + lw)`. A 1 px outer ring is therefore `stroke_rect(0.0, 0.0, r.w, r.h)`; the focus ring inset by one pixel, `stroke_rect(1.5, 1.5, w - 3.0, h - 3.0)`, becomes `stroke_rect(1.0, 1.0, w - 2.0, h - 2.0)`.
- **Scope**: the ui family (overlay / datepicker / form / virtlist / table / nav / datetime / scroll / message_box / declarative), the chart family (tooltip / boxplot / draw / treemap) and two examples (sysmonitor, showcase events/canvas) - 28 sites in total. `fill_rect` / `clip_rect` are unaffected (they were always integer-box semantics).
- **One test followed the geometry**: the button focus-ring case in form_wbtest sampled column 1 - it had been written against the offset geometry - and now samples column 0, with the comment stating the contract (inner stroke, ring flush with the box edge). A new swatch symmetry pin (left column equals right column, top equals bottom, border differs from fill) turns any regression red.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 155/155, library-wide 920/920, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean. Device check (this machine, Linux/X11): every swatch border is equal on all four sides with no exposed fill, and the 1 px borders of buttons / inputs / panels / overlays line up on all edges.

## Text inputs could not take keyboard focus by default: the `focus_id=0` trap (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptoms**: on the showcase form page an `input` could not be clicked into or typed in; the EP-style clear ✕ was missing; and the stepper's three cells looked like they had different heights and did not join into one control.
- **Root cause ① (cannot focus)**: the host decides focusability as "the first non-zero `focus_id` on the hit path" (`Widget::focusable_at`), while `input` / `textarea` default `focus_id?` to **0** - without an explicit number the box is **permanently unfocusable**. Every call site had to remember to assign one (probe: with no focus_id, `focus_id=0` and `focusable_at` returns `None`).
- **Fix ①**: text components now **auto-allocate** a focus number (`auto_focus_id`: when nothing was passed, count up from 1000000 so it never collides with hand-written small ids like 21/101; an explicit value still wins). Tab cycling follows registration order = mount order = tree order. `select` / `button` keep the opt-in semantics (their focusability is deliberate and involves overlay and activation-key behaviour, out of scope here).
- **Root cause ② (focus ring / caret never repaints)**: `App::mark_focus_rect` fed the **focus-stack id** straight into `mark_id_rect`, which looks widgets up by **instance id** (`root.by_id`). Two different id spaces: a miss falls back to a whole-window repaint (wasted), and an accidental hit marks **some other widget's** rect, so the newly focused box's ring and caret never repaint that frame.
- **Fix ②**: new `App::mark_focus_id_rect` looks the widget up with `find_by_focus_id` (whole-window fallback only when it truly is not in the tree); the Down-path `mark_id_rect(id)` switched over too.
- **`clearable` (the ✕)**: `input` gained the same-named parameter (off by default, like EP): with content and (hovered or focused) it paints a 14x14 ✕ at the right edge - a small background patch first so long text cannot collide with it; clicking it clears the box, writes back to the Store, and consumes that press instead of placing the caret. Disabled and read-only boxes never show it, and password fields get none (EP parity).
- **Stepper shares one frame**: the old `[-] value [+]` drew three independent borders (rounded buttons, square middle), which is what read as "not stuck together" - measured, the three cells were already the same height (`control_height`); the mismatch was the borders. Now the row container draws the frame once (background / border / radius / 1px from the style slot) and each cell paints only its hover fill (inset 1px so the shared frame survives), its glyph, and the one separator line on its inner edge. The group width is fixed at 108 so it no longer stretches with its parent.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 154/154 with 3 new cases - an input with no focus_id is hit by `focusable_at` and typing writes back to the Store (explicit ids still win, two auto ids differ); `clearable` changes pixels in the hot region on hover and clicking empties the Store; the stepper's three cells are equal-height and contiguous, tapping +/- changes the value and a bound side stops responding. Library-wide 918/918, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean. Device check: the form page should accept clicks and keystrokes, show the ✕ on hover and clear on click, and the stepper should read as one control.

## The frame-batching stubs got defined twice: Linux link failure (2026-10-14, reported by `moon run examples/showcase`)

- **Symptom**: `/usr/bin/ld: ... multiple definition of pt_flush` (plus `pt_flush_if_overlap` / `pt_set_batching`), the two definitions coming from `gui/build/libyue_text_pango.a(stub.o)` and `_build/native/debug/build/NoahLiu/native-gui/text/libtext-*.a(stub.o)` - two compilations of the same `stub.c`.
- **Root cause**: on Linux `gui/text/stub.c` is compiled **twice**: prebuild compiles it with `-DWM_STUB_PANGO` into `build/libyue_text_pango.a` (the real implementation), and moon's native-stub pass compiles it again without the macro because it cannot see the pango headers - that second pass **must come out empty** (the comment at the end of the file states exactly that discipline: fall into no branch, become an empty translation unit, let the archive provide the real code). The three functions added by the frame-batching batch sat in an `#ifndef _WIN32` block **after** the outermost `#endif`, i.e. outside every branch, so both compilations emitted them. Windows escaped via the `_WIN32` guard; Linux did not.
- **Fix**: the three functions moved inside the branches - one copy in the `WM_STUB_PANGO` branch (Pango paints and blends per run and never batches, so flushing is a no-op) and one in the `#elif !defined(__linux__)` branch (macOS placeholder); the real Windows implementation stays only in `dwrite_text.cpp`, and the Linux native-stub pass is an empty TU again.
- **General rule**: when adding a cross-platform function to `gui/text/stub.c` (and to the same shape of platform stubs - `render`, `win`, anything organised as "one stub.c with branches + prebuild macros"), it must go **inside an existing branch** or open a new `#elif`; anything placed after the outermost `#endif` collides with the prebuild archive. One-command judge: `nm --defined-only _build/native/debug/build/NoahLiu/native-gui/text/libtext-*.a | grep -c <symbol>` must print **0**.
- **Verification**: `moon build examples/showcase --target native` passes from both the workspace root and the `examples` module and produces `showcase.exe`; `nm` confirms 0 `pt_flush` definitions in the moon-side archive; `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, text+core+ui+render 360/360.

## Timers armed during page construction never fire: the charts page sat at "no data" (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on showcase "图表 -> 折线" both canvases stayed at "暂无数据" with the y axis pinned at 0..1, the gauge did not move, and the 暂停推点 checkbox appeared dead (the leftover half of the earlier "推点按钮没用" report).
- **Root cause**: `@core.set_timeout` uses a **process-level** delay channel that is installed by `App::new` (via `install_host_hooks`); with no host it is a **no-op returning 0** (the contract in `core/loop.mbt`). In the example, `page_charts(app)` is an **argument** to `@ui.mount_window([...])`, so it is evaluated before the window - and thus before the host - exists: the first tick went into the empty channel, the callback never ran, and the self-rearming loop never started. The data path itself is fine: `series_push` uses `set_always` (bypassing Store dedup) and the canvas invalidates from its subscription.
- **Fix (example-side rule)**: **arm the first tick from the mount callback** - `@ui.row([...charts...], handle=fn(_) { line_pump() })`. `view`'s `handle` runs **after** `mount_children`, and `mount_window` builds the App before mounting the tree, so by then the channel is installed and the chart children are already subscribed. The two build-time `let _ = @core.set_timeout(...)` lines are gone, and the on-page code snippet now shows the mount-time form too (otherwise the demo text keeps teaching the broken pattern).
- **General rule**: anything that arms a timer inside a page/component *constructor* - or stores `set_timeout`'s return value to cancel later - belongs after mount (or after the host App exists); a returned 0 means "no host", so never keep 0 as a handle.
- **Same batch (same screenshot)**: the section description `@ui.text(s.desc, max_width=900.0)` had no `wrap=true`, so a long description was **one unwrapped 900 px line** whose tail was cut off. It now wraps, so the section container is no longer stretched by a single long line.
- **Verification**: `cd examples && moon check showcase --deny-warn` clean, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, library-wide 915/915 with no regression. Device check (user): open the charts page - both line canvases should climb one point every 0.5 s with an adaptive y axis and the latest value annotated, 暂停推点 should freeze and resume the curve, and the left gauge should sweep at 2 Hz.

## yoga-mbt left the line gaps out of free space, so a grow item ate them and pushed the row out of its container (2026-10-14, real-hardware report: the copy button is still clipped)

- **Symptom**: in a showcase section body the code row (left "代码" label + a growing spacer + the "复制" button on the right) had its button half clipped outside the window. That shape is used all over the app (`@ui.row([..., spacer, button], column_gap=...)`); elsewhere it only overflowed by a few pixels of margin, which nobody noticed.
- **Root cause**: in this port, the §9.7 distribution computes `initial_free`, the loop's `remaining`, the auto-margin `free`, and justify-content's `free2` by subtracting only the sum of the items' outer sizes - **never the line's total gap** `gap x (n-1)` - while the positioning pass adds `main_gap` between items. So whenever an item grows, it over-grows by exactly `gap x (n-1)`.
- **Minimal judge** (pinned in `modules/yoga-mbt/src/layout_wbtest.mbt`): row width 100, `column_gap=10`, children [fixed 30, grow 1] - the grow item must be 60; before the fix it came out 70 (row total 110).
- **Fix**: compute `gap_total = main_gap x (line.length()-1)` once before the line is distributed and subtract it in all four free-space computations (spec: gaps occupy space and do not participate in grow/shrink).
- **Measured** (headless probe over the real page tree; window 1010, sidebar 180, viewport 830): before, spacer=728 and the button at `x=962 w=50`, right edge 1012 - past both the window (1010) and the scrollbar (1002); after, spacer=712 and the button at `x=946..996`, exactly on the row's right edge (content 802 + 14 padding per side).
- **Verification**: yoga-mbt `moon test src` 102/102 (including the new pin), library-wide 916/916, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and the examples module clean, `moon build examples/showcase --target native` passes. This is a third, independent defect - stacked with the strip wrapping and the viewport min-size it produced the collapsed content plus clipped button seen on device.

## The section strip never wrapped: `Style` gains `flex_wrap` (2026-10-14)

- **Symptom**: in showcase the top section strip (segmented) overflows horizontally and gets clipped once a page has many sections, even though `shared/section.mbt` documents "wraps when too wide".
- **Root cause**: `ui` never writes `flex_wrap`, and the style layer had **no such property** (CSS has `flex-wrap`; our `Style` was missing it), so every container is `NoWrap` - the comment described a capability that does not exist.
- **Fix (added to the style layer per "property name = CSS property name", not as another component parameter)**: `Style` gets `flex_wrap : FlexWrap?` (the short type name comes from `pub using @yoga {type FlexWrap}`), the factory takes `flex_wrap=`, `merge` / `empty_style` / the setter follow, and `apply` routes it to `Node::set_flex_wrap`.
- **Two companions wrapping needs (the first device build shipped only `flex_wrap` and it squashed the content)**:
  1. **Put the strip in a column, not a row**: a row does not stretch its cross axis, so the wrapping container sized to its content (measured: 450 of 1000 available) and the segments folded into six or seven lines. A column stretches children across by default, so the strip gets the full width and folds only when it truly needs to.
  2. **Give the wrapping container - and every level of its parent chain - an explicit `min_height=0` (and `min_width=0`)**: per spec §4.5 a column child's `min-height:auto` resolves to its **min-content height**, and the min-content height of a wrapping row container is *all children stacked* (6 segments x30 + 5 gaps = 200). That floor propagates upward - measured: strip 224 px, content area down from 646 to 476. The engine is spec-correct, not buggy; explicit zeros are how a strip like this is meant to be written.
- **Final shape**: `strip = @ui.col([@ui.segmented(names, selected, class=[style(flex_wrap=Wrap, row_gap=4.0), style(min_height=0.0, min_width=0.0)])], padding=10.0, class=[style(min_height=0.0, min_width=0.0)])`.
- **Verification**: `moon test style` 26/26 with 1 new layout pin (a 100-wide row container with two 60-wide children: `NoWrap` keeps the second at `top=0`, `flex_wrap=Wrap` moves it to `top>=20`), plus 1 new yoga-mbt pin freezing the §4.5 floor and its escape (same tree: `min:auto` gives the strip height 200, explicit `min_height=0` gives 30). After the fix a headless probe over the real page tree (1200x700) measures: strip 54, content container 646, and on the code page viewport 652 vs content 808 (155 px scrollable) - the strip no longer eats height, and a definite viewport with taller content scrolls. `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, library-wide 915/915, yoga-mbt green.

## Scroll viewport was stretched by its own content, so nothing could scroll (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: in showcase a section body taller than the window was simply cut off at the bottom - neither the wheel nor the scrollbar had anything to move.
- **Root cause**: `scroll_view` never set `min-height` on its viewport node. Yoga flex items default to **min-size = auto**, so the viewport was pushed out to its content's natural height: `view_h == content_h`, and `max() = content_h - view_h` is always 0 - the wheel and the bar both conclude there is nothing to scroll. Call sites that pass `handle=fn(v){ v.node.set_flex_grow(1.0) }` only set grow, which does not override that default.
- **Controlled measurement** (headless probe: 20 rows x 20 px inside a 240x140 parent): before `viewport=676 content=676` (viewport equals content height); after `viewport=200 content=676` (viewport stays at the parent's size, content keeps its natural height).
- **Fix**: `scroll_view` now writes `min_height=0` and `min_width=0` on its viewport (closed at the library level, so every call site benefits - showcase section bodies and sidebar, native-app, `virt_list` hosts). Call sites that pass an explicit `height=` are unaffected (an explicit size beats the min).
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 151/151 with 1 new pinned case - with only `flex_grow` and no height: viewport height <= parent, `content_h=400`, `view_h=140`, `max()>200`, and after `scroll_by(60)` the content node really moves up; library-wide 914/914, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, examples module clean. Device check: showcase section bodies and the long sidebar list should now scroll with the wheel and drag with the bar.

## Markdown page, two defects: wrong range unit + rich lines that never wrap (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptoms** (showcase "代码文档 -> Markdown" section): ① in mixed CJK/Latin paragraphs the bold / italic / inline-code / link runs came out as tofu boxes drawn on top of each other; ② the right edge of the text was cut off; ③ the "复制" button on the code row was half outside the visible area; ④ the content area would not scroll vertically (root cause found in the same batch - see the next entry).
- **Root cause ① (unit contract)**: `md_span_ranges` accumulated range endpoints in **UTF-16 code units**, while the `AttributedText` contract is **UTF-8 byte indices** (both the header note in `text/attributed.mbt` and the `tlen` handed to C are bytes; on the Pango side `start_index/end_index` are used as byte indices directly, and the Windows/DWrite side converts them with `ptw_widx`). One CJK character is 3 bytes but 1 code unit, so every range shifted backwards and even split characters mid-cluster - that is the tofu and the overlapping glyphs. Pure-ASCII paragraphs agree in both units, which is why English samples always looked right.
- **Root cause ② (no wrapping at all)**: `md_rich_label` used `wrap=false` and sized the node to the **natural width of the whole paragraph** (measured: one paragraph came out 656 px inside a `width=480` document). Consequence: the right edge is cut, and that over-wide row stretches the section container past the viewport - the 复制 button sits at the row end, so it is half clipped.
- **Fix ①**: `md_byte_len` (UTF-8 sequence length per code point) now produces the ranges; the doc comment of `md_span_ranges` and the file header say "UTF-8 bytes".
- **Fix ②**: greedy wrapping at construction time - CJK and full-width punctuation break **per character** (Chinese has no spaces, so breaks can only fall between characters), a Latin word plus its trailing space is one unit (no mid-word break), `\n` is a hard break, and a **single unit wider than the line** (long URLs) is hard-cut per character. One `AttributedText` per line; strike-through lines use that line's pixel ranges, and link hit-testing first picks the line by `y`, then the segment by the in-line pixel range. Wrap width is derived per container: `md_narrow(ctx, by)` shrinks it for list items (marker column / checkbox), blockquotes (the bar), footnote definitions and definition lists, while table cells get `max_w=ctx.width/columns`.
- **Toolchain reading**: MoonBit's `String.length()` returns the **UTF-16 code unit count** (`"甲乙"=2`, `"👍"=2`, `"abc"=3`) - neither bytes nor code points - and `String.sub(start=,end=)` is not byte-based either. So neither can serve as a length oracle for a byte-indexed API; byte length has to be computed per code point or via `@utf8.encode`.
