- **Verification**: `moon test mdview` 31/31 with 4 new cases - byte readings (`前👍中` puts the following bold run at `[10,16)`), every endpoint must land on a character boundary, no descendant is wider than the document width and the paragraph is clearly taller than one line, and a long URL must be hard-cut. **The judges were proven to bite**: restoring the old UTF-16 accumulation turns exactly those new cases red (`4 != 10`, "区间终点切进字符内部: 4") and reverting makes them green. Library-wide 913/913, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean.
- **Still needs the device** (user): do the tofu / overlap / cut right edge / clipped copy button disappear? The "content area does not scroll" half was diagnosed in the same batch (viewport stretched by its own content) - see the next entry.

## Visual fix: `badge_dot` now paints a real circle (2026-10-14)

- **Symptom / root cause**: `badge_dot` is documented and named as a round dot badge but drew with `fill_rect` - an 8x8 square. It was recorded as a P2-e boundary item (that batch only moved the style channel, it did not change how anything is painted).
- **Fix**: it now calls `Painter::fill_round_rect` with a default radius of half the short side (inscribed circle). A `border_radius` from `class` wins, clamped to half the short side, so `border_radius=0.0` still gets the old square. The fill colour is still taken from the style slot (`color_of(s.background_color, "")`), so theme switches keep working.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 150/150 with 1 new pixel regression - at 16x16 the top-left cell is empty while the centre has ink (inscribed-circle criterion), and the `class=[style(border_radius=0.0)]` variant puts ink in that corner. On-device look (showcase data page / native-app dots) goes to `_人工测试清单.md`.

## Dragging the splitter handle did not follow the mouse: the delta was taken in the handle\'s own local coordinates, so each frame subtracted the previous frame\'s move (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on the nested-layout page, dragging the `split` handle does not track the pointer — it snaps backwards mid-drag.
- **Root cause**: the drag delta in `splitter_impl` used the **handle\'s local coordinates** (`ev.x` is delivered as `ev.x - w.rect.x` by the host), while the handle itself slides as the first pane widens. Every frame\'s delta therefore lost the previous frame\'s handle displacement: `nw = first0 + (local pointer - local start)`, where the local pointer has already had the *new* handle origin subtracted. Measured (400 wide, ratio 0.5, starting at the handle centre): the first move (pointer +50) lands the handle at 250 and **looks right**; the second move (pointer +60 cumulative) sends it back to **210** — the correct value is 260. The further you drag, the more it snaps back, which is exactly the "it won\'t follow / it bounces back" feel.
- **Fix**: both the start and the move now use **window coordinates** (`ev.x + s.rect.x`), decoupling the delta from the handle\'s own slide. This stays exact even when a batch of events is drained before relayout: the local value is by construction "window minus that same rect.x", so adding the same rect back restores it. Clamping is unchanged (`min_first` / `min_second` still apply).
- **Verification**: new `gui/ui/split_wbtest.mbt` with two tests (this component had **no tests at all** before) — (1) the initial hit must land on the handle, drag +50 -> x0+50, drag a further +10 -> x0+60 (the old code snaps back to 210 here), and after Up further moves change nothing; (2) both minimum widths still clamp (the handle stops at `total - 8 - min_second` = 312). The tests drive the same path the host uses: `dispatch_pointer_to(handle_id, ...)` to emulate the pointer capture window — calling `dispatch_pointer` on the root carries no capture, so the second event hits a different pane instead (that is how the first draft of the test false-failed). **The judge\'s bite was proven by a control experiment**: replacing `win = at + origin` with `win = at` turns the second-move assertion red immediately (measured 210). `moon test ui` 160/160.
- **The remaining half**: `@core.invalidate()` broadcasts to the whole window (`host_repaint_all` sets `dirty_full`), so one drag step costs one full-viewport repaint — around a hundred milliseconds per frame in debug builds. That is the frame-cost item already listed under "known differences" (TODO §6.6), not a coordinate problem; re-check the feel after this fix before deciding whether it needs its own work.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): does the handle track the pointer now (fast back-and-forth drags without snap-back), do both minimum-width clamps still hold, and does it stop following after release.

## The GTK backend left the window-enhancement calls empty: "set title" and "borderless" did nothing (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on the window page only "fullscreen" works; "set title" and "toggle borderless" do nothing at all, and "restore size" shows no effect either.
- **Root cause (three different ones)**: (1) set title / borderless — the **GTK branch of `gui/win/stub.c` still held the W20e empty implementations** (`wm_set_title` / `wm_set_borderless` were just `(void)id;`); the section comment at the time declared them "internal business of the Linux backend, not on the roadmap", while the Win32 branch implemented both, so the MoonBit facade call was harmless and painted nothing. (2) Restore size — `wm_window_set_size` **is real** on GTK (`gtk_window_resize`), but the demo button hardcoded `set_size_pixels(1280, 720)`, which is exactly the showcase\'s launch size: unless you drag-resize first there is nothing to observe, a missing judge in the demo rather than a backend gap. (3) Fullscreen — implemented on both branches, which is why it was the only one that "worked".
- **Fix**: (1) GTK now calls `gtk_window_set_title` (with three guards: id out of range, `win->window == NULL`, `title == NULL`) and `gtk_window_set_decorated(on ? FALSE : TRUE)`; the remaining calls in that block — icon, minimum size, custom-titlebar drag region — **stay empty on purpose**, with the reasons written into the section comment (a GdkPixbuf source is not an HICON handle; `geometry_hints` is not wired; `begin_move_drag` needs the pointer and button of the press, which this stack never forwards to the window layer), so nothing looks "not obviously broken". (2) The demo button became a two-step toggle 1280x720 <-> 960x540 (renamed "尺寸切换", section copy updated) so every click has a visible result. (3) The MoonBit docs for `set_title` / `set_borderless` now **name each backend** (Win32 recomputes the frame from style bits / GTK uses `set_decorated` / macOS still empty) instead of "no-op on non-Windows".
- **Verification**: window-level actions cannot be judged headlessly (they need a real window plus a window manager), so per rule 6 they go to real-hardware re-check. Gate readings for this batch: `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, library-wide 937/937, `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links (gtk-3 is already on the link line).
- **Rule**: when a backend lacks a capability, **the demo must not ship the button** (this repo already does that for "maximize/restore" and "always on top", absent from the Win32 section). An empty implementation may stay in the facade — the cross-platform shape is the same — but the demo and the docs must name which backend does not carry it.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): "set title" should change the title bar to 组件库演示板 · 标题已改 (also proving the UTF-8 title path); "toggle borderless" should drop the title bar and frame and come back on the second click; "尺寸切换" should visibly alternate the two sizes; fullscreen should behave as before.

## `alert(closable=true)` had a hit zone but no mark: an invisible hit zone is not a button (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom / root cause**: the `on_draw` of `alert(..., closable=true)` painted only the light background, the 3 px accent bar and the title/description; the `closable` branch **installed only a 24x24 `on_pointer` hot zone in the top-right corner and never drew a single pixel of a mark**. The demo text says "click the ✕ on my right", yet there was nothing there — hence the report "icon rendering is broken". Unrelated to the icon-sizing batch (that one drew glyphs at 176% of the box and got clipped); here the mark was simply missing.
- **Fix**: the mark now shares its source of truth with the hot zone and is painted in the same `on_draw` — `@icons.draw_icon(p, @icons.IconKind::Close2, r.w - 12.0, 12.0, 6.0, main)`: centred in the 24x24 zone, glyph radius 6 (ink ~10.6 px, the "s is the content radius" contract), coloured with the banner\'s main colour `main` (a semantic token, re-read per frame, so it follows theme switches). Naming note: the bare ✕ is `Close2` (font_class `close`); `CircleClose` / `CloseCircleFill` are the circled variants — do not mix them.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 158/158; the old "close hot zone only in the top-right" test became "hot zone and mark both only in the top-right" with two judges — inside the 24x24 corner of a closable banner, pixels differing from the light background must exceed 8, and the same corner of a non-closable banner must be exactly 0 (no stray ink). Ink is filtered by "sum of the three channel deltas from the background > 30", the sampled range avoids the 3 px accent bar, and no concrete colour is pinned (theme switches cannot false-red it). **The judges\' bite was proven by a control experiment**: deleting the `draw_icon` line turns the mark judge red immediately (`false` is not true); restoring it turns green. Library-wide 932/932, `cd examples && moon build showcase --target native` links. The repo-wide `--deny-warn` gate is still blocked by the 5 sys warnings from parallel batch 60e094e (4 in `appfind.mbt`, 1 in `recent_files.mbt`), which are not files in this batch.
- **Ruled out along the way**: the two "✕" in the demo copy are **U+2715 characters inside running text**, and this machine covers that code point in DejaVu Sans and WenQuanYi Micro Hei (measured with `fc-list :charset=2715`) — they are not missing-glyph tofu; the three red rectangles in the screenshot are the user\'s own annotations.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): the closable banner on the feedback page should now show a ✕ at its right end and dismiss when clicked; non-closable banners must show no mark.

## The hard shadow piled up into dark blobs at the four corners: neither backend has reverse arcs, so the "outer ring minus inner ring" trick never cut a hole (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on the 过渡与效果 page, the `box_shadow=8` card shows a dark blob in each of its four corners while the four straight sides look like an even ring.
- **Root cause**: `Painter::box_shadow` built each band as "outer ring + reversed inner ring + non-zero-winding fill", with the inner ring traced by `round_rect_path_ccw`, which expressed the reverse direction by feeding **decreasing angles** to `arc`. But `arc`'s `ccw` parameter **does nothing on either backend** (the doc comment in `painter.mbt` says so: `cairo_arc` normalises `ea < sa` into the long clockwise sweep, and the pure raster replicates that normalisation) - so every inner segment actually swept 270°, the inner boundary never landed where it should, the corner was never cut out, and the stacked bands overlapped at the corners. Offscreen PNG comparison: even a single layer shows the corner blobs, and more layers make them darker (16 layers gives solid black circles).
- **Fix**: draw **one stroke** instead - line width = `spread`, centreline offset by `spread/2`, which tiles exactly `[0, spread]`; each backend uses its own stroke path (wide strokes are already a `stroke_polys` capability in the raster backend, the same route every border takes). The `layers` parameter was dropped along the way: all bands share the same alpha and never overlap, so it only chopped one uniform band into N segments with no visual effect. The now-unused `round_rect_path_ccw` helper was deleted (no references outside the package).
- **The other report on that page (font size never changes)**: not a transition problem - the demo wrote `font_size` on the **container**'s `class`, while state buckets are per widget, so a parent's hover never swaps a child's font size. It now lives on the text widget's own slot, and the text spans the whole block so a pointer anywhere inside hits the text itself.
- **Verification**: the render `box_shadow` regression now samples the **four corners at 45°** (one point per corner between the inner and outer boundary - the old drawing either missed it or over-painted it) plus "body interior and far outside must be 0"; `moon test render ui` 206/206, library-wide 939/939, `--deny-warn` clean under both `cd gui` and `cd examples`, `moon build showcase --target native` links, and the rendered comparison confirms the blobs are gone with an even ring all round.
- **Rule**: **fills that need a reversed subpath (cutting a hole) do not work on either backend of this stack** - `arc` only sweeps forward. Cutting a hole means polyline approximation (the precedent is the chart donut) or switching to a stroke; prefer shapes that do not depend on winding.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): the shadow card should show an even ring with no dark corners; hovering 字号+底色 should jump the font from 13 to 17 in **one step** (font size is not interpolated) while the background still eases over 300 ms.

## The code "copy" button claimed success but nothing reached the clipboard: the message threw away the return value, and a cross-process judge was missing (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: clicking 复制 on a code block showed "代码已复制 ✓", yet Ctrl+V elsewhere produced nothing.
- **Root cause 1 (the message)**: `examples/showcase/shared/section.mbt` did `let _ = @sys.clipboard_set_text(...)` - **the Bool was discarded**, so success and failure printed the same line and the toast had nothing to do with reality. (The write path itself had moved to GtkClipboard in the previous batch.)
- **Root cause 2 (missing judge)**: the existing clipboard regression is an **in-process** write-then-read, which cannot prove "another program can read it" - on X11 the CLIPBOARD selection is answered by the owning process, so once that process exits (and no clipboard manager holds it) outside readers get nothing. **A green in-process test can coexist with a broken cross-process path**, so this class of capability needs a second process as a witness.
- **New judge**: `experiment/clipboard_probe` - it calls `@win.init_backend()` first (same shape as a real GUI app: the GTK branch runs `gtk_init_check`), writes, then **stays alive for six seconds** while an external process reads back. Measured here (external reader: a separate python-gi process): `backend=true / available=true / set_ok=true`, the outside read returned all 100 characters including Chinese, curly quotes, a double backslash, a tab and the trailing marker, and `PROBE-AFTER-HOLD` matched the write exactly - **the Linux GtkClipboard write path is sound across processes**, so what was broken here was the reporting, not the write.
- **One correct behaviour not to chase as a bug**: in a plain command-line process (window backend never initialised) `clipboard_available()` is false and the code falls back to the xclip command line; **xclip is not installed on this machine**, so such a process genuinely fails to write - that is semantic degradation, not a defect. Reproducing in-app behaviour requires `init_backend` first, otherwise probe readings mislead (my first probe run read `available=false` and nearly sent me down the wrong path).
- **Fix**: the button now branches on the real result - success prints 代码已复制 ✓, failure prints 复制失败：剪贴板不可用 (Danger). The other `let _ = clipboard_set_text` in the repo sits in `gui/ui/clipboard.mbt`'s kernel slot: the kernel's `copy()` has no reporting channel and the internal clipboard still backstops a failure, so it stays as designed.
- **Verification**: `moon check clipboard_probe --deny-warn` clean, `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links, no library changes (core+ui+sys re-run 486/486). The visible outcome (pasting in a browser) goes to real-hardware re-check per rule 6 (checklist S15).
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): click 复制 on a code block, then Ctrl+V in a browser/editor - the same code should appear. If it now says 复制失败：剪贴板不可用, paste that text back to me: it would mean the in-app probe is not reaching GtkClipboard, a different root cause - but at least now the two are distinguishable.

## "Reveal in file manager" opened the folder but selected nothing: the two channels have to meet in core (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on XFCE, pressing "在文件管理器显示 TODO.md" opened the folder in Thunar, but `TODO.md` was not selected.
- **Root cause**: the Linux branch of `@sys.reveal_in_file_manager` is simply `xdg-open <parent dir>`, and its own doc comment said "the bus channel's open-and-select arrives with the DBus line; for now everything falls back to opening the parent directory (selection lost)" - yet that half **already lives in `@dbus`** (`filemanager_available` / `show_items` / `file_uri`, with test coverage for the percent-encoding); **nobody had connected the two**. The layering is why they could not connect on their own: `@dbus` depends on `@sys`, so sys cannot import dbus back - the meeting point can only be core.
- **External measurement (this machine)**: `gdbus` answers `(true,)` for `NameHasOwner org.freedesktop.FileManager1` (Thunar registers it), and `thunar --help` has **no** `--select`-style switch - the bus is the only channel that can carry a selection, so falling back to `xdg-open` necessarily loses it.
- **Fix**: new `gui/core/reveal.mbt` with `@core.reveal_in_file_manager(path)`: on Linux, when FileManager1 is online it calls `show_items([reveal_uri(path, cwd)])`, and only when the name is absent or the call fails does it fall back to the `@sys` command-line path; Windows still goes through sys's direct `explorer /select`. The pure helper `reveal_uri(path, cwd)` is exposed separately so it can be pinned by tests (the selection hinges on exactly this string: absolute path plus percent-encoding). The sys doc now names itself as the fallback ("need selection? use `@core`"), and both demo call sites (the showcase's external section and systemprobe's reveal) call the core version.
- **Verification**: one new core URI regression (relative path absolutization, space, Chinese - three cases) -> `moon test core` 138/138, library-wide 939/939, `--deny-warn` clean under both `cd gui` and `cd examples`, and `moon build showcase` / `moon build systemprobe` link. The visible outcome (the highlighted row in Thunar) goes to real-hardware re-check per rule 6 (checklist S14).
- **Rule**: **when two channels live in two layers, the meeting point must be the layer that sees both**, and the docs must name what the fallback loses (here: the selection). Otherwise the capability is fully present while the behaviour stays on the fallback path and simply looks "not implemented".
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): from the showcase's external section, revealing TODO.md should open the repository root with **TODO.md selected**; systemprobe's reveal (`/tmp`) should select `/tmp` itself.

## The Linux tray "never started": the demos gated tray creation on an icon handle (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on Linux the tray icon never appeared in showcase / native-app; the in-page note stayed at "建图标失败".
- **Root cause**: `wm_icon_from_bgra` is **a W20f failure stub on the GTK branch** (HICON is a Win32 concept; it always returns handle 0), so `@win.icon_from_bgra` yields `None` - and both demos wrapped the whole creation in `match icon_from_bgra(...) { Some(ic) => Tray::new(...)  None => note a failure }`, meaning **`Tray::new` was never reached on Linux**. This is not a library gap: the SNI backend never reads that handle (its icon comes from a panel theme icon name or the library's built-in bitmap - see `Tray::set_icon_name` and `default_icon_pixmap` inside `Item::create`).
- **Evidence chain** (three independent legs): (1) externally, `gdbus` reports `NameHasOwner org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher` as `(true,)` on this session (the XFCE panel's notification area is up); (2) the four pre-existing "real bus" SNI tests in `moon test dbus` (multi-item paths, DBusMenu routing, symmetric cleanup on unregister, reconnect self-heal) all pass on this machine, so the registration chain works; (3) reading `Tray::new`: whenever `sni_watcher_present()` is true it goes through `@dbus.Item::create`, independent of the `icon` argument.
- **Fix (demos only)**: the handle is now **passed through instead of used as a gate** - `let ic = icon_from_bgra(...)` then `Tray::new(id, icon=ic)` directly; on success the handle stays in the holder (a Win32 HICON must outlive the tray), and it is freed only on failure (on Linux `ic` is `None` anyway, so both paths go through Option).
- **Rule**: **an optional, platform-specific input must not double as a capability switch** - handle-style resources may be permanently empty on another backend, and using them as a precondition blocks that platform's entire path. This is the mirror image of "don't ship a button for a capability no backend has": here a button that should have worked was hidden.
- **Verification**: `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase` and `moon build native-app` both link; no library changes (library-wide 938/938, see the commit). The **visible outcome** on Linux (icon in the panel, left-click counter, right-click menu) goes to real-hardware re-check per rule 6 (checklist S13).
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): open the tray section of the system page - the note should read 已建（sni） and an icon should appear in the XFCE notification area; left click should increment, 挂菜单 should give a right-click menu, 改提示 should change the tooltip. If the icon still does not appear, paste the note text - that would be a different root cause.

## The periodic timer could not be stopped: the self-rearm dropped the new handle, so cancel hit an already-fired id (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: in the timer block of the system-integration page, the "hint after 1.5s" one-shot worked and "start periodic (1s)" ticked, but pressing "stop" left the counter climbing.
- **Root cause**: the demo's periodic timer is **self-rearming at the tail of the callback** (this stack's `timer` hands out one-shot handles), and the re-arm line read `let _ = a.timer(1000, tick.val)` - **the new handle was discarded**, while `tick_id` still held the very first one. That id had already been removed from the queue on the previous tick, so `cancel_timer(stale)` found nothing and cancelled nothing; the timer kept ringing. `Loop::cancel` is not at fault: it looks the id up and returns false when absent, which is the correct semantics.
- **Fix**: record the handle on every re-arm (`tick_id.val = a.timer(1000, tick.val)`), same as the initial arming; the demo's code sample now says "re-arm each tick and store the new handle, otherwise it cannot be stopped".
- **Rule**: one-shot handle + self-rearm means **the handle must be refreshed every tick**. Like the Store dedup rule, this is a constraint the shape itself carries, so it belongs in design.md section 6 and in the component samples, not only in a commit message.
- **Verification**: a new `gui/core/loop_wbtest.mbt` test pins the semantics - after several ticks, (1) cancelling the first (already-fired) id leaves the timer ringing and (2) only cancelling the newest id stops it (`hits` stops growing). `moon test core` 137/137, library-wide 938/938, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): start the periodic timer, press stop - the counter should freeze at once; pressing stop again should report "not running"; restarting it should still be stoppable.

## Linux clipboard now goes through GtkClipboard - and that exposed a missing `moonbit_make_bytes` declaration truncating pointers in the win stub (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on this machine (Ubuntu 24.04 / X11 / XFCE) the clipboard was reported unavailable - the read/write buttons on the system-integration page did nothing and Ctrl+C/X/V in inputs never reached the system clipboard.
- **Root cause 1 (missing capability)**: the only Linux backend was the command line `xclip`, and **xclip is not installed here** (`which xclip` empty), so `clipboard_available()` was permanently false, `ui`'s `attach_system_clipboard` never installed a slot, and every facade read/write degraded to failure.
- **Fix 1**: the GTK branch of `win/stub.c` gains `wm_clipboard_available / get_text / set_text` (GtkClipboard), and the sys side externs straight into the win stub - **the same precedent as the file dialog's `wm_file_*`** (the MoonBit layer does not import the win package; prebuild ships the link line). Probe order is GTK then xclip; after `gtk_clipboard_set_text` the code also calls **`gtk_clipboard_store`** (registering ownership alone is not enough - without the store, other apps lose the content once we exit); the watcher's `cbw_get_text` follows the same preference and thereby drops a subprocess per poll tick. Win32 and the placeholder branch keep identically named symbols (Windows' real path stays in `sys_stub.c`'s CF_UNICODETEXT, untouched). The demo adds `@ui.use_system_clipboard(true)` (previously only native-app opted in).
- **Root cause 2 (ABI, shaken out by the first)**: **`win/stub.c` called `moonbit_make_bytes` without declaring it** - the GTK branch is compiled by prebuild directly, which never gets moon's `moonbit.h` include path (`compile_stub` only adds pkg-config cflags). An implicit declaration assumes an `int` return, so on x86-64 the returned pointer is cut to its low 32 bits. **Proven by disassembly**: with the declaration removed, `wm_clipboard_get_text` contains `mov %eax,%edi` (passing a 32-bit return value on as a 64-bit pointer argument), while this machine's heap addresses are of the form `0x61bc76xxxxx` - truncated, i.e. garbage. Adding `extern void *moonbit_make_bytes(int size, int value);` (the same line `sys_stub.c:57` already has; matching `~/.moon/include/moonbit.h`'s `moonbit_bytes_t moonbit_make_bytes(int32_t, int)` bit for bit) leaves the TU with zero implicit-declaration warnings.
- **A pre-existing hazard fixed on the way**: `wm_file_dialog_get_result` (the Linux file-dialog result path) had been returning a truncated pointer all along - it was simply never reached by a headless test, because it takes a real file selection to trigger.
- **Control experiment**: declaration removed + forced relink -> `moon test sys` dies with SIGSEGV; restored -> 188/188. **Trap worth recording**: changing only the C in a stub does **not** make moon relink the test executable (dirtiness is tracked per .mbt package), which is why the first control run falsely passed; a `touch` of some .mbt source (or deleting the archive plus dirtying the package) is required for the comparison to count.
- **Readings and rule**: the GTK path really read this user's clipboard in the test run (the watcher printed `cbw_get_text: Ok (13 chars) ...`). Note that `gdk_display_get_default()` returns non-NULL even in a test process that never ran gtk_init (GDK opens lazily from `$DISPLAY`), so the Linux probe is also true under headless tests - clipboard regressions must therefore be written as "if available, do a save -> set -> get -> restore round trip and never pin the user's content" (which `clipboard_wbtest.mbt` already is, so a backend swap cannot false-red it).
- **Verification**: `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, library-wide 937/937, `moon build showcase --target native` links. Real-hardware checklist gains S11 (the system page's read/write buttons, and Ctrl+C/V interoperating with external apps).
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): write from the system page and paste into a browser/terminal to see the same Chinese text; the page's read button should pick up text copied elsewhere; Ctrl+C/X/V inside inputs should interoperate with the system; and after quitting the app the clipboard content should still be there (that is the `store` call).

## The section strip was squeezed flat by its own body: a fixed-height header needs flex_shrink=0 while the stretchable body needs min=0 (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom**: on the 代码文档 page, switching the segmented control to the Markdown section made the strip at the top of the page collapse in height, so the labels poked out of the band; the 代码高亮 section looked fine.
- **Root cause**: the page assembly is a column holding "strip + body column (`grow=1.0`)", and both sides were given `min=0` (the strip got it in the previous batch, for "wrap when too wide": the CSS §4.5 main-axis `min:auto` of a wrapping container equals its min-content height, which otherwise eats the whole body height). **Zeroing the minimum without locking the shrink** leaves the reverse channel open: the body column was *not* given `min=0`, so its min-content height is the entire body (the Markdown section is the whole document, ~2000 px), the window is only 720, and once the parent column overflows yoga puts **all of the shrink onto the only freely-shrinkable layer — the strip** (min=0, while yoga-mbt defaults `flex_shrink` to **1.0** — `modules/yoga-mbt/src/style.mbt:53`). Sections with short bodies never trigger it, which is why it looked like switching tabs caused it.
- **Headless repro readings** (temporary probe in `gui/ui`, structure identical to the assembly: root 300 tall, strip 24 + 10 padding, body 2000): the strip measured **23.32** (natural height 44, so the 24-px label sticks out); after "strip `set_flex_shrink(0.0)` + body column `min=0`" it measured **44**. The probe was deleted after measuring; the fix landed in `examples/showcase/shared/section.mbt`.
- **Rule**: in a column with "fixed header + stretchable body", lock the two things separately — **header: shrink off** (`flex_shrink=0`), **body: shrink allowed** (`min_height=0` + `grow`), and let the body's `scroll_view` absorb the overflow. `min=0` alone is necessary but not sufficient: it lets a container wrap, and at the same time makes it the only layer that can absorb shrink.
- **This stack's boundary**: the `Style` field set has `flex` (=grow) but no `flex_shrink`, so locking the header goes through `handle=` with `w.node.set_flex_shrink(0.0)` (same route as the `scroll_view` content column). Whether to add `flex_shrink` to the style factory is tracked separately (design.md §6).
- **Verification**: `cd examples && moon build showcase --target native` passes; this batch touches only the demo assembly, no library changes. The repo-wide `--deny-warn` gate is still blocked by 5 sys warnings from the parallel batch 60e094e (4 in `gui/sys/appfind.mbt`, 1 in `recent_files.mbt`), which are not files in this batch.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): after switching to the Markdown section the strip should keep its fixed height (labels no longer poking out), the strip should still wrap in a narrow window, in-section scrolling should be unchanged; also click through the other 14 pages to see whether any other fixed-height band gets squeezed.

## A thousand scatter points merged into a solid band: dot diameter must respect "dot area x point count <= plot area" (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom / root cause**: `scatter_t` defaulted to `dot=6.0` and painted opaque `fill_rect` squares. The demo pushes 2000 points into a 320x260 chart (plot area 260x224), so the dots' total area, 2000x36 = 72000 px^2, was already 1.24x the plot area — **the default dot size never matched the point count**. Neighbouring points bit into each other and the whole correlation band came out as one solid mass with no readable shape; squares also eat the pi/4 of visual whitespace, leaving no gap in dense regions.
- **Fix**: (1) default `dot` drops to **3.0**, and the sizing rule is now written into the component docs (dot area x count <= plot area; pass a larger value explicitly); (2) the dot is drawn with `Painter::fill_round_rect` (radius = half the short side) = **circle**, which leaves gaps between points by construction; (3) the point layer is wrapped in `set_global_alpha(0.7)` (inside the existing `save()`/`restore()`), so overlaps darken by accumulation instead of plastering, and sparse areas still show the grid. The trend line and grid are unaffected (drawn before the alpha change).
- **Measured readings** (offscreen `Painter::new(Bitmap)` session; the point mask is "closer to the dot colour than to the panel colour", which excludes grid lines and tick labels by nature): a single `dot=15` point inks a 14x14 bounding box whose four corners are clean and whose centre is inked (old code: 15x15 with **all four corners inked** = square); with 2000 points at the default, pixels at the pure dot colour number 201 (old code: 20189 = 35% of the 58240-px plot area, i.e. solid). Both judges are now regression tests in `moon test chart`.
- **Toolchain readings** (moon 0.1.20260920): (1) the demo's LCG cannot use `% 2147483647` to get a positive remainder — **MoonBit's `%` takes the sign of the dividend**, and once `seed * 1103515245` overflows int64 and wraps negative the remainder is negative, so the scatter spread into the negative half-axis (real-hardware reading: the axes became -100..100 while the comment said 0..100). Masking with `& 0x7fff_ffff` removes the overflow and the sign in one step. (2) **Operators cannot start a line** in a multi-line expression: `a + b\n< c` fails with `[3002]`/`[4139]`; the comparison has to move into an intermediate `let`.
- **Verification**: `moon test chart` 190/190 (one new two-judge regression). **The judges' bite was proven by a control experiment**: restoring the old painting path and default (`fill_rect` + `dot=6.0`) makes the corner judge true and the pure-colour count 20189, above the bound; the fix turns both green. Library 931/931, `cd examples && moon build showcase --target native` links. The repo-wide `--deny-warn` gate is currently blocked by 5 warnings from the parallel batch (60e094e, sys platform batch 3: a duplicated `#borrow` in `gui/sys/appfind.mbt`, an unused `ffi_file_mtime`, two deprecated string-slice spellings); those files are not touched here.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): both scatter panels should read as "a cloud of points" rather than a solid band, the trend line should stay legible, and the small panel in `native-app` (250x130) should not regress.

## Icon glyphs were drawn at 176% of their box and then clipped: the content-radius contract belongs in the component layer (2026-10-14, real-hardware report)

- **Symptom / root cause**: parameter 5 of `@icons.draw_icon` has always been a **content radius** (ink ≈ 1.76×s, since path data is normalized to ±1000 and scaled by `s*0.88/1000`), but `@ui.icon` and `@ui.icon_button` passed the **box edge** as that radius (`size` / `icon_size`), so glyphs were drawn at 176% of the box's short side — and the host clips each widget to its rect, so all four edges were cut. Offscreen pixel probe (ink bounding box measured on a `Painter::new(Bitmap)` session): `icon(size=24)` reported a 24×24 box that the glyph **filled exactly** (unclipped it is 44×44 — calling `draw_icon(...,24.0,...)` directly reads `10,10-53,53`), and `icon_button(box_size=28, icon_size=16)` likewise filled its 28-px box. That is the reported "glyphs burst out of their container, edges sliced, container and content out of agreement". The three other direct call sites already used the radius convention and were unaffected: `message_box` (24-px box, s=11), the `overlay` snackbar (s=7), `nav` sidebar items (s=6.72).
- **Fix**: `@ui.icon` now derives the radius from the **live rect** (`r.w.min(r.h) / 2.0`), so the glyph follows the box even when `class` overrides width/height; `icon_button` passes `icon_size / 2.0`, keeping the button box (`box_size`) and the glyph box (`icon_size`) separate concerns — the default 28/16 pair inks ≈16 px with more than 6 px of margin. The doc comments on `draw_icon` and `icon` state the contract at the entry point ("s is the content radius = half the box edge", "the glyph is centered with 88% optical margin of the short side"). Nothing was needed on the data side: all 803 paths were scanned, each fills the ±1000 space and its geometric center is off by at most 60 fixed-point units (~3% of the box), so no icon is lopsided or under-sized.
- **Toolchain readings** (moon 0.1.20260920): `println` takes exactly one `String` (more arguments give `[4015]`); `debug_printline` has no overload for mixed "4 Int + 2 Double" payloads (`%g` is not accepted either, `[4021]`) — multi-value diagnostics go through string concatenation.
- **Verification**: `moon test ui` 158/158, one new pixel regression — `icon(size=24)` must ink inside `[1,1]-[22,22]` with equal horizontal/vertical centers (measured `1,1-22,22`), and `icon_button(box_size=28, icon_size=16)` inside `[5,5]-[22,22]` with a width of 10..17 (measured `6,6-21,21`). **The judge's bite was proven by a control experiment**: restoring the old arguments (`size` / `icon_size` passed directly) turned it red immediately (`a.0=0`, `b.0=0`, i.e. ink pinned to the clipped box edge); the fix turns it green again. Library 13 packages 926/926, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links.
- **Pending real-hardware re-check** (user): the large icons in empty/result states, the sidebar light/dark toggle (moon glyph), the icon wall page (`size=30`), and the small prefix/suffix and table-sort icons — none should break out of its container, none should have sliced edges, all should sit centered.

## Declarative frontend P5: `transition` property animation (bucket interpolation + frame driver, 2026-10-14)

- **The interpolation set is drawn along the "read from the style slot every frame" line**: only `color` / `background_color` / `border_color` / `border_width` / `border_radius` animate. The reason is not convenience - any layout property (font size, line height, sizes, padding) that animates forces a full relayout every frame, while `opacity` / `box_shadow` take effect through `apply` writing `Widget.alpha_paint` / `shadow_paint`, so interpolating the slot would paint nothing. Properties outside the set **switch immediately**: `step` compares a projection of the animatable fields and settles when the projection is unchanged.
- **State machine rules**: ① the first time a widget is seen, only the target is recorded - no animation (otherwise building or remounting a tree would fade the whole page in); ② when the target changes mid-flight the animation continues from the **currently displayed value** (`from = shown`), so reversing a half-finished hover does not jump; ③ the same target keeps the original clock while non-animatable fields follow the new target; ④ `transition_ms` absent or 0 settles at once and **leaves no account behind** (`style_for` calls `forget_anim`). Easing is smoothstep (`t*t*(3-2t)`).
- **One timer drives all animations**: a process-level `@core.set_timeout(16)` starts on demand, each tick only calls `@core.invalidate_id(id)` for widgets still animating (the window that owns the id gets just that rect; only when no window owns it does it fall back to a whole-window broadcast), and the timer stops as soon as everything converged - no per-widget timer, no idle spinning. Values are computed from **elapsed real time**, not tick counts, so dropped frames cost frames, not speed. An account that never got painted (its block hidden) hands over the endpoint value and marks itself dirty once on the expiry tick, leaving no long tail.
- **How the two colour endpoints are taken**: `Var` tokens resolve against the current theme before mixing (`@theme.mix_hex`), so switching themes mid-animation keeps that run's endpoints on the pre-switch colours - theme switches repaint the whole window anyway, so it is not a visible defect. If either endpoint is missing or is not hex (named colours), `mix_hex` returns an endpoint: middle frames stay on the start colour and the final frame is still the target, so **the wrong colour is never painted**.
- Toolchain readings (moon 0.1.20260920, judged by `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn`):
  - **`pub(all) enum Color` carries no `==`**: a tuple comparison containing `Option[Color]` fails with `[4018] Type Color does not implement trait Eq`. Same family as the existing note (`derive(Eq)` on a value type promotes impl methods to plain methods and warns), so **state comparison goes through a string projection** (`#` / `n` / `@` prefixes for Hex/Named/Var) instead of deriving.
  - **Package-private structs must be declared `priv struct`**: a bare `struct Anim` emits the `missing_priv` warning, which fails under `--deny-warn`.
  - **`}` and the next branch's `{` cannot be split across lines**: writing `} else if cond` then `{` on the following line gives `[3002] Unexpected line break here, missing {` - the brace must end the condition line (same discipline as `test "name" {`).
  - **`moon fmt` rewrites unrelated files**: the committed tree is **not** in this toolchain's fmt shape (`moon fmt style core` touched 42 files, e.g. inserting a blank line before every `pub const` in `keys.mbt`). Format only the files of the current batch and read the diff afterwards; this batch kept to the lines it actually wrote.
- **Verification**: 6 new style tests (lerp endpoints / midpoint / one-sided default, first frame does not animate, the 150 ms reading `#000000 -> #808080 -> #d7d7d7 -> #ffffff`, mid-flight reversal continuing from `#282828`, out-of-set property settling at once, no account left without `transition_ms`) + 1 new core test (`invalidate_id` marks only the owning window, leaves the other window alone, unknown id falls back to whole window), `moon test style core` 159/159, all 13 library packages green at 908/908, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `cd examples && moon check showcase --deny-warn` clean. Demo page: showcase gains a "过渡与效果" page (0/150/600 ms side by side + reversal + the text/effect fields); the on-device look is the user's check.

## Declarative frontend P1: gui/style package and yoga-mbt z_index (2026-10-13)

Toolchain boundaries (moon 0.1.20260920 / moonc v0.10.14 line; re-measure after upgrades, mirrored in design.md §1.6):
- **`alias` / `abbrev` are reserved keywords - there is no function or type-alias syntax.** Short names for external types come from `using @pkg {type X}` only, and the declaration is **package-scoped**: writing it in two files hits `[4051] declared twice`; declare once via `pub using` in one file and the short name serves the whole package and external callers.
- **Labelled arguments have no forwarding channel**: `f(width=width)` fails typing (the in-body optional is `T?`), `f(width?)` / `f(width!)` / `f(...)` are all `[3002]` parse errors, and binding `pub let g = f` then calling `g(width=1.0)` gives `[4117]` ("labelled functions can only be applied directly"); `moon.pkg` imports do not accept key-value form (`unexpected token :`), so package names cannot be shortened either. Conclusion: the factory call site is fixed as `@style.style(...)`, and the draw-property builder inside `.hover/.active/.focus` is written out three times.
- A top-level `priv name = ...` does not parse; package-private values are plain `let`.
- `Array::contains` requires `T: Eq`; test optionals with `is Some(_)` (`is_some()` is deprecated-warned); `Map::new()` is deprecated - use the `Map([])` literal.
- Range loops only accept the `for i in (0..<n)` form; outside `for`, `0..n` / `0..=n` do not parse. This package uses `while` instead.

Architecture decisions:
- **core must not depend on style** (style depends on core's Widget/yoga; a reverse edge would form a cycle). The Widget resolved-Style slot is implemented as a **process-level side table** `Map[widget_id, Style]` inside `@style` (same precedent as overlay_native's handle table): `apply` writes, drawing reads `resolved(w)` / `style_for(w)` (bucket selection by `hovered/pressed/focused`); widgets never applied read back an empty style. **Stale-row cleanup shipped with the P2-e wrap-up**: core gained a process-level `set_widget_discard_hook`; `clear_children` and `remove_child` report every id of the detached subtree, and `@style` installs the hook itself on its first `apply` so those rows disappear - dynamic tree swaps (`bind_node` rebuilds, `for_list` row removal, virtual-list window remounts, overlay teardown) no longer accumulate. **Rule: detaching means discarding; moving must go through `move_child`** (core cannot tell move from drop - the hook only fires on those two APIs). `@style.resolved_count()` exists as the diagnostic, and a regression test pins "remove a widget with a subtree => the count falls back".
- **cursor is a draw-period property and may live in state buckets**; `apply` writes the widget's static `cursor` field directly, bucket cursors are consumed by P2's hover-path cursor dispatch via `style_for`.
- yoga-mbt `z_index` is paint-only: the setter does not `mark_changed` (geometry-independent) and the engine **never physically reorders children** (incremental layout caches by index); it exposes a `Node::paint_order` index projection. On the gui side `Widget.z_index_paint` is written by `apply`, and `widget_paint_order` stable-insertion-sorts kids (identity order when all zeros, so existing examples paint frame-for-frame unchanged).

Verification: style 18/18, yoga-mbt 100/100 (baseline 95 + 5 new), core 120/120, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and repo-wide `moon check` clean; the `set_margin` labelling conversion covered all 14 call sites in one batch - library tests 856/858 (the 2 reds are pre-existing W9/sort gaps, same reds on a stashed clean tree).

## Toasts carried a whole-window background plate: overlays now open content-sized (2026-10-14, on-device feedback)

- **Environment**: Ubuntu 24.04 + X11 + XFCE, `examples/showcase` (the browser page lives in the same `bind_node` tree as the others, so a webview surface is permanently mounted).
- **Symptom**: when a message toast appeared, **the entire window looked covered by an opaque background** on top of the strip itself (user's words: "the message should not have a mask background").
- **Root cause**: `toast_layer` opened its strip with `overlay_show_full`. Once the host window carries a native child surface (the webview), overlays switch to the **native tier** (`overlay_native_tier()` = `Window::has_child_surfaces()`), and the full-window tier opens **a real window** sized to the owner's content area - while every `App` root paints `theme_bg_page()` by default (`core/app.mbt`). The result was a page-coloured window pinned over the main window, with the toast bars drawn on it. The in-tree tier had the same shape: the strip was `absolute` with all four insets 0, so it drew nothing but owned every hit in the window.
- **Fix**: toasts open **content-sized** (`overlay_show_at`, `y=0`, `x` centred on the window width; the centring arithmetic extracted as the pure `toast_left` so it can be pinned by tests). The strip is no longer absolute - a column with `align-items: center` makes its width equal the widest bar by itself. Two supporting changes:
  - core gained `overlay_set_pos(id, x, y)` (window coordinates, moves a **layer-tier** overlay; the native-tier window's position belongs to the window system, so it returns false and leaves it alone) - needed because a later, wider toast has to re-centre the group.
  - the native-tier fit rule grew an exception: after `overlay_fit_pass` spends its three-frame budget it still allows **growing** - if the content gets wider/taller and the box does not follow, the extra content is clipped; shrinking stays budget-limited (no size ping-pong in a width feedback loop).
- **One thing deliberately not changed, recorded here**: the layer is a full-window absolute child of the root, so while *any* overlay is open a press outside the overlays is still consumed by the layer (`core/overlay.mbt`, "click outside dismisses the top tier"). For the ~2 seconds a toast is on screen, a click meant for a page control does not reach it. That is the shared input model of the whole overlay family; this batch only shrank the visual footprint, dispatch is untouched - whether overlays with no "dismiss on outside click" semantics (toasts) should let clicks through is a separate decision.
- **Verification**: `moon test core ui` 299/299 (1 new core test: after `overlay_set_pos` the `overlay_rect_of` lands on the new window coordinates, unknown handles give false; the ui toast case now also asserts the overlay rect is smaller than the window in both axes, plus `toast_left` centring and clamping), library-wide 914/914, `cd gui && moon check --deny-warn` and `cd examples && moon check --deny-warn` clean, `moon build showcase --target native` links. The on-device look (no plate beyond the strip, still centred as bars of different widths arrive) is S18 in `_人工测试清单.md`.

## macOS backend, first batch (mac platform batches 1-8, 2026-10-15; compile/link proven by the CI macOS job, on-device items still pending)

- **Environment**: GitHub Actions `macos-latest` (Apple Silicon; clang and the SDK frameworks come with Xcode CLT). **The development machine here is Ubuntu/X11 and cannot touch the macOS window server**, so "compiles and links" is proven by CI, while "runs and looks right" is on-device work per AGENTS rule 6 (checklist: section T of `_人工测试清单.md`).
- **Branch shape (same "one stub.c, several branches, prebuild macro" rule as Linux/Windows)**: `WM_STUB_MAC` in `gui/win/stub.c` (Cocoa, Objective-C), `WM_STUB_MAC` in `gui/render/stub.c` (ImageIO, plain C), `WM_STUB_MAC` in `gui/text/stub.c` (CoreText, plain C), `WB_HAVE_WKWEBVIEW` in `gui/web/webkit_stub.c` (WebKit, Objective-C). **Every placeholder branch gains a `&& !defined(__APPLE__)` guard**: on macOS the macro-less native-stub pass must compile to an empty translation unit, otherwise it double-defines the prebuild archive. The Darwin branch of prebuild compiles the archives with `cc -x objective-c -fobjc-arc` (`link_configs` has no compile-time fields - `c_flags` is silently ignored, same finding as GTK/Pango) and hands out `-framework` flags per package.
- **Pitfalls CI threw at us on the first round** (worth writing down so they don't recur):
  1. CoreText puts **the text object first** - `CTLineDraw(line, ctx)` - opposite to the `CGContext` family intuition;
  2. `CFAttributedStringReplaceString` / `CFAttributedStringSetAttribute` take a **`CFRange`**, not location + length, and the length comes from `CFAttributedStringGetLength` (`CFStringGetLength` does not compile: different type name);
  3. non-mac one: `kill` in the POSIX arm of `sys_stub.c` was reached through some other header on Linux, while macOS clang treats an implicit function declaration as an error - explicit `#include <signal.h>`;
  4. Cocoa detail: ARC-managed **properties can only be read with `.`** (`s.win`), while the `@public` plain fields of the same struct use `->` - two notations in one function is a real trap; `setAllowsMultipleSelection:` / `setCanChooseDirectories:` are declared on `NSOpenPanel`, not `NSSavePanel` (a save panel is single-choice by nature);
  5. two "warning level but genuinely broken" ones: `NSTextInputClient`'s marked-text method is the **three-part** `setMarkedText:selectedRange:replacementRange:` (writing it with two parts earns only a `-Wprotocol` warning, and the input-method composition channel never connects at runtime), and `resignFirstResponder` returns **`BOOL`** (declaring it `void` is `-Wmismatched-return-types`). The macOS stub gate therefore reads warnings too, not just errors.
- **Pitfalls, rounds two and three - "no longer declared in the current SDK" and "wrong name"**:
  1. `setCanShowHiddenFiles:` (NSOpenPanel), `setSendActionOn:` (the status-item button) and `setApplicationNameForUserAgent:` (WKPreferences) are **not publicly declared on the macOS 26 SDK** (clang reports `no visible @interface` / `no known instance method`), while they do exist on older SDKs. One shape covers all three: an **informal declaration** on `NSObject` keeps the selector visible to the compiler, and every call site asks `respondsToSelector:` first - if the runtime has no such method we fall back to the default behaviour (the show-hidden-files switch, tray right-clicks and the UA suffix stop working) and **we do not guess at replacement names**.
  2. `WKWebsiteDataStore` has no `dataStoreWithURLStoragePath:error:` (that name was inferred from the Linux `WEBKIT_DATA_DIR` semantics and does not compile). The public one is **`+[WKWebsiteDataStore dataStoreForIdentifier:]`** - macOS 14+, takes an `NSUUID`, and needs a bundle identifier (libyue uses exactly this at `nativeui/mac/browser_mac.mm:86`). A directory path cannot be fed to it, so data_dir is hashed into a stable UUID (two FNV-1a 64-bit chains, then RFC 4122 version/variant bits - `NSUUID` only accepts well-formed strings): the same directory keeps one session across runs. Where that cannot be honoured we still fall back to `nonPersistentDataStore` and say so through `wb_persistence()`.
  3. The cookie-store property of `WKWebsiteDataStore` is named **`httpCookieStore`** (`.cookieStore` does not compile; clang's fix-it supplies the right name).
  4. **An Objective-C parameter must not be named `id`**: `wb_evaluate(int64_t id, ...)` shadows the type name, so the block signature `^(id result, NSError *error)` parses as a variable declaration and one parameter name produces a cascade of `type-id cannot have a name` plus a dozen `undeclared identifier` errors.
- **Display enumeration (mac platform batch 8, `sys/monitor.mbt` plus the `__APPLE__` arm of `sys_stub.c`)**: CoreGraphics covers it in one family - `CGGetOnlineDisplayList` ("online" only, i.e. attached and powered; macOS has no public list of attached-but-off displays, so the mac side never yields `connected=false` entries), `CGDisplayBounds` for the origin in points, the scale derived as "current mode pixel width / logical width" to turn origins and sizes into **physical pixels**, `CGDisplayCopyAllDisplayModes` for the mode table (deduplicated by pixel width x height x refresh - CG lists several timing configurations for the same pixel size and duplicates mean nothing to layout; when the table is unavailable the current mode is emitted alone), `CGDisplayScreenSize` for millimetres. **The two mode predicates are no longer declared in the current SDK** (`CGDisplayModeIsCurrentMode` / `CGDisplayModeIsPreferred` - clang says `call to undeclared function`; only these two were pulled from this family, `CGDisplayCopyAllDisplayModes` / `GetPixelWidth` / `GetRefreshRate` are still there): "current" is now decided by "same pixel size and same refresh rate as `CGDisplayCopyDisplayMode`", which appears exactly once in the deduplicated list and is equivalent at reading level; `preferred` has no public signal, so it is reported as 0 rather than faked with the current mode. Three things it cannot give, stated plainly: a name (`NSScreen.localizedName` is out of reach for a plain-C stub and CG has no public name - built-in panels are `Built-in`, others `Display-<CGDirectDisplayID>`, machine-specific strings like the other platforms' output names), millimetres (0 when the engine withholds them), and built-in refresh rates (`CGDisplayModeGetRefreshRate` reports 0 = unknown for some built-in modes; 0 is passed through rather than a faked 60 Hz). `MonitorInfo` needs no scale field: every number in the list is already physical pixels, the same basis as xrandr.
- **Deliberate differences from the other two platforms, now part of the contract**:
  - **Event loop**: no `[NSApp run]`. `wm_wait` uses `nextEventMatchingMask:untilDate:` (AppKit's own blocking fetch), `wm_wakeup` posts one `NSApplicationDefined` marker event to wake it - the macOS equivalent of "the loop belongs to MoonBit" + "no C-to-MoonBit callbacks". Known limit: during window drag / live resize AppKit enters tracking mode and our pump does not see those events (T6b).
  - **Pixel scale**: the stack speaks physical pixels, Cocoa speaks points; one conversion per boundary using the window's screen `backingScaleFactor`. The drawing view deliberately **does not override `isFlipped`** - in a non-flipped view `CGContextDrawImage`/`CTLineDraw` land memory row 0 at the top, which matches this stack's Bitmap; flipping the CTM would render text upside down.
  - **Premultiplication rounding belongs to ImageIO/Quartz**: Quartz composites only in premultiplied space, so an RGB bitmap context cannot give us a non-premultiplied form and the `(c*a+127)/255` step other platforms take is skipped here. Byte order and semantics are unchanged; the difference is +/-1 rounding, and the "encode -> decode -> re-encode is byte-stable" criterion must be read as "stable against ImageIO itself" on macOS.
  - **GIF animation falls back to a still frame on macOS**: per-frame compositing needs the frame rectangle plus disposal method, and disposal is not on Apple's public GIF property list - without on-device proof we do not guess. `img_anim_decode_file` returns 0 and the component shows frame 1.
  - **Menu / tray / notification are app-level**: `wm_menu_apply` becomes `setMainMenu:` (the menu bar belongs to the application, last apply wins), tray is `NSStatusItem`, notification is `NSUserNotification` - **whether a non-bundle binary can actually present a notification is an on-device question** (T4e).
  - **Taskbar progress: unsupported on macOS** - the dock badge is a text counter; it cannot express progress, and we do not fake it.
  - **webview**: `wb_signal_mask()` returns 10 on mac - downloads (need macOS 15 `WKDownload`) and permission requests have no public hook, so we report exactly which proofs are missing; `wb_persistence()` probes "macOS 14+ **and** a bundle identifier", a bare binary returns 0 and falls back to a non-persistent store.
- **Backend choices across `sys`**: public CLI whenever it suffices - `open` / `open -R` (reveal with selection), `sw_vers` / `ioreg` / `sysctl` (system info), `osascript` for `volume settings` and the System Events desktop picture, LaunchAgents plist + `launchctl load/unload` (autostart), `lpstat` / `lp` (macOS ships CUPS - same line as Linux, **a declared choice, not a fall-through to the Linux branch**). Only kernel-ish readings go into the stub: `hw.memsize` + `host_statistics64`, `kern.boottime`, `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` for idle. Four families stay explicitly unsupported behind a platform gate: windowctl (Accessibility requires user consent), nightlight (CoreBrightness is private), powerprofile (`pmset` does not match the three-scheme semantics), media (no MPRIS counterpart).
- **The first real `moon test` run on the macOS job: 4 red out of 851, all "declared contract not followed through in code", not broken plumbing**:
  1. **The animation fallback kept the picture but lost the size**: when `Animation::new_from_file` returns None, `ui/gif.mbt` set no width/height, so the node occupied 0x0 - the image itself decodes fine (`ui_decode_guard()` passes on mac; ImageIO gives the 120x120 first GIF frame), it just could not be laid out. The None branch now follows the still image's size, and both animation tests (`render/image_wbtest`, `ui/basic_wbtest`) became **probe-branching**: no animation support means we assert "the still path yields the first frame + the component queues no self-advance + it paints ink", while platforms that do decode animation keep the full frame-advance assertions (no coverage lost on Linux/Windows).
  2. **The underline assertion counted rows**: `text_decoration` was checked as "one more inked row", but on macOS the 1px line falls inside the last glyph row's line box, so the row count stays equal and the test goes red. It now compares **inked pixel counts**, which is what "was an underline drawn" actually means - one criterion for all three platforms.
  3. **The launcher platform gate had gone stale**: the test still asserted "anything that is not Linux gives `Unsupported`", while batch 5 gave macOS a real `open`-based implementation. Mac now shares the Windows shape: probe with an invalid target and only require "no longer `Unsupported`" (`Ok` or `SpawnFailed`), which opens neither a browser nor a Finder window.
- **How this is verified**: the CI macOS job starts with a **native stub compile gate** - the same clang flags prebuild uses (`-fsyntax-only -O2 -x objective-c -fobjc-arc -D<macro>`) over the four mac stubs plus the plain-C `sys_stub.c`. That step is not optional: `moon check` never compiles those stubs, so an API-shape error only surfaces at `moon test` link time, and there it shows up as a pile of `Undefined symbols` (the archive failed to build, so its link entry is never handed out) with no trace of the real cause. After the gate: `moon check` + 12-package `moon test` + `moon build --target native` + examples/hello link, plus section T on real hardware. **Still unproven**: everything in T1-T6, the real display-enumeration readings, whether launchd picks up a freshly written plist, osascript automation consent, whether notifications appear, Retina appearance, and loop behaviour while dragging a window.

## Maintenance

1. Add new conclusions to the matching section, recording only the pitfall and the fix; protocol-interop conclusions must come from the real bus and real panels — unit-test self-consistency does not count.
2. Keep both language versions (this file and docs/zh/adaptation.md) in sync in the same batch.
